### [1] [Large $N$ limit of the ratio of Lukyanov integrals](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00662)
*Dong Wang*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper analyzes large-N asymptotics of Lukyanov integral ratios for real coupling b ≥ 1 using biorthogonal ensembles and vector Riemann–Hilbert methods; it derives logarithmic ratios via a Fredholm determinant constant term, with separate even/odd N expansions, and verifies hypotheses against the ground-state thermodynamic Bethe ansatz.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To rigorously characterize the large-N behavior of Lukyanov integrals—central in integrable quantum field theory—particularly their ratios, which encode physical one-point functions and require control beyond standard orthogonal polynomial methods.

Method: Exploits the biorthogonal ensemble representation of Lukyanov integrals; applies vector Riemann–Hilbert problem techniques to obtain asymptotics; extracts the logarithmic ratio via its constant term in a large-N expansion; expresses that term as a Fredholm determinant; handles parity-dependent structure by separating even and odd particle numbers.

Result: A locally uniform (in real α) large-N asymptotic expansion of the logarithmic ratio of Lukyanov integrals; explicit formulation of the constant term as a Fredholm determinant; verification that the analytic hypotheses hold for the correction term arising from the physical ground-state thermodynamic Bethe ansatz.

Conclusion: The biorthogonal Riemann–Hilbert framework successfully captures the fine asymptotic structure of Lukyanov integral ratios, confirming consistency with integrable QFT predictions and enabling rigorous access to quantum-field-theoretic one-point functions.

Abstract: We study the large-$N$ asymptotics of ratios of Lukyanov integrals for fixed real coupling $b\geq1$. Our approach uses their biorthogonal ensemble structure and vector Riemann--Hilbert techniques. Under stated analytic hypotheses, we derive the logarithmic ratio through its constant term, which is expressed by a Fredholm determinant. The expansion is locally uniform in the real parameter $\alpha$ and is formulated separately for even and odd particle numbers. We verify the hypotheses for the correction defined by the physical ground-state thermodynamic Bethe ansatz. The relation to quantum-field-theoretic one-point functions is discussed separately.

</details>


### [2] [On Emergent Geometry of the Generalized BGW Theory](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.01507)
*Dingkang Xiong,Jian Zhou*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper establishes that the generalized Brézin-Gross-Witten (BGW) theory's multidifferentials satisfy the Eynard-Orantin topological recursion in a generic background, and characterizes its transition to the Witten-Kontsevich model, thereby proving the Okuyama-Sakai conjecture.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To systematically understand the mathematical structure of the generalized BGW theory and clarify its relationship with the Witten-Kontsevich model via tools from statistical physics and topological recursion.

Method: Application of statistical physics methods and rigorous analysis of the Eynard-Orantin topological recursion to the generalized BGW theory in a generic background with arbitrary coupling constants; characterization of the model transition via asymptotic and structural comparison.

Result: Proof that the multidifferentials of the generalized BGW theory are governed by the Eynard-Orantin topological recursion; explicit characterization of the transition between the Witten-Kontsevich and generalized BGW models; confirmation of the Okuyama-Sakai conjecture.

Conclusion: The generalized BGW theory is fully embedded in the framework of topological recursion, revealing a unified geometric and integrable structure shared with the Witten-Kontsevich model, strengthening foundational links between matrix models, enumerative geometry, and quantum field theory.

Abstract: We present a systematic study of the mathematical theory of the generalized Brézin-Gross-Witten (BGW) theory using methods from statistical physics. We prove the multidifferentials of the generalized BGW theory are given by the Eynard-Orantin topological recursion in a generic background with general coupling constants. Furthermore, we characterize the transition between the Witten-Kontsevich model and the generalized BGW theory. This approach yields a new proof of the Okuyama-Sakai conjecture, further elucidating the deep structural connections between these two fundamental models.

</details>


### [3] [Projective relativistic dynamics from a quantum Lorentz algebra: time-dependent deformed Hamiltonians on (anti-)de Sitter spaces](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.02242)
*Rutwig Campoamor-Stursberg,Oscar Carballal,Francisco J. Herranz*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper constructs time-dependent quantum-deformed Hamiltonian systems on (1+1)-dimensional (anti-)de Sitter spacetimes using a non-standard quantum deformation of the Lorentz algebra so(1,3), linking the deformation parameter z to curvature κ and cosmological constant Λ = −z; it applies Poisson–Hopf deformation theory to yield deformed oscillators, Bateman, and Caldirola–Kanai systems.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To systematically generate integrable, time-dependent Hamiltonian systems on curved spacetimes with quantum-deformed symmetry, bridging noncommutative geometry, Lie–Hamilton theory, and cosmological models.

Method: Applies Poisson–Hopf deformation theory to a non-standard quantum deformation of 𝔰𝔬(1,3), identifies projective canonical variables related to Beltrami coordinates via canonical transformation, and constructs deformed Hamiltonian vector fields preserving the deformed Lorentz symmetry.

Result: Derives a family of time-dependent deformed Hamiltonians on (1+1)-d (anti-)de Sitter spaces; establishes identification z ≡ κ ≡ −Λ; obtains explicit deformed oscillators, Bateman, and generalized Caldirola–Kanai systems; confirms constant curvature geometry and correct classical limits.

Conclusion: The Poisson–Hopf framework provides a robust, symmetry-preserving method to deform Lie–Hamilton systems on curved backgrounds; the deformation parameter z acquires geometric meaning as spacetime curvature, unifying anti-de Sitter (z > 0), de Sitter (z < 0), and Minkowski (z = 0) cases within a single quantum-algebraic structure.

Abstract: A (non-standard) quantum deformation of the Lorentz algebra $\mathfrak{so}(1,3)$ is used to construct novel time-dependent Hamiltonian systems on the (anti-)de Sitter spacetimes in $(1+1)$-dimensions. The specific deformation considered here contains, among others, the non-standard quantum deformation of $\mathfrak{sl}(2, \mathbb{R})$ and that of the `book' algebra $\mathfrak{b}_{2}$ as quantum subalgebras. The guiding idea is to apply the theory of Poisson--Hopf deformations of Lie--Hamilton systems to the Lorentz deformation, giving rise to a broad family of time-dependent deformed systems that share the same deformed Poisson--Hopf Lorentz algebra symmetry. In particular, this approach provides Hamiltonian vector fields expressed in terms of certain projective canonical variables, which, in turn, are related to the usual Beltrami variables via a canonical transformation. Under this construction, it is shown that the underlying Lorentizan spaces possess constant curvature $\kappa$ and the quantum deformation parameter $z$ can be identified with $\kappa$, such that the cosmological constant is $\Lambda=-z\equiv -\kappa$. Therefore, we deal with the anti-de Sitter, de Sitter and Minkowskian spaces for $z>0$, $z<0$ and $z=0$ (the undeformed case), respectively. Finally, as applications, we study time-dependent deformed oscillators on the (anti-)de Sitter spaces in detail, as well as time-dependent deformed Bateman and generalised coupled Caldirola--Kanai Hamiltonians.

</details>


### [4] [Phase-space dynamics and multicomponent wave-packet structures in the Dunkl anharmonic oscillator](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00280)
*D. Ojeda-Guillén,R. D. Mota*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper analyzes phase-space dynamics and wave-packet structures of the Dunkl anharmonic oscillator (modeling a Kerr medium), deriving analytical expressions for Dunkl-Husimi Q-function and angular Husimi profile; it shows how the Dunkl parameter μ controls angular anisotropy, asymmetric peak formation, revival timing, and modality—recovering standard Kerr dynamics as μ→0.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To extend Kerr-medium quantum dynamics by incorporating Dunkl-deformed symmetries and investigate how the Dunkl parameter μ influences phase-space structure, coherence, and nonlinear revival behavior beyond standard harmonic/anharmonic models.

Method: Analytical derivation of time-evolved states, Dunkl-Husimi Q-function, and normalized angular Husimi profile for the Dunkl anharmonic oscillator; systematic analysis of μ-dependent features including parity-driven phase accumulation, peak asymmetry, reconstruction times, and multimodality.

Result: Dunkl parameter μ induces angular anisotropy and asymmetric phase-space separation; μ=0.5 yields early reconstruction at t=π/λ and unequal-height multicomponent Husimi peaks; μ=1.0 preserves multimodality with broadening under unitary evolution; full wave-packet reconstruction occurs at fundamental nonlinear revival time for selected parameter ratios; standard Kerr limit recovered as μ→0.

Conclusion: The Dunkl deformation enriches Kerr-medium phase-space dynamics with controllable symmetry breaking, tunable revival times, and nontrivial multicomponent structures—providing a generalized framework for studying nonlinear quantum optics and deformed oscillator systems.

Abstract: We study the phase-space dynamics and multicomponent wave-packet structures of the Dunkl anharmonic oscillator (Kerr medium). Starting from the time-evolved state, we derive analytical expressions for the Dunkl-Husimi $Q$-function and a normalized angular Husimi profile. The Dunkl parameter modifies the coherent-state excitation weights and the parity-dependent phase accumulation, producing angular anisotropy and asymmetric phase-space separation. For the half-integer deformation $\mu=0.5$, the dynamics generate asymmetric multicomponent Husimi patterns with unequal peak heights and an early reconstruction at $t=\pi/\lambda$. For the deformation $\mu=1.0$, the Husimi distribution exhibits broadening while retaining its multimodal structure and unitary evolution. At the fundamental nonlinear revival time, the wave packet is reconstructed for the selected parameter ratio. The standard Kerr dynamics are recovered in the limit $\mu\rightarrow 0$.

</details>


### [5] [Painlevé VI superintegrability and Heun-Racah algebras](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00322)
*Ian Marquette,Sarah Post*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper analyzes two quantum superintegrable systems in the Euclidean plane—oscillator and extended-Coulomb type—that separate in polar coordinates with angular potentials governed by the full four-parameter Painlevé VI transcendent; it constructs their fourth-order integrals, cubic symmetry algebras (specializations of the Heun–Racah algebra), deformed-oscillator realizations, and relates them via Levi-Civita transformation and coupling-constant metamorphosis; classical Poisson counterparts are also compared.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To systematically characterize quantum superintegrable systems whose separable angular potentials are governed by the general four-parameter Painlevé VI equation—a transcendental function beyond standard special functions—and to unify oscillator and Coulomb systems under a common algebraic framework involving Heun–Racah symmetry.

Method: Explicit construction of fourth-order quantum integrals; derivation of cubic symmetry algebras and their Casimir operators; construction of deformed-oscillator realizations; operator-level Levi-Civita transformation and coupling-constant metamorphosis; comparison of quantum symmetry algebras with classical Poisson algebras; analysis of structure functions separating angular (Painlevé VI–dependent) and radial (oscillator vs. Coulomb) contributions.

Result: Two distinct quantum superintegrable systems are fully characterized: (i) oscillator-type and (ii) extended-Coulomb-type, both with angular potentials from the full 4-parameter Painlevé VI; each admits a fourth-order integral, a cubic symmetry algebra isomorphic to a specialization of the Heun–Racah algebra, and a deformed-oscillator realization; their algebras share the same quartic angular structure function (determined by Painlevé VI parameters) but differ in radial factors; Levi-Civita and coupling-constant metamorphosis establish exact parameter and representation correspondences; earlier exceptional-Jacobi models emerge only at rational parameter subcases.

Conclusion: The full four-parameter Painlevé VI transcendent naturally governs the angular sector of quantum superintegrable systems in the Euclidean plane with polar separation, unifying oscillator and Coulomb dynamics under a common Heun–Racah algebraic structure; this extends previous results limited to rational or degenerate Painlevé cases and demonstrates deep connections between Painlevé transcendents, superintegrability, and quadratic algebras.

Abstract: We study the two fourth-order quantum superintegrable systems in the Euclidean plane that separate in polar coordinates and whose angular potentials are determined by the general four-parameter sixth Painlevé transcendent. One system has an oscillator radial term and the other an extended-Coulomb radial term. For both systems we give the fourth-order quantum integral explicitly, derive the cubic symmetry algebra and its Casimir, and construct a deformed-oscillator realization. At fixed energy the two algebras are specializations of the Heun--Racah algebra. Their structure functions contain the same quartic angular factor, determined by the Painlevé~VI parameters, while different radial factors distinguish oscillator and Coulomb dynamics. The Coulomb integral is obtained by transforming the Cartesian Hermitian symmetry at the operator level. The lower-order terms generated by the polar coordinate change are essential for commutation with the Hamiltonian. The two quantum systems are also related by the Levi-Civita transformation and coupling-constant metamorphosis, which gives a direct correspondence between their Painlevé parameters, algebras, Casimirs and representations. Earlier exceptional-Jacobi models arise at special rational parameter values, whereas the results here apply to the full four-parameter Painlevé~VI family. We also compare the quantum results with the corresponding classical Poisson algebras.

</details>


### [6] [Stability of Solitons in the Parametrically Driven and Damped Nonlinear Schrödinger Equation in Arbitrary Dimension](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00346)
*Fernando Carreño-Navas*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper analyzes spectral stability of soliton solutions to the parametrically driven and damped nonlinear Schrödinger (PDDNLS) equation with power-law nonlinearity in N dimensions, identifying two stationary solution families, proving one is always unstable and performing a complete sector-wise spectral analysis for the other, including new instability criteria and phenomena beyond 1D.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand spectral stability of solitons in the parametrically driven and damped nonlinear Schrödinger equation—generalized to N dimensions and arbitrary power-law nonlinearity—beyond prior one-dimensional studies, and to uncover dimension- and nonlinearity-dependent stability mechanisms.

Method: Linearization around two identified stationary soliton families (Ψ₊ and Ψ₋); reduction to a dissipationless eigenvalue problem via ε-parametrization; spectral decomposition into angular momentum sectors 𝒮ₗ; application of Vakhitov–Kolokolov-type argument combined with local eigenvalue analysis near zero; analytical derivation of instability criteria per sector; numerical computation of stability diagrams for N = 1, 2, 3.

Result: Ψ₋ is proven universally unstable; for Ψ₊, instability arises only in sectors 𝒮₀ and 𝒮₁, with no complex quadruplets in 𝒮₁ and no instabilities for l ≥ 2; an analytical instability criterion is derived for each 𝒮ₗ; novel instability behaviors are found at small and large κ, unobserved in 1D; full stability diagrams are provided for dimensions 1–3.

Conclusion: Spectral stability of PDDNLS solitons depends critically on spatial dimension N, nonlinearity exponent κ, forcing amplitude r, and damping ρ; the Ψ₊ family exhibits rich, dimension-sensitive stability structure amenable to rigorous sector-wise analysis, revealing previously unknown regimes and validating the extended framework beyond one dimension.

Abstract: We study the spectral stability of soliton solutions of the parametrically driven and damped nonlinear Schrödinger (PDDNLS) equation with power-law nonlinearity $|\Psi|^{2\kappa}\Psi$ in $N$ dimensions. We identify two families of stationary solutions, $\Psi_\pm$, whose existence is determined by two conditions: one involving the nonlinearity exponent~$\kappa$ and the spatial dimension~$N$, and another involving the forcing amplitude~$r$ and the damping coefficient~$\rho$. By linearising around these solutions and introducing a convenient parametrisation through~$\varepsilon$, we reduce the stability problem to a dissipationless eigenvalue problem for two Schrödinger-type operators, $L_0$ and $L_1$. We prove that $\Psi_-$ is always unstable, while for $\Psi_+$, we carry out a complete spectral analysis by decomposing the problem into angular momentum sectors $\mathcal{S}_l$. We show that modes with $l \geq 2$ generate no instabilities and that no complex quadruplets arise on~$\mathcal{S}_1$. We also derive an analytical instability criterion on $\mathcal{S}_l$ by combining a Vakhitov--Kolokolov-type argument with a local analysis that determines the direction of the eigenvalue splitting near the origin. In addition, we uncover new phenomena at both small and large values of $\kappa$ that were not investigated in earlier one-dimensional studies. Numerical stability diagrams for $N = 1, 2, 3$ are presented.

</details>


### [7] [A block-encoding approach to the Segawa-Suzuki spectral mapping theorem](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00498)
*Yohei Tanaka*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper simplifies and generalizes the Segawa--Suzuki spectral mapping theorem for Szegedy-type quantum walks using a block-encoding perspective, yielding unified spectral results (spectrum, point spectrum, Fredholm essential spectrum) and explicit descriptions of endpoint eigenspaces via Schur decomposition.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide a simpler, more transparent, and broadly applicable proof of the Segawa--Suzuki spectral mapping theorem by leveraging block-encoding structure and chiral symmetry, avoiding technical invariant-subspace and approximate-eigenvector arguments.

Method: Uses a natural 2×2 block representation induced by the chiral-symmetric involution; applies elementary Schur decomposition to derive spectral mapping; extends analysis to spectrum, point spectrum, and Fredholm essential spectrum; explicitly characterizes endpoint (inherited and birth) eigenspaces via block-theoretic arguments.

Result: A simplified derivation of the non-endpoint spectral mapping theorem; unified spectral mapping results across spectrum types; explicit block-theoretic characterization of endpoint eigenspaces; illustration via one-dimensional split-step quantum walk.

Conclusion: The block-encoding viewpoint unifies and simplifies spectral analysis of abstract Szegedy-type quantum walks, revealing structural insights into chiral symmetry and endpoint spectra while enhancing generality and accessibility of the Segawa--Suzuki theorem.

Abstract: We revisit the Segawa--Suzuki spectral mapping theorem for abstract Szegedy-type quantum walks from a block-encoding viewpoint. Retaining both eigenspaces of the involution underlying the chiral symmetry yields a natural two-by-two block representation in which the usual discriminant operator and its counterpart form the diagonal blocks. The non-endpoint spectral mapping theorem then follows from an elementary Schur decomposition, substantially simplifying the original proof by avoiding its invariant-subspace decomposition and approximate-eigenvector arguments. The same framework establishes the result for the spectrum, point spectrum, and Fredholm essential spectrum and gives a direct block-theoretic description of the endpoint inherited and birth eigenspaces. We illustrate the construction with a one-dimensional split-step quantum walk.

</details>


### [8] [Boosted Physics-Informed Neural Networks for Solving Homogeneous and Non-Homogeneous Lane-Emden Type Equations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00701)
*Luke Oluwaseye Joel*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper proposes a hybrid method combining Physics-Informed Neural Networks (PINNs) with ensemble regression models (e.g., LightGBM, XGBoost) to correct PINN residuals and improve accuracy in solving Lane-Emden equations.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Standard PINNs suffer from optimization difficulties and approximation errors when solving stiff or highly nonlinear Lane-Emden equations; improved robustness and accuracy are needed for astrophysical modeling.

Method: A two-stage framework: (1) train a standard PINN enforcing the Lane-Emden equation via physics-informed loss; (2) apply boosting-based regression (LightGBM, RF, XGBoost, SVR) on PINN residuals, then aggregate corrected predictions.

Result: Boosted PINN achieves significantly lower prediction error and better generalization than vanilla PINN across multiple Lane-Emden test cases, as confirmed by quantitative metrics and comparative analysis.

Conclusion: Residual boosting with interpretable, high-performance regressors enhances PINN fidelity without modifying the PDE-constrained optimization — offering a modular, scalable strategy for scientific machine learning.

Abstract: The Lane-Emden Equation is a class of nonlinear differential equations commonly used to model phenomena in astrophysics and mathematical physics, such as stellar structure and thermal behavior in polytropic gas spheres. This study presents a novel hybrid framework that integrates Physics-Informed Neural Networks (PINNs) with boosting methods to enhance the accuracy and robustness of solutions for Lane-Emden type differential equations. Standard PINNs leverage physical laws encoded within the loss function, but their predictive performance can be constrained by optimization challenges and approximation errors. To address this, we introduce a post-processing boosting stage where the residual errors from the PINN prediction are modeled using some regression algorithms - including LightGBM, Random Forest, XGBoost, and Support Vector Regression (SVR). The outputs from these boosted models are aggregated to generate a corrected, high-fidelity prediction. The proposed methodology is systematically validated through quantitative metrics and comparative analysis against the standard PINN baseline. The results demonstrate that the boosted PINN framework significantly reduces prediction error, and improves generalization, offering a powerful and flexible approach for solving scientific and engineering problems governed by differential equations.

</details>


### [9] [A Representation Theorem for Fractional Viscoelastic Networks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00750)
*Andrea Giusti,Ivano Colombaro,Andrea Mentrelli,Francesco Mainardi*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper proves a representation theorem for finite series-parallel viscoelastic networks with springs and spring-pots (fractional elements) of order α ∈ (0,1), showing their complex modulus admits rational forms in an auxiliary variable, leading to fractional Prony series with non-negative coefficients for relaxation and creep.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To rigorously establish a corrected version of the Mainardi-Spada conjecture for finite series-parallel viscoelastic networks containing spring-pots, ensuring physically admissible (non-negative) fractional Prony representations.

Method: Complex variable analysis and Laplace transform theory: deriving rational forms of the complex shear modulus and its reciprocal in a suitably chosen auxiliary variable; performing partial-fraction decomposition with constraints on pole locations and residue signs; inverting Laplace transforms to obtain time-domain representations.

Result: Finite fractional Prony representations for both relaxation modulus and creep compliance, with provably non-negative amplitudes; rational structure of the complex modulus and its reciprocal in an auxiliary variable; explicit characterization of pole locations (negative real axis, possibly origin) and residue signs.

Conclusion: The Mainardi-Spada conjecture holds in corrected form for finite series-parallel networks of springs and spring-pots of common order α ∈ (0,1); the resulting fractional Prony series are thermodynamically admissible due to non-negativity of coefficients.

Abstract: We prove a representation theorem for finite series-parallel viscoelastic networks composed of springs and spring-pots of a common order $0<\alpha<1$. The associated complex shear modulus, expressed in an appropriately chosen auxiliary variable, is rational and admits a finite partial-fraction representation with simple poles on the negative real axis and negative residues. Its reciprocal admits an analogous representation with positive residues and a possible additional pole at the origin. Inverting the corresponding Laplace transforms gives finite fractional Prony representations of the relaxation modulus and creep compliance with non-negative amplitudes. These results provide a rigorous proof of a corrected version of the Mainardi-Spada conjecture for this network class. A six-element network illustrates the construction.

</details>


### [10] [Lieb-Robinson Light Cones in Long-Range Bosonic Systems](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00776)
*Carla Rubiliani,Cheng Shang,Tomotaka Kuwahara*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper derives improved Lieb-Robinson bounds for interacting bosonic systems with long-range interactions, revealing a nontrivial light-cone exponent governed by the interplay of interaction decay exponent α and bosonic interaction order p; optimal exponents are found for p ≥ 4 and 2d < α ≤ 2d + 1, showing richer causal structure than in bounded or short-range cases.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand emergent causality (via Lieb-Robinson bounds) in interacting bosonic systems where standard assumptions—short-range interactions and uniformly bounded local energy scales—fail due to long-range couplings and unbounded bosonic degrees of freedom.

Method: Derives matched upper and lower Lieb-Robinson bounds using a block-based propagation protocol that concentrates bosons to form a mediating sublattice with amplified interactions; employs a fixed-k extension of bounded-interaction techniques for the upper bound at α = 2d + 1.

Result: Light-cone exponents depend nontrivially on α and p; for p ≥ 4 and 2d < α ≤ 2d + 1, upper and lower bounds coincide, yielding optimal leading power-law light cones; the upper bound holds at α = 2d + 1 with arbitrarily small exponent loss.

Conclusion: The combination of long-range interactions and unbounded bosonic operators generates a qualitatively richer light-cone structure—distinct from both long-range spin systems and short-range bosonic systems—highlighting the necessity of jointly accounting for interaction range and operator unboundedness in quantum dynamics.

Abstract: Lieb-Robinson bounds formulate emergent causality in quantum many-body dynamics. In this Letter, we study interacting bosonic systems with long-range interactions, in which both the standard assumptions of short-range interactions and uniformly bounded local energy scales are absent. We derive upper and lower bounds whose effective light-cone exponents are governed by a nontrivial interplay between the decay exponent $\alpha$ and the bosonic interaction order $p$. The propagation protocol concentrates bosons within blocks, creating a mediating sublattice with amplified interactions. For $p\ge4$ and $2d<\alpha\le2d+1$, the resulting exponents coincide, determining the optimal leading light-cone power. The upper bound includes $\alpha=2d+1$ with arbitrarily small exponent loss, using a fixed-$k$ extension of the bounded-interaction result. Our results reveal that the interplay between long-range couplings and unbounded bosonic degrees of freedom gives rise to a richer light-cone structure than in either long-range bounded systems or short-range bosonic systems alone.

</details>


### [11] [Explicit Boundary-Entropy Deficits in the XX Spin Chain with arbitrary boundary fields](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00946)
*Yi Qiao,Pei Sun,Junpeng Cao,Wen-Li Yang,Yupeng Wang*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper derives exact low-temperature thermodynamics of the open XX spin-1/2 chain with arbitrary Hermitian boundary fields using fusion techniques, revealing distinct boundary entropy contributions and crossover scales depending on transverse vs. diagonal boundary fields.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To establish a rigorous, exact benchmark for boundary thermodynamics in open quantum integrable systems and dot defects in quantum critical systems, where boundary conditions strongly influence low-energy properties.

Method: Fusion techniques applied to the integrable XX spin-1/2 chain with arbitrary Hermitian boundary fields, enabling exact derivation of boundary free energy and entropy at low temperatures.

Result: Each nonzero transverse boundary field contributes +½T ln 2 to low-T free energy; nondiagonal ends exhibit a boundary crossover temperature T_B ∝ h_⊥²/J, and for T ≪ min{J, T_B}, boundary entropy is reduced by ½ ln 2 relative to diagonal ends; the limit h_⊥ → 0 leads to nonuniform low-T expansion.

Conclusion: The work provides an exact, analytically controlled description of boundary thermodynamics, distinguishing universal boundary entropy shifts and crossover physics, serving as a benchmark for open integrable systems and quantum impurity problems.

Abstract: The exact thermodynamics of the XX spin-1/2 chain with arbitrary Hermitian boundary fields is constructed via fusion techniques. The rigorous result shows that (1) each end with a nonzero transverse field contributes $+\frac12T\ln2$ to the low-temperature free energy, whereas strictly diagonal and free ends contribute no such linear term; (2) each nondiagonal end has a characteristic temperature $T_B$, which scales as $T_B\propto h_\perp^2/J$ in the weak-field regime. For $T\ll\min\{J,T_B\}$, the boundary entropy approaches a deficit of $\frac12\ln2$ relative to a diagonal end. As the transverse field tends to zero, the boundary crossover scale vanishes and the low-temperature expansion becomes nonuniform. The present result provides a clear benchmark not only for the boundary thermodynamics of open boundary quantum integrable systems, but also for dot defects in quantum critical systems.

</details>


### [12] [Equivalence of superintegrable Poisson projection chains: magnetic cotangent bundles and spin Calogero-Moser models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00992)
*Kai Jiang,Guorui Ma,Junze Zhang*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper establishes spectral equivalence between two geometric reduction methods—Poisson projection chains on T*(G/A) with magnetic symplectic form and left-right Hamiltonian reduction of T*G—yielding identical superintegrable systems (e.g., spin Calogero-Moser), with matching phase spaces, Poisson structures, and commuting Hamiltonians on regular principal orbits.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To unify and clarify the geometric origins of superintegrable Hamiltonian systems by comparing distinct but related symplectic reduction procedures that yield seemingly different models.

Method: Rigorous comparison of two reduction schemes: (i) magnetic cotangent bundle reduction on T*(G/A) where A = Z_G(γ), using orbit geometry G·γ ≅ G/A; and (ii) left-right Hamiltonian reduction of T*G. Equivalence is proved via identification of phase spaces, intermediate Poisson manifolds, and base spaces along open dense subsets of regular principal orbit type.

Result: The two Poisson projection chains are spectrally equivalent on an open dense set of regular points: their phase spaces, Poisson structures, and projection hierarchies coincide, and they generate identical families of commuting Hamiltonians.

Conclusion: Superintegrability in these settings arises from a common underlying geometric structure—spectral equivalence of Poisson projection chains—thereby unifying magnetic cotangent bundle and left-right reduction approaches to integrable systems like spin Calogero-Moser models.

Abstract: We study superintegrable Hamiltonian systems in terms of Poisson projection chains and their (spectral) equivalence. We compare two reduction constructions: (i) the cotangent bundle $T^*(G/A)$, $A=Z_G(\gamma)$, with the magnetic symplectic form defined by the orbit $G\cdot \gamma\cong G/A$, and (ii) the left-right Hamiltonian reduction of $T^*G$ that gives spin Calogero-Moser type systems. We prove that the two resulting chains are equivalent on an open set of regular points of principal orbit type: the phase spaces, the intermediate Poisson manifolds and the base spaces are identified compatibly with the projections. In particular, both constructions give the same commuting Hamiltonians.

</details>


### [13] [Canonical quantum curve I](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00997)
*Shuai Guo,Qingsheng Zhang*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper formulates and proves a canonical quantum curve conjecture $\widehat H \Psi_*=0$ for spectral curves of positive genus, where $\Psi_*$ is a non-perturbative wave function evaluated at a canonical vanishing point — a distinguished zero of the closed non-perturbative partition function. The quantum operator $\widehat H$ arises from Weyl quantization of the classical curve $H(x,y)$ with universal quantum corrections depending only on closed moduli and $\hbar$, vanishing classically. Geometrically, these corrections are conjectured to be normalized Wilson-loop expectations; in refined settings, the canonical vanishing point links to the Nekrasov–Shatashvili free energy. The conjecture is proven for Weierstrass curves and mirror curves of canonical bundles over toric weak del Pezzo surfaces (including local $\mathbb{P}^2$), with explicit identifications in the latter case.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To extend the quantum curve formalism beyond genus-zero spectral curves by introducing a canonical, geometrically meaningful non-perturbative wave function $\Psi_*$ and a systematically corrected quantum operator $\widehat H$, grounded in Eynard–Orantin topological recursion, refined BPS invariants, and gauge-theoretic expectations (e.g., Wilson loops, NS free energy).

Method: Weyl quantization of the classical spectral curve $H(x,y)$, augmented by universal quantum corrections to coefficients determined solely by closed moduli and $\hbar$; identification of the canonical vanishing point as a distinguished zero of the closed non-perturbative partition function; rigorous verification via comparison with known enumerative/geometric data (e.g., Gromov–Witten invariants, NS free energy derivatives, Wilson-loop vevs) for Weierstrass and toric mirror curves.

Result: Proof of the quantum curve conjecture for Weierstrass curves and mirror curves of canonical bundles over smooth projective toric weak del Pezzo surfaces (including local $\mathbb{P}^2$); explicit relation between the canonical vanishing point and derivative of the closed Nekrasov–Shatashvili free energy for these surfaces; identification of the quantum correction coefficient with a normalized geometric NS Wilson-loop expectation value for local $\mathbb{P}^2$.

Conclusion: The canonical quantum curve conjecture provides a consistent, geometrically anchored framework for quantizing higher-genus spectral curves within topological recursion, unifying quantum algebraic geometry, refined topological strings, and supersymmetric gauge theory expectations. Its validity in key examples supports its role as a fundamental principle in the non-perturbative structure of spectral curves.

Abstract: We formulate a canonical quantum curve conjecture $\widehat H\Psi_*=0$ for spectral curves of positive genus. Here $\Psi_*$ is the non-perturbative wave function associated with Eynard--Orantin topological recursion, specialized at the \emph{canonical vanishing point}, a distinguished zero of the closed non-perturbative partition function. The operator $\widehat H$ is obtained by Weyl quantization of $H(x,y)$, with $\widehat x=x\cdot$ and $\widehat y=\hbar\partial_x$, together with quantum corrections to its coefficients. These corrections depend only on closed moduli and $\hbar$ and vanish classically. Whenever a geometric definition is available, we conjecture that the corrected coefficients are suitably normalized Wilson-loop expectation values. When a corresponding refined theory exists, we conjecture a relation between the canonical vanishing point and the closed Nekrasov--Shatashvili free energy. We prove the quantum curve conjecture for Weierstrass curves and the mirror curves of canonical bundles of smooth projective toric weak del Pezzo surfaces, including local $\mathbb P^2$. For the local surfaces considered here, we relate the canonical vanishing point to a derivative of the closed NS free energy. For local $\mathbb P^2$, we also identify the corrected coefficient with a suitably normalized, geometrically defined NS Wilson-loop expectation value.

</details>


### [14] [Semiclassical Wave Packet Dynamics For Nonlinear Schrödinger Equations with Harmonic Potentials](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.01108)
*Jared Smith,Tomoki Ohsawa*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: The paper introduces an approximate Hamiltonian dynamics for the Gross–Pitaevskii equation using semiclassical wave packets (extending Hagedorn’s framework), derived from a symplectic/Hamiltonian perspective—contrasting and unifying with the earlier Lagrangian Gaussian ansatz of Perez-Garcia et al.—and validates it numerically.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To develop a more general and geometrically principled approximation framework for the Gross–Pitaevskii equation beyond the restrictive Gaussian ansatz, leveraging the richer structure and accuracy of semiclassical wave packets while preserving Hamiltonian/symplectic consistency.

Method: Extends the Gaussian ansatz to Hagedorn’s semiclassical wave packets; derives evolution equations via Hamiltonian reduction on the symplectic manifold of wave packet parameters; proves equivalence with the Lagrangian approach in the Gaussian limit; generalizes to arbitrary semiclassical wave packets; validates via numerical experiments.

Result: Derives explicit symplectic evolution equations for semiclassical wave packet parameters under the Gross–Pitaevskii Hamiltonian; establishes formal equivalence with prior Gaussian dynamics; demonstrates improved or comparable accuracy in numerical tests compared to Gaussian approximations.

Conclusion: The Hamiltonian/symplectic formulation provides a natural, generalizable, and physically consistent framework for approximating nonlinear Schrödinger dynamics using semiclassical wave packets—offering advantages in structure preservation and potential extension to higher-order approximations.

Abstract: We propose an approximate Hamiltonian dynamics for the Gross--Pitaevskii equation $i\hbar\psi_t = -\frac{\hbar^2}{2m}\Delta \psi + V(x)\psi + \hbar^2 \lambda |\psi|^2\psi$ with harmonic potential $V(x)$ and $x\in\mathbb{R}^d$. We do this by extending the Gaussian ansatz of Perez-Garcia et al.~(Phys.~Rev.~A 56, 1424 (1997)) to the semiclassical wave packets of Hagedorn. We derive the evolution equations for the wave packets from the Hamiltonian/symplectic perspective, rather than the Lagrangian/variational one taken by Perez-Garcia et al. We first show that these two perspectives yield identical evolution equations in the Gaussian case. We then derive the evolution equations for an arbitrary element of the semiclassical wave packets. Finally, we demonstrate the efficacy of the approximate dynamics with numerical experiments.

</details>


### [15] [Density functionals for the Pauli-Fierz Hamiltonian](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.01208)
*Mihály A. Csirik,Andre Laestadius,Markus Penz,Erik I. Tellgren*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper formulates a density-functional theory for the Pauli-Fierz Hamiltonian in quantum electrodynamics using a convex-analytic Lieb-type approach, introducing electron density and a combined (paramagnetic + field-induced) current density as basic variables; it proves representability, establishes equivalence of mixed-state constrained-search and Lieb functionals, shows convexity (but not attainment) of the pure-state Levy-Lieb functional due to photon softening, recovers the Vignale-Rasolt functional in the zero-coupling limit, and connects to the length-gauge dipole Hamiltonian in the long-wavelength limit.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To extend rigorous density-functional theory to quantum electrodynamical systems—specifically, nonrelativistic electrons coupled to quantized transverse photons—by identifying physically appropriate density variables and establishing foundational representability and variational structure.

Method: Convex-analytic formulation à la Lieb; constrained-search over mixed and pure states; functional analytic treatment of the Pauli-Fierz Hamiltonian with UV cutoff; analysis of photon softening effects on state purification; asymptotic analysis for zero-coupling and long-wavelength limits.

Result: 1) Representability of density–combined-current pairs is proven; 2) Mixed-state constrained-search functional equals the convex Lieb functional and attains its infimum; 3) Pure-state Levy-Lieb functional equals the Lieb functional (hence convex), though infimum may not be attained due to soft-photon purification; 4) Zero-coupling limit yields the Vignale-Rasolt current-density functional; 5) Long-wavelength limit recovers the length-gauge dipole Hamiltonian via constrained search.

Conclusion: A mathematically rigorous, convex DFT framework for cavity QED-type systems is established, unifying electronic density and electromagnetic response into joint density variables, and recovering known limits while revealing new structural properties—especially convexity without attainment—stemming from gauge-invariant photon coupling.

Abstract: We develop a density-functional theory for the Pauli-Fierz Hamiltonian of nonrelativistic quantum electrodynamics in the spirit of the convex-analytic formulation of Lieb. We consider finitely many electrons, subject to external scalar and vector potentials, coupled to the full continuum of massless transverse photons with an ultraviolet cutoff. The natural basic variables are the standard electron density and a combined current density, the sum of the paramagnetic current density and a field-induced current density generated by the quantized vector potential. We prove representability results for these density-current pairs and show that the mixed-state constrained-search functional attains its infimum and coincides with the Lieb functional obtained by convex conjugation. Since arbitrarily soft photons purify mixed states at a negligible energy cost without changing their density and combined current density, the pure-state Levy-Lieb functional equals the Lieb functional and is therefore convex, even though its infimum need not be attained. In addition, we establish the zero-coupling limit, which recovers the mixed-state Vignale-Rasolt functional of current-density-functional theory. Finally, we consider the long-wavelength (dipole) limit and show that the limiting functional can be written as a constrained search for the well-known length-gauge dipole Hamiltonian.

</details>


### [16] [Exponential suppression of bit-flip errors in dissipative cat qubits](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.01296)
*Ruikang Liang,Rémi Robin*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper analyzes the Lindblad generator for a dissipative cat qubit under single-photon loss, proving exponential suppression of bit-flip errors via spectral analysis of a near-zero two-dimensional subspace.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To demonstrate robustness of the dissipative cat qubit against bit-flip errors induced by single-photon loss, a key challenge in quantum error correction.

Method: Spectral analysis of the infinite-dimensional Lindblad generator $L_\epsilon = D[a^2 - \alpha^2] + \epsilon D[a]$, identifying a two-dimensional invariant subspace near eigenvalue zero and bounding its slowest decay rate.

Result: An exponentially decaying upper bound on the slow decay rate — scaling as $\sim e^{-c\alpha^2}$ — is established, uniform in small $\epsilon > 0$, confirming exponential bit-flip error suppression.

Conclusion: The dissipative cat qubit encoded in the low-lying spectral subspace of $L_\epsilon$ exhibits intrinsic, parameter-tunable protection against bit-flip errors, validating its suitability for fault-tolerant quantum computation.

Abstract: We study the infinite-dimensional Lindblad generator $L_\epsilon=D[a^2-\alpha^2]+\epsilon D[a]$, describing a dissipative cat qubit subject to single-photon loss. For sufficiently large $\alpha$ and sufficiently small $\epsilon>0$, we identify a two-dimensional spectral subspace near zero and establish an upper bound on the slow decay rate that decreases exponentially with $\alpha^2$, uniformly in $\epsilon$. This proves exponential suppression of bit-flip errors.

</details>


### [17] [Eynard-Orantin Topological Recursions for Higher Weil-Petersson Volumes on Moduli Spaces of Super Riemann Surfaces](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.01497)
*Dingkang Xiong,Jian Zhou*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper establishes an Eynard-Orantin topological recursion for intersection numbers involving Norbury's Θ-class, ψ-classes, and higher κ-classes, which compute higher Weil-Petersson volumes of super Riemann surface moduli spaces; the recursion generalizes the Bessel-curve topological recursion (linked to the Brézin-Gross-Witten model) by incorporating higher-κ deformations.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To compute higher Weil-Petersson volumes of moduli spaces of super Riemann surfaces via intersection theory on moduli spaces, extending known recursions to include Norbury's Θ-class and higher κ-classes.

Method: Derivation of an Eynard-Orantin topological recursion using intersection theory on the moduli space of curves, incorporating Norbury’s Θ-class, ψ-classes, and higher κ-classes, and relating it to spectral curve geometry (Bessel curve deformation).

Result: A new topological recursion formula governing intersection numbers with Θ-, ψ-, and higher κ-classes; this recursion computes higher Weil-Petersson volumes for super Riemann surfaces and deforms the Bessel-curve recursion associated with the Brézin-Gross-Witten model.

Conclusion: The derived recursion provides a unified geometric-combinatorial framework for higher Weil-Petersson volumes in supergeometry and enriches the landscape of topological recursions by introducing higher-κ deformations beyond the standard Bessel case.

Abstract: We derive an Eynard-Orantin topological recursion for intersection numbers involving Norbury's $\Theta$-class together with $\psi$-classes and higher $\kappa$-classes. These intersection numbers encode higher Weil-Petersson volumes of moduli spaces of super Riemann surfaces. The resulting recursion is a higher-$\kappa$ deformation of the topological recursion associated with the Bessel curve, which encodes the Brézin-Gross-Witten model.

</details>


### [18] [The massive phase of the two-dimensional Heisenberg model is stable under small anisotropy](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.01532)
*Yan Ru Pei*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper proves that the massive (exponentially decaying correlation) phase of the classical Heisenberg model on ℤ² persists under small anisotropic perturbations—including easy-plane, single-ion, and general O(n) (n≥3) anisotropies—and quantifies how the critical inverse temperature β_c(λ) diverges as anisotropy approaches isotropy (e.g., λ↑1), yielding β_c ∼ |log(1−λ)|^{1/2} for free-boundary systems.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To extend Polyakov’s prediction—verified recently for the isotropic classical Heisenberg model on ℤ²—to physically relevant anisotropic variants, addressing whether exponential decay of correlations survives small symmetry-breaking perturbations and how the BKT transition temperature shifts in the easy-plane limit.

Method: Adapts the sign-cluster argument from the isotropic proof, leveraging Ginibre’s correlation inequality to handle planar anisotropic energies; exploits rotation symmetry only on a single finite annulus where anisotropy acts as a bounded Hamiltonian perturbation; uses cluster expansion and comparison techniques robust under small coupling variations.

Result: For any β, there exists δ(β) > 0 such that the S²-valued anisotropic Heisenberg model with couplings |Jⁱ − 1| ≤ δ(β) exhibits uniform exponential decay of spin two-point functions across all finite subgraphs with free boundary conditions and edge strengths up to β. For the easy-plane model with coupling λ, exponential decay holds for all β ≤ c|log(1−λ)|^{1/2}, implying the BKT transition is pushed to zero temperature as λ ↑ 1; analogous results hold for single-ion and O(n) (n ≥ 3) anisotropies.

Conclusion: The massive phase is stable under small anisotropy; the isotropic Heisenberg model’s exponential decay is not fine-tuned but structurally robust. The logarithmic scaling of the critical temperature with anisotropy strength confirms universality of the mechanism while refining physical predictions (which expect |log(1−λ)|, not its square root).

Abstract: Polyakov predicted that the classical Heisenberg model on $\mathbb{Z}^2$ has exponentially decaying correlations at every temperature, and a proof of this has recently been posted. We show that the massive phase survives small anisotropy. For every inverse temperature $\beta$ there is $\delta(\beta)>0$ such that the $S^2$-valued model with couplings $\sum_iJ^i\sigma_x^i\sigma_y^i$ has exponentially decaying spin two-point functions, uniformly in finite subgraphs with free boundary conditions and in edge strengths up to $\beta$, whenever $|J^i-1|\le\delta(\beta)$ for $i=1,2,3$. For the easy-plane model with couplings $\sigma^x\sigma^x+\sigma^y\sigma^y+\lambda\sigma^z\sigma^z$ this shows that the onset of the Berezinskii-Kosterlitz-Thouless phase, proved by Garban and de Montgolfier for every $\lambda\in(-1,1)$, moves to zero temperature as $\lambda\uparrow1$: in free-boundary limits, correlations decay exponentially for every $\beta\le c |\log(1-\lambda)|^{1/2}$. The physical prediction is of order $|\log(1-\lambda)|$. The same holds for aligned single-ion anisotropy, which includes a confining potential $\varepsilon\sum(\sigma^z)^2$, and for nearly isotropic $O(n)$ models with $n\ge3$. The proof keeps the sign-cluster argument of the isotropic proof, using Ginibre's correlation inequality for anisotropic planar energies, and uses the rotation symmetry only on one finite annulus, where the anisotropy is a bounded perturbation of the Hamiltonian.

</details>


### [19] [Covariant Calculus and Hamiltonian Flows for Nonlocal Wasserstein Metrics](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.01979)
*Yuanke Chen,Jinqiao Duan,Qiao Huang*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper develops a geometric calculus for nonlocal metrics on probability densities over closed manifolds, using symmetric jump kernels and density-dependent mobilities; it introduces a density-variation operator, establishes isometric tangent-space representations, derives Levi-Civita connections and Hamiltonian dynamics, and applies the framework to entropy diffusion, mean-field, Keller–Segel, and Vlasov-type equations—with special results for geometric-mean mobility linking nonlocal Schrödinger bridges and equations via Hopf–Cole and Madelung transformations.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To extend Riemannian geometric methods—originally developed for local Wasserstein-type metrics—to nonlocal settings arising in jump-diffusion processes, mean-field interactions, and quantum-inspired stochastic control, enabling rigorous Hamiltonian, gradient-flow, and variational analysis of nonlocal PDEs.

Method: Introduces a density-variation operator to compute functional derivatives of Dirichlet forms and kinetic energy; constructs dual tangent-space representations (density and velocity-field); proves their isometric equivalence; uses the generator’s pseudoinverse to define Levi-Civita connections and covariant differentials; derives Hamiltonian dynamics via action principles and Legendre duality; applies to entropy diffusion, nonlocal mean-field, Keller–Segel, and Vlasov equations; analyzes geometric-mean mobility via Hopf–Cole and Madelung transformations.

Result: A fully consistent nonlocal Riemannian structure on probability densities with explicit connections, covariant derivatives, and Hamiltonian equations; zero-energy Hamiltonian lifts for smooth negative gradient flows; hyperbolic (Hopf–Cole) and trigonometric (Madelung) phase energies for geometric-mean mobility; second-order recovery of metric kinetic energy at zero momentum.

Conclusion: The proposed nonlocal geometric calculus unifies and generalizes several important classes of nonlocal evolution equations under a single metric–geometric framework, enabling new analytical tools—including Hamilton–Jacobi, Newtonian, and gradient-flow formulations—and revealing deep structural links between stochastic control, mean-field theory, and quantum hydrodynamics in nonlocal settings.

Abstract: In this paper, we study nonlocal metrics on smooth positive probability densities on a closed manifold, induced by symmetric jump kernels and density-dependent mobilities. First, we introduce the density-variation operator to express the density derivatives of the Dirichlet form and kinetic energy. Second, we construct density and velocity-field representations of the tangent space and prove their isometric equivalence. We use the generator's pseudoinverse to derive Levi-Civita connections and covariant differentials in both representations. Third, we establish mechanical Hamiltonian dynamics in both tangent representations; an action principle and Legendre duality identify the Hamilton--Jacobi and covariant Newton equations; smooth negative gradient flows admit a zero-energy Hamiltonian lift. Entropy diffusion, nonlocal mean-field equations, Keller--Segel dynamics, and a nonlocal Vlasov equation illustrate the resulting calculus. For geometric-mean mobility, the Hopf--Cole transformation of the nonlocal Schrödinger bridge system and the Madelung transformation of the nonlocal Schrödinger equation yield hyperbolic and trigonometric phase energies, respectively. At zero momentum, both recover the metric kinetic energy to second order.

</details>


### [20] [Nonlinear adiabatic theorem for the Gross-Pitaevskii equation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.02231)
*Sven Bachmann,Severin Schraven*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper proves a nonlinear adiabatic theorem for the repulsive Gross-Pitaevskii equation with time-dependent trapping potentials, showing that solutions remain close to instantaneous ground states under mild regularity or asymptotic conditions on the potential — provided global existence holds.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To extend adiabatic theory to nonlinear quantum many-body systems modeled by the Gross-Pitaevskii equation, where standard linear adiabatic theorems fail due to nonlinearity and lack of spectral gap.

Method: Functional analytic approach using energy estimates, uniform bounds on the GP functional, and perturbation arguments; distinguishes cases based on spatial growth and regularity of the time-dependent trapping potential V(x,t).

Result: Global well-posedness and adiabatic approximation are established under two distinct classes of potentials: (i) smooth subquadratic potentials with bounded higher-order derivatives, and (ii) bounded perturbations of power-law potentials |x|^m (m ≥ 2); crucially, adiabaticity follows almost automatically once global existence is secured.

Conclusion: Nonlinear adiabatic behavior in the GP equation is robust and hinges primarily on global existence rather than stringent spectral or smoothness assumptions—highlighting a sharp dichotomy between existence and approximation in nonlinear adiabatic theory.

Abstract: We prove a nonlinear adiabatic theorem for the repulsive Gross-Pitaevskii (GP) equation with a time-dependent trapping potential $V(x,t)$. If either (i) $V(\cdot,t)$ is smooth, subquadratic in space and with uniformly bounded spatial derivatives of order $\geq 2$, or (ii) $V(\cdot,t)$ is a uniformly bounded perturbation of a fixed non-negative potential which is asymptotically $|x|^m, m\geq 2$, then the GP equation has a global solution, which remains uniformly close to the instantaneous minimizer of the time-independent GP functional, provided the initial condition is a ground state. In fact, we show that the existence of a solution of the GP equation is the real bottleneck: If existence is assumed, then adiabaticity holds under very minimal assumptions.

</details>


### [21] [Lieb-Robinson bounds for Bose-Hubbard Hamiltonians via path expansions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.02263)
*Alexander Elgart,Marius Lemm*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper provides simplified proofs for sharp bounds on boson transport and Lieb-Robinson velocity in Bose-Hubbard systems, introduces weighted extended particle number operators for transport analysis, adapts the Chen-Lucas path expansion for bosons to recover near-sharp light-cone scaling $t^{d-1}$ (up to logs), and extends transport bounds to long-range hopping with $\alpha > d$, including the previously uncovered regime $\alpha \in (d, d+1)$ yielding an algebraic light cone.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To simplify and unify the proofs of sharp transport and Lieb-Robinson bounds for Bose-Hubbard Hamiltonians, and to extend boson transport bounds to physically relevant long-range interactions not previously covered—particularly the algebraic light-cone regime $\\alpha \\in (d, d+1)$.

Method: For boson transport: construction and use of weighted extended particle number operators to close differential inequalities. For Lieb-Robinson bounds: development of a bosonic adaptation of the Chen-Lucas path expansion technique. For long-range extension: generalization of transport estimates to power-law hopping $\\propto |x-y|^{-\\alpha}$ with $\\alpha > d$.

Result: Simpler, shorter proofs of sharp particle transport bounds and Lieb-Robinson velocity $v_{\\text{LR}} \\sim t^{d-1}$ (up to logarithmic corrections); extension of transport bounds to long-range hopping with $\\alpha \\in (d, d+1)$, establishing an algebraic light cone for boson dynamics in that regime.

Conclusion: The new methods—weighted particle number operators and bosonic path expansion—not only streamline existing results but also enable novel extensions into long-range interaction regimes, revealing algebraic light-cone structure for bosonic transport when $d < \\alpha < d+1$, thereby broadening the scope of rigorous quantum many-body transport theory.

Abstract: We study the transport properties of Bose-Hubbard-type Hamiltonians. Kuwahara, Vu, and Saito proved sharp bounds on (i) particle transport and (ii) the Lieb-Robinson velocity for initial states with controlled boson density. We provide simpler and shorter proofs for both results using a new set of methods. For (i), boson transport, we use a class of weighted extended particle number operators that directly enables closing the associated differential inequalities. For (ii), the Lieb-Robinson bounds, we develop a bosonic version of the Chen-Lucas path expansion technique and recover the sharp velocity bound $t^{d-1}$ up to logarithmic corrections, where $d$ denotes the lattice dimension. We also extend the transport bounds (i) to long-range hopping with a power-law exponent $\alpha>d$. The range $\alpha\in (d,d+1)$ corresponds to an algebraic light cone for boson transport and has not been previously covered.

</details>


### [22] [Quantum 6j-symbols arising from a subfactor of finite index and finite depth and the zipper condition for 4-tensors in two-dimensional topological order](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.02582)
*Yasuyuki Kawahigashi*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper establishes a precise correspondence between 3-tensors satisfying the 'zipper condition'—central in tensor network descriptions of 2D topological order—and the quantum 6j-symbols (F-symbols) of the unitary multi-fusion category associated with a finite-depth subfactor derived from bi-unitary connections.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To rigorously connect tensor network structures (specifically zipper-condition 3-tensors) used in topological order to foundational algebraic structures in operator algebras—namely, subfactor theory and fusion categories—thereby unifying physical tensor constructions with mathematical categorical data.

Method: Leverages the identification of 4-tensors with bi-unitary connections from Jones subfactor theory; imposes finite-depth condition on the associated subfactor; uses categorical reconstruction to show that the resulting 3-tensors encode the associator (F-symbols) of the unitary multi-fusion category obtained from the subfactor.

Result: A one-to-one correspondence is proven: under finite-depth assumption, zipper-condition 3-tensors are exactly the quantum 6j-symbols of the unitary multi-fusion category arising from the subfactor—distinct from the flat bi-unitary connection realization via higher relative commutants.

Conclusion: The zipper condition physically encodes the pentagon equation (i.e., coherence of associativity) in the underlying unitary multi-fusion category, providing a direct bridge between tensor network ansätze and categorical data of topological phases.

Abstract: We study 3-tensors satisfying the ``zipper condition,'' which play an important role in tensor network approaches to two-dimensional topological order. In our previous work, we identified the 4-tensors appearing in these approaches with bi-unitary connections in the Jones theory of subfactors in operator algebras. These bi-unitary connections give rise to subfactors of finite index. We prove that, when the associated subfactor has finite depth, the 3-tensors satisfying the zipper condition correspond precisely to the quantum $6j$-symbols, also known as $F$-symbols, of the unitary multi-fusion category arising from the subfactor. This realization of quantum $6j$-symbols differs from their realization through a flat bi-unitary connection arising from the higher relative commutants of a subfactor.

</details>


### [23] [Derivation of Novel Isotropic Metamaterials at the Hashin Shtrikman Stiffness Limit Enabled by Physics Informed Neural Networks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.02622)
*Manish Kumar Singh,Dominic Kang Jueh Lim,Anson Wen Han Cheong,Adams Wai Kin Kong,Chang Quan Lai*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper introduces a Physics-Informed Neural Network (PINN)-driven approach to systematically design ultrastiff, isotropic closed-cell plate-based metamaterials—bypassing heuristic or topology-optimization limitations—demonstrating that 6-plate lattices achieve near-optimal stiffness (90% of Hashin-Shtrikman upper bound) and high isotropy (Zener ratio = 0.95), outperforming most existing isotropic architectures in specific stiffness.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To overcome inefficiency and human bias in conventional heuristic or topology-optimization methods for designing ultrastiff isotropic metamaterials.

Method: Physics-Informed Neural Networks (PINNs) are applied to analytical mechanical equations to solve for optimal plate orientations in closed-cell lattices; resulting geometries (6–10 plate configurations) are modeled, validated via finite element analysis, and experimentally realized using LAPIS-based additive manufacturing.

Result: A minimum of 6 plates is required for ultrastiff isotropy; 6-plate PINN lattices reach 90% of the Hashin-Shtrikman upper bound at relative density 0.4 and maintain near-isotropy (Zener ratio = 0.95 at ρ = 0.1); specific stiffness exceeds most reported isotropic architectures despite minor bending from suboptimal plate placement.

Conclusion: PINNs enable systematic, physics-grounded design of ultrastiff isotropic plate lattices, with 6-plate configurations representing an optimal balance of performance, manufacturability, and isotropy—establishing a new paradigm beyond heuristic design.

Abstract: Current methods of deriving ultrastiff isotropic metamaterials require heuristics or topology optimization, which are highly inefficient and limited by human input. To circumvent this, Physics Informed Neural Networks are applied to analytical equations to systematically solve for plate orientations in closed cell lattices. Following a standardized procedure, the plate orientations were modelled and assembled into geometries resembling quasi random foams for validation via finite element simulations. Closed cell 6-plate geometries were also additively manufactured using LAPIS and experimentally characterized. The results indicate that a minimum of 6 plates is required to constitute an ultrastiff isotropic lattice, 7 plates would return the established plate Cubic Octet structure while 8 to 10 plate geometries did not show significant differences in properties. Despite the introduction of some plate bending due to sub-optimal plate placement strategy, relative stiffness of the PINNs lattices adheres closely to the Hashin Shtrikman upper bound, 90 percent of Hashin Shtrikman upper bound at a relative density of 0.4, and the lattices maintain near isotropic behaviour, achieving a Zener ratio of 0.95 at a relative density of 0.1. Benchmarking results show that specific stiffness of PINNs lattices is higher than the majority of isotropic architectures reported in the literature.

</details>


### [24] [Mathematical and Numerical Analysis of Tsunami Generation and Propagation: The Initial Tsunami Condition](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.02708)
*Noureddine Lamsahel,Min Chen*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper establishes the mathematically appropriate initial free-surface condition for tsunami propagation models—exactly the final bottom deformation for nonlinear shallow-water (NSW) equations, and a Boussinesq-filtered version thereof for dispersive Boussinesq systems—and proves that the error between kinematic and static formulations scales linearly with earthquake rise time, vanishing for instantaneous slip.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To rigorously justify and quantify the widely used static (bottom-fixed) approximation in tsunami modeling, and explain observed amplitude overestimation in prior simulations when using unfiltered seabed deformation as initial condition.

Method: Mathematical analysis of the 2D nonlinear shallow-water and dispersive abcd-Boussinesq systems; derivation of exact initial conditions via asymptotic consistency; rigorous error estimation linking solution discrepancy to earthquake rise time; numerical validation using Okada seabed deformation.

Result: 1) For NSW: optimal initial free surface = final bottom deformation. 2) For Boussinesq: optimal initial free surface = Boussinesq-filtered bottom profile. 3) Error between kinematic and static solutions is O(τ), where τ is rise time. 4) Both NSW and unfiltered passive initialization overestimate wave amplitudes vs. linear potential theory.

Conclusion: The static approximation is justified only with model-appropriate initial conditions and becomes exact in the limit of instantaneous faulting; using unfiltered bathymetry introduces systematic amplitude errors, especially in dispersive regimes.

Abstract: This work investigates the appropriate initial condition for tsunami propagation that allows replacing a kinematic system with its corresponding static formulation, where the bottom is fixed at its final earthquake-induced deformation. Using the two-dimensional (2D) nonlinear shallow-water equations (NSW) and a 2D dispersive $abcd$-Boussinesq system, we prove that for the NSW system, the appropriate initial free-surface condition is exactly the final bottom deformation. For the Boussinesq model, this condition is obtained by first applying a \textit{ Boussinesq filtering operator} to the bottom profile. Furthermore, for both models, we establish an error estimate between the kinematic and static solutions that depends linearly on the earthquake rise time. This result provides a rigorous mathematical explanation for the errors observed in previous numerical tsunami simulations. As a direct consequence, the error vanishes as the bottom motion becomes instantaneous. We validate the theoretical results using an Okada solution for the seabed deformation. In particular, both the NSW model and the passive approach, which initializes the free surface with the unfiltered bottom deformation, overestimate the amplitudes of the tsunami waves relative to the linear potential theory for the configurations considered.

</details>


### [25] [All-Disorder Localization for the One-Dimensional Anderson Model with Power-Law Hopping](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.02724)
*Huajian Jiang,Yunfeng Shi*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper proves spectral localization (pure point spectrum with polynomially decaying eigenfunctions) for a 1D long-range random Schrödinger operator with decay exponent r > 3/2, confirming r = 3/2 as the critical threshold.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To rigorously establish spectral localization for a one-dimensional long-range random Schrödinger operator, resolving the predicted marginal exponent r = 3/2 from prior physics literature (analytical and numerical studies).

Method: Adaptation and refinement of the fractional moment method and multiscale analysis tailored to long-range hopping terms decaying as |n−m|^{−r}, combined with probabilistic estimates for i.i.d. uniform disorder.

Result: For all λ > 0 and r > 3/2, H_{λ,ω} exhibits almost-sure pure point spectrum; moreover, every eigenfunction ψ_j satisfies ∑_n (1+|n|)^{2τ}|ψ_j(n)|² < ∞ for all 0 ≤ τ < r − 3/2.

Conclusion: Spectral localization holds strictly above the critical exponent r = 3/2, confirming its role as the sharp phase transition threshold for delocalization-to-localization in this long-range disordered model.

Abstract: We consider the one-dimensional random operator \[ (H_{\lambda,\omega}u)(n) = \sum_{m\ne n}\frac{u(m)}{|n-m|^r} +\lambda\omega_nu(n), \] where the random variables $\omega_n$ are independent and uniformly distributed on $[-1,1]$ (i.e., \mbox{$\omega_n\stackrel{\mathrm{i.i.d.}}{\sim}\operatorname{Unif}[-1,1]$}). For every $\lambda>0$ and $r>3/2$, we prove that $H_{\lambda,\omega}$ almost surely has pure point spectrum and admits an orthonormal eigenbasis $\{\psi_j\}$ satisfying \[ \sum_{n\in\bbZ}(1+|n|)^{2\tau}|\psi_j(n)|^2<\infty \qquad \text{for every }j\text{ and }0\le\tau<r-\frac32. \] These conclusions establish spectral localization, thereby rigorously confirming the prediction of earlier analytical and numerical studies in the physics literature, which identified $r=3/2$ as the marginal exponent.

</details>


### [26] [Correlators of the Jacobi Unitary Ensemble on a General Interval and Hurwitz Numbers](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.02795)
*Matvey Sushkov*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper extends the large-N genus expansion of correlators for the Jacobi Unitary Ensemble from the standard interval [0,1] to arbitrary [p,q], expresses Schur–Selberg integrals via symmetric group characters and content products, and recovers GUE polygon-gluing structure in a double-scaling limit.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To generalize prior results on Jacobi Unitary Ensemble correlators (previously limited to [0,1]) to arbitrary spectral intervals [p,q], while preserving structural insights from Hurwitz theory and 2D Toda tau functions; also to unify with Gaussian Unitary Ensemble asymptotics.

Method: Uses triple monotone Hurwitz numbers for genus expansion; applies Frobenius characteristic map and idempotent basis of Z(C[S_n]) to derive explicit Schur–Selberg integral formula; analyzes scaling limits and tau-function specializations in the 2D Toda hierarchy.

Result: Large-N correlator expansion generalized to arbitrary [p,q] with fixed α/N, β/N; closed-form Schur–Selberg integral expressed in terms of irreducible S_n characters and content products; zero-mean ensemble characterized; GUE polygon-gluing formula recovered termwise as c_A = c_B → ∞.

Conclusion: The interval shift [0,1] → [p,q] preserves the hypergeometric weight of the associated 2D Toda tau function and only modifies the second time specialization—revealing deep structural invariance in integrable hierarchies underlying random matrix ensembles.

Abstract: We study correlators of the Jacobi Unitary Ensemble $\mathbf J(p,q,\alpha,\beta,N)$ of Hermitian matrices with spectrum in an arbitrary real interval $[p,q]$. For $(p,q)=(0,1)$, Gisonni, Grava and Ruzza expressed the large-$N$ expansion of the correlators $\langle\prod_j \mathrm{tr}\,X^{\lambda_j}\rangle$ through triple monotone Hurwitz numbers. We extend this genus expansion to arbitrary $p,q$ with $\alpha/N$ and $\beta/N$ fixed. Using the Frobenius characteristic map and the idempotent basis of the center of $\mathbb C[\mathfrak S_n]$, we also obtain an explicit formula for the Schur--Selberg type integral $\int_{(p,q)^N}\chi_\lambda(x)\,\Delta^2(x)\prod_a (q-x_a)^\beta (x_a-p)^\alpha\,\mathrm{d}^Nx$ in terms of irreducible characters of the symmetric group and content products. As consequences, we describe the zero-mean ensemble, and we recover, term by term in the genus expansion, the polygon-gluing formula for the Gaussian Unitary Ensemble in the limit $c_A=c_B\to\infty$. Structurally, passing from $[0,1]$ to $[p,q]$ leaves the hypergeometric weight of the associated 2D Toda tau function unchanged and only changes the specialization of its second set of times.

</details>


### [27] [Microscopic Origin of the Equation of Motion for Open Thermodynamic Systems](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.03235)
*E. Aydiner*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper independently derives the same second-order equation of motion for open thermodynamic systems—previously obtained from the First Law—from a mesoscopic particle-transfer model between two reservoirs, revealing inertial, dissipative, and restoring terms with explicit physical parameter dependence; it confirms damping regimes, resonance, relaxation times, recovers Onsager linear response in the slow limit, and strengthens the dynamical unification of thermodynamics with mechanics and circuits.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide independent theoretical justification and deeper physical grounding for the previously proposed second-order thermodynamic equation of motion by deriving it from a mesoscopic particle-transfer model, rather than solely from macroscopic thermodynamics.

Method: Derives the equation of motion from first principles of collective momentum balance, particle conservation, and reservoir chemical-potential response in a two-reservoir system connected by a channel; identifies effective inertial, dissipative, and restoring coefficients in terms of microscopic/physical parameters.

Result: Recovers identical second-order ODE structure; obtains explicit expressions for effective mass, damping, and stiffness coefficients; predicts distinct damping regimes (underdamped/overdamped), characteristic relaxation times, resonance under periodic driving, and natural frequency determined by physical parameters; reduces to Onsager linear flux–force relation in the slow-transport limit.

Conclusion: The independent emergence of the same dynamical structure from two physically distinct approaches (macroscopic thermodynamics vs. mesoscopic transport) validates the proposed thermodynamic equation of motion and supports a unified dynamical framework across thermodynamics, mechanics, and electrical circuits.

Abstract: In my previous work (arXiv:2609.07810) \cite{Aydiner_2026}, I derived a second-order equation of motion for open thermodynamic systems from the First Law of Thermodynamics and established its dynamical equivalence with mechanical oscillators and electrical RLC circuits. In the present study, I independently recover the same mathematical structure of the equation of motion from a mesoscopic model of particle transfer between two reservoirs connected by a channel. Starting from collective momentum balance, particle conservation, and reservoir chemical-potential response, I derive the inertial--dissipative--restoring dynamics and obtain explicit expressions for its effective coefficients in terms of the physical parameters of the system. The resulting equation predicts distinct damping regimes, characteristic relaxation time scales, and resonance under periodic driving. The natural frequency and resonance conditions are explicitly determined by the physical parameters of the particle-transfer system. In the slow-transport limit, the model recovers the conventional Onsager-type linear flux--force relation. The independent emergence of the same dynamical structure from two distinct physical approaches provides theoretical support for the previously proposed thermodynamic equation of motion. By identifying the physical mechanisms underlying its effective coefficients, the present study strengthens the physical foundations of the proposed framework and supports the possibility of describing open thermodynamic systems within a common dynamical framework shared with mechanical and electrical systems.

</details>


### [28] [Anderson localisation in infinite and finite resonator crystals with long-range interactions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.03286)
*Habib Ammari,Xin Fu,Wenjia Jing,Jiayu Qiu,Alexander Uhlmann*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper proves Anderson localization for random capacitance operators modeling subwavelength resonators in periodic 2D/3D media with high-contrast, disordered material parameters; it quantifies mode concentration, provides explicit localization windows scaling with disorder strength, and establishes uniform spectral and dynamical localization near the upper band edge under reflection symmetry.
