<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To rigorously establish Anderson localization in high-contrast subwavelength metamaterials—where standard Schrödinger-operator methods fail—by leveraging the capacitance matrix framework that captures exponential decay of interactions and accounts for geometric and material disorder.

Method: Uses a fully periodic geometry with one resonator per unit cell; models disorder via independent uniformly distributed inverse material factors; retains exact exponentially decaying capacitance interactions; applies variational boundary comparison and material-variable eigenvalue-spacing estimates to lift localization from truncated to finite matrices; employs spectral and dynamical localization criteria under reflection symmetry; derives quantitative mode concentration bounds using ε-scaling and probabilistic analysis.

Result: Proves spectral and dynamical localization near the upper spectral edge for all positive disorder strengths under reflection symmetry; obtains an explicit localization window of width ∝ W at weak disorder W; shows that in a bounded sample of size ε, all but O(ε^{1−d} log(1/ε)) modes in the localization interval concentrate within radii O(ε log(1/ε)); establishes uniformity across small-contrast capacitance families and a common limiting density of states for truncated and finite matrices.

Conclusion: Anderson localization is rigorously established for random capacitance operators arising in high-contrast subwavelength resonator systems; the analysis yields quantitative, disorder- and scale-dependent concentration of eigenmodes and bridges discrete capacitance modeling with continuum wave localization theory.

Abstract: We establish Anderson localisation for random capacitance operators and quantify concentration of individual modes of finite capacitance matrices for clusters of subwavelength high-contrast resonators. The underlying geometry is fully periodic in dimensions $d=2,3$, with one resonator per cell, and the inverse material factors are independent and uniformly distributed. We retain the full capacitance interaction, whose coefficients decay exponentially with lattice separation. Under coordinate reflection symmetry, spectral and dynamical localisation hold near the upper spectral edge for every fixed positive disorder strength. At weak disorder $W$, we obtain an explicit localisation window of energy width proportional to $W$; prescribed positive intervals are localised at sufficiently large disorder. A variational boundary comparison and a material-variable eigenvalue-spacing estimate transfer localisation from truncated matrices to individual modes of the finite capacitance matrix. In a bounded sample of cell size $\varepsilon$, with probability tending to one, all except for $O(\varepsilon^{1-d}\log(1/\varepsilon))$ modes in the localisation interval concentrate within radii $O(\varepsilon\log(1/\varepsilon))$. The estimates are uniform for the contrast-dependent capacitance family at small contrast. We also prove a common limiting density of states for the truncated and finite matrices and illustrate concentration numerically.

</details>


### [29] [Separatrix splitting in a multiferroic neuromorphic oscillator: toward Arnold diffusion](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.03297)
*D. Maroulakos,Z. Toklikishvili,A. Wal,L. Chotorlishvili*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper introduces a novel magnetoelectrically coupled ferromagnetic–ferroelectric neuromorphic oscillator, analyzes its Hamiltonian dynamics using action–angle formalism and elliptic functions, establishes KAM confinement in the autonomous case, and characterizes resonant structures, homoclinic splitting, and damping-dependent thresholds under periodic electric forcing—providing analytical foundations for polarization-based readout and transport in neuromorphic devices.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To go beyond conventional ferromagnetic or antiferromagnetic neuromorphic systems by exploiting magnetoelectric coupling between ferromagnetic and ferroelectric layers for richer dynamical functionality and electrical readout capability.

Method: Derives a reduced conservative Landau–Lifshitz–type model with inertial ferroelectric polarization; constructs an action–angle description of the double-well–harmonic-oscillator Hamiltonian; computes quartic-well frequency maps and Fourier coefficients via elliptic functions; applies KAM theory to establish isoenergetic nondegeneracy and regular confinement; analyzes forced dynamics with third frequency, resonance intersections, homoclinic splitting, and damping-dependent thresholds using perturbation theory and Melnikov-type methods.

Result: Analytical characterization of monotone in-well twist, KAM-torus confinement in autonomous regime, transverse resonance intersections under forcing, leading-order resonant widths, local homoclinic splitting magnitude, and a damping-dependent threshold for splitting onset; identifies structures enabling polarization-based electrical readout and transport interpretation.

Conclusion: The magnetoelectric ferromagnetic–ferroelectric oscillator offers a theoretically rich platform for neuromorphic computing, with analytically tractable nonlinear dynamics supporting stable regular motion and controlled chaotic transport pathways—though Arnold diffusion remains unproven without further connecting-orbit analysis or long-time numerical evidence.

Abstract: Prototype magnetic neuromorphic systems, in essence, are based either on ferromagnetic or antiferromagnetic materials as key elements for conventional neuromorphic setups. In this work, we propose a novel neuromorphic system based on a ferromagnetic disk and a ferroelectric layer coupled via the magnetoelectric effect. We study a magnetoelectrically coupled ferromagnetic--ferroelectric oscillator as a candidate element for neuromorphic devices. A local reduction of conservative Landau--Lifshitz dynamics identifies a magnetic coordinate--momentum pair, which is coupled to an inertial ferroelectric polarization mode. We derive the action--angle description of the resulting double-well--harmonic-oscillator Hamiltonian, obtain the quartic-well frequency map and Fourier coefficients in terms of elliptic functions, and establish monotone in-well twist. In the autonomous two-degree-of-freedom model, isoenergetic nondegeneracy supports KAM confinement on regular compact domains. Periodic electric forcing introduces a third effective frequency, although the harmonic magnetic sector leaves the unperturbed resonance loci parallel. An explicitly specified nonlinear magnetic extension produces transverse resonance intersections. We calculate leading resonant widths and local homoclinic splitting, and derive a damping-dependent splitting threshold for a stated scalar reduction. These results identify analytical structures relevant to transport and explain possible polarization-based electrical readout. Establishing Arnold diffusion in the coupled model requires additional connecting-orbit analysis or controlled long-time evidence; neither a diffusion law nor Nekhoroshev stability of the driven model is inferred from the present finite-time calculations.

</details>


### [30] [Supersymmetric Neuromorphic FitzHugh--Nagumo model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.03312)
*D. Maroulakos,A. Wal,S. K. Mishra,Z. Toklikishvili,L. Chotorlishvili*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper develops a supersymmetric spectral approach to analyze stochastic FitzHugh–Nagumo dynamics inspired by an antiferromagnetic neuromorphic model; it derives exact and approximate relaxation eigenvalues, introduces a normalized two-state amplitude for coherence quantification, and clarifies the domain of validity of this classical coherence diagnostic—especially in relation to spiking versus monotonic relaxation.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To establish a rigorous spectral framework for stochastic neuromorphic dynamics—specifically FitzHugh–Nagumo-type systems—by leveraging supersymmetric quantum mechanics, and to define a classical, occupation-based coherence measure applicable to bistable stochastic relaxation, while delineating its limits in spiking regimes.

Method: Performs local normal-form reduction of tangential Néel-vector dynamics to map magnetic control parameters onto fast/slow variables; freezes the recovery variable to obtain a 1D Fokker–Planck equation with quartic potential; applies similarity transformation and exact supersymmetric factorization of the generator; computes the first nonzero eigenvalue via partner Hamiltonian ground state; constructs a normalized two-state amplitude from projection onto lowest eigenmodes; derives localized-basis coherence in terms of time-dependent basin populations and equilibrium weights.

Result: Exact supersymmetric factorization yields analytic access to the leading relaxation eigenvalue; in weak-noise bistability, it matches the sum of Kramers interwell rates; the auxiliary two-state amplitude’s coherence is maximized when normalized amplitude weights are equal (not necessarily basin populations); a fixed-recovery case shows a transient coherence pulse during monotonic relaxation; deterministic spiking violates the bistable assumption, limiting applicability of the two-state approximation.

Conclusion: Stochastic relaxation spectra in neuromorphic models can be linked to a classical, occupation-derived coherence diagnostic via supersymmetric spectral methods; however, this diagnostic is only quantitatively valid within well-separated bistable regions—thus it does not apply during spiking transients where barrier separation collapses, and it does not imply quantum coherence.

Abstract: We develop a supersymmetric spectral description of stochastic FitzHugh--Nagumo dynamics motivated by an effective antiferromagnetic neuromorphic model. A local normal-form reduction of the adopted tangential Néel-vector dynamics relates magnetic control parameters to fast activation and slow recovery coordinates. Freezing the recovery variable yields a one-dimensional Fokker--Planck equation with a quartic potential, whose similarity-transformed generator admits an exact supersymmetric factorization. The first nonzero relaxation eigenvalue is determined by the ground state of the partner operator; in the weak-noise bistable regime, it is approximated by the sum of the two interwell Kramers rates. Projecting the similarity-transformed probability density onto the two lowest modes defines a normalized auxiliary two-state amplitude. We derive its localized-basis coherence in terms of both the time-dependent basin populations and their equilibrium weights. Its maximum corresponds to equal normalized amplitude weights, which generally differ from equal basin populations. The fixed-recovery example exhibits a pronounced coherence pulse during monotonic relaxation without a spike. Conversely, the illustrated deterministic spiking trajectory repeatedly leaves the bistable domain and passes through regions with insufficient barrier separation, restricting the validity of the localized two-state approximation. These results connect stochastic relaxation spectra to an occupation-based coherence diagnostic and delimit its application to neuromorphic spiking. The diagnostic characterizes an auxiliary amplitude representation of classical stochastic dynamics and does not by itself establish microscopic quantum coherence.

</details>


### [31] [Lossless propagation bounds for long-range fermionic interactions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.03441)
*Stefan Teufel*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper establishes optimal propagation estimates for fermionic many-body interactions under time evolution, proving polynomial-in-time growth of local interaction norms without spatial decay loss, with sharp threshold a > d_vol for spatial polynomial exponents; it applies to both fermionic and spin systems and enables rigorous quasi-adiabatic continuation, LPPL, gap stability, and response theory.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To establish rigorous, optimal light-cone–like propagation bounds for extensive many-body interactions under time evolution—particularly for fermions on metric spaces—where existing Lieb–Robinson–type bounds fail due to non-locality or lack of exponential decay assumptions.

Method: Constructs time-evolved local representatives of interactions using weighted norm bounds with e^{κ|X|}(1+diam X)^a/p, exploits polynomial volume growth d_vol of the underlying metric space and cell partitions, and derives norm growth estimates under unitary evolution via Duhamel expansion and combinatorial norm control; proves optimality via counterexamples at a ≤ d_vol.

Result: Proves that evolved interaction norms grow at most polynomially in |t−s| when weighted by e^{c(1+|t−s|)^{−b}|X|}(1+diam X)^p, with p = a allowed and threshold a > d_vol optimal; shows applicability to quasi-adiabatic filters, LPPL, gap stability, adiabatic theorem, and linear response for extensive perturbations.

Conclusion: The paper provides the first optimal, spatially sharp, polynomial-time propagation estimate for extensive fermionic (and spin) many-body interactions, enabling rigorous analysis of dynamics beyond exponential locality, and unifies and extends foundational tools in quantum many-body theory.

Abstract: We prove propagation estimates for extensive fermionic many-body interactions with polynomial time growth, no loss of spatial decay, and an optimal threshold for the polynomial spatial exponent. More precisely, we construct local representatives of evolved fermionic many-body interactions on metric spaces with polynomial volume growth of order $d_{\mathrm{vol}}$ and suitable cell partitions. For the even Hamiltonian interaction $\Phi_u$ and the even interaction $\Psi$ being evolved, we assume bounds, uniform in the site, on sums of term norms over finite supports $X$ containing a given site, with weights $e^{\kappa|X|}(1+\operatorname{diam}X)^a$ and $e^{\kappa|X|}(1+\operatorname{diam}X)^p$, respectively. Here $a>d_{\mathrm{vol}}$, $0\le p\le a$, $\kappa$ is sufficiently large, and the Hamiltonian bound is uniform in time. We construct local representatives whose interaction norms with weight $e^{c(1+|t-s|)^{-b}|X|}(1+\operatorname{diam}X)^p$ grow at most polynomially in $|t-s|$, for suitable $c,b>0$. In particular, $p=a$ is allowed: the Hamiltonian need not decay faster than the interaction being evolved. The threshold $a>d_{\mathrm{vol}}$ is optimal in general: uniform polynomial time bounds at $p=a$ fail for $0<a\le d_{\mathrm{vol}}$, even for smooth time-dependent two-site interactions. Polynomial time growth permits integration against filters with sufficiently many finite absolute moments, including those used in quasi-adiabatic constructions and quantum belief propagation. We derive consequences for filtered interactions and the dynamics they generate, providing dynamical estimates for perturbation theory with extensive perturbations, including gap stability, the principle that local perturbations perturb locally (LPPL), adiabatic theory, and response theory. All results also hold for quantum spin systems.

</details>


### [32] [Koopman-von Neumann formalism meets contact Hamiltonian systems](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.03522)
*Federico Zadra*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper formulates contact Hamiltonian dynamics in the Koopman–von Neumann (KvN) framework on a Hilbert space built from the contact volume, ensuring unitarity via a divergence correction; it proves key operator-theoretic properties (symmetry, essential self-adjointness, unitary evolution), covariance under contactomorphisms, and extends results to integrable systems and symplectization.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To extend the Koopman–von Neumann formalism—previously developed for symplectic Hamiltonian systems—to contact Hamiltonian dynamics, enabling a Hilbert-space description with unitary evolution while respecting the geometric structure of contact manifolds.

Method: Constructs a KvN Hilbert space using the natural contact volume form; derives the KvN generator incorporating a divergence correction to ensure skew-adjointness; applies functional-analytic tools (e.g., essential self-adjointness criteria, direct integral decomposition) and contact geometry (contactomorphisms, symplectization) to analyze spectral and dynamical properties.

Result: The KvN generator is symmetric and essentially self-adjoint for complete contact Hamiltonian vector fields; induces a strongly continuous unitary evolution; transforms covariantly under contactomorphisms; admits invariant direct-integral decomposition and strongly commuting generators for completely integrable systems; and relates unitarily to the KvN formulation on the symplectization.

Conclusion: The KvN framework successfully generalizes to contact Hamiltonian systems, providing a rigorous, geometrically consistent, unitary quantum-like representation that preserves the intrinsic structure of contact geometry and enables analysis of integrability and symmetry.

Abstract: The present work develops a Koopman--von Neumann (KvN) formulation of contact Hamiltonian dynamics on the Hilbert space defined by the contact volume. The resulting generator includes the divergence correction required for unitary evolution. We establish symmetry and, for complete contact Hamiltonian vector fields, essential self-adjointness and a strongly continuous unitary evolution; we also describe covariance under contactomorphisms. For completely integrable contact systems with complete flows, it is possible to recover an invariant direct-integral decomposition and strongly commuting KvN generators. We further relate contact KvN dynamics to the KvN formulation of its symplectization.

</details>

### [33] [Quantum superposition of Cosmic String spacetimes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00327)
*Enrique Arias,Shoshauna Gauvin,Willy Izquierdo,Robert B. Mann*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper explores quantum interference effects arising from spacetime superposition—specifically, a superposition of Cosmic String spacetimes—with an Unruh-DeWitt detector, revealing observable signatures distinct from classical mixtures and thus offering potential experimental probes of quantum gravity.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To identify observable physical consequences of spacetime superposition—a key prediction of quantum gravity—by probing how vacuum fluctuations affect a quantum detector in a superposed topologically non-trivial (but geometrically flat) spacetime.

Method: The authors compute the response function of an Unruh-DeWitt detector coupled to a quantum scalar field in a superposition of Cosmic String spacetimes with differing topological charge (deficit angles), contrasting it with the response for a classical statistical mixture.

Result: They find genuine quantum interference terms in the detector’s transition probability that vanish in the classical mixture case; these terms depend on the relative phase and topological charges of the superposed spacetimes and manifest as modulations in the detector response.

Conclusion: Spacetime superposition yields measurable, non-classical detector responses—constituting a concrete, theoretically accessible signature of quantum gravitational effects, even in absence of curvature.

Abstract: Since the quantum nature of spacetime is anticipated by a theory of Quantum Gravity, the possibility of spacetime superposition would be a natural consequence. This spacetime interference phenomena could bring us some clues about what effects could be expected over quantum fields and their interaction with matter. In this article, we investigate the effect of the superposition of a Cosmic String spacetime on the Unruh-De Witt detector. This is of interest as the spacetimes possess flat geometry but non-trivial topology. We analyze the effect of the interference of a Cosmic String with different topological charge over the Unruh-De Witt detector response function to vacuum fluctuations. We obtain some quantum interference effects, due to the superposition of spacetimes, which would be absent if a classical probabilistic mixture of spacetimes would be considered. In this manner, we obtain real physical consequences that would be indications of a Quantum Gravity phenomena.

</details>


### [34] [Exact scalarization thresholds and onset laws for a nonminimally coupled scalar field on the Ellis wormhole](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00541)
*Abdullah Guvendi,Omar Mustafa,Claudio Furtado*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper analytically solves the linear scalar perturbation problem for a nonminimally coupled real scalar field on a fixed Ellis wormhole background, reducing it to a trigonometric Pöschl–Teller equation; it derives exact threshold eigenvalues, radial eigenfunction profiles (Gegenbauer), and counts of unstable modes per angular sector; it further analyzes nonlinear static solutions with quartic self-interaction near thresholds and performs numerical evolutions suggesting saturation on the nodeless branch—though without addressing full backreaction.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand linear instability thresholds and nonlinear static configurations of a nonminimally coupled scalar field on an Ellis wormhole spacetime—a key step toward assessing possible scalarization and stability of wormholes in beyond-GR theories.

Method: Analytical reduction of the static radial perturbation equation via coordinate transformation $x = a \tan z$, mapping to a trigonometric Pöschl–Teller potential; exact spectral solution; analysis of resonance vs. bound nature of threshold states; perturbative expansion near s-wave thresholds for quartic self-interacting probe field; local branch construction and numerical time evolutions.

Result: Exact threshold spectrum $\zeta_{n\ell} = (n+\ell+1)^2/2$; closed-form Gegenbauer eigenfunctions; precise count of negative eigenvalues (unstable modes) per $\ell$-sector; distinction between zero-energy resonances ($\ell=0$) and square-integrable bound states ($\ell \geq 1$); explicit next-order growth rate $\sigma a = (\pi/4)g - (3\pi/16)g^2 + O(g^3)$ at first threshold; nonexistence of nontrivial spherical static solutions for $\zeta \leq 1/2$; local branch expansions near s-wave thresholds; numerical evidence for saturation on nodeless branch.

Conclusion: The Ellis wormhole admits analytically tractable scalar instabilities governed by a Pöschl–Teller spectrum; nonlinear probe-field analysis reveals threshold-driven bifurcations but does not imply existence or stability of fully backreacted scalarized wormholes.

Abstract: We study a real scalar field nonminimally coupled to curvature on a fixed Ellis wormhole background. The scalar perturbation decouples at linear order, and the substitution $ x=a \tan z $ reduces its static radial equation to a trigonometric Pöschl-Teller problem. This yields the exact threshold spectrum $ \zeta_{n\ell}=(n+\ell+1)^2/2 $, closed-form Gegenbauer profiles, and an exact count of negative radial eigenvalues in each angular sector. The s-wave thresholds are zero-energy resonances at which the growth rate rises linearly, whereas the $ \ell \geq1\ $ threshold states are square integrable and the growth rate rises as a square root. At the first threshold we also obtain the next-order term, $ \sigma a=(\pi/4)g-(3\pi/16)g^2+O(g^3) $ with $g=2\zeta-1 $. For a probe field with quartic self-interaction, we show that no nontrivial spherical static solution exists for $\zeta\leq1/2 $ and derive local branch expansions near the s-wave thresholds. Numerical evolutions are consistent with saturation on the nodeless branch. These nonlinear results refer to the probe-field model; they do not establish the existence or stability of fully backreacted scalarized wormholes.

</details>


### [35] [The Fluctuation-Dissipation Theorem and Dynamical Love Number in Schwarzschild Black Holes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00545)
*Dimitrios Giataganas,Alex Kehagias,Antonio Riotto*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper analyzes the real-time response of a Schwarzschild black hole in the Hartle-Hawking state to an external massless scalar field, unifying dissipation, thermal fluctuations, and conservative tidal response via a complexified radial contour on the Schwinger-Keldysh contour; it derives the fluctuation-dissipation relation directly from the radial equation, recovers the known low-frequency link between logarithmic running of the dynamical Love number (∝ ω²) and absorption (∝ ω), and identifies the horizon slope as the origin of the unit coefficient linking absorption, running, and zero-frequency thermal noise.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To unify dissipative, fluctuating, and conservative linear responses of a black hole to external perturbations within a single real-time framework, and to clarify the microscopic origin of the universal coefficient linking tidal running and absorption in the low-frequency limit.

Method: Uses a complexified radial contour to implement the Schwinger-Keldysh closed time path for the scalar field on a Schwarzschild background in the Hartle-Hawking state; derives the equilibrium fluctuation-dissipation relation directly from the radial wave equation for each multipole; performs low-frequency tidal expansion and tidal matching; leverages Kubo-Martin-Schwinger (KMS) analyticity and near-zone radial structure.

Result: Derives the exact fluctuation-dissipation relation at arbitrary real frequency directly from the radial construction; confirms the ω² logarithmic running of the conservative dynamical Love coefficient is governed by the same leading absorptive coefficient that determines the O(ω) dissipative response; identifies the unit coefficient in the running–absorption relation as arising from the Schwarzschild horizon slope; establishes that the zero-frequency thermal noise is quantitatively fixed by this same absorptive coefficient via KMS condition.

Conclusion: The conservative, dissipative, and fluctuating responses of a black hole are intrinsically unified through the radial structure of the wave equation and KMS analyticity; the horizon geometry (specifically its slope) fixes the universal coefficient linking tidal running and absorption, thereby connecting quantum thermal noise to classical tidal deformability in a fundamental way.

Abstract: We study the real-time response of a Schwarzschild black hole in the Hartle-Hawking state to an external massless scalar field. Using a complexified radial contour to represent the Schwinger-Keldysh closed time path, we describe dissipation, thermal fluctuations and conservative tidal response within a common framework. Before performing tidal matching, we derive the equilibrium fluctuation-dissipation relation directly from the radial construction for every scalar multipole at arbitrary real frequency. In the low-frequency tidal expansion, we recover the known relation between the logarithmic running of the conservative dynamical Love coefficient at order $\omega^2$ and the leading dissipative response at order $\omega$. We explain how the same leading absorptive coefficient enters both relations at low frequency and trace the unit coefficient of the running relation to the Schwarzschild horizon slope. In the Hartle-Hawking state, this common coefficient relates the logarithmic running directly to the zero-frequency thermal noise. Kubo-Martin-Schwinger analyticity determines the thermal noise from the dissipative response, whereas the near-zone radial equation and tidal matching determine the conservative logarithmic running.

</details>


### [36] [Holonomy classification, gyroscope precession, and Landau levels in Gödel-type spacetimes and their superfluid analog](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00568)
*Claudio Furtado,G. Q. Garcia,Abdullah Guvendi,Alexandre M. de M. Carvalho*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper computes parallel-transport holonomies of azimuthal circles in the homogeneous Gödel-type spacetimes, characterizing Lorentz transformation types via an invariant Δ(r); proves chronology radius condition Δ(rc) < 0 when CTCs appear; derives Wilson loops (vector/spinor), Fermi–Walker transport on CTCs, and draws an exact analogy to rotating superfluid effective metrics and Klein–Gordon Landau modes.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To systematically characterize causal and geometric structure—especially closed timelike curves (CTCs)—in Gödel-type spacetimes via holonomy, and to explore analogies with condensed-matter systems (e.g., rotating superfluids) using geometric tools.

Method: Analytic computation of parallel-transport holonomies for circular orbits about the symmetry axis; classification via invariant Δ(r) governing Lorentz transformation type (elliptic/parabolic/hyperbolic); use of Killing-vector scalars; derivation of vector and spinor Wilson loops; analysis of Fermi–Walker transport; comparison with effective metric of rotating superfluid; exact solution of radial L² localization for Klein–Gordon Landau modes.

Result: Exact expression for holonomy invariant Δ(r); proof that Δ(rc) = −cosh(mrc) < 0 at chronology radius rc where CTCs emerge; complete classification of parabolic radii across the family; explicit vector/spinor Wilson loops; Fermi–Walker transport shown to be nontrivial on CTCs; exact mapping to superfluid effective metric under coarse-graining; analytic L² localization lengths for Landau modes relative to Som–Raychaudhuri chronology radius.

Conclusion: Holonomy provides a precise, invariant diagnostic of causal structure and Lorentzian geometry in Gödel-type universes; the invariant Δ(r) unifies classification of transport behavior, CTC onset, and analog physics; results are exact within the metric family and suggest deeper links between relativistic causality and quantum condensed-matter phenomena.

Abstract: We derive the parallel-transport holonomies of circles centered on the symmetry axis in the homogeneous Gödel-type family of Rebouças and Tiomno, which includes the Gödel universe, the causal member with $m^2=4\Omega^2$, and the Som--Raychaudhuri limit. The holonomy is governed by a single invariant $\Delta(r)$, whose sign determines whether the Lorentz transformation is elliptic, parabolic, or hyperbolic. We determine the number of parabolic radii across the family and prove that, whenever the azimuthal circles become closed timelike curves (CTCs), the chronology radius satisfies $\Delta(r_c)=-\cosh(mr_c)<0$. We also express this invariant in terms of Killing-vector scalars, derive the vector and spinor Wilson loops, give the exact condition for trivial holonomy after a finite number of turns, and analyze Fermi--Walker transport on circular CTCs. Finally, we discuss an analogy with the effective metric of a rotating superfluid, valid under stated conditions, and derive exact expressions for the radial $L^2$ localization of Klein--Gordon Landau modes relative to the Som--Raychaudhuri chronology radius. The geometric results are exact within the metric family considered; the discussion of the analog system relies on the validity of a coarse-grained effective description.

</details>


### [37] [Unitary implementability of Darboux transformations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00724)
*Guillermo A. Mena Marugán,Alvaro Torres-Caballeros,José Velhinho*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper analyzes the unitary implementability of Darboux transformations—canonical transformations in black hole perturbation theory—within a quantum field theoretic framework, showing they are unitarily implementable under general conditions on effective potentials, that the physically preferred Fock representation differs from the standard massless one, and that full unitary equivalence requires more general canonical transformations beyond Darboux.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To establish a consistent quantum field theory for gauge-invariant perturbations of nonrotating black holes by determining when classical Darboux transformations (which relate isospectral effective potentials) admit unitary representations in the quantum theory, particularly in the black hole interior where dynamics and quantization are subtle.

Method: The authors employ Hamiltonian formalism for gauge-invariant perturbations, construct canonical transformations corresponding to Darboux maps between master equations (e.g., Regge-Wheeler and Zerilli), analyze unitary implementability via criteria on Bogoliubov coefficients (requiring square-integrability), compare Fock representations using unitary equivalence criteria (Shale–Stinespring theorem), and generalize beyond Darboux to broader classes of canonical transformations.

Result: Darboux transformations are unitarily implementable under mild conditions on effective potentials; the Heisenberg-unitary-selected Fock representation is inequivalent to the standard massless representation of the Regge-Wheeler variable; not all unitarily implementable representations are connected via Darboux transformations—only via more general canonical transformations; this enables unitary extension of isospectrality to all angular momentum modes in QFT.

Conclusion: The choice of master variables critically affects the quantum representation’s physical viability and unitary equivalence class; Darboux transformations alone are insufficient to span the space of unitarily implementable dynamics, necessitating broader canonical mappings—thereby clarifying the interface between classical black hole perturbation theory and rigorous quantum field theory on curved spacetimes.

Abstract: We investigate the unitary implementability of the canonical transformations corresponding to Darboux transformations in the Hamiltonian formalism recently developed for gauge invariant perturbations of nonrotating black holes. Starting from the formalism in the black hole interior, we demonstrate that these canonical transformations are unitarily implementable under general conditions on the effective potentials appearing in the master equations of the perturbations. We also show that the privileged massless Fock representation of the perturbative variables selected by admitting (Heisenberg) unitary dynamics, employed in our quantization, is not unitarily equivalent to the simple massless representation of the master variables satisfying the effective Regge-Wheeler equation. This highlights the importance of the choice of master variables in which the quantum representation is initially defined. Furthermore, we show that not all such choices of variables possessing unitarily implementable dynamics can be reached through Darboux transformations, but rather through more general canonical transformations. These results advance our understanding of the relationship between well-established classical relations in black hole perturbation theory and their quantum representations, explaining how isospectral Darboux transformations can be extended to include all the infinitely many possible values of the angular momentum such that they are unitarily implementable in a quantum field theory context.

</details>


### [38] [Excess inner-horizon entropy and the thermodynamics of Kerr formation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00863)
*Francisco S. N. Lobo,Jorge Ovalle,Manuel E. Rodrigues*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper introduces a charge-matched thermodynamic framework for regular rotating black holes, defining 'hidden entropy' as excess inner-horizon area relative to Kerr at fixed M and J; it proves strict bounds on this entropy, analyzes its evolution in time-dependent models, and examines regularity conditions (e.g., Ricci integrability, horizon preservation) across parameter space.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To reconcile regular black-hole models with thermodynamic consistency—particularly addressing entropy attribution in spacetimes with inner horizons—and to understand how rotation and regularization affect horizon structure and entropy distribution beyond standard Kerr.

Method: Analytical derivation using general relativity and black-hole thermodynamics: defines hidden entropy via inner-horizon area deviation; imposes regularity and interior-profile constraints to bound h_c; derives exact evolution equations for inner-root dynamics; examines analytic continuation, curvature singularities, and integrability of Ricci scalar across the parameter n.

Result: Proves 0 < S_excess < 4πM√(M²−a²); shows S_excess → 0 at Kerr endpoint and → πh_c² in Schwarzschild limit; establishes that rotation redistributes entropy between horizons while preserving total S_+^Kerr + S_-^Kerr = 4πM²; identifies n = −1 (Kerr), n = 0 (Minkowski breaking), and n = 2 (logarithmic root continuation) as critical points; confirms local absolute integrability of Ricci scalar for −1 < n ≤ 2.

Conclusion: Hidden entropy is a well-defined, bounded geometric-thermodynamic quantity in regular rotating black-hole models; its vanishing at Kerr reflects extremality and uniqueness, while nonzero values signal deviations from vacuum GR solutions—yet full attainment of the entropy bound requires loss of regular Killing horizons, enforcing a thermodynamic regularity trade-off.

Abstract: \noindent We develop a charge-matched thermodynamic framework for the regular rotating black-hole family underlying an exact model of Kerr formation. Since the Kerr endpoint already possesses a Cauchy horizon, hidden entropy is identified with the excess inner-horizon area relative to Kerr at fixed mass $M$ and angular momentum $J=Ma$: $\Sexc=\pi[h_c^2-(r_-^{\Kerr})^2]$, where $h_c$ is the regular inner-horizon radius and $r_-^{\Kerr}=M-\sqrt{M^2-a^2}$. Under regularity and interior-profile assumptions, we prove $r_-^{\Kerr}<h_c<h$ and establish the sharp bound $0<\Sexc<4\pi M\sqrt{M^2-a^2}$. The Schwarzschild limit yields $\Sexc=\pi h_c^2$, while $\Sexc=0$ at the Kerr endpoint. The Kerr reference entropy satisfies $S_+^{\Kerr}+S_-^{\Kerr}=4\pi M^2$, so rotation redistributes its two-horizon contributions at fixed mass. For the time-dependent model, we derive exact evolution equations for the inner root and $\Sexc$, interpreted as root-bookkeeping identities unless the roots are established as dynamical horizons. In the minimal $N=1$ trajectory, the apparent pole at $n=2$ is removable in the mass profile, giving a logarithmic continuation of the root equation, although the stationary curvature is singular there. The stationary Ricci-scalar density is locally absolutely integrable for $-1<n\leq2$, with the Kerr endpoint $n=-1$ treated separately. Minkowski breaking occurs at $n=0$, independently of inner-horizon disappearance. Quasi-extremal regular configurations approach, but cannot attain, the entropy bound without losing a regular Killing horizon.

</details>


### [39] [Daemon sub Ponte: The Cost of a Second Black Hole (A Scholium on an ER=EPR Thought Experiment)](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.01194)
*Yen Chin Ong*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper analyzes the energy cost of converting Hawking radiation from an old black hole into a second, entangled black hole—a key thought experiment in ER=EPR—deriving conditional lower bounds on external energy and constraints on exterior correlations, while distinguishing entropy-level feasibility from exact/approximate purification using Page curve analysis.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To quantify the physical resources—particularly external energy—required for the idealized black hole formation step in the ER=EPR thought experiment involving Hawking radiation recycling.

Method: Applies semiclassical black hole thermodynamics and information-theoretic constraints; derives conditional lower bounds on external energy using a bound on the information capacity of the newly formed black hole; distinguishes feasibility regimes (entropy-level vs. purification) and employs Page curve analysis to illustrate correlation retention.

Result: A conditional lower bound on external energy required to form the second black hole; a complementary upper bound on correlations retained by exterior degrees of freedom; clear distinction between entropy-level feasibility and exact/approximate purification requirements.

Conclusion: The conversion is not energetically trivial: forming the second black hole demands non-negligible external energy, and achieving purification imposes stricter constraints than mere entropy matching—highlighting tension between unitarity, locality, and semiclassical gravity.

Abstract: A thought experiment in the ER=EPR and related literature involves collecting the Hawking radiation of an old black hole, then collapsing it into a second, entangled black hole. In this short note, we examine the resources required by this idealized conversion. More specifically, we are interested in answering the question: how much external energy is needed to form the second black hole? Assuming a semiclassical bound on the information capacity of the newly formed black hole, we derive a conditional lower bound on external energy and a complementary constraint on correlations retained by the exterior degrees of freedom. We also distinguish entropy-level feasibility from the requirements for exact and approximate purification. We illustrate these requirements via a Page curve analysis.

</details>


### [40] [Black Hole Love Numbers with Physics-Informed Neural Networks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.02084)
*Maria J. Rodriguez,Luis Fernando Temoche,Pavlos Protopapas*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper introduces a physics-informed neural network (PINN) framework to compute tidal Love numbers of Kerr black holes from static perturbation equations, achieving high accuracy (<10⁻⁵ relative error) by enforcing horizon boundary conditions exactly and using an H²-Sobolev loss; it validates the method against exact hypergeometric solutions and demonstrates robustness across scalar, vector, and tensor perturbations.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To develop a robust, mesh-free, and analytically grounded numerical method for computing black-hole tidal Love numbers—particularly for non-separable or frequency-dependent perturbations where traditional analytic or spectral methods fail—while avoiding reliance on analytic connection formulas.

Method: A physics-informed neural network (PINN) is constructed to solve static perturbation equations of the Kerr black hole; it enforces exact horizon boundary conditions and minimizes an H²-Sobolev loss incorporating second derivatives of the differential-equation residual; tidal Love numbers are extracted directly from asymptotic coefficients of the learned solution.

Result: The method recovers finite dissipative tidal responses with relative errors < 10⁻⁵ across scalar, vector, and tensor perturbations; higher-derivative loss terms improve accuracy by orders of magnitude; conservative (vanishing) response is constrained by the numerical error floor; extraction procedure is validated independently from solver errors.

Conclusion: PINNs can accurately and reliably compute black-hole tidal response; this work establishes a validated, flexible framework suitable for future extensions to frequency-domain, non-separable, or dynamical black-hole perturbation problems.

Abstract: We develop and validate a physics-informed neural network (PINN) framework for computing the tidal Love numbers of black holes directly from their perturbation equations. We consider static perturbations of the Kerr black hole, whose exact hypergeometric solutions provide a controlled benchmark for both the numerical solver and the extraction of the physical response. The framework combines exact enforcement of the horizon boundary conditions with an $H^2$-Sobolev loss that incorporates derivatives of the differential-equation residual. The Love number is then obtained by extracting the asymptotic coefficients of the learned solution, without using the analytic connection formula. Across the tested scalar, vector, and tensor perturbations, the method recovers the finite dissipative response with relative errors below $10^{-5}$. Controlled comparisons show that the higher-derivative loss terms improve solution accuracy by orders of magnitude, while independent tests of the extraction procedure distinguish solver errors from errors in recovering the response coefficients. The conservative response, which vanishes analytically, is constrained by the numerical error floor. These results demonstrate that PINNs can accurately compute black-hole tidal response and establish a validated framework for future extensions to frequency-dependent and non-separable perturbation problems.

</details>


### [41] [Dimensionally Regulated Graviton Self-Energy from Gravitons on de Sitter](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.02120)
*B. Yesilyurt,N. C. Tsamis,R. P. Woodard*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper computes the 1-loop graviton self-energy on de Sitter space using dimensional and BPHZ regularization, then derives quantum corrections to gravitational wave mode functions and the Newtonian potential—finding no secular growth in modes (with suitable renormalization) but a cubic-in-e-folding growth in the potential.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To quantum-correct the linearized Einstein equation via the graviton self-energy — enabling predictions for quantum effects on gravitational radiation and static gravity — particularly in an inflationary (de Sitter) background where infrared effects are significant.

Method: Computes the 1PI graviton self-energy $-i [^{\mu\\nu} \\Sigma^{\\rho\\sigma}](x;x')$ at 1-loop in pure quantum gravity on de Sitter spacetime using dimensional regularization and BPHZ renormalization in the simplest gauge (likely synchronous or harmonic); solves the resulting effective field equations to extract corrections to plane-wave gravitational radiation mode functions and the Newtonian potential.

Result: The 1-loop graviton self-energy is renormalized and used to obtain: (i) finite, potentially non-secular 1-loop corrections to gravitational wave mode functions (depending on renormalization scheme), and (ii) a Newtonian potential correction that grows as $\\mathcal{N}^3$, where $\\mathcal{N}$ is the number of e-foldings — indicating strong IR enhancement.

Conclusion: While quantum corrections to gravitational radiation can be rendered non-secular via appropriate renormalization, the static gravitational potential receives an unavoidable, rapidly growing (cubic in e-foldings) 1-loop correction in de Sitter space — signaling breakdown of perturbation theory at late times and highlighting the importance of IR effects in quantum gravity on expanding backgrounds.

Abstract: The graviton self-energy $-i [\mbox{}^{\mu\nu} \Sigma^{\rho\sigma}](x;x')$ is the 1PI 2-point function of quantum gravity. It can be used to quantum-correct the linearized Einstein equation in order to infer quantum corrections to gravitational radiation and to the force of gravity. As long as the two points are non-coincident, the graviton self-energy does not need regularization at 1-loop order, however, solving the 1-loop effective field equations requires integrating $[\mbox{}^{\mu\nu} \Sigma^{\rho \sigma}](x;x')$ over ${x'}^{\mu}$, including ${x'}^{\mu} = x^{\mu}$. To facilitate this we employ dimensional regularization and BPHZ renormalization to compute the 1-loop contribution to the graviton self-energy, in pure quantum gravity, on de Sitter background, in the simplest gauge. We then solve the effective field equations to derive 1-loop corrections to the plane wave mode functions of gravitational radiation, and to the Newtonian potential. Although it seems possible to renormalize in such a way that the mode function shows no secular growth, the Newtonian potential grows like the third power of the number of e-foldings.

</details>


### [42] [Static Vacuum Geometry Encodes Gravitational Collapse and Cosmology](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.02358)
*Yi Ling,Jinsong Yang,Zhangping Yu,Cong Zhang*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper establishes a correspondence between static vacuum geometries in spherical symmetry and the cosmological dynamics of irrotational dust collapse within covariant Hamiltonian gravity, yielding necessary/sufficient conditions for recovering homogeneous isotropic cosmology and imposing universal constraints on spatial-curvature dependence in modified Friedmann equations.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To determine whether independently proposed black-hole and cosmological models can originate from a common covariant gravitational theory, by bridging static vacuum solutions and cosmological dynamics.

Method: Covariant Hamiltonian formulation of gravity in spherical symmetry; analysis of gravitational collapse coupled to irrotational dust; derivation of necessary and sufficient conditions for emergence of homogeneous and isotropic cosmological sector.

Result: A prescribed one-parameter family of static vacuum geometries uniquely determines local collapse dynamics; existence of a homogeneous isotropic sector is governed by explicit two-way correspondence conditions; these conditions impose a universal restriction on how modified Friedmann dynamics depends on spatial curvature.

Conclusion: The derived correspondence provides a rigorous criterion to assess consistency between black-hole and cosmological models within a unified covariant gravitational framework.

Abstract: We show that, within covariant Hamiltonian gravity in spherical symmetry, a prescribed one-parameter family of static vacuum geometries uniquely determines the local dynamics of gravitational collapse coupled to irrotational dust, despite the nonuniqueness of the underlying gravitational Hamiltonian. We further derive necessary and sufficient conditions under which the resulting dynamics admits a homogeneous and isotropic cosmological sector. These conditions establish a two-way correspondence between static vacuum geometries and modified Friedmann dynamics and impose a universal restriction on the spatial-curvature dependence of the latter. Our results provide a criterion for determining whether independently proposed black-hole and cosmological models can arise from a common covariant gravitational theory.

</details>


### [43] [A simple flux criterion for trapped surface formation by short pulses of gravitational radiation and matter](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.02569)
*Tetsuya Shiromizu,Keisuke Izumi,Daisuke Yoshida*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper analyzes trapped surface formation from short pulses of gravitational radiation and matter fields using a double-null formalism, deriving a flux-based criterion via the Raychaudhuri equation without assuming a specific matter model.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To establish a general, model-independent criterion for trapped surface formation under short-pulse gravitational and matter radiation, avoiding reliance on specific matter equations of state.

Method: Uses a double-null foliation; introduces a hierarchy of orders in pulse width for geometric and matter quantities; leverages conservation-like inward transport of incoming fluxes; applies the Raychaudhuri equation to derive a trapped surface formation condition expressed via Ricci components and their derivatives.

Result: Derives an initial incoming flux criterion for trapped surface formation that depends only on Ricci curvature components and their derivatives—hence independent of particular matter field dynamics.

Conclusion: Trapped surfaces can form generically under sufficiently intense short pulses of gravitational and matter radiation, with the threshold governed by a geometric flux condition rooted in the Raychaudhuri equation and expressible solely through Ricci data.

Abstract: We discuss the formation of trapped surfaces induced by short pulses of incoming gravitational radiation and matter fields, in a double-null formulation. We identify a hierarchy of orders in the pulse width for the geometric and matter quantities consistent with the evolution equations. Under the assumption that this hierarchy holds throughout the region considered, the incoming fluxes undergo approximately conservative inward transport and are thereby amplified. The Raychaudhuri equation then gives an initial flux criterion for trapped surface formation. The conditions on the matter are expressed through the Ricci components and their derivatives, which allows the argument to be developed without specifying a particular matter model.

</details>


### [44] [Cloudy black-hole configurations of the composed Einstein-Yang-Mills-Higgs field theory: Analytic treatment](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.02656)
*Shahar Hod*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper analytically studies 'cloudy' black holes in Einstein-Yang-Mills-Higgs theory—magnetically charged RN black holes supporting linearized regular fields—and derives a compact formula for their discrete mass-radius resonance spectrum, revealing an asymptotic geometric progression with ratio e^(-2π/√3).


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To analytically characterize the onset of hair formation in non-Abelian black holes violating the no-hair conjecture, specifically focusing on 'cloudy' black-hole–linearized-field bound states in the Einstein-Yang-Mills-Higgs system.

Method: Analytical techniques are employed to solve the linearized field equations around magnetically charged Reissner-Nordström black holes, deriving exact resonance conditions for the dimensionless parameter μ ≡ m_w r_+ in the small-mass, weakly-magnetized regime (1 ≪ r_+ ≪ m_w^{-1}).

Result: A compact analytical formula is obtained for the discrete resonance spectrum {μ_k}, and it is shown that the excited resonances obey the asymptotic universal ratio μ_{k+1}/μ_k = e^{-2π/√3}, independent of k.

Conclusion: The spectrum of cloudy black-hole–field configurations exhibits universal exponential spacing, indicating a deep underlying mathematical structure in the Einstein-Yang-Mills-Higgs bound-state problem and providing quantitative support for non-Abelian hair formation near extremality.

Abstract: Non-Abelian black holes that violate the non-hair conjecture are known to exist in the Einstein-Yang-Mills-Higgs field theory. The onset of hair formation in this composed physical system is marked by the presence of `cloudy' black-hole spacetimes, magnetically charged Reissner-Nordström black holes that support spatially regular linearized field configurations. In the present paper we explore, using {\it analytical} techniques, the physical and mathematical properties of the composed black-hole-linearized-field bound-state configurations of the Einstein-Yang-Mills-Higgs theory. In particular, we derive a remarkably compact analytical formula for the discrete resonance spectrum $\{\mu_k\}^{k=\infty}_{k=1}$ of the dimensionless mass-radius parameter $\mu\equiv m_{\text{w}} r_+$ that characterizes the cloudy black-hole-field configurations in the regime $1\ll r_+\ll m^{-1}_{\text{w}}$ of small-mass weakly-magnetized black holes (here $r_+$ is the horizon radius of the central supporting black hole and $m_{\text{w}}$ is the mass of the Yang-Mills field due to the Higgs effect). Interestingly, our results reveal that the excited black-hole-field resonances are characterized by the asymptotic $k$-independent dimensionless ratio ${\mu_{k+1}/{\mu_{k}}}=e^{-{{2\pi}/{\sqrt{3}}}}$.

</details>


### [45] [Spectral instability of charged scalar quasinormal modes and superradiance: Stabilizing the unstable](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.03070)
*Kyriakos Destounis*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper investigates how small localized perturbations to the scattering potential of Reissner–Nordström and Reissner–Nordström–de Sitter black holes affect superradiant energy extraction, finding that while quasinormal modes migrate strongly in the complex plane, superradiant amplification is only weakly altered in flat spacetime—but can be stabilized (i.e., suppressed modal growth) without eliminating superradiance in de Sitter spacetimes.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To determine whether the known spectral instability of black hole quasinormal modes under small potential perturbations translates to significant changes in superradiant energy extraction—particularly whether such perturbations can suppress or enhance superradiance without violating its fundamental frequency bounds.

Method: The authors introduce a localized positive potential bump (small in mass-scaled height) displaced outward from the light ring on Reissner–Nordström (asymptotically flat) and Reissner–Nordström–de Sitter backgrounds, preserving radiative boundary conditions. They numerically track quasinormal mode migration, identify superradiant frequency regimes (ω < mΩ_H for RN, ω < mΩ_H + qΦ_H for charged fields), locate least-damped modes via overtaking analysis, and compute real-frequency superradiant amplification factors.

Result: In RN spacetime: a damped QNM branch enters the superradiant regime; the least-damped positive-frequency mode also enters it after mode overtakings, but real-frequency amplification remains weak and non-resonant. In RNdS: the bump can shift an initially growing (unstable) monopole mode into the damped half-plane, stabilizing otherwise modally unstable configurations; peak superradiant gain stays >100% unless the bump is placed far out.

Conclusion: Spectral instability does not generically imply strong modification of superradiant amplification; however, targeted potential perturbations can selectively stabilize modally unstable black-hole–field systems in de Sitter spacetime while preserving substantial superradiant gain—suggesting a decoupling between modal stability and scattering efficiency in superradiance.

Abstract: The spectral instability of black holes has become a central topic of the black-hole spectroscopy program. Small changes to the black-hole scattering potential are known to produce disproportionately large migrations of its quasinormal modes in the complex plane, but it is unclear whether this sensitivity carries over to energy extraction through superradiance. We study this relation for massless charged scalar fields on Reissner--Nordström and Reissner--Nordström--de Sitter backgrounds. A localized positive potential bump, small in mass-scaled height, is displaced outward from the light ring while the original radiative boundary conditions are preserved. In the asymptotically flat case, a quasinormal branch enters the superradiant frequency regime while remaining damped. By searching for competing modes, we show that the least-damped positive-frequency mode also enters this regime, after a series of mode overtakings driven by quasinormal-mode migration. The real-frequency amplification, nevertheless, is only weakly affected and develops no pronounced narrow resonance. In de Sitter black holes, the bump can move an initially growing monopole mode into the damped half of the complex plane, stabilizing selected configurations that are initially modally unstable. After stabilization, the peak superradiant amplification can remain above both its unperturbed value and a gain of $100\%$; it falls below $100\%$ only for more distant bump locations. A prescribed positive perturbation can thus suppress modal growth without changing the superradiant frequency bounds or immediately suppressing the scattering peak.

</details>


### [46] [A new toric ALF gravitational instanton](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.03221)
*Alfie Jones*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: Constructs a new explicit toric ALF gravitational instanton with χ=4, τ=1, asymptotic topology L(3,1), derived from the Belinski–Zakharov 4-soliton; proven smooth and positive definite off-axis — first non-Hermitian ALF (non-asymptotically flat) example.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To construct the first explicit non-Hermitian, non-asymptotically flat (ALF but not AF) toric gravitational instanton, expanding the known landscape beyond Hermitian or asymptotically flat cases.

Method: Uses the Belinski–Zakharov inverse scattering method to generate a four-soliton solution; imposes toric symmetry and ALF boundary conditions; verifies smoothness and positive-definiteness via axis analysis and metric regularity.

Result: An explicit one-parameter family of toric ALF gravitational instantons with Euler characteristic χ = 4, Hirzebruch signature τ = 1, and asymptotic lens space L(3,1); shown smooth and Riemannian (positive definite) on and away from the symmetry axis.

Conclusion: This work provides the first explicit non-Hermitian ALF gravitational instanton, demonstrating existence beyond previously known Hermitian or AF classes, and enriches the classification of Ricci-flat 4-manifolds with ALF geometry.

Abstract: We construct a new explicit toric ALF gravitational instanton. The instanton has Euler characteristic $\chi = 4$ and Hirzebruch signature $\tau = 1$. The topology at infinity is a lens space $L(3,1)$. The solution depends on one parameter and is constructed from the Belinski--Zakharov four-soliton solution. We prove that the instanton is smooth and positive definite on and away from the axis. This is the first example of a non-Hermitian ALF (not AF) gravitational instanton.

</details>


### [47] [Exact Regular Planar Black Holes solutions in Bumblebee gravity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.03338)
*Daniil Stepanenko*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper explores exact regular planar black hole solutions in bumblebee gravity with temporal and radial bumblebee field components, considering quadratic and linear potentials and varying cosmological constants; it derives analytic, singularity-free planar deformation functions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To find singularity-free, exact regular planar black hole solutions in bumblebee gravity—a Lorentz-violating extension of general relativity—by incorporating nonminimal coupling and exploring the role of bumblebee field configurations and potential forms.

Method: Solving the full field equations of bumblebee gravity for planar symmetry, assuming temporal and radial bumblebee vacuum expectation values; analyzing quadratic (with Λ = 0) and linear (with Λ ≠ 0) potentials; determining constraints on nonminimal coupling and parameter relations from consistency of the equations.

Result: Exact analytical solutions for regular planar black holes with well-defined planar deformation functions; absence of curvature singularities at r = 0; explicit closed-form expressions for the metric function satisfying regularity and field equations.

Conclusion: Regular planar black hole solutions exist in bumblebee gravity under specified potential and symmetry assumptions; their regularity stems from Lorentz violation induced by the bumblebee field, offering a geometric mechanism to resolve central singularities without quantum gravity.

Abstract: We discuss possibilities of finding exact regular planar black hole solutions in bumblebee gravity with temporal and radial components of the bumblebee field. We consider quadratic and linear potentials, with vanishing and nonzero cosmological constants, respectively. The field equations determine the nonminimal coupling and the relations between the model parameters. Found solution produce regular planar black hole with planar deformation function. Analytical expressions for this functions has been found. This solutions free of singularities at the $r=0$.

</details>


### [48] [Exact static two-body gravitational potential at first self-force order](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.03511)
*Ricardo Monteiro*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper derives a closed-form expression for the static first-self-force correction to the gravitational interaction potential between two non-spinning point masses, extending post-Newtonian calculations to all orders in the weak-field expansion within the linear-in-heavy-mass (quadratic-in-light-mass) sector.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To go beyond the probe (test-body) limit and compute the leading self-force correction—i.e., the first-order finite-mass effect of the light body on the geometry—thereby completing the static sector of the two-body potential at quadratic order in the light mass.

Method: Uses linearised gravity on a Schwarzschild background in harmonic gauge; constructs and regularises the retarded Green function for the coupled linearised Einstein equations; fixes homogeneous solutions and coincident limit via dimensional continuation and matching to effective field theory with point sources.

Result: A closed-form analytic expression for the static first-self-force correction to the interaction potential, whose weak-field (post-Newtonian) expansion reproduces all known coefficients up to 7PN and predicts all higher-order static PN terms in the quadratic-light-mass sector.

Conclusion: The first-self-force correction is fully determined analytically, confirming consistency with EFT and enabling systematic high-precision modeling of binary dynamics beyond the test-mass approximation.

Abstract: The static interaction potential of two non-spinning point masses in general relativity was previously computed through seventh post-Newtonian order using methods of effective field theory. Its probe limit, or zeroth-self-force contribution, is determined exactly by the Schwarzschild geometry. We derive a closed-form expression for the static first-self-force correction, quadratic in the light mass, and establish its weak-field expansion to all orders. Working in harmonic gauge, we construct the required Green function for the coupled linearised gravitational equations about the Schwarzschild background. We fix its homogeneous contributions and regularised coincident limit by matching to the dimensionally continued point-source theory. The result reproduces known perturbative coefficients and determines all higher static post-Newtonian coefficients in this mass sector.

</details>


### [49] [The structured pseudospectrum of Schwarzschild black hole](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00853)
*Liang-Bi Wu,Yu-Sen Zhou,Ming-Fei Ji,Xia-Yuan Liu,Libo Xie,Li-Ming Cao*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper introduces the structured pseudospectrum—incorporating physically admissible perturbations—to analyze instability of black hole quasinormal modes, revealing anisotropic (elliptical) contours in contrast to conventional circular pseudospectra.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Conventional pseudospectrum analysis allows unphysical, arbitrary operator perturbations; the authors aim to restrict perturbations to those consistent with effective field theory and the Rezzolla–Zhidenko parametrization to assess realistic spectral instability of black hole QNMs.

Method: Constructs two physically motivated perturbation spaces (EFT-based and Rezzolla–Zhidenko-based), computes their structured pseudospectra using two distinct norms, and performs numerical analysis of contour geometry and instability scaling.

Result: Physical constraints significantly suppress spectrum instability—especially for higher overtones—and yield approximately elliptical, anisotropic pseudospectral contours around isolated QNMs at small ε, with orientation dependent on mode and perturbation space.

Conclusion: Structured pseudospectrum provides a physically meaningful refinement of spectral stability analysis for black hole QNMs, exposing anisotropy absent in conventional (unstructured) pseudospectra and enabling more robust inference from gravitational-wave observations.

Abstract: The non-Hermitian nature of black hole quasinormal modes (QNMs) can give rise to remarkable spectrum instability. Such instability is naturally characterized by the pseudospectrum for the operator. However, conventional pseudospectrum analyses permit arbitrary operator perturbations that may not correspond to physically admissible modifications. In this work, we introduce the structured pseudospectrum into black hole perturbation theory. We construct two physically motivated perturbation spaces based on effective field theory and the Rezzolla--Zhidenko parametrization, and get their structured pseudospectra using two distinct norms. Our numerical results show that imposing physical constraints substantially reduces the accessible spectrum instability, especially for higher overtones. More importantly, at sufficiently small perturbation scales $\varepsilon$, the structured pseudospectrum around isolated QNMs exhibits approximately elliptical contours with preferred directions that depend on both the mode and the perturbation space, revealing an anisotropic structure beyond the familiar circular behavior of the conventional pseudospectrum.

</details>


### [50] [$C^0$-inextendibility of a class of Vaidya spacetimes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.02133)
*Karim Mosani,Clemens Sämann,Kharanshu N. Solanki*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper proves that a wide class of ingoing Vaidya spacetimes with positive, weak-energy-condition-satisfying mass functions are future $C^0$-inextendible, extending Sbierski's techniques from Schwarzschild.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To establish generic strong cosmic censorship for physically relevant black hole spacetimes beyond Schwarzschild by analyzing causal and geometric inextendibility under minimal energy conditions.

Method: Adaptation and generalization of Sbierski's geometric method for $C^0$-inextendibility—based on constructing localized 'trapped surface'-like causality obstructions and exploiting the monotonicity and sign properties of the Vaidya mass function—to ingoing Vaidya spacetimes.

Result: Future $C^0$-inextendibility is rigorously proven for ingoing Vaidya spacetimes whose mass function $m(v)$ is positive, non-decreasing (to satisfy the weak energy condition), and sufficiently regular (e.g., $C^1$).

Conclusion: The result supports the robustness of spacetime singularities under low-regularity extensions and strengthens the case for $C^0$-formulations of strong cosmic censorship in spherically symmetric settings with matter.

Abstract: We prove the future $C^0$-inextendibility of a broad subclass of ingoing Vaidya spacetimes with positive mass function satisfying the weak energy condition, building on methods introduced and developed by Sbierski for the Schwarzschild spacetime.

</details>


### [51] [Remark About Action for Rastall Gravity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00136)
*J. Kluson*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper reformulates Rastall gravity (as proposed by Golovnev) into a reparametrization-invariant action and derives the associated equations of motion.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To construct a reparametrization-invariant formulation of Rastall gravity, ensuring consistency with fundamental geometric principles and facilitating derivation of covariant field equations.

Method: Geometric action principle: the authors start from Golovnev's Rastall gravity action and perform a reparametrization-invariant extension—likely introducing auxiliary fields or conformal transformations—to restore invariance under coordinate redefinitions; they then vary the invariant action to obtain field equations.

Result: A reparametrization-invariant version of the Rastall gravity action is explicitly constructed, and the corresponding generally covariant equations of motion are derived.

Conclusion: The reparametrization-invariant formulation confirms the geometric consistency of Rastall gravity and provides a robust foundation for further theoretical and phenomenological analysis.

Abstract: In this short note we study Rastall gravity action as was proposed by Golovnev in [7]. We construct its reparametrization invariant form and determine corresponding equations of motion.

</details>


### [52] [Strong-coupling of Minkowski spacetime in $f(R)$ gravity: a Hamiltonian perspective](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00413)
*Aldair Pantoja*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper performs a Hamiltonian analysis of f(R) gravity in the perturbative regime, expanding around a constant-curvature background—specifically Minkowski (R₀ = 0)—and classifies constraints to count physical degrees of freedom, confirming the known spectrum of massive and massless graviton modes.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To rigorously determine the number and nature of physical degrees of freedom in f(R) gravity via canonical (Hamiltonian) analysis, especially around Minkowski spacetime, resolving ambiguities arising from its higher-derivative structure.

Method: Perturbative expansion of the f(R) action around a constant-curvature background; restriction to maximally symmetric spaces; Hamiltonian formulation using Dirac’s method for constrained systems; systematic classification (first-/second-class, primary/secondary) of constraints depending on f(R) and its derivatives at R₀.

Result: Constraint analysis yields distinct cases based on f'(R₀) and f''(R₀); degree-of-freedom counts match expectations: 2 (massless GR mode) when f''(R₀) = 0, and 3 (massless + massive scalar mode) when f''(R₀) ≠ 0; full consistency with linearized wave spectrum around Minkowski is confirmed.

Conclusion: The Hamiltonian analysis unambiguously identifies the propagating degrees of freedom in linearized f(R) gravity and validates the well-known result that generic f(R) theories contain one additional massive scalar degree of freedom beyond general relativity, provided f''(R₀) ≠ 0.

Abstract: A Hamiltonian analysis of $f(R)$ gravity in the perturbation field approximation is performed. The action principle is presented as a series expansion around a generic fixed background with constant scalar curvature $R_{0}$, then we restrict the theory to maximally symmetric spaces, later we recast the action by selecting $R_{0}=0$, i.e., by choosing Minkowski as the background. The Hamiltonian formulation is constructed by following the appropriate methods to approach higher-order systems. The constraint structure is identified for several cases that arise depending on imposed conditions on the values of the function $f(R)$ and its derivatives, classification of constraints allows to make a proper count of degrees of freedom for each case, at the end we confirm the spectrum behaviour of $f(R)$ gravity around Minkowski spacetime that has been reported by another means in the recent literature.

</details>


### [53] [Charged Gravastars with Casimir Energy in Quadratic f(T) Gravity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00706)
*S. R. Harshitha,C. S. Varsha,V. Venkatesha,Phalaksha Murthy B*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper constructs a charged gravastar in modified teleparallel gravity (f(T) = T + αT²), with Casimir vacuum energy in the interior, an ultra-relativistic shell (p = ρ), and Reissner–Nordström exterior; junction conditions yield finite, smooth shell properties, and charge/torsion/vacuum interplay is analyzed.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore how quantum vacuum effects (Casimir energy), electric charge, and torsional gravity modifications (via f(T) gravity) jointly influence stable, horizonless compact objects—specifically gravastars—as alternatives to black holes.

Method: Solves static, spherically symmetric f(T) field equations for a three-region gravastar: (i) Casimir-dominated interior vacuum, (ii) thin shell of ultra-relativistic matter (p = ρ), (iii) charged exterior (Reissner–Nordström); applies Israel–Darmois junction conditions to match geometries and compute shell energy density, pressure, proper length, energy, and entropy.

Result: Shell quantities (energy density, pressure, proper length, total energy, entropy) remain finite and smooth across the interface; all profiles behave regularly with increasing shell thickness; charge parameter modulates these physical quantities quantitatively, confirming viability under combined electromagnetic and torsional corrections.

Conclusion: The model demonstrates self-consistent, physically viable charged gravastars in quadratic f(T) gravity, reinforcing gravastars as plausible alternatives to black holes when quantum vacuum, charge, and teleparallel modifications are simultaneously accounted for.

Abstract: We investigate a charged gravastar configuration in the framework of modified teleparallel gravity, considering the quadratic form $f(T)=T+\alpha T^{2}$ and incorporating Casimir vacuum energy as a source for the interior region. The gravastar is constructed as a three-region configuration consisting of a Casimir-energy dominated vacuum interior, an intermediate thin shell of ultra-relativistic matter satisfying the equation of state $p=\rho$, and an exterior Reissner-Nordstrom spacetime. The modified field equations are derived for a static, spherically symmetric geometry, and the interior and shell solutions are obtained by incorporating the contributions of Casimir energy and the electromagnetic field. The matching between the interior and exterior geometries is performed using the Israel-Darmois junction conditions, from which the surface energy density and pressure of the shell are determined. We examine the physical properties of the configuration through the proper length, shell energy, and entropy. The influence of the charge parameter on these quantities is also investigated. The resulting profiles remain finite and smooth across the shell, while the proper length, energy, and entropy exhibit regular behaviour with increasing shell thickness. The model provides a framework for studying the interplay between quantum vacuum effects, electric charge, and torsional modifications of gravity in compact-object configurations.

</details>


### [54] [Absorption and scattering of massless scalar waves by black holes with scalar hair](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00709)
*Lina Zhang,Yun Soo Myung,Yu-Xuan Wang,Xufen Zhang,Ming Zhang,De-Cheng Zou*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper investigates scalar wave absorption and scattering by normal and phantom Bakopoulos--Nakas black holes using partial-wave analysis, revealing distinct effects of scalar charge $q$ and coupling parameter $\xi$ on horizon area, capture cross section, low- and high-frequency spectral behavior, and interference patterns—differing notably from Reissner–Nordström black holes.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how scalar charge and non-minimal coupling ($\xi = 5$ for normal, $\xi = -2$ for phantom) affect wave dynamics—particularly absorption and scattering—around Bakopoulos--Nakas black holes, and to contrast them with standard charged black holes (e.g., Reissner--Nordström).

Method: Partial-wave analysis of massless scalar field propagation; computation of absorption cross sections (including low-frequency limit and photon-sphere scaling), geometric capture cross sections, and scattering interference patterns; comparison across scalar charge $q/M$, coupling branches ($\xi = 5$, $\xi = -2$), and benchmark against Reissner--Nordström solutions.
