Result: Increasing $q/M$ shrinks horizon area and capture cross section in the normal branch ($\xi=5$) but enlarges both in the phantom branch ($\xi=-2$); low-frequency absorption approaches horizon area; photon-sphere scaling unifies high-frequency absorption spectra; scattering fringes narrow at higher frequencies and depend sensitively on $q$ and $\xi$; finite-frequency deviations persist even when matching capture/glory scales with Reissner--Nordström black holes.

Conclusion: Scalar charge and coupling sign fundamentally alter black hole wave phenomenology: phantom and normal branches exhibit opposite thermodynamic and scattering responses, and their spectral signatures are not fully mimicked by electromagnetic charge, highlighting intrinsic scalar-field gravity effects beyond classical electrovacuum analogs.

Abstract: We use partial waves to study massless scalar absorption and scattering by the normal ($\xi=5$) and phantom ($\xi=-2$) Bakopoulos--Nakas black holes, described by mass $M$ and scalar charge $q$. At fixed mass $M=1$, increasing $q/M$ decreases the horizon area and geometric capture cross section in the normal branch, but it increases both in the phantom branch. The low-frequency absorption tends to the horizon area, while photon-sphere scaling brings the absorption spectra close together. In scattering picture, charge and branch change the interference pattern, and higher frequencies make the fringes narrow. However, matching the capture or glory scale of the normal branch with Reissner--Nordström black holes indicates finite-frequency differences.

</details>


### [55] [Near-extremal critical dynamics driven by a phantom field in Einstein-Maxwell-scalar theory](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00792)
*Zhen-Kun Jia,Lang Zhao,Lin Chen,Peng-Cheng Li,Cheng-Yong Zhang*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper uses a phantom (negative-energy) field to dynamically access near-extremal Einstein-Maxwell-scalar black hole solutions, revealing a critical threshold $p_\ast$ separating evolution toward near-extremal Reissner–Nordström vs. nonextremal hairy black holes; the transition exhibits Type I critical behavior with logarithmic scaling and a long-lived, regular scalarized intermediate state.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore the low-mass, near-extremal region of Einstein-Maxwell-scalar (EMS) black hole solutions—a regime inaccessible via standard positive-energy perturbations—by leveraging controllable negative-energy transport.

Method: Numerical relativity simulations of fully nonlinear Einstein-Maxwell-scalar dynamics with an auxiliary phantom scalar field; initial amplitude $p$ of the phantom field is tuned across a critical value $p_\ast$, while other initial data parameters are fixed; quasi-local mass and horizon quantities (surface gravity, curvature) are monitored during evolution.

Result: A critical threshold $p_\ast$ is identified, where sub- and super-critical evolutions both transiently approach a long-lived, regular, scalarized intermediate state before bifurcating to either near-extremal 'bald' (Reissner-Nordström) or nonextremal 'hairy' black-hole end states; residence time near the intermediate stage obeys Type I logarithmic scaling $\tau \sim -\gamma \ln|p - p_\ast|$, with growth rate matching the inverse lifetime coefficient; horizon surface gravity is small but finite, and no curvature singularity forms at the horizon within simulation time.

Conclusion: Phantom-assisted negative-energy transport enables dynamical entry into the near-extremal EMS solution space and uncovers a novel threshold-mediated phase transition between bald and hairy black-hole families, governed by Type I critical phenomena and characterized by a stable, nonsingular scalarized intermediate geometry.

Abstract: We investigate the fully nonlinear dynamics of Einstein-Maxwell-scalar black holes in the low-mass, near-extremal region of the solution space, which is difficult to access with conventional positive-energy perturbations. An independent phantom field is introduced as a controllable negative-energy channel, and its initial amplitude is varied while the remaining parameters of the initial data are held fixed. The phantom contribution is concentrated during the early-time infall and reduces the quasi-local mass in the black-hole region at fixed electric charge, thereby allowing the evolution to approach near-extremal configurations. Near a critical amplitude $p_\ast$, evolutions on both sides of the threshold enter the same long-lived scalarized intermediate stage before separating toward either a near-extremal Reissner-Nordstrom black hole or a nonextremal hairy black hole. The residence time near this intermediate stage obeys the logarithmic scaling law of Type I critical behavior, and the growth rate extracted from the departure is consistent with the inverse lifetime coefficient. The intermediate stage is characterized by a small horizon surface gravity and finite horizon curvature, with no curvature blow-up resolved at the horizon during the simulated interval. These results demonstrate that phantom-assisted negative-energy transport provides a dynamical route into the near-extremal sector of the EMS solution space and reveals a threshold-mediated transition between bald and hairy black-hole end states.

</details>


### [56] [Dimensional dependence of critical phenomena in black hole interiors](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00829)
*Li-Jie Xin,Xiangdong Zhang*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper numerically studies how scalar field perturbations affect the inner horizon of D-dimensional Reissner-Nordström black holes, finding a universal critical scaling law for the inner horizon radius near a threshold amplitude, with exponent γ ≈ 1/(D−2).


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand the stability and internal structure of charged black holes under nonlinear scalar perturbations, particularly the fate of the inner (Cauchy) horizon and its dependence on dimensionality and perturbation strength.

Method: Numerical relativity simulations of spherically symmetric, D-dimensional Reissner-Nordström black holes perturbed by a real scalar field; systematic variation of initial amplitude A and charge Q to identify critical behavior and compute scaling exponents.

Result: A critical amplitude A* separates spacelike-singularity formation (strong perturbations) from inner-horizon preservation (weak perturbations); inner horizon radius rs vanishes continuously at A*, obeying rs ∝ |A − A*|^γ with γ ≈ 1/2 in D=4 and γ ≈ 1/(D−2) for 4 ≤ D ≤ 9.

Conclusion: The scaling law γ = 1/(D−2) reveals geometric universality: the deviation from criticality scales with the area of the inner horizon’s spherical cross-section, suggesting a deep connection between critical collapse and horizon geometry in higher dimensions.

Abstract: We numerically investigate the interior dynamics of $D$ dimensional Reissner-Nordström (RN) black holes perturbed by a real scalar field. Strong perturbations destroy the null inner horizon and produce a spacelike singularity, whereas weaker perturbations preserve it with an areal radius that decreases as the perturbation amplitude increases. As the initial scalar field amplitude $A$ approaches its critical value $A^{*}$, the inner horizon radius decreases continuously to zero. Near criticality, this radius obeys the power law $r_{s}\propto|A-A^{*}|^\gamma$. In four dimensions, different initial scalar field profiles and initial RN black hole charges yield nearly identical critical exponents, $\gamma\simeq1/2$, supporting the universality of the scaling law. For $4\leq D\leq9$, the numerical results are well described by $\gamma\simeq1/(D-2)$. The corresponding scaling, $|A-A^{*}|\propto r_{s}^{D-2}$, indicates that the deviation of the initial parameter from its critical value is proportional to the area of the spherical cross section of the inner horizon.

</details>


### [57] [Probing Hidden Dimensions with Multimessenger Gravitational Waves](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00980)
*Hanlin Song,Minghui Du,Peng Xu*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper studies how gravitational waves (GWs) can probe compact extra dimensions in a Kaluza–Klein framework, derives Newtonian binary dynamics and leading-order extra-dimensional corrections to GW energy flux and waveforms (within ppE formalism), combines them with 1PN amplitude corrections, constructs modified waveform templates, and uses multimessenger Bayesian analysis of GW170817 + EM data to constrain the phenomenological dimensional parameter n < 0.11 (95% C.L.), improving to n < 0.00178 with Taiji for massive BH binaries.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To experimentally test whether 4D spacetime is embedded in higher-dimensional geometry using gravitational waves—particularly through strong-field binary coalescences—as a direct probe of compact extra dimensions.

Method: Derives Newtonian conservative binary dynamics and leading-order extra-dimensional correction to GW energy flux in a compactified Kaluza–Klein framework; incorporates these and prior 1PN internal-volume-perturbation amplitude corrections into the parameterized post-Einsteinian (ppE) waveform model; constructs modified GW templates for ground- and space-based detectors; performs multimessenger Bayesian inference combining GW170817 strain data with electromagnetic observations, and simulates Taiji observations of massive black hole binaries.

Result: Multimessenger analysis of GW170817 yields 95% credible upper bound n < 0.11 on the phenomenological dimensional parameter; Taiji simulations for massive BH binaries improve this bound to n < 0.00178.

Conclusion: Multimessenger gravitational-wave observations provide a powerful and competitive probe of extra-dimensional gravity in the strong-field, dynamical regime—complementing collider and tabletop experiments—and enable stringent tests of fundamental gravity beyond general relativity.

Abstract: The dimensionality of spacetime is a fundamental property of nature, yet whether our observed four-dimensional spacetime is embedded in a higher-dimensional geometry remains experimentally unresolved. Gravitational waves (GWs) offer a direct probe of compact extra dimensions through the strong-field dynamics of binary coalescences. In this work, we investigate extra-dimensional modifications to GW generation within a compactified Kaluza--Klein framework. We derive the Newtonian conservative binary dynamics and the leading-order extra-dimensional correction to the GW energy flux and formulate the corresponding waveform modifications within the parameterized post-Einsteinian (ppE) framework. Combining these results with the previously derived 1PN amplitude correction arising from perturbations of the internal volume, we construct modified GW waveform templates for ground- and space-based detectors. To alleviate parameter degeneracies in GW parameter estimation, we combine the observed strain data of GW170817 with independent electromagnetic observations in a multimessenger Bayesian analysis, yielding a 95\% credible upper bound of $n<0.11$ on the phenomenological dimensional parameter. We further investigate injected multimessenger massive black hole binary observations with Taiji, finding that the upper bound could be improved by nearly two orders of magnitude to $n<0.00178$. These results highlight the potential of multimessenger GW observations to probe extra-dimensional effects and test fundamental gravity in the strong-field and dynamical regime.

</details>


### [58] [Strong gravity physics of dynamical horizons in black hole mergers and its imprint in their gravitational radiation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.01275)
*Vaishak Prasad*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This thesis establishes a direct geometric correspondence between gravitational waveforms and black hole horizon dynamics in binary mergers, revealing that horizon shear mimics the inspiral chirp, horizon multipole moments encode non-vanishing 'horizon Love numbers' with universal behavior, and the common dynamical horizon absorbs gravitational radiation during ringdown while exhibiting quasi-normal ringing signatures.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To bridge the gap between observed gravitational waveforms and the underlying black hole horizon geometry across inspiral, merger, and ringdown phases—particularly lacking a detailed understanding of how horizon dynamics map to waveform features.

Method: Analysis of numerical relativity simulations of binary black hole mergers; computation of horizon shear and source multipole moments of dynamical horizons; derivation and interpretation of horizon-based (as opposed to field-based) tidal Love numbers for axisymmetric and non-axisymmetric modes; comparison of horizon multipole evolution with gravitational radiation at null infinity and with the final Kerr geometry.

Result: 1) Horizon shear reproduces the inspiral chirp waveform in frequency/amplitude evolution, with consistent parameter estimation; 2) Dynamical horizons possess non-zero, universal horizon Love numbers for axisymmetric modes—even for non-spinning black holes—unlike vanishing field tidal Love numbers; 3) Non-axisymmetric horizon multipoles are strongly correlated with the asymptotic gravitational field; 4) The outer common dynamical horizon absorbs gravitational radiation during ringdown, exhibits quasi-normal ringing in its deformation, and converges toward the final Kerr state while sharing multipolar structure with radiation at future null infinity.

Conclusion: Black hole horizons are not passive boundaries but active, information-rich dynamical entities whose geometry directly encodes and correlates with gravitational radiation across all merger phases; horizon-based observables (e.g., shear, source multipoles, horizon Love numbers) provide a geometric foundation for waveform modeling and testing general relativity in strong-field regimes.

Abstract: In a binary black hole merger, the inspiral portion of the gravitational waveform corresponds to two distinct horizons orbiting each other, and the merger and ringdown signals correspond to the final horizon being formed and settling down to equilibrium. However, we still lack a detailed understanding of the relation between the horizon geometry in these three regimes and the observed waveform. In this thesis, we show that the well-known inspiral chirp waveform has a clear counterpart on black hole horizons, namely the shear of the outgoing null rays at the horizon. The shear behaves very much like a compact binary coalescence waveform with increasing frequency and amplitude, and the parameters of the system estimated from the horizon agree with those estimated from the waveform. We then track the source multipole moments of the dynamical horizons in simulations of several binary black hole mergers and calculate the corresponding Love numbers for axisymmetric modes. Unlike the (field) tidal Love numbers of non-spinning black holes, these do not vanish. We present evidence that, at least for modest mass ratios, their behaviour is universal. We extend this to the non-axisymmetric moments and show that the dynamical horizons encode detailed information about the dynamics of the binary, and that their source multipole moments are strongly correlated with the gravitational field at null infinity. Finally, we study the outer common dynamical horizon formed during the merger and its relationship with the outgoing gravitational waves, and show how the deformed common horizon approaches the final Kerr state by absorbing gravitational radiation with signatures of quasi-normal ringing. Its gravitational field shares a similar multipolar structure as that at future null infinity, and we point out some limitations, inconsistencies, and anomalies that need to be addressed.

</details>


### [59] [Gravitational dynamics and backward ray-traced shadows of bumblebee AdS black holes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.01413)
*Muhammad Yasir,Senyue Lou,Zelin Zhang,Abdul Jawad,Faren Wang*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper studies photon frequency shifts, ISCO properties, and black hole shadows in bumblebee AdS black hole spacetimes, linking bumblebee-induced anisotropy and cosmological effects to observable relativistic astrophysical signatures.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore observational consequences of the bumblebee gravity model—specifically how Lorentz violation via a spontaneous vector field vacuum expectation value affects photon redshift/blueshift, orbital stability (ISCO), and black hole shadow morphology in asymptotically AdS spacetimes.

Method: Analytical derivation of geodesic equations and effective potential for circular orbits; computation of total gravitational redshift/blueshift for photons from equatorial circular emitters; numerical backward ray tracing using Arcmancer to generate BH shadows; analysis of cosmological evolution and anisotropy generation via bumblebee field dynamics.

Result: A functional dependence between black hole mass and observed frequency shift is established; ISCO radius is modified relative to standard AdS BHs due to bumblebee parameters; shadow size and deformation are quantified and linked to bumblebee-induced anisotropy and cosmological background; bumblebee field acts as both Lorentz-violating source and cosmological anisotropy driver.

Conclusion: The bumblebee AdS black hole yields distinct, potentially observable imprints in accretion disk spectroscopy and imaging—particularly in redshift asymmetry and shadow ellipticity—providing a pathway to constrain Lorentz violation and vector-tensor gravity with next-generation VLBI and X-ray timing observations.

Abstract: We investigate the total observed frequency shift of photons emitted by massive geodesic particles in circular orbits near the event horizon of bumblebee AdS black holes. The cosmological effects of the bumblebee field are investigated through dynamical equations governing the evolution and characteristic features of the Universe. In addition, the bumblebee field generates cosmological anisotropies by acting as a source of anisotropy and selecting a preferred spatial direction. We formulate the characteristic equations for the innermost stable circular orbit using the effective potential approach. Further analysis is devoted to establishing a functional relationship between the BH mass and the observed redshift and blueshift of photons emitted from the accretion disk. Finally, we study the shadow cast by the bumblebee AdS BH using the backward ray tracing process implemented in the Arcmancer code, where photon geodesics are initialized at the observers location and integrated backward in time to generate BH images, typically illuminated by electromagnetic radiation from nearby accretion flows.

</details>


### [60] [Effects of radiation on the Laser Interferometer Lunar Antenna (LILA)](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.01499)
*James M. Trippe,Brett N. Shapiro,Chloe A. Champagne,Brian D. Sierawski,Michael D. Hu,Volker Quetschke,Teviet Creighton,Philippe Lognonné,Mark P. Panning,John W. Conklin,Robert A. Reed,Karan Jani*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper evaluates radiation-induced noise sources (test-mass charging, momentum transfer, thermoelastic deformation) for the Laser Interferometer Lunar Antenna (LILA) on the Moon, using Geant4, electrostatic modeling, and thermoelastic kernels; it finds that with charge-aware design—including balanced electrode actuation and ideal current compensation—radiation noise remains subdominant to other noise sources across LILA’s midband, even under extreme 1989-solar-flare conditions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To assess whether radiation-induced disturbances (e.g., from cosmic rays, solar particles, lunar neutrons) pose a limiting noise source for gravitational-wave detection by LILA—a lunar-based midband interferometer—and to inform robust optical test-mass design.

Method: Quantitative modeling of three radiation coupling mechanisms: (1) Geant4 Monte Carlo simulation for particle transport and energy deposition; (2) finite-element electrostatic force mapping for charge-to-force coupling under uniform-charge and compensated-current assumptions; (3) spatial thermoelastic response kernel for thermal strain-induced displacement. Two shielding thicknesses (50–1000 mil Al-equivalent) and two electrostatic actuator configurations (including balanced drive B) are compared under solar-minimum galactic cosmic rays + lunar albedo neutrons (background) and 1989 solar flare SEP spectrum (extreme case).

Result: Charge-related noise dominates below ~10 mHz; thermoelastic deformation dominates above ~100 mHz; momentum transfer is negligible. Balanced electrode configuration (B) suppresses linear charge-to-force coupling; ideal continuous mean-current compensation eliminates coherent charge buildup with negligible shot noise penalty. Radiation noise remains below LILA’s total noise budget in all evaluated cases when charge-aware design is applied.

Conclusion: Radiation-induced noise is not a fundamental limitation for LILA’s midband sensitivity, provided optics employ charge-aware engineering—specifically balanced electrostatic actuation and active mean-charge compensation—validating the feasibility of lunar GW observation despite harsh radiation environment.

Abstract: The Moon provides a promising environment for gravitational-wave (GW) observations in the midband. The Laser Interferometer Lunar Antenna (LILA) combines a lunar deformation channel (LDC) and a geodesic deformation channel (GDC) to cover the midband. The LDC uses the Moon's resonant response to GW for the lower portion of the midband while the GDC measures displacement of suspended test masses to measure the latter. Radiation on the lunar surface can produce spurious signals through test-mass charging, momentum transfer, and thermoelastic deformation. We estimate these contributions for a notional 100 kg fused-silica optic using Geant4 transport, a finite-element electrostatic force map, and a spatial thermoelastic response kernel. Solar-minimum galactic cosmic rays and lunar albedo neutrons define a background case; a worst-day solar energetic particle spectrum (1989 solar flare conditions) defines an extreme, but rare, case. Aluminum-equivalent shielding from 50 to 1000 mils (1.27 to 25.4 mm) and two electrostatic actuator voltage configurations are compared. In the uniform-charge approximation, balanced drive with electrode configuration (B) substantially reduces linear charge-to-force coupling. Ideal continuous compensation of the mean charging current suppresses coherent charge accumulation while introducing negligible discharge shot noise. With compensation, charge-related noise dominates the low-frequency response, whereas thermoelastic deformation dominates at higher frequencies. Accumulated-dose and photodetector transient estimates identify additional component-qualification considerations. Estimates indicate that even under solar flare conditions, radiation noise is not a significant contributor to LILA's overall noise budget as long as charge-aware designs are followed.

</details>


### [61] [Choosing a generator for streaming Gaussian noise in gravitational-wave mock data: accuracy, cost and reproducibility](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.01624)
*Isaac C. F. Wong*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper evaluates noise generation methods for gravitational-wave mock data challenges, finding that the crossfade method with long windows (≥256 s) and smoothly continued target spectra achieves <10⁻³ covariance error across all 11 detector curves, outperforming overlap-save FIR filtering, ARMA models, and current default implementations that truncate the spectrum at the band edge.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To identify the most accurate, efficient, and reproducible method for generating long stretches of colored Gaussian noise matching prescribed detector sensitivity curves—critical for realistic gravitational-wave mock data challenges.

Method: Algebraic evaluation of covariance error against target power spectral density (PSD) over the analysis band; empirical measurement of throughput, memory footprint (retained state), and cost of bit-exact regeneration; comparison across crossfade (with varying window lengths), overlap-save FIR filtering, autoregressive (AR), ARMA, and PyCBC’s reproducible filter; analysis under two target PSD conditions: smooth continuation below band vs. hard cutoff at band edge.

Result: Crossfade with ≥256 s windows achieves <10⁻³ covariance error on all 11 detector curves at ~4.7 MSPS with only hundreds of bytes state; overlap-save is faster but requires >10⁴ taps for ~few-% error; no AR/ARMA configuration Pareto-dominates in accuracy-throughput; truncating target at band edge causes catastrophic error (>0.5) on aLIGO O4 and five other curves; cyclostationarity of crossfade contributes significantly to error only at short windows (8 s) on specific detectors.

Conclusion: The crossfade method with detector-specific window lengths (from provided table) and smooth PSD continuation below the analysis band is recommended as the optimal trade-off of accuracy, speed, low memory, and reproducibility for gravitational-wave noise simulation.

Abstract: Mock data challenges need long stretches of colored Gaussian noise with a prescribed spectrum. The candidates are the crossfade of independently drawn windows that the standard gravitational-wave libraries implement, overlap-save finite-impulse-response filtering, autoregressive and autoregressive moving-average (ARMA) models, and the reproducible filter of PyCBC. One measure ranks them all: the covariance error against the target over the analysis band, evaluated from each generator's algebra; generated noise only checks the implementations. We also measure throughput, retained state and the cost of bit-exact regeneration. With the target continued smoothly below the band, the crossfade with windows of 256 s or more reaches an error below $10^{-3}$ on all eleven detector curves as tabulated, at about 4.7 million samples per second from a state of a few hundred bytes. An overlap-save filter is faster but needs tens of thousands of taps for an error of a few percent, and no tested autoregressive or ARMA configuration lies on the accuracy--throughput fronts. An error of $10^{-3}$ needs a window of 8 to 235 s, longest on the design curves with narrow lines, where it also depends on the tabulation. With the target cut at the band edge, as current generators do by default, every method has an error above one half on the projected Advanced LIGO (aLIGO) O4 curve, and the crossfade on five other curves. The crossfade is cyclostationary. With the continued target, its cyclic part is the larger part of the error only at 8 s, on the aLIGO O4 and the Virgo and KAGRA design curves, and stays within a factor of two of the stationary part on the aLIGO O3 and the Virgo and KAGRA design curves. We recommend the crossfade with a window from the table of detector curves, and a smooth continuation of the target below the band.

</details>


### [62] [Spherically-symmetric solutions with internal Ricci-flat spaces in Brans-Dicke theory](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.01691)
*K.K. Ernazarov*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper constructs new exact spherically symmetric solutions in D-dimensional Brans-Dicke theory with n Ricci-flat internal spaces, derives horizon and regularity conditions, and analyzes photon spheres and ISCOs, showing that dynamical extra dimensions generically prevent regular horizons except in Schwarzschild or frozen-internal-space limits.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To generalize known Brans-type and multidimensional black-hole solutions to D dimensions with Ricci-flat internal spaces, and to clarify how nonminimal Brans-Dicke coupling affects horizon structure and regularity in such geometries.

Method: Uses conformal transformation to Einstein frame where the scalar field is minimally coupled; leverages known exact solution there; transforms back to Jordan (Brans-Dicke) frame to obtain explicit metric and scalar field; imposes algebraic constraints on exponents; analyzes causal structure, Kretschmann scalar, photon sphere, and ISCO radii in the 4D section.

Result: New family of exact D-dimensional Brans-Dicke solutions with n Ricci-flat internal spaces; explicit metric and scalar field expressions; horizon condition nu >= 1 for R = L; generic absence of regular horizons when internal spaces are dynamical (except Schwarzschild or frozen cases); derived photon-sphere and ISCO radii for the 4D sector with Schwarzschild limits.

Conclusion: Nonminimal Brans-Dicke coupling combined with dynamical Ricci-flat extra dimensions generically spoils horizon regularity; the Schwarzschild limit and frozen internal spaces are exceptional cases where regularity is restored; the results deepen understanding of black-hole geometry in scalar-tensor multidimensional gravity.

Abstract: We construct a new family of exact spherically symmetric solutions in $D$-dimensional Brans-Dicke theory with $n$ internal Ricci-flat spaces. The spacetime is assumed to be a product of a four-dimensional spherical sector and $n$ Ricci-flat internal manifolds. By performing a conformal transformation to the Einstein frame, the problem is reduced to Einstein gravity with a minimally coupled scalar field, for which the exact solution is already known. Applying the inverse transformation to the Jordan frame yields explicit expressions for the metric and the Brans-Dicke scalar field, together with a generalized algebraic constraint on the exponents. The obtained solutions generalize the four-dimensional Brans solutions and the multidimensional solutions with Ricci-flat internal spaces. We analyze the causal structure of the four-dimensional section and derive the necessary condition for the surface $R=L$ to be an event horizon, $\nu \equiv \left|a+b+\frac12\sum_i a_iN_i\right| \ge 1$. A Kretschmann-scalar analysis shows that, apart from the Schwarzschild limit and the case of frozen internal spaces, a regular horizon is generically absent when the extra dimensions are dynamical. For the four-dimensional section we also obtain the photon-sphere and ISCO radii and discuss their Schwarzschild limits. The results clarify the role of nonminimal Brans-Dicke coupling in multidimensional black-hole geometries with Ricci-flat internal factors.

</details>


### [63] [Ricci-Non-metric Holographic Dark Energy](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.01766)
*Sukanta Bhunia,Prabir Rudra,Abhijit Lahiri*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper proposes a novel holographic dark energy model using a combination of Ricci scalar R and non-metricity scalar Q as the infrared cutoff, yielding a cosmologically viable model that reproduces all major cosmic epochs and exhibits flexible dark energy behavior (quintessence, phantom-divide crossing, or phantom) depending on parameter choices.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To construct a more fundamental and causally local holographic dark energy model by replacing conventional infrared cutoffs with geometric invariants—specifically, a combination of the Ricci scalar R and non-metricity scalar Q—that depend only on current spacetime properties, avoiding dependence on past or future evolution.

Method: Formulation of a holographic dark energy density where the infrared cutoff is defined as a linear combination of R and Q; derivation of Friedmann equations and dynamical evolution equations within a homogeneous, isotropic FLRW background; analysis of key cosmological parameters (density parameter Ω_Λ, equation of state w_Λ, deceleration q) as functions of redshift or scale factor.

Result: The model successfully reproduces radiation-, matter-, and dark energy-dominated eras; w_Λ evolves dynamically and can cross the phantom divide (w = −1), exhibiting quintessence (w > −1), crossing, or phantom (w < −1) behavior depending on parameter space; all standard cosmological constraints on background evolution are satisfied.

Conclusion: The R–Q-based holographic dark energy model is a theoretically well-motivated, observationally viable extension of holographic principles to metric–affine geometry, offering richer dynamical behavior than conventional cutoffs and reinforcing the role of geometric invariants in fundamental cosmology.

Abstract: In this work we propose a holographic dark energy model inspired by the Ricci scalar $R$ and non-metricity scalar $Q$. The infrared cutoff of the holographic dark energy is described by a combination of the Ricci scalar and the non-metricity scalar. Such a structure is very helpful because the evolution is entirely dependent on the universe's current characteristics rather than its previous or future characteristics. Additionally, the theory becomes more fundamental due to the application of invariants. We have built the model and examined its cosmological characteristics. The behavior of several cosmological parameters, including the density parameter, equation of state parameter, and deceleration parameter, is examined. It is observed that the holographic dark energy model may depict every cosmological epoch, beginning with radiation in the early universe, moving on to matter, and concluding with the dark energy-dominated period in later times. Depending on the parameter spaces selected, the equation of state parameter demonstrates that the model may have quintessence nature, phantom-divide crossing, or even phantom nature in future times.

</details>


### [64] [Optical Characteristics and Frequency Shifts of Quartic Wormholes in $f(T,τ)$ Gravity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.01811)
*Sourav Chaudhary,S. K. Maurya,Jitendra Kumar*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper constructs traversable wormhole solutions in $f(T,\tau)$ gravity using a quartic inverse-power density profile ($\rho \propto 1/r^4$), derives the exact shape function, confirms standard wormhole geometry (throat, flaring-out, asymptotics), verifies NEC violation and exotic matter at the throat, computes gravitational deflection via Gauss-Bonnet formalism, analyzes scalar/electromagnetic perturbations, and introduces a new electromagnetic potential to study red-blue shifts — yielding a unified characterization of geometry, stability, and optics.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To construct and comprehensively characterize traversable wormhole solutions in $f(T,\tau)$ teleparallel gravity using a physically motivated quartic inverse-power energy density profile ($\rho \propto r^{-4}$), addressing geometry, exotic matter requirements, gravitational lensing, and perturbative stability.

Method: Analytical derivation of the shape function from the specified $1/r^4$ energy density within $f(T,\tau)$ gravity; verification of wormhole geometry (throat radius, flaring-out condition, asymptotics); computation of energy conditions (especially NEC); application of the Gauss-Bonnet theorem to compute the weak-field gravitational deflection angle; linear stability analysis under scalar and electromagnetic field perturbations; introduction and analysis of a novel electromagnetic potential to model redshift/blueshift profiles.

Result: An exact, asymptotically flat traversable wormhole solution is obtained with a well-defined throat and flaring-out geometry; NEC is violated at the throat due to negative radial pressure, confirming exotic matter; the deflection angle depends significantly on parameters $\gamma$ and $\rho_0$; the configuration shows stable behavior under scalar and EM perturbations; the new EM potential yields analytically tractable red-blue shift variations.

Conclusion: Quartic inverse-power density profiles support viable, geometrically consistent, lensing-active, and perturbatively stable wormholes in $f(T,\tau)$ gravity, providing a unified framework linking matter content, spacetime structure, optical effects, and stability—advancing the theoretical viability of such objects beyond general relativity.

Abstract: This work explores traversable wormhole solutions characterized by a quartic inverse-power density profile within $f(T,{\tau})$ gravity. A specific inverse-power density profile, characterized by proportional to $1/r^4$ is introduced to establish the corresponding exact form of the shape function. The derived configuration exhibits all the necessary geometrical features of a traversable wormhole, such as throat formation, flaring-out behavior, and acceptable asymptotic characteristics. To gain further insight into the matter sector, we analyze the corresponding radial profiles of the energy density and pressure components. The throat region is characterised by negative radial pressure and null energy condition violation, both of which are relevant to the exotic matter content of the wormhole solution. To examine the lensing characteristics of the obtained spacetime, we employ the Gauss-Bonnet formalism to evaluate the gravitational deflection angle. Our findings indicate that the parameters $\gamma$ and $\rho_0$ exert a considerable influence on the resulting light trajectories. The perturbative behavior of the obtained configuration is investigated within the scalar and electromagnetic field sectors. Subsequently, a novel electromagnetic potential is introduced to examine its influence on the radial variation of the red-blue shifts characteristics. Our findings establish a unified picture of the geometrical, perturbative, and optical characteristics of quartic density-supported wormholes.

</details>


### [65] [Vanishing Island: Spectral Criticality in Boson Stars](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.01826)
*Bo-Xuan Ge*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: The paper identifies a cusp-type critical endpoint in solitonic boson star parameter space where radial stability is lost due to coalescence of two zero-mode boundaries and vanishing quadratic/cubic stiffness; the soft mode exhibits square-root scaling, indicating a divergent timescale, and the structure is robust under scalar self-interaction deformations.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand the onset of radial instability in solitonic boson stars and characterize the nature of the stability boundary, particularly whether it features generic or singular (e.g., cusp) critical behavior.

Method: Analysis of radial perturbation spectra and constrained effective potential coefficients (quadratic, cubic, quartic) near stability boundaries; identification of zero-mode coalescence and scaling of the soft mode frequency Ω_soft with distance from the cusp point σ_cusp − σ.

Result: A cusp-type endpoint is found where two radial zero-mode boundaries merge; constrained quadratic stiffness and cubic coefficient vanish simultaneously, quartic coefficient remains finite and non-zero; soft mode obeys Ω_soft ∝ (σ_cusp − σ)^{1/2}, implying divergent linear response timescale; critical structure is preserved under varied scalar self-interactions.

Conclusion: The radial stability loss in solitonic boson stars occurs via a universal cusp singularity, characterized by mean-field critical exponents and robust against changes in scalar potential details—suggesting a generic mechanism for soliton destabilization in relativistic self-gravitating fields.

Abstract: We identify a cusp-type endpoint at which a finite radially stable region of solitonic boson stars disappears. Two fundamental radial zero-mode boundaries coalesce there, while the constrained quadratic stiffness and cubic coefficient vanish and the quartic coefficient remains finite and non-zero. The soft radial mode obeys \(\Omega_{\mathrm{soft}}\propto (\sigma_{\mathrm{cusp}}-\sigma)^{1/2}\), revealing a divergent linear timescale at the endpoint. The same critical structure persists under the tested deformation of the scalar self-interaction.

</details>


### [66] [Light Ring Stability Across Spacetime Dimensions and Theories of Gravity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.01917)
*Kajol Paithankar,Sanved Kolekar*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper generalizes the light ring (LR) theorem beyond 4D General Relativity to modified gravity (f(R), scalar-tensor, Horndeski) and higher dimensions (d ≥ 4), showing LR existence persists but stability is no longer guaranteed—depending on theory-specific couplings and dimensionality.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To assess viability of horizonless ultracompact objects (UCOs) as black hole mimickers in modified gravity and higher dimensions, by determining whether stable light rings—which may trigger nonlinear instabilities—necessarily exist.

Method: Topological analysis of null geodesic equations in stationary, axisymmetric, horizonless spacetimes; stability analysis via second-derivative conditions on effective potential; extension to f(R), scalar-tensor, and restricted Horndeski theories in d ≥ 4 dimensions.

Result: In 4D modified gravity, LR stability depends on coupling parameters and NEC—stable LRs are not guaranteed. In d > 4, LRs still appear in pairs topologically, but four distinct types (including two saddle-point types) emerge; stability constraints forbid certain pairings, so a stable LR need not accompany an unstable one.

Conclusion: Light ring stability—and thus UCO viability as long-lived black hole mimickers—is highly sensitive to both gravitational theory and spacetime dimensionality, weakening generic GR-based instability arguments and impacting strong-field tests using UCOs.

Abstract: The light ring theorem states that four-dimensional stationary, axisymmetric, horizonless ultracompact objects (UCOs) possess light rings (LRs) in pairs, one of which is stable if the Null Energy Condition (NEC) holds in General Relativity (GR). The existence of a stable LR may trigger nonlinear spacetime instabilities, thereby raising questions about the viability of UCOs as long-lived black hole mimickers. We extend the LR existence and stability theorem beyond four-dimensional GR to modified gravity theories, in particular, $f(R)$ gravity, scalar-tensor theories, and restricted Horndeski gravity, in ($d \geq 4$) dimensional spacetimes. Our findings show that in four-dimensional modified gravities, the stability of LRs is governed by theory-specific coupling parameters in addition to the NEC. Consequently, a stable LR is not guaranteed to occur in certain parameter regimes. In higher dimensions ($d>4$), we confirm using topological arguments that LRs still emerge in pairs; however, the topological charges are now associated with four distinct types of LRs, including two types of saddle points. The stability analysis further constrains the allowed LR pairings, implying that, unlike in four-dimensional GR, the emergent pair does not necessarily include a stable LR. These findings directly impact tests of GR using UCOs, demonstrating that LR stability is highly sensitive to both the underlying gravitational theory and spacetime dimensionality.

</details>


### [67] [Spin-dependent topological structure of black-hole thermodynamics](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.02035)
*Erdem Sucu,İzzet Sakallı,Yusuf Sucu,Emmanuel N. Saridakis*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper connects quantum-corrected Hawking radiation with thermodynamic topology for massive-gravity AdS black holes, showing that particle spin and GUP modify local defect structure (positions, bifurcations) but preserve global topological charge (W = +1 or 0).


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore whether quantum-corrected Hawking radiation (carrying particle-spin information) is linked to thermodynamic topology (defined via off-shell thermodynamic field defects), a connection previously unexplored.

Method: Combines arbitrary-spin zitterbewegung tunneling and generalized-uncertainty-principle (GUP) corrections for a massive-gravity AdS black hole; derives spin-dependent Hawking temperature; constructs channel-dependent effective entropy via first law; formulates off-shell free energy and thermodynamic vector field; compares with Bekenstein-Hawking and exponentially corrected entropies.

Result: Global topological charge W is branch-dependent (W = +1 for γ = −1; W = 0 for γ = +1), unchanged by spin or GUP; however, particle spin shifts defect positions and controls the fold separating two-defect and defect-free regions jointly with GUP parameter and cosmological constant.

Conclusion: Quantum properties of emitted particles do not alter global thermodynamic topology but reorganize its local defect and bifurcation structure—establishing a direct link between quantum-corrected radiation channels and local thermodynamic topology, enabling information retention in the defect configuration.

Abstract: Thermodynamic topology classifies black-hole equilibrium states through topological defects of an off-shell thermodynamic field, while quantum-corrected Hawking radiation can retain information about the quantum numbers of the emitted particle. Whether these two structures are connected has remained largely unexplored. In this work we investigate this question for a massive-gravity AdS black hole by combining arbitrary-spin zitterbewegung tunneling with generalized-uncertainty-principle corrections. We derive the particle-spin-dependent Hawking temperature, construct the corresponding channel-dependent effective entropy through the first law, and formulate the associated off-shell free energy and thermodynamic vector field. As reference sectors, we also analyze the Bekenstein-Hawking and exponentially corrected entropies. We find a clear separation between local and global thermodynamic topology. For the $\gamma=-1$ branch, a single positively wound defect persists, yielding $W=+1$, whereas for $\gamma=+1$ the defects occur in oppositely wound pairs and the global charge remains $W=0$. The particle spin nevertheless shifts the defect positions and the critical configurations at which the $\gamma=+1$ pair merges and annihilates. The fold separating the two-defect and defect-free regions is controlled jointly by the particle spin, the GUP parameter, and the cosmological constant. Thus, the quantum properties of the emitted particle do not alter the global topological class, but they reorganize the local defect and bifurcation structure within it. Our results establish a direct connection between quantum-corrected Hawking radiation and the corresponding effective off-shell thermodynamic topology, showing that its local defect structure can retain information about the quantum channel through which the black hole radiates.

</details>


### [68] [Torsion, Frames and Quantum Bounces: Inflation and Ekpyrotic Contraction in Quantum Trajectory Cosmology with a Non-Minimally Coupled Scalar](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.02036)
*Rikpratik Sengupta,Paulo Vargas Moniz*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper investigates de Broglie-Bohm quantum cosmology for a scalar field non-minimally coupled to Riemann-Cartan geometry (with torsion), comparing inflation and ekpyrotic contraction; it finds torsion does not cause bounces—interference does—and only modifies late-time exponents and spectral tilts, with negligible effect on tensor-to-scalar ratio r.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To assess whether spacetime torsion in Riemann-Cartan geometry can qualitatively alter quantum cosmological dynamics—particularly the occurrence of a bounce—in de Broglie-Bohm interpretation, relative to standard inflationary or ekpyrotic scenarios.

Method: Analyzes minisuperspace quantum cosmology using the Wheeler-DeWitt equation in both Jordan and Einstein frames; eliminates torsion trace algebraically pre-quantisation; exploits conformal flatness of minisuperspace metric to establish frame covariance of Bohmian trajectories, quantum potential, and Laplace-Beltrami operator; solves exactly for massless and exponential/quadratic potentials; computes Bohmian trajectories, spectral indices, and tensor ratios.

Result: Torsion does not induce bounces—bounces arise from wavefunction interference; torsion modifies late-time expansion exponent (Jordan frame) and spectral tilt at order √ξ and ξ^(3/2); tensor modes remain blue and nearly unaffected by the Bohmian bounce; r-shift due to torsion is <1% in quadratic inflation; single-field ekpyrosis yields universal blue spectra independent of torsion.

Conclusion: Torsion in this Riemann-Cartan scalar-tensor model is dynamically inert at the qualitative level: it cannot replace or enable bouncing cosmologies, but only induces quantitatively small, frame-dependent corrections to late-time evolution and perturbation spectra.

Abstract: We study the de Broglie-Bohm quantum cosmology of a scalar field non-minimally coupled to a metric-compatible Riemann-Cartan geometry, comparing inflation with ekpyrotic contraction. The torsion trace is auxiliary and is eliminated algebraically before quantisation. The minisuperspace metric depends on the coupling $F(\phi)$ and a constant $\kappa$ for an independent quadratic torsion term ($\kappa=0$ Einstein-Cartan, $\kappa\to\infty$ torsion-free). For every $F$, $\kappa$ and potential this metric is conformally flat, so torsion acts only through the Einstein-frame kinetic function $K(\phi)$. Its curvature is therefore not a diagnostic of torsion, and the Laplace-Beltrami Wheeler-DeWitt equation, quantum potential and Bohmian trajectories are frame covariant. For a massless scalar the equation is solved exactly; in a two-packet state all 41 flux-weighted Bohmian trajectories bounce in the Einstein frame. In the Jordan frame the bounce at the symmetric event is independent of torsion and persists only for $\xi<\xi_c$ (closed form); torsion changes the late-time exponent from $1-\sqrt{6\xi/(1+6\xi)}$ to $1-\sqrt{6\xi}$. The bounce comes from interference in the state, not from torsion. For exponential potentials, exact Wheeler-DeWitt solutions show that in the sector containing the ekpyrotic attractor no Bohmian trajectory bounces, and single-momentum states bounce only where the potential is negligible and end in the crunch. For quadratic inflation the torsion-specific shift of $r$ is below one per cent. A single ekpyrotic field gives blue spectra, $n_s=3+2/(\epsilon-1)$ and $r=16\epsilon$, whatever the torsion, also for running $\epsilon$. In an entropic two-field version the tilt shifts at order $\sqrt\xi$ (non-minimal coupling) and $\xi^{3/2}$ (torsion). Tensor modes cross the massless Bohmian bounce almost unchanged and stay blue.

</details>


### [69] [A Singularity Theorem for Quasi-Topological Gravities](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.02190)
*Francesco Di Filippo*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper extends Penrose's singularity theorem to quasi-topological gravity theories with non-singular black hole solutions, proving that spherical collapse under the null energy condition still leads to future null geodesic incompleteness—implying unavoidable predictability breakdown (Cauchy horizons) even dynamically, not just in vacuum.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To assess whether quasi-topological gravity theories genuinely resolve the black hole singularity problem or merely replace curvature singularities with Cauchy horizons that undermine predictability.

Method: Generalization of Penrose's null geodesic incompleteness theorem using causal structure analysis and energy condition assumptions, applied to all known classes of quasi-topological gravity admitting regular black hole solutions.

Result: All such quasi-topological theories inherit the same fundamental limitation as general relativity: formation of a trapped sphere during spherical collapse under the null energy condition implies future null geodesic incompleteness—hence, a Cauchy horizon forms dynamically.

Conclusion: The Cauchy horizon is not an artefact of static vacuum solutions but an inevitable feature in dynamical collapse; thus, these theories do not resolve the predictability problem inherent in black hole interiors.

Abstract: Quasi-topological theories of gravity have recently attracted substantial attention as several theories in this class have a non-singular black hole as a vacuum and spherically symmetric solution. This motivates the expectation that these theories can provide a resolution to the singularity problem and a description of black holes cores. The known solutions, however, possess a Cauchy horizon which denotes the end of predictability of the theory, as the initial data do not determine a unique continuation past this type of horizon. In this paper, we show that Penrose singularity theorem can be extended to all the known classes of quasi-topological theories that have non-singular black holes as solutions. We prove that spherical collapse satisfying the null energy condition necessarily leads to future null geodesic incompleteness once a trapped sphere forms. This implies that the lack of predictability of the theories is not an artefact of vacuum solutions and these theories are as problematic as general relativity for the dynamical studies of black hole interiors.

</details>


### [70] [Pilot search for continuous gravitational waves from Hercules X-1 in O3 LIGO data: enhancements to a hidden Markov model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.02307)
*Nicholas Kah Yean Low,Andrew Melatos*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper presents a semi-coherent search for continuous gravitational waves from stellar oscillations in Hercules X-1 using LIGO O3 data and an enhanced hidden Markov model algorithm; it validates several algorithmic improvements that boost sensitivity depth by ~14%, and reports four surviving candidates in the 60–200 Hz band, with a 95% confidence upper limit on strain of 9.3×10⁻²⁶ at 174 Hz.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To implement and test algorithmic enhancements—such as second-order projected semi-major axis correction, orbital eccentricity correction, subband width adjustment, and propagation of orbital parameters with nonzero orbital period derivative—for future continuous gravitational-wave searches targeting neutron star oscillations.

Method: Semi-coherent search using an established hidden Markov model (HMM) to track stochastic frequency wandering; synthetic signal injections into Gaussian noise used for validation; five-level veto procedure applied to candidate selection; frequentist upper limit calculation assuming uniform polarization prior.

Result: Algorithmic enhancements improve sensitivity depth by ≈14% for a Hercules X-1-like target; four candidates survive vetoes at terminating frequencies 76.41 Hz, 109.47 Hz, 144.40 Hz, and 189.24 Hz; 95% confidence upper limit on wave strain is 9.3×10⁻²⁶ at 174 Hz.

Conclusion: The proposed enhancements are empirically validated and significantly improve search sensitivity; the pilot search yields plausible candidates warranting follow-up, demonstrating readiness for more sensitive future analyses with upgraded detectors and refined models.

Abstract: A semi-coherent search is conducted for continuous gravitational waves from stellar oscillations in Hercules X-1 with data from Observing Run 3 of the Laser Interferometer Gravitational-wave Observatory, using an established hidden Markov model algorithm to track stochastic wandering of the signal frequency. The primary goal is to implement and test several algorithmic enhancements relevant to future searches, including a second-order projected semi-major axis correction, an orbital eccentricity correction, an adjustment to the width of a search subband, and propagation of the electromagnetically measured orbital parameters with a nonzero orbital period derivative. The enhancements are validated by injecting synthetic signals into Gaussian noise and improve the sensitivity depth by $\approx$ 14% for a Hercules X-1-like target. In the 60--200 Hz band, the pilot search yields four candidates that survive a five-level veto procedure, whose terminating frequencies are 76.41 Hz, 109.47 Hz, 144.40 Hz, and 189.24 Hz. The frequentist upper limit on the maximum detectable wave strain at 95% confidence is arbitrarily set at 174 Hz and found to be $9.3 \times 10^{-26}$, assuming a uniform prior on polarization.

</details>


### [71] [First law of thermodynamics for interacting charged black holes and applications](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.02663)
*Qing Han,Marcus Khuri,Gilbert Weinstein,Jingang Xiong*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: Derives a mass variation identity for black hole thermodynamics in Einstein-Maxwell theory with multiple charged horizons and conical singularities; establishes monotonicity of angular momentum Hessian along paths of increasing Weyl gaps.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To generalize the first law of black hole thermodynamics to multi-horizon, asymptotically flat, stationary axisymmetric Einstein-Maxwell solutions—including those with conical axis singularities—and to understand how geometric and thermodynamic quantities vary across parameter families.

Method: Uses variational methods on the Einstein-Maxwell action under fixed horizon topology and asymptotic conditions; derives a first-law-type identity; combines it with a sign theorem for conical deficit parameters; analyzes Hessian structure on special subfamilies (all-degenerate, Majumdar-Papapetrou).

Result: Obtains a precise mass variation identity valid for independent parameter variations; defines a corrected ADM mass whose angular momentum derivatives yield horizon angular velocities; proves positive definiteness of its angular momentum Hessian on Majumdar-Papapetrou solutions and its monotonic nondecrease (in positive-semidefinite order) along admissible paths of increasing Weyl gaps at fixed charges.

Conclusion: The derived identity unifies thermodynamic behavior across multi-horizon black hole configurations and reveals structural constraints—via Hessian monotonicity—on how angular momenta and conical deficits coevolve in parameter space, supporting stability and classification arguments in higher-genus or multi-black-hole spacetimes.

Abstract: We derive a mass variation identity expressing the first law of black hole thermodynamics for asymptotically flat, stationary, axisymmetric Einstein-Maxwell solutions with multiple electrically charged horizons and finite conical axis singularities. Under the stated asymptotic and parameter regularity hypotheses, the identity holds for independent variations of all parameters within families of fixed horizon types. On the all-degenerate family, a correction to the ADM mass defines a scalar function whose derivatives with respect to angular momenta are the horizon angular velocities. At the Majumdar-Papapetrou family, its angular momentum Hessian is positive definite. Combining the first variation identity with a separate theorem on the sign of the conical parameters shows that this Hessian is nondecreasing in the positive-semidefinite order along admissible paths of increasing Weyl gaps at fixed positive charges.

</details>


### [72] [Charge-Activated Birefringence and Shadow Mimicry around Reissner-Nordström Black Holes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.02727)
*Mao-Yuan Wan,Chen Wu*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper shows that electric charge can induce birefringence in a neutral vector field coupled to the Einstein tensor near a Reissner-Nordström black hole; nonzero charge *and* coupling are jointly required to split shadow edges between parities and generate a helicity-reversed backward-scattered signal—effects absent in the uncoupled case.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To distinguish gravitational parity violation (via Einstein-tensor coupling) from mere electromagnetic parameter variation (e.g., black hole charge) using observable signatures in black hole shadows and scattering.

Method: Analytical and semi-analytical study of propagation and scattering of a neutral vector field coupled to the Einstein tensor in the Reissner-Nordström spacetime; comparison of effective potentials, shadow radii, and backward-scattering amplitudes for left- and right-handed polarizations.

Result: Charge alone cannot reproduce parity-split shadow edges or helicity-reversed backward scattering; both nonzero charge *and* coupling are necessary. Positive (negative) coupling raises (lowers) the effective potential, suppresses (enhances) low-frequency absorption, and shrinks (expands) average shadow radius. Backward signal arises solely from parity-asymmetric complex scattering amplitudes and is purely helicity-flipped.

Conclusion: The split shadow edge and helicity-reversed backward signal are unique joint signatures of Einstein-tensor coupling *and* electric charge—providing a clean observational discriminant between fundamental coupling and astrophysical parameter degeneracy, pending detection mechanisms for the field.

Abstract: We discover that electric charge can activates birefringence for a neutral vector field coupled to the Einstein tensor around a Reissner-Nordström black hole. Changing the electric charge of an uncoupled black hole can mimic one parity's shadow radius of a coupled black hole. The coupling makes the two parity looks differently. It separates their shadow edges of two parities and produces a backward-scattered signal with reversed helicity. However changing the charge alone cannot reproduce both effects. They require both nonzero charge and coupling. At weak coupling, the leading correction is the same for both potential. Positive coupling raises the potential, reduces low-frequency absorption and decrease the average shadow radius. Negative coupling reverses these trends. The backward signal is nonzero, this is due to the difference between the complex scattering amplitudes of the two parities. It is entirely helicity reversed. For the uncoupled field, the shadows is the same for both parities and the backward intensity vanishes exactly at every charge. We can use the split edge and backward signal to distinguish the coupling from a change of charge alone. A comparison with observations still requires a mechanism that produces and detects this field.

</details>


### [73] [Integrable mass of Kerr-Newman-Bertotti-Robinson black hole](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.03123)
*Li Hu,Rong-Gen Cai,Shan-Wen Jiang,Shao-Jiang Wang*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper solves the long-standing problem of determining the conserved mass of rotating black holes in the Bertotti-Robinson universe using integrability methods and rigorous analytic techniques, yielding a closed-form expression for the Kerr-Newman-Bertotti-Robinson mass—without relying on thermodynamic assumptions—and thereby rigorously proving the first law and Smarr formula for this family.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The nontrivial asymptotic structure of rotating black holes in the Bertotti-Robinson universe prevents direct determination of conserved mass; prior work depended on a priori thermodynamic input, leaving the mass definition unjustified from first principles.

Method: Integrability methods are employed; local analytic existence and uniqueness are established via the Cauchy-Kovalevskaja theorem; a rescaled recurrence relation is derived and its termination proved at all orders to obtain a closed-form mass expression.

Result: A closed-form expression for the Kerr-Newman-Bertotti-Robinson mass is obtained, matching previous results derived via the canonical first law identity and correctly reducing to the Kerr-Bertotti-Robinson subcases; the first law and Smarr formula are rigorously proven without thermodynamic input.

Conclusion: The conserved mass of Kerr-Bertotti-Robinson black holes is now fixed from geometric and analytic principles alone—constituting the first derivation independent of thermodynamic identities—and provides a foundation for future thermodynamic and holographic applications.

Abstract: The nontrivial asymptotic structure of rotating black holes in the Bertotti-Robinson universe complicates a direct determination of their conserved mass, leading previous studies to rely on a priori thermodynamic input. We address this long-standing problem using integrability methods, establish local analytic existence and uniqueness via the Cauchy-Kovalevskaja theorem, and obtain a closed expression via a rescaled recurrence whose termination is proved at all orders. The resulting Kerr-Newman-Bertotti-Robinson mass coincides with the expression previously derived using the canonical first law identity and reproduces the masses of both Kerr-Bertotti-Robinson subcases. With the mass thus fixed without invoking thermodynamic relations for the first time, we rigorously establish the first law of black-hole thermodynamics and the Smarr formula for a whole family of Kerr-Bertotti-Robinson black holes suitable for future applications.

</details>


### [74] [Surrogate model of gravitational waveforms for quasi-circular, spin-aligned, asymmetric-mass black hole binaries based on the hybridization of WaSABI-C inspirals with numerical relativity waveforms](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.03564)
*H. L. Iglesias,D. M. Shoemaker*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: WaSABINRHybSur2dq15 is the first gravitational waveform surrogate model integrating self-force (WaSABI-C), post-Newtonian, and numerical relativity (SXS Catalog 3) data for aligned-spin, unequal-mass black hole binaries; it covers q ∈ [1.25, 15], |χ₁z| ≤ 0.5, five dominant ℓ=m modes, and achieves <10⁻³ mismatch for M ∈ [80, 300] M⊙ in LVK band.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To bridge accuracy gaps across binary parameter space—especially at high mass ratios and moderate spins—by unifying high-precision inspiral (self-force), intermediate dynamics (PN), and merger–ringdown (NR) physics into a single fast, accurate surrogate model for LIGO-Virgo-KAGRA data analysis.

Method: Constructed a reduced-order surrogate model trained on 65 hybrid waveforms: WaSABI-C self-force inspirals matched to SXS Catalog 3 NR simulations; uses Gaussian process regression with domain decomposition over mass ratio q and dimensionless spin χ₁z; includes (ℓ,m) = (2,2), (2,1), (3,3), (4,4), (5,5) modes; calibrated to LVK sensitivity band (f_low = 20 Hz).

Result: Faithful reproduction of hybrid waveforms with mismatches < 10⁻³ for total masses 80–300 M⊙; coverage of q ∈ [1.25, 15], |χ₁z| ≤ 0.5, and full LVK band for M ≳ 111.5 M⊙; first model combining self-force, PN, and NR in a single surrogate.

Conclusion: WaSABINRHybSur2dq15 establishes a new benchmark for rapid, physically informed waveform modeling of spinning, asymmetric black hole binaries, enabling robust parameter estimation and testing of strong-field gravity in current and future ground-based GW observatories.

Abstract: With the incorporation of aligned-spin dynamics into self-force waveform models, such as the WaSABI-C inspiral model, and with the release of the SXS collaboration's third public catalog of NR simulations, the gravitational waveform modeling community has made significant advances in the modeling of spinning, asymmetric-mass black hole binaries. In light of these developments, we present WaSABINRHybSur2dq15, the first surrogate model to incorporate information from self-force theory, post-Newtonian theory, and numerical relativity. The model is trained on 65 hybrid waveforms for quasi-circular, spin-aligned, asymmetric-mass black hole binaries with mass ratios spanning 1:1.25 to 1:20 and aligned-spins for the primary black hole ranging -0.5 to 0.5. The hybrid waveforms are generated from a set of WaSABI-C inspirals and NR waveforms from the third SXS public catalog. The model covers the parameter domain $1.25 \leq q \leq 15$ and $|\chi_{1z}| \leq 0.5$, and includes the $(2,2),\, (2,1),\, (3,3),\, (4,4),$ and $(5,5)$ spin-weighted spherical harmonic modes. The model also spans the entire LIGO-Virgo-KAGRA (LVK) frequency band (with $f_{\text{low}}=20$ Hz) for total masses $M \gtrsim 111.5\,M_{\odot}$. We find that the WaSABINRHybSur2dq15 model faithfully reproduces the hybrid waveforms with mismatches less than $10^{-3}$ for total masses in the range $80\,M_{\odot} \leq M \leq 300\,M_{\odot}$.

</details>


### [75] [Tracing source imprints on the gravitational direct wave](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.03632)
*Andrew Laeuger,Colin Weller,Ling Sun,Aleyna Koro,Patrick P. Chen,Sizheng Ma,Yanbei Chen*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper advances the modeling of black hole ringdown direct waves by introducing a frequency-dependent filter and complex-coordinate analytic continuation of Teukolsky sources, enabling a semi-analytic, highly accurate description of the plunging perturber’s imprint beyond light-cone approximations.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To better understand how the trajectory of a plunging perturber imprints on the direct wave component of black hole ringdown—beyond standard quasinormal-mode filtering—and to bridge the gap between source dynamics and observed waveform features.

Method: Introduces a frequency-dependent black hole response filter for improved kernel localization; analytically continues Teukolsky source variables into complex coordinates; applies a generalized steepest-descent method to model coherent emission and its influence on the direct wave.

Result: The new frequency-dependent filter yields a more localized kernel than rational filters; complex-coordinate continuation enables a semi-analytic direct wave that matches full numerical integration across the entire plunge; the generalized steepest-descent analysis reveals how coherent emission shapes the dynamical imprint beyond the light-cone picture.

Conclusion: The direct wave carries rich, non-quasinormal-mode information about plunging dynamics, and its accurate modeling requires going beyond real-axis approximations—via complex-source continuation and frequency-resolved response kernels—to capture coherent emission effects essential for precision gravitational-wave physics.

Abstract: The direct wave component of black hole (BH) ringdown, revealed through quasinormal-mode filters, carries a non-quasinormal-mode imprint of the plunging perturber's dynamics. However, the connection between the source trajectory and the observed direct wave remains incompletely understood. We first show that the full waveform filtered by the frequency-dependent black hole response gives a substantially more localized kernel than the standard rational filter approach. We then show that analytically continuing the Teukolsky source variables to complex coordinates allows for a semianalytic direct wave which closely reproduces numerical integration throughout the entire plunge. Our generalized steepest-descent method clarifies how coherent emission shapes the imprint of source dynamics on the observed direct wave beyond the light-cone picture.

</details>

# hep-ph [[Back]](#toc)

### [76] [The Standard Model with Non-Anticommuting $γ_5$ in the BMHV Scheme](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00141)
*Paul Kühler,Dominik Stöckinger,Matthias Weißwange*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper completes the 1-loop renormalisation of the full Standard Model in the Breitenlohner-Maison/'t Hooft-Veltman (BMHV) scheme for γ₅ in dimensional regularisation, including all singular and finite symmetry-restoring counterterms in general Rξ-gauge; it clarifies the role of evanescent gauge interactions and recommends a 4-dimensional treatment of electroweak currents to ensure consistency.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To enable multi-loop electroweak precision calculations with a fully consistent treatment of γ₅ in dimensional regularisation, while addressing gauge/BRST symmetry breaking induced by the BMHV scheme and its restoration via counterterms.

Method: Systematic 1-loop renormalisation of the full Standard Model in the BMHV scheme within general Rξ-gauge; explicit computation of all UV-divergent and finite counterterms required to restore gauge and BRST invariance; analysis of evanescent operators arising from D-dimensional vs. 4-dimensional treatment of photon/gluon currents.

Result: Complete set of 1-loop counterterms—including both singular and finite parts—for all SM interactions in BMHV scheme; demonstration that QCD unavoidably contributes to chiral-symmetry-breaking evanescence, necessitating full-SM treatment; identification of optimal prescription: 4-dimensional electroweak currents, D-dimensional QCD currents.

Conclusion: The BMHV scheme requires full-SM renormalisation due to QCD-induced evanescent symmetry breaking; a hybrid regularisation—4D electroweak currents and D-dimensional QCD—is recommended for consistency and practicality in high-precision calculations.

Abstract: The Breitenlohner-Maison/'t Hooft-Veltman (BMHV) scheme provides a treatment of $\gamma_5$ in dimensional regularisation (DReg) that is guaranteed to be self-consistent at all orders, but it breaks gauge and BRST invariance at the regularised level, which must be restored by suitable counterterms. We present the complete 1-loop renormalisation of the full Standard Model in the BMHV scheme in general $R_\xi$-gauge, including all singular and all finite symmetry-restoring counterterms. We further analyse the impact of evanescent gauge interactions, which allow the photon and gluon currents to be treated either 4- or $D$-dimensionally. We show that QCD always contributes to the symmetry breaking, so that the Standard Model must be treated as a whole, and we recommend a purely 4-dimensional treatment of electroweak gauge interactions. Our results provide a necessary step towards multi-loop electroweak precision calculations with a fully consistent treatment of $\gamma_5$.

</details>


### [77] [Interacting scalar-fermion two-component dark matter in a gauged $\boldsymbol{U(1)_{B-L}}$ model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00232)
*Jacinto P. Neto,Farinaldo S. Queiroz,Carlos E. Yaguna*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: Proposes a two-component dark matter model with Dirac fermion χ and complex scalar S in a gauged U(1)_{B−L} extension of the SM, incorporating neutrino mass via type-I seesaw; features gauge-stabilized stability, Yukawa-coupled dynamics enabling conversion/semi-annihilation freeze-out, and viable parameter space testable by next-gen direct detection.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To construct a theoretically consistent, multi-component dark matter framework where stability arises naturally from gauge symmetry (not ad hoc discrete symmetries), while simultaneously explaining neutrino masses and evading stringent direct-detection limits.

Method: Builds a U(1)_{B-L}–gauged extension of the Standard Model featuring a Dirac fermion χ and complex scalar S in the dark sector; imposes gauge invariance to ensure stability; computes coupled Boltzmann equations for freeze-out including conversion (χS ↔ χ̄S†), semi-conversion (χS ↔ χ̄χ), and semi-annihilation (χχ ↔ χS); confronts results with LHC Z′ searches, relic density (Planck), and direct-detection bounds (XENONnT/LZ).

Result: Identifies viable thermal freeze-out solutions for both mass orderings (m_S > m_χ and m_S < m_χ) away from the Z′ resonance; finds surviving parameter space with spin-independent cross sections σ_SI ~ 10^{−47}–10^{−46} cm², within sensitivity reach of next-generation experiments like DARWIN.

Conclusion: A minimal, gauge-stabilized two-component dark matter scenario is phenomenologically viable and testable; coupled freeze-out dynamics relax direct-detection constraints significantly compared to single-component models, offering a robust pathway to probe B−L–mediated dark sectors.

Abstract: We propose an interacting two-component dark matter model based on a gauged $U(1)_{B-L}$ extension of the Standard Model, which also accommodates neutrino masses through the type-I seesaw mechanism. The dark sector consists of a Dirac fermion $\chi$ and a complex scalar $S$, which can both be stable without imposing an additional discrete symmetry and are directly coupled by a gauge-invariant Yukawa interaction. The resulting conversion, semi-conversion, and semi-annihilation processes lead to a genuinely coupled freeze-out. After imposing collider and dark matter constraints, we identify viable solutions away from the $Z'$ resonance for both mass hierarchies, $m_S > m_\chi$ and $m_S < m_\chi$. Despite the strong direct-detection constraints, part of the surviving parameter space remains within the reach of next-generation experiments.

</details>


### [78] [A microscopic stochastic field theory for nonlinear pomeron--odderon evolution based on a Doi--Peliti framework](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00268)
*Anderson Kendi Kohara*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper constructs a reaction-based microscopic model for nonlinear pomeron-odderon evolution in QCD high-energy scattering, using Doi-Peliti formalism to derive exact master equations, moment hierarchies, and functional integrals; it yields complex logistic dynamics for pomerons and pomeron-driven odderon evolution, with analytical solutions revealing nonmonotonic forward ratios and pp/p̄p splitting.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide a consistent, reaction-based microscopic foundation for nonlinear pomeron-odderon evolution that respects conservation laws and connects to observable elastic-scattering features (e.g., forward ratio, pp vs p̄p differences, diffractive minima), beyond mean-field approximations.

Method: Doi-Peliti field-theoretic formalism applied to stochastic reaction-diffusion processes in rapidity and impact-parameter space; derivation of exact master equations, factorial moment hierarchy, coherent-state path integral; imposition of algebraic conservation law on gain-loss generator; mean-field factorisation yielding complex logistic equation for pomerons and coupled odderon dynamics via catalysis and ternary fusion.

Result: Analytical solution in quadrature for evolution without transverse diffusion; emergence of nonmonotonic forward scattering ratio; channel splitting between pp and p̄p; phase-sensitive interference near diffractive minima; mapping of first moments to even/odd scattering profiles.

Conclusion: The framework provides a mathematically controlled, unitarity-compatible (though not proven) effective dynamics for high-energy hadronic scattering, capturing key nonlinear and interference phenomena absent in linear or purely mean-field approaches.

Abstract: We construct a reaction-based microscopic realisation of nonlinear pomeron--odderon evolution in rapidity and impact-parameter space. Using the Doi--Peliti formalism, we derive the master equations, their operator representation, the exact hierarchy of factorial moments, and the coherent-state functional integral. The construction admits normalised complex configuration weights while retaining the algebraic conservation law of the gain--loss generator. Pomeron branching and merging yield the complex logistic equation \cite{Kohara:2026} under mean-field factorisation. Pomeron-catalysed odderon branching and ternary odderon fusion provide an odd sector whose evolution is driven by the pomeron background. Without transverse diffusion, the resulting equations admit an analytical solution in quadrature, allowing us to examine amplification, phase evolution, and nonlinear regulation. A specified matching between the first moments and the even and odd scattering profiles connects this evolution to elastic-scattering observables.
