Illustrative initial conditions produce a nonmonotonic forward ratio and a splitting between the $pp$ and $p\bar p$ channels, while the interference near a diffractive minimum remains sensitive to the relative phases and transverse profiles. These examples illustrate the effective dynamics without constituting a fit to data or a general proof of scattering unitarity.

</details>


### [79] [Behavior of the zero modes in the Light-front for pseudo scalar particles: pion case](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.01154)
*J. Pacheco B. C. de melo*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper investigates zero-mode contributions to the pion's electromagnetic current within the Light-Front Constituent Quark Model, analyzing their frame dependence, impact on observables (form factors, radius, fπ), and parametric dependence on Q² and model parameters.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To address the explicit covariance breaking and frame dependence in light-front formalism by rigorously analyzing zero-mode contributions to the pion’s electromagnetic current and their effects on key observables.

Method: Light-Front Constituent Quark Model (LFCQM); systematic analysis of zero-mode contributions to the electromagnetic current; evaluation of electromagnetic form factors, charge radius, and weak decay constant fπ; parametric study varying momentum transfer Q², constituent quark masses m_{q,\bar{q}}, and regulator mass m_R.

Result: Zero-mode contributions exhibit strong frame dependence; maximal zero-mode effect occurs at a specific Q² = q_Z^{2(Max.)}; zero modes significantly influence electromagnetic form factors, pion radius, and f_π; all depend sensitively on m_{q,\bar{q}} and m_R.

Conclusion: Zero modes are non-negligible and frame-sensitive in LFQFT calculations of pion structure; their proper treatment is essential for quantitative predictions of electromagnetic and weak observables in light-front approaches.

Abstract: Light-front Quantum Field Theory (LFQFT) stands as an optimal framework for delving into the intricate substructure of hadronic bound states. In this study, we employ a Light-Front Constituent Quark Model (LFCQM) to investigate the pion's internal structure. This model has previously demonstrated significant success in describing the properties of pseudoscalar mesons. A crucial consideration within the light-front formalism is the dependence on the chosen reference frame, stemming from the explicit breaking of covariance. Herein, we meticulously analyze the contribution of zero modes to the pion's electromagnetic current. Subsequently, we explore the impact of these zero modes on observable quantities, including electromagnetic form factors, the pion's electromagnetic radius, and its weak decay constant, $f_\pi$. Furthermore, we comprehensively investigate the behavior of zero modes and their dependence on both the momentum transfer, $Q^2$, and the model's intrinsic parameters, such as constituent quark masses ($m_{(q,\bar{q})}$) and the regulator mass ($m_R$). Our findings reveal a substantial dependence of light-front zero modes on the specific frame employed for the calculation of the electromagnetic current. Interestingly, we observe that the maximal contribution of the zero mode occurs at a specific four-momentum transfer, denoted as $Q^2 = -q^2 = q_Z^{2(Max.)}$.

</details>


### [80] [Heavy quark thermalization using phonon-heavy quark EFT](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.01309)
*Andreas Kirchner,Berndt Mueller,Jyotirmoy Roy,Sebastian Waeber*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper develops an effective field theory (EFT) describing heavy quarks interacting with QGP phonons, computes the drag coefficient using heavy-quark expansion, calibrates unknown Wilson coefficients by matching to AdS/CFT results, and solves the Boltzmann equation to study heavy-quark thermalization via soft phonon scattering.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Heavy quarks at low momenta in the QGP are poorly described from first principles due to strong coupling and high gluon occupation; a phonon-based EFT offers a systematically improvable perturbative framework.

Method: Constructs and employs a phonon-heavy quark EFT; combines phonon-mediated scattering cross sections (from prior work) with the heavy quark expansion to compute the drag coefficient; matches to AdS/CFT drag results to fix Wilson coefficients; solves the Boltzmann equation for heavy-quark distribution evolution.

Result: Quantitative drag coefficient expressed in terms of phonon couplings; calibrated Wilson coefficients for quark-phonon interactions; Boltzmann solutions showing thermalization dynamics driven by soft phonon exchange.

Conclusion: Phonon-based EFT provides a viable, power-countable framework for low-momentum heavy quark dynamics in QGP; calibrated couplings enable predictive modeling of thermalization, bridging weak-coupling EFT and strong-coupling holographic results.

Abstract: The description of heavy quarks inside the QGP medium, especially at low momenta, remains challenging from first principles, due to large coupling strength and gluon occupation numbers. An alternative way to formulate a description in terms of perturbative degrees of freedom is the interaction of the heavy quarks with the collective excitations of the medium in the form of phonons. This description is derived as an effective field theory (EFT) and therefore allows for a natural occurring power counting of the allowed interactions. This EFT has already been established in our previous work, where we have derived the phonon-heavy quark interactions and calculated their thermal scattering cross section. In this work, we use these results, together with the heavy quark expansion, to calculate the heavy quark drag coefficient. Subsequently, we compare our results with the drag coefficient obtained from AdS/CFT calculations to determine the previously unknown Wilson coefficients for the quark-phonon coupling. Finally, we will use these coefficients to solve the Boltzmann equation for the heavy quark distribution, studying its thermalization in the QGP via soft phonon interactions.

</details>


### [81] [Second-order evolution of spin shear tensor for massive spin-1/2 particles](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.03086)
*Pushpa Panday,Samapan Bhadury,Sadhana Dash*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper derives a second-order evolution equation for the spin shear component of the spin tensor in spin-polarized fluids using classical spin description and semi-classical kinetic theory for massive spin-1/2 particles under relaxation time approximation, leading to a relaxation-type equation with new transport coefficients for dissipative spin-shear dynamics.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To formulate a consistent hydrodynamic framework for spin-polarized fluids by extending relativistic dissipative hydrodynamics to include spin degrees of freedom beyond equilibrium, particularly capturing non-equilibrium spin-shear dynamics.

Method: Classical description of spin degrees of freedom; semi-classical kinetic theory for massive spin-1/2 particles; relaxation time approximation; derivation of second-order evolution equation for the spin shear component of the spin tensor including thermodynamic force couplings and high-order gradient corrections.

Result: A relaxation-type second-order evolution equation for the spin shear tensor; identification and characterization of a new set of transport coefficients governing dissipative spin-shear dynamics.

Conclusion: The derived equation provides a foundational element for spin hydrodynamics, enabling quantitative modeling of spin transport in strongly interacting spin-polarized systems such as quark-gluon plasma or ultracold atomic gases.

Abstract: We present the second-order evolution equation for the spin shear component of the spin tensor in spin-polarized fluids, using a classical description of the spin degrees of freedom. Employing a semi-classical kinetic theory for the massive spin-1/2 particles within the relaxation time approximation we obtain a relaxation-type equation describing the evolution of spin shear driven by various thermodynamic forces and high-order gradient corrections. This equation yields a new set of transport coefficients that govern the dynamics of the dissipative spin-shear tensor.

</details>


### [82] [The quantum magic of top quarks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.03211)
*Chris D. White*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper reviews the quantum information concept of 'magic' (non-stabiliserness) and its recent applications in high-energy physics, particularly collider physics and top quark production.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore how quantum informational measures—specifically 'magic', a quantifier of non-classical computational resources—can provide new insights into high-energy physics phenomena.

Method: Conceptual review and synthesis: traces the origin of magic in quantum computation and surveys its emerging applications in high-energy contexts, especially in collider physics.

Result: Demonstrates that magic serves as a useful diagnostic for quantum complexity in scattering processes, with illustrative application to top quark production.

Conclusion: Magic is a promising tool bridging quantum information and high-energy physics, enabling characterization of genuine quantumness beyond entanglement in dynamical QFT processes.

Abstract: In recent years, a growing community of researchers has been applying ideas from Quantum Information theory to high energy physics. Entanglement is one such quantity, but there are by now many others. This contribution will examine the property of ``magic" (also known as ``non-stabliserness") which, roughly speaking, measures whether quantum systems have a genuine computational advantage over their classical counterparts. We will review the origin of this concept, before describing recent applications in collider physics, including top quark production.

</details>


### [83] [Cherenkov radiation constraint on the photon mass from a two-form gauge potential](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.03488)
*Patricio Gaete,José Helayël-Neto*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper explores massive electrodynamics—derived from $B \wedge F$ models and dualized Stueckelberg theories—with photon–3-form mixing, analyzes how photon mass modifies permittivity, susceptibility, and refractive index, and shows that Cherenkov-like radiation in media yields a photon mass bound of 1 eV using Super-Kamiokande data.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To constrain the photon mass via electromagnetic radiation in massive antisymmetric tensor theories, leveraging theoretical structure (e.g., $B \wedge F$ models, Stueckelberg dualization) and observational signatures in media.

Method: Theoretical derivation of massive electrodynamics from dualized Stueckelberg-like gauge theories with $U(1)$–3-form mixing; analysis of dispersion relations to obtain mass-dependent permittivity, susceptibility, and refractive index; computation of radiation from a charged particle moving in a medium; application to Super-Kamiokande Cherenkov data.

Result: Photon mass modifies the refractive index through altered permittivity/susceptibility; radiation exhibits Cherenkov-like behavior driven by the medium; Super-Kamiokande data yield a photon mass upper bound of 1 eV ($10^{-36}$ kg).

Conclusion: Massive electrodynamics with $U(1)$–3-form mixing provides a consistent framework for probing photon mass via medium-induced radiation; the derived bound of 1 eV is competitive with superconductivity-based limits and validates the utility of astrophysical Cherenkov measurements for fundamental parameter constraints.

Abstract: We investigate how electromagnetic radiation in massive antisymmetric tensor theories can be used to constrain the photon mass. In this context, massive electrodynamics constitutes one realization of the $B \wedge F$ class of models in $(3+1)$ dimensions and is obtained by dualizing Stueckelberg-like massive gauge theories. Notably, this model is characterized by the mixing of a $U(1)$ gauge potential with an Abelian $3$-form field of the type found in the topological sector of QCD. We then examine how mass affects permittivity and susceptibility and, consequently, modifies the refractive index through the dispersion relation. We subsequently investigate the electromagnetic radiation emitted by a moving charged particle as it interacts with a medium within the framework of massive electrodynamics. Our findings indicate that the medium through which the particle travels drives the radiation, in a manner analogous to the Cherenkov effect. Applying our results to data on Cherenkov radiation produced at Super-Kamiokande, we derive a photon mass bound of 1 eV ($ 10^ {- 36} $ kg), which is consistent with known estimates based on superconductors.

</details>


### [84] [Tests of an EP-Preserving Wake Force](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00200)
*Abhishek Banerjee,Dawid Brzeminski*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper shows that universal quadratic coupling of ultralight scalar dark matter induces composition-independent differential acceleration between concentric test masses due to mutual field distortion — a novel violation signature not suppressed by the weak equivalence principle — and reinterprets MICROSCOPE data to constrain scalar mass (< ~8×10⁻⁹ eV) and coupling strength relative to astrophysical bounds.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To identify observable signatures of ultralight scalar dark matter with universal quadratic coupling that evade standard equivalence principle tests yet remain detectable in precision experiments like MICROSCOPE.

Method: Analytical calculation of scalar field distortion induced by concentric spherical test masses under weak and stronger screening regimes; derivation of differential acceleration including mutual backreaction; application to MICROSCOPE’s SUEP (compositionally distinct) and SUREF (identical composition) sensors; incorporation of Earth-bound dark matter wake for signal modeling.

Result: Predicts measurable differential acceleration in both SUEP and SUREF sensors; recasting of MICROSCOPE results yields new constraints on scalar mass up to ~8×10⁻⁹ eV and couplings below supernova cooling bounds; suggests multi-frequency analysis could probe couplings below BBN limits.

Conclusion: Universal quadratic scalar dark matter can produce detectable, composition-independent equivalence-principle-violating signals via field distortion — challenging the assumption that universal coupling implies no EP violation for extended bodies — and existing space-based tests already constrain previously unexplored parameter space.

Abstract: A universal quadratic coupling of ultralight scalar dark matter to ordinary matter preserves the weak equivalence principle for unscreened point particles. A finite test body is nevertheless not a passive probe: it distorts the scalar field that accelerates it. We show that this field distortion produces a differential acceleration between concentric test masses, even when their microscopic coupling is universal. We calculate this signal including how each test mass distorts the field acting on the other, deriving the weak-screening limit and extending the calculation to stronger screening using concentric spherical layers. Applying the model to MICROSCOPE, we find differential signals in both its equivalence-principle sensor SUEP, whose test masses have different compositions, and its reference sensor SUREF, whose test masses have the same composition. We combine both sensors with the dark matter wake near Earth to recast the published MICROSCOPE measurements. Within this model, existing measurements probe scalar masses up to approximately $8 \times 10^{-9}\,$eV and couplings below the supernova bound. An analysis using the additional frequency components of the scalar signal could extend the mass reach and probe couplings below the BBN bound.

</details>


### [85] [Quantum Simulation of Hadronization: From String Breaking to Event Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00214)
*Christian W. Bauer,Anthony N. Ciavarella,Jad C. Halimeh,Yannick Meurice,Stephen Mrenna,Martin J. Savage,Giovanni Cataldi,Henry Froland,Dorota M. Grabowska,Sebastian Grieninger,Kenneth Heitritter,Michael Hite,Marc Illa,Saurabh V. Kadam,Henry Lamm,Zhiyao Li,Zane Ozzello,Vincent R. Pascuzzi,Blake Senseman,Kaidi Xu,Xiaojun Yao,Nikita A. Zemlevskiy*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This white paper outlines a roadmap for using quantum computers to simulate QCD hadronization—currently modeled phenomenologically in event generators—by progressively scaling up from toy gauge theory models to realistic QCD, aiming first for qualitative and later quantitative input to collider physics.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Current event generators rely on phenomenological hadronization models with parameters fitted to data, not derived from first-principles QCD; this introduces systematic uncertainties limiting LHC measurement precision. Quantum simulation offers a path to ab initio, real-time, non-perturbative modeling of hadronization.

Method: Proposes a staged, incremental approach: (1) extend existing quantum simulations of string-breaking in simplified gauge theories (e.g., 1+1D lattice gauge models), (2) define a sequence of increasingly realistic toy models bridging to 3+1D QCD, (3) identify key observables (e.g., hadron multiplicity, momentum spectra, string tension, breaking time scales), and (4) map quantum hardware constraints (qubit count, coherence, gate fidelity) to required model sizes and accuracy.

Result: A concrete roadmap is established, including prioritized toy models, target observables, identified technical challenges (e.g., fermion encoding, real-time evolution fidelity, continuum/large-volume extrapolation), and a phased deployment strategy—from qualitative guidance for event generator tuning to eventual quantitative constraints on hadronization parameters.

Conclusion: Quantum simulation of hadronization is feasible in principle but requires coordinated advances in quantum algorithms, hardware, and high-energy theory. Near-term quantum devices can already inform event generator design qualitatively; quantitative QCD predictions demand longer-term co-development across disciplines.

Abstract: High-energy collisions produce large numbers of quarks and gluons that are bound into hadrons by the confining interactions of quantum chromodynamics (QCD). This hadronization process is intrinsically real-time and non-perturbative. Event generators describe hadronization using phenomenological models with parameters fitted to data rather than calculated from QCD. Uncertainties in these models limit the precision of certain measurements at the Large Hadron Collider. Quantum computers could enable first-principles simulations of these dynamics. Existing quantum simulations of gauge theories have demonstrated string-breaking dynamics. This white paper summarizes the conclusions of the InQubator for Quantum Simulation workshop \textit{From String Dynamics to Event Generation with Quantum Simulation}. We develop a roadmap for extending these simulations to inform phenomenological models of hadronization. A sequence of toy models is proposed for connecting current quantum simulations to QCD. The observables that should be extracted are listed, and the challenges that separate present simulations from quantitative predictions are identified. We propose a staged program in which quantum simulations first provide qualitative modifications to event generators and later provide quantitative input.

</details>


### [86] [Heavy-meson molecules with dynamical pions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.01768)
*Yan-Ke Chen,Liang-Zhen Wen,Lu Meng,Shi-Lin Zhu*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper introduces a coupled Fock-space framework for heavy-meson molecules, treating the pion as dynamical and solving the full two- and three-body Schrödinger equation non-perturbatively to locate poles and extract wave functions—applied to $T_{cc}^+$, $X(3872)$, and several $B/D$-meson–pion systems, predicting new bound states and resonances.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To go beyond energy-dependent two-body approximations and consistently incorporate genuine three-body dynamics (e.g., pion emission/absorption) in heavy-meson molecular systems where constituent instability changes particle number.

Method: Constructs an energy-independent Hamiltonian coupling two-body ($H_1 H_2^*$) and three-body ($H_1 H_2 \pi$) Fock sectors; uses Gaussian expansion + complex scaling to solve the coupled Schrödinger equation as a linear generalized eigenvalue problem for pole extraction and wave function decomposition.

Result: Reproduces known poles of $T_{cc}^+$ and $X(3872)$; predicts one isoscalar bound state in $BB\pi$, $B\bar{B}\pi$, $DB\pi$, and $D\bar{B}\pi$; identifies subthreshold resonance in $DB\pi$ and suprathreshold resonance in $D\bar{B}\pi$; proposes experimental searches via $DB^* \to DB\gamma$ and $D\bar{B}\pi$ final states.

Conclusion: The coupled Fock-space approach provides a rigorous, unified treatment of meson-molecule formation with explicit multi-channel wave functions and is extensible to other few-body hadronic systems involving unstable constituents.

Abstract: We investigate heavy-meson molecules by treating the pion as a dynamical degree of freedom. We construct an energy-independent Hamiltonian that couples the two- and three-body Fock spaces. The two-body sector contains a pseudoscalar meson and a vector meson $H_1H_2^{*}$, while the three-body sector consists of two pseudoscalar mesons and a pion $H_1H_2 \pi$. The $H^*$ self-energy, the $H_1H_2^{*}$ pion-exchange interaction, and the $H_1H_2\pi$ three-body propagation are consistently generated through the coupling between the two Fock sectors. We use the Gaussian expansion and complex scaling methods to solve the coupled Schrödinger equation. Previous approaches usually reduce three-body effects to energy-dependent two-body kernels and solve the equations iteratively. Our approach obtains the poles by solving a single linear generalized eigenvalue problem. The eigenvectors also provide explicit wave functions in both Fock sectors, allowing a qualitative analysis of channel weights and spatial structure. We benchmark the framework in $T_{cc}^{+}$ and $X(3872)$. The benchmark results are consistent with the current experimental pole determinations. We then apply this formalism to the $BB\pi$, $B\bar{B}\pi$, $DB\pi$ and $D\bar{B}\pi$ systems. We predict one isoscalar bound state in each of these systems. The $DB\pi$ and $D\bar{B}\pi$ systems also contain resonances lying below and above their three-body threshold, respectively. We propose complementary searches for these resonances through $DB^*\to DB\gamma$ and $D\bar B\pi$. This coupled Fock-space framework can be readily extended to other few-body systems in which unstable constituents can decay and change the particle number.

</details>


### [87] [A General Manifestly Covariant Construction of Partial-Wave Amplitudes in the Rarita-Schwinger Formalism](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.01824)
*Hao-Jie Jing,Shu-Ming Wu,Jia-Jun Wu*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: Introduces Relative Spin Wave Functions (RSWFs) — covariant, boost-defined spin wave functions that decouple spin transport from orbital-spin coupling while retaining partial-wave angular structure; enables explicit, on-shell, Lorentz-covariant partial-wave amplitudes for arbitrary-spin two-body decays within the Rarita-Schwinger formalism.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To construct a manifestly Lorentz-covariant framework for spin-angular decomposition in multi-spin hadronic processes — particularly lacking in conventional partial-wave analyses where spin and orbital degrees of freedom are entangled under boosts.

Method: Defines Relative Spin Wave Functions (RSWFs) via covariant pure boosts to a common rest frame; embeds them in the Rarita-Schwinger formalism to derive on-shell, covariant partial-wave amplitudes for two-body decays of massive particles with arbitrary spin, labeled by definite orbital angular momentum L and total final-state spin S.

Result: Derives explicit, fully covariant, on-shell partial-wave amplitudes for arbitrary-spin decays; establishes RSWFs as a foundational Lorentz basis compatible with LS-coupling schemes, applicable to Lattice QCD and QCD sum rules.

Conclusion: RSWFs provide a rigorous, manifestly covariant generalization of partial-wave analysis that cleanly separates spin and orbital dynamics, enabling consistent LS-organized operator construction and amplitude modeling in relativistic hadron physics.

Abstract: We propose a new set of functions, named Relative Spin Wave Functions (RSWFs), defined via covariant pure boosts to a common rest frame. RSWFs separate spin transport from orbital-spin coupling while preserving the conventional partial-wave angular structure in a manifestly covariant form. Within the Rarita-Schwinger formalism, this approach generates explicit, covariant on-shell partial-wave amplitudes for two-body decays of massive particles of arbitrary spin, with definite orbital angular momentum and total final-state spin. This general framework provides a foundational Lorentz basis for hadronic reaction studies and $LS$-organized operators in theoretical investigations, such as Lattice QCD and QCD sum rules.

</details>


### [88] [Unitarity and Positivity Constraints on C- or P-odd Anomalous Quartic Couplings](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00133)
*Oscar J. P. Éboli,Álvaro Lozano-Onrubia,Samuel Rosende Herrero*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper derives new unitarity and positivity bounds on dimension-eight anomalous quartic gauge couplings in SMEFT, completing coverage to all 34 operators—including CP-odd ones—and showing CP-odd coefficients require non-zero CP-even contributions to satisfy positivity; combined constraints tighten bounds on 23 Wilson coefficients.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To fully constrain the dimension-eight genuine aQGC sector of SMEFT by extending partial-wave unitarity and forward-limit positivity analyses to include all C- or P-odd (i.e., CP-odd) operators—previously omitted—thereby closing a gap in the theoretical consistency bounds.

Method: Analytical derivation of partial-wave unitarity constraints extended to all 34 dimension-eight aQGC operators (including CP-odd ones); derivation of forward-limit positivity bounds incorporating all CP-odd Wilson coefficients using dispersion relations and crossing symmetry; systematic combination of both sets of constraints.

Result: First complete set of partial-wave unitarity bounds for all 34 aQGC operators; first analytical forward-limit positivity bounds including CP-odd coefficients; demonstration that non-zero CP-odd coefficients necessitate non-zero CP-even contributions to satisfy positivity; tightened numerical bounds on 23 of the 34 Wilson coefficients when combining both constraints.

Conclusion: The combined unitarity and positivity framework significantly enhances the constraining power on the dimension-eight aQGC sector, reveals an intrinsic coupling between CP-odd and CP-even sectors, and provides a more complete and rigorous consistency test for SMEFT parameter space.

Abstract: In this work, we provide a comprehensive study of partial-wave unitarity and positivity constraints in the dimension-eight genuine anomalous quartic gauge coupling (aQGC) sector of the Standard Model Effective Field Theory (SMEFT). We extend previous partial-wave unitarity analyses to include all C- or P-odd operators, thereby completing the coverage of the full basis of 34 operators. We also derive an analytical set of necessary forward-limit positivity bounds that include all CP-odd Wilson coefficients. We demonstrate that non-vanishing CP-odd Wilson coefficients require non-vanishing contributions from the CP-even sector to satisfy the derived positivity conditions. Combining the positivity conditions with partial-wave unitarity, we obtain stronger bounds on twenty-three of the thirty-four Wilson coefficients in this sector of the SMEFT.

</details>


### [89] [Heavy quarkonium dynamics beyond the binding energy over temperature expansion](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00194)
*Nora Brambilla,Arthur Yi-Ting Lin,Tom Magorsch,Antonio Vairo*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper revisits the pNRQCD open-quantum-system description of bottomonium in quark-gluon plasma, deriving a spherical-basis master equation without binding-energy-over-temperature expansion; it introduces an optimized positive-sector Lindblad approximation for efficient QTraj simulation, treats negativity as systematic uncertainty, and computes in-medium widths and Bjorken-evolved survival probabilities for Υ(1S,2S,3S) across the full heavy-ion collision temperature range.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To overcome limitations of conventional transport-coefficient-based approaches and positivity-breaking issues in existing open-quantum-system descriptions of quarkonium in QGP, enabling reliable, first-principles quantum-trajectory simulations across the full relevant temperature range.

Method: Derives a spherical-basis pNRQCD master equation retaining Born/Markov/dilute approximations but avoiding binding-energy-over-temperature expansion; uses Coulombic Green's function–chromoelectric correlator convolutions; applies optimal operator rotations to minimize negative jump-operator contributions; constructs a pseudo-Lindblad positive-sector approximation solvable with QTraj; quantifies discarded negative sector as systematic uncertainty; employs infrared- and UV-constrained spectral model for chromoelectric correlators.

Result: First large-scale quantum-trajectory simulations of bottomonium (Υ(1S,2S,3S)) across the full QGP temperature range; computed in-medium widths and Bjorken-evolved survival probabilities; demonstrated convergence of binding-energy-over-temperature expansion at high T; quantified positivity-breaking effects as controllable systematic uncertainty.

Conclusion: The positive-sector pseudo-Lindblad framework enables numerically stable, physically interpretable, and systematically improvable open-quantum-system simulations of bottomonium suppression in heavy-ion collisions, bridging high- and low-temperature regimes where prior methods fail.

Abstract: We revisit the pNRQCD open-quantum-system description of bottomonium in a quark-gluon plasma, writing the full master equation in a spherical basis and establishing a framework for its numerical treatment. The equation does not rely on an expansion in the ratio of the binding energy over the temperature, while retaining the Born, Markov, and dilute approximations. It depends on suitable convolutions of Coulombic Green's functions with real-time chromoelectric correlators, rather than a finite set of transport coefficients. It contains jump-operator contributions that enter with both positive and negative signs, so it is not of a Lindblad type. We utilize optimal operator rotations to minimize the negative sector and define a positive-sector approximation that retains only the optimized positive terms, obtaining in this way a Lindblad equation that can be simulated efficiently with the open-source code QTraj for arbitrary chromoelectric spectral functions. We treat the discarded sector as a systematic uncertainty associated with positivity-breaking effects of the pseudo-Lindblad generator. Positivity breaking effects can be attributed to the approximations made in the derivation of the quarkonium master equation, such as the Born--Markov approximations. Using a spectral model constrained by known infrared and ultraviolet limits, we compute the in-medium width and Bjorken-evolved survival probabilities of the $\Upsilon(1\mathrm{S},2\mathrm{S},3\mathrm{S})$. The computations show the convergence of the binding-energy-over-temperature expansion at high temperatures and provide the first large-scale quantum-trajectory simulation results for bottomonium in the low temperature regime. We can thus follow the bottomonium evolution over the full temperature range relevant in heavy-ion collisions.

</details>


### [90] [From High Energy Astrophysical Neutrinos to the Nobel : The History, Legacy and Science Behind the 2026 Nobel Prize in Physics](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00239)
*Dr. Maibam Ricky Devi*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This article recounts the development of the world's largest neutrino telescope—originating from AMANDA—and highlights Francis Halzen’s visionary leadership and persistent efforts that culminated in breakthroughs in high-energy neutrino astronomy, earning him the 2026 Nobel Prize in Physics.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To document the scientific vision, perseverance, and collaborative engineering effort behind transforming Antarctic ice into a high-energy neutrino observatory, thereby establishing neutrino astronomy as a new observational window to the universe.

Method: Historical narrative and tribute-based exposition, focusing on the evolution from AMANDA to IceCube, emphasizing conceptual innovation, site selection (South Pole ice), photodetector deployment, and large-scale international collaboration.

Result: Successful realization of IceCube—the world's largest neutrino telescope—enabling detection of astrophysical neutrinos and inaugurating neutrino astronomy as a viable field.

Conclusion: The AMANDA-to-IceCube journey exemplifies how bold scientific ambition, sustained determination, and innovative engineering can open entirely new observational windows in astrophysics, leaving a lasting inspirational legacy.

Abstract: This article briefly discusses the journey behind the discovery of the high-energy astrophysical neutrinos and the making of the world's largest neutrino telescope that opened new portal to neutrino astronomy. It all started with the AMANDA experiment and how the strong conviction by Prof. Francis Halzen to transform a huge block of Antarctic ice into the largest and the most successful neutrino detector in the world and to work on it with relentless grit and determination to make it come true since Day 1 of the AMANDA project, led him to win the 2026 Nobel Prize in Physics. This is the story of some great scientists and engineers who dared to dream the impossible and made it happen through bold decisions, bold ideas, and innovative engineering heralding a new branch of astronomy. This leaves an enduring legacy of inspiration for future generations of aspiring scientists, who will look up to these scientific pioneers as role models and strive to follow in their footsteps.

</details>


### [91] [Proposal for the resolution of the disagreement between DAMA and other Underground Dark Matter Experiments](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00306)
*C. D. Froggatt,H.B.Nielsen*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: Proposes that dark matter becomes excited during passage through rock shielding, explaining DAMA/LIBRA’s positive signal (due to deeper location and longer excitation time) while xenon-based experiments—sensitive mainly to nuclear recoils—remain null; excited dark matter predominantly emits electrons, evading xenon detectors.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve the long-standing discrepancy between DAMA/LIBRA’s persistent annual modulation signal (interpreted as dark matter) and null results from xenon-based (e.g., LUX, XENON) and other NaI(Tl)-based (ANAIS, COSINE-100) experiments using the same target material.

Method: Hypothesizes an excitation mechanism for dark matter particles traversing geological overburden; leverages depth-dependent transit time differences (DAMA ~1.4 km.w.e. vs. ANAIS/COSINE ~0.7–0.9 km.w.e.) to explain enhanced signal in DAMA; assumes excited-state decays dominantly to electrons (not nuclear recoils), rendering them invisible to xenon recoil-based searches.

Result: Provides a qualitative consistency argument: deeper site → longer excitation time → larger excited DM flux → observable electron-mediated signal in NaI(Tl); xenon experiments remain insensitive due to recoil-only thresholds; rare nuclear-recoil events (e.g., LUX/ZEPPELIN single event) may arise as subdominant decay channels.

Conclusion: The depth-dependent excitation hypothesis offers a testable explanation for the DAMA anomaly without contradicting other direct detection limits, contingent on DM having metastable excited states coupled preferentially to electrons.

Abstract: It is a great mystery still needing a solution that the underground experiments looking for dark matter disagree, in tIt is a great mystery still needing a solution that the underground experiments looking for dark matter disagree, in the sense that DAMA/LIBRA has found dark matter with a confidence level of several standard deviations, while neither the xenon based experiments nor even the experiments using the same target material NaI(Tl) as DAMA see the same amount of dark matter. We propose to solve this enigma by postulating that the dark matter gets excited during its passage through the mountain shielding from outer space down to the experimental halls; the DAMA experiment is namely about twice as deep as the ANAIS and COSINE-100 experiments, so that there is longer passage time for DAMA, which thus gets more excited dark matter particles. The xenon-scintillator experiments have their strong limits for dark matter making itself felt via nucleus recoils, and thus we can just assume that the excited dark matter mainly emits e.g. electrons, and the xenon-experiments would hardly see such dark matter. The single event recently seen by LUX/ZEPPELIN could be due to a low rate of nucleus emission in addition to the hypothesised electrons.

</details>


### [92] [Top quark couplings at the muon collider](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00349)
*Nicolás Urcelay,Antonio O. Bouzas,F. Larios*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper evaluates the sensitivity of a muon collider to top-quark electroweak couplings via dimension-six Warsaw-basis operators, focusing on six of nine relevant operators. It derives 95% CL limits on Wilson coefficients across center-of-mass energies (3–14 TeV), using six top-production channels; finds significant improvements over current LHC bounds—up to an order of magnitude—for dipole, ttZ, tbW, and tth couplings.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To assess the unique capability of a muon collider—compared to the LHC—to constrain flavor-diagonal electroweak top-quark couplings encoded in dimension-six SMEFT operators, especially where existing bounds are weak or model-dependent.

Method: Uses leading-order SMEFT predictions in the Warsaw basis; computes statistical (2σ cross-section) and systematic uncertainties; analyzes six top-quark production processes (ttbar, tth, ttνν, ttμμ, single-top t b-bar μ ν-bar, t h μ ν-bar) across √s = 3, 6, 10, 14 TeV; extracts 95% CL limits on six Wilson coefficients by profiling likelihoods per channel.

Result: Muon collider achieves up to ~6× tighter bounds on electroweak dipole couplings (vs. LHC) at 3–6 TeV, and >10× at 10–14 TeV; improves ttZ and tbW coupling limits by 1.5–10×; tth constraints become competitive only at √s ≥ 6 TeV; strongest channels identified per operator (e.g., s-channel tt/tth for dipoles, ttνν for ttZ/tth, single-top for tbW).

Conclusion: A muon collider offers substantially enhanced sensitivity to top-quark electroweak structure beyond the SM, particularly for dipole and gauge/Yukawa couplings, establishing it as a powerful future probe of top-sector EFT physics; results validate prior studies and guide detailed experimental and theoretical follow-ups.

Abstract: We present an overview of the sensitivity of a muon collider to the flavor-diagonal electroweak couplings of the top quark, using dimension-six operators of the Warsaw basis. Of the nine fermion-boson operators involving the top quark, the bounds on three are not competitive with existing limits, and we focus on the remaining six. We estimate individual $95\%$ CL limits on the corresponding Wilson coefficients at $\sqrt{s} = 3$, $6$, $10$, and $14$~TeV from $2\sigma$ statistical cross-section uncertainties, and assess the effect of an assumed systematic uncertainty. We consider six top-quark production modes: $t\bar t$, $t \bar t h$, $t \bar t \nu \bar \nu$, $t \bar t \mu^+ \mu^-$, single-top $t\bar b \mu^- \bar \nu_\mu$, and single-top-plus-Higgs, the last three for several rapidity coverages of the final-state muons. The $s$-channel $t\bar t$ and $t\bar t h$ processes give the strongest sensitivity to the electroweak dipole couplings, with allowed intervals narrower than the current LHC ones by factors of about $3$ to $6$ at $\sqrt{s} = 3$ and $6$ TeV, and by an order of magnitude or more at $10$ and $14$ TeV. The left- and right-handed $ttZ$ couplings, as well as the Yukawa $tth$ couplings (for $\sqrt{s} \geq 6$ TeV), are best probed in $t \bar t \nu \bar \nu$ production, while the right-handed $tbW$ coupling is best probed in single-top production. For the $ttZ$ and $tbW$ couplings, the limits improve on the current LHC bounds by factors from about $1.5$ to more than an order of magnitude. Our results agree with previous analyses of the Yukawa and the dipole couplings, and provide a roadmap for more in-depth studies.

</details>


### [93] [Exploring the properties of low-lying excited $Ξ_c$ and $Ξ_c'$ baryons](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00666)
*Yi-Heng Wang,Xin-Yu Chang,Hui-Hua Zhong,Li-Ye Xiao*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper calculates mass spectra and strong decays of excited Ξc baryons (1S, 1P, 1D waves) to assign quantum numbers and internal structures to recently observed states (e.g., Ξc(2790)–Ξc(3123)), proposing specific J^P, orbital configurations (λ/ρ), and optimal decay channels for experimental confirmation.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To interpret the internal structure of experimentally observed but theoretically ambiguous excited Ξc baryon states, leveraging growing experimental data and providing testable predictions for future searches.

Method: Systematic calculation of mass spectra using a quark model framework, combined with analysis of strong decay properties (e.g., Ξcπ, Ξc′π, ΛD, ΣD) via hadronic transition operators; assignments rely on matching predicted masses and decay patterns to observed resonances.

Result: Specific quantum number and configuration assignments are made for eight Ξc states: Ξc(2790)/Ξc(2815) as 1P λ-states (J^P = 1/2^−, 3/2^−); Ξc(2882) as 1P ρ-states; Ξc(2923)/Ξc(2939)/Ξc(2965) as 1P λ-excited Ξc′ states; Ξc(3055)/Ξc(3080) as 1D λλ Ξc states; and Ξc(3123) as a candidate 1D λλ Ξc′ state—supported by predicted dominant decay modes.

Conclusion: The study provides coherent theoretical interpretations for multiple excited Ξc states, clarifies their underlying quark configurations (λ vs. ρ excitations, ground vs. radially excited diquark), and identifies key decay channels (e.g., Ξcπ, Ξc′π, ΛD, ΣD) to guide future experiments and distinguish between competing assignments.

Abstract: Motivated by the rapidly accumulating experimental data, we systematically calculate the $\Xi_c$ and $\Xi_c'$ mass spectra for the $1S$-, $1P$-, and $1D$-wave states and investigate the strong decay properties of the $1P$- and $1D$-wave states, thereby interpreting the internal structure of the currently controversial excited states and providing useful guidance for future experimental searches. The main conclusions are summarized as follows. $\Xi_c(2790)$ and $\Xi_c(2815)$ can be well explained as $\Xi_c1P\big|J^P=1/2^-,1\big\rangle_{\lambda}$ and $\Xi_c1P\big|J^P=3/2^-,1\big\rangle_{\lambda}$, respectively. $\Xi_c(2882)$ may correspond to $\Xi_c1P\big|J^P=1/2^-,0\big\rangle_{\rho}$ or $\Xi_c1P\big|J^P=1/2^-,1\big\rangle_{\rho}$; under this interpretation, $\Xi_c\pi$ and $\Xi_c'\pi$ would be promising channels for its detection. $\Xi_c(2923)$ might be assigned to $\Xi_c'1P\big|J^P=3/2^-,1\big\rangle_{\lambda}$, but further confirmation is required. $\Xi_c(2939)$ is very likely to be $\Xi_c'1P\big|J^P=3/2^-,2\big\rangle_{\lambda}$ and has a good potential to be observed in the final states $\Xi_c\pi$ and $\Xi_c'^{*}\pi$. $\Xi_c(2965)$ can be explained as $\Xi_c'1P\big|J^P=5/2^-,2\big\rangle_{\lambda}$ and $\Xi_c\pi$ decay channel is a promising additional mode for future experimental searches. $\Xi_c(3055)$ and $\Xi_c(3080)$ can be assigned as the quark configurations $\Xi_c1D\big|J^P=3/2^+,2\big\rangle_{\lambda\lambda}$ and $\Xi_c1D\big|J^P=5/2^+,2\big\rangle_{\lambda\lambda}$, respectively. $\Xi_c(3123)$ is possibly state $\Xi_c'1D\big|J^P=1/2^+,1\big\rangle_{\lambda\lambda}$ or $\Xi_c'1D\big|J^P=5/2^+,3\big\rangle_{\lambda\lambda}$, with the latter being more favorable. Decay channels $\Lambda/\Sigma D$ are very likely to serve as a criterion for distinguishing between these two possible candidates.

</details>


### [94] [Sensitivity of the IceCube atmospheric muon rate to decadal trends in the effective atmospheric temperature](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00918)
*Tanvi Krishnan,Gabriela Barenboim,Carlos A. Argüelles*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper evaluates IceCube's potential to detect decadal stratospheric temperature trends in Antarctica using atmospheric muon rates, finding that detector stability (≤0.3% per decade) is the limiting factor—not statistics—and that ~30 years of data would be needed to resolve the observed ~0.9 K/decade cooling trend.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To address the scarcity of independent long-term atmospheric measurements over the Antarctic interior, and to exploit IceCube’s muon rate as a proxy for effective atmospheric temperature (T_eff), sensitive to stratospheric changes linked to ozone recovery and polar circulation shifts.

Method: The authors model IceCube’s muon rate using atmospheric reanalysis data (e.g., ERA5), cosmic-ray, solar, and geomagnetic variability to reproduce seasonal modulation; they then assess whether this model extrapolates to decadal T_eff trends, quantifying systematic limits—especially detector stability—and estimating statistical sensitivity via atmospheric variability in a 14-year record.

Result: Detector stability is the dominant constraint: an unmodeled 1%/decade muon rate drift mimics a 2.8 K/decade T_eff trend; thus stability must be ≤0.3%/decade. Snow accumulation effects can be constrained within this budget. The 14-year record yields a one-sided 95% CL statistical sensitivity of 1.6 K/decade; detecting the observed ~0.9 K/decade cooling requires ~30 years of data.

Conclusion: IceCube muon rates are a viable, albeit stability-limited, probe of Antarctic stratospheric temperature trends; achieving robust detection of current climate-scale trends demands multi-decadal monitoring and stringent control of instrumental drift.

Abstract: Decadal changes in the Antarctic stratosphere, generally associated with ozone recovery and shifts in polar circulation, are of broad geophysical interest. However, independent long-term measurements of the atmosphere above the Antarctic interior are scarce. We assess whether the atmospheric muon rate recorded by IceCube could constrain decadal changes in the effective atmospheric temperature, $T_\mathrm{eff}$, a weighted average of temperature through the atmosphere that is most sensitive to the stratosphere. Using public atmospheric reanalysis data and records of cosmic-ray, solar, and geomagnetic variability, we reproduce the measured seasonal response of the IceCube muon rate. We then test whether the same response applies to long-term temperature changes with a different vertical temperature profile. The limiting requirement is detector stability rather than counting statistics: an unmodeled muon rate drift of $1\%\,\mathrm{decade^{-1}}$ would mimic a temperature trend of $2.8\,\mathrm{K\,decade^{-1}}$. The long-term rate must therefore be stable to approximately $0.3\%\,\mathrm{decade^{-1}}$. Within this budget, snow accumulation contributions can be constrained. For a 14-year record, atmospheric variability gives a one-sided 95% CL statistical sensitivity of $1.6\,\mathrm{K\,decade^{-1}}$. Given this sensitivity, the $\sim0.9\,\mathrm{K\,decade^{-1}}$ cooling indicated by the 2011-2025 atmospheric records would become distinguishable after approximately 30 years of measurement.

</details>


### [95] [From Kination to Detection: Scotogenic Scalar Dark Matter](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00930)
*Eung Jin Chun,Ranjeet Kumar,Sanjoy Mandal,Abhishek Roy*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper explores scalar dark matter in the scotogenic model under early kination domination, finding that enhanced expansion raises viable DM mass range to 200 GeV–1 TeV, relaxes co-annihilation requirements, and yields testable indirect, direct, and collider signatures.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To reconcile scalar dark matter in the scotogenic model with observed relic abundance by considering non-standard cosmological expansion (kination-dominated epoch), which alters freeze-out dynamics and expands viable parameter space.

Method: Analytical and numerical computation of thermal freeze-out in a kination-dominated universe; integration of Boltzmann equations for scalar DM; evaluation of constraints from current indirect detection (e.g., gamma-ray searches) and projections for CTA, direct detection (e.g., XENONnT, DARWIN), and collider observables (HL-LHC, lepton colliders).

Result: Relic abundance increases due to earlier freeze-out, enabling scalar DM masses 200 GeV–1 TeV; co-annihilation with singlet fermion becomes less necessary; (100–200) GeV region is excluded by current indirect searches; remaining parameter space is testable via CTA, next-gen direct detection, and collider signatures (MET, displaced leptons, disappearing tracks).

Conclusion: Kination domination significantly broadens the viable scalar dark matter mass window in the scotogenic model and generates distinctive multi-messenger phenomenology, making it highly testable across astroparticle and collider experiments.

Abstract: We investigate scalar dark matter in the scotogenic model during an early kination-dominated epoch. The enhanced expansion rate causes earlier freeze-out and increases the relic abundance, opening viable dark matter masses from approximately $200~\mathrm{GeV}$ to $1~\mathrm{TeV}$ while reducing the need for co-annihilation with a nearly degenerate singlet fermion. Current indirect searches strongly constrain the $(100-200)~\mathrm{GeV}$ region, whereas much of the surviving parameter space can be probed by CTA and next-generation direct detection experiments. The viable spectra also lead to complementary collider signatures, including missing transverse energy, displaced leptons, and disappearing charged tracks, providing promising tests at the HL-LHC and future lepton colliders.

</details>


### [96] [Gravitational form factors of heavy quarkonia and their nonrelativistic expansion in a self-consistent light-front quark model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00952)
*Ho-Meoyng Choi,Chueng-Ryong Ji,Hyeon-Dong Son*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper computes gravitational form factors A_q(t) and D_q(t) of pseudoscalar heavy quarkonia (η_c, η_b) in a light-front quark model using physical radial wave functions as orthogonal mixtures of 1S and 2S harmonic-oscillator states; it validates consistency between relativistic and nonrelativistic (O(v²)) calculations, reveals leading-order D-term contributions from 2S admixture, and quantifies flavor and radial-structure effects on mass and stress distributions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To reliably extract gravitational form factors—key probes of hadron mass and mechanical structure—from heavy quarkonia, requiring consistent relativistic treatment and realistic wave functions beyond pure Gaussian approximations.

Method: Light-front quark model with physical radial wave functions expressed as orthogonal mixtures of φ_{1S} and φ_{2S} harmonic-oscillator basis states; Bakamjian–Thomas prescription applied at integrand level for constituent matrix elements and Lorentz prefactors; A_q(t) extracted from T_q^{++}, T_q^{+i}, T_q^{ij}; D_q(t) extracted from T_q^{+-}, T_q^{-i}, T_q^{ii} via momentum-transfer projections in the Δ⁺=0 frame.

Result: Independent extractions of A_q(t) and D_q(t) agree fully relativistically and analytically up to O(v²); D_q(0) for pure 1S is −v² + O(v⁴), for pure 2S is −2/3 − (13/3)v² + O(v⁴), and cross-term contributes −(4/√6)v² + O(v⁴); nonzero 2S admixture yields an O(v⁰) D-term in physical ground state; numerical results show distinct flavor (c vs. b) and radial-structure dependencies in mass and stress distributions.

Conclusion: The D-term of physical η_c and η_b ground states is dominated by 2S admixture rather than pure 1S component, implying sensitivity to radial excitations even at t=0; consistent relativistic treatment confirms robustness of gravitational form factor extraction; results establish that internal stress distributions encode detailed information about both heavy-quark flavor and wave-function composition.

Abstract: We investigate the gravitational form factors of the pseudoscalar heavy quarkonia $\eta_c(1S,2S)$ and $\eta_b(1S,2S)$ in a light-front quark model with physical radial wave functions represented by orthogonal mixtures of the two lowest harmonic-oscillator basis states $\phi_{1S}$ and $\phi_{2S}$. The Bakamjian--Thomas prescription is applied consistently to constituent matrix elements and Lorentz prefactors at the integrand level. Working in the $\Delta^+=0$ frame with $\Delta=P'-P$ and $t=\Delta^2$, we extract $A_q(t)$ from the quark energy--momentum tensor components $T_q^{++}$, $T_q^{+i}$, and $T_q^{ij}$, and $D_q(t)$ from $T_q^{+-}$, $T_q^{-i}$, and $T_q^{ii}$ using appropriate momentum-transfer projections. The independent extractions agree in the full relativistic calculation and in the analytic nonrelativistic expansions through $ O(v^2)$, where $v=\beta/m_Q$, $\beta$ is the Gaussian width, and $m_Q$ is the constituent mass. At zero momentum transfer, the expansions give $D_q^{\phi_{1S}}(0)=-v^2+ O(v^4)$ and $D_q^{\phi_{2S}}(0)=-2/3-(13/3)v^2+O(v^4)$. The interference, defined as the sum of the two cross overlaps, first contributes as $D_q^{\mathrm{cross}}(0)=-(4/\sqrt{6})v^2+O(v^4)$. Thus, the pure Gaussian $1S$ contribution is suppressed, whereas a nonzero $2S$ admixture at fixed mixing angle leaves an $O(v^0)$ D-term in the physical ground state. Full numerical results for the mixed states illustrate the effects of heavy-quark flavor and radial structure on mass distributions and internal stresses.

</details>


### [97] [Source shapes in coordinate and momentum space in $\sqrt{s_\text{NN}}$ = 200 GeV Au+Au collisions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00996)
*Emese Árpási,László Kovács,Dániel Kincses,Máté Csanád*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper performs a 3D femtoscopic analysis of particle-source shapes in 200 GeV Au+Au collisions using EPOS simulations, fitting the full 3D correlation function C(q) instead of 1D projections; it finds reduced Lévy indices (α), consistent direction-averaged Lévy scale and correlation strength (except for kaons), and improved agreement with PHENIX pion data above 20% centrality.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Experimental observations that particle source functions are well described by Lévy distributions motivate more realistic, three-dimensional femtoscopic analyses to better constrain space-time emission geometry and compare with data.

Method: Three-dimensional reconstruction and direct fitting of the correlation function C(q) for pion, kaon, and proton pairs from EPOS-simulated √s_NN = 200 GeV Au+Au collisions; full 3D Lévy–stable source fits (vs. prior 1D projections of D(ρ_j)), with comparison to PHENIX experimental data.

Result: 3D fitting yields smaller Lévy index α values compared to 1D projection fits; direction-averaged Lévy scale and correlation strength remain stable across pair types except kaons; pion α values show improved agreement with PHENIX data above 20% centrality.

Conclusion: Direct 3D fitting of C(q) provides more accurate and experimentally faithful constraints on Lévy source parameters, enhancing the reliability of femtoscopic interpretations of heavy-ion collision dynamics.

Abstract: In high-energy heavy-ion physics, femtoscopy aims to explore and understand the space-time characteristics of the collisions through investigating the correlation function of particle pairs. In recent years, experimental measurements have shown that the shape of the particle source function can be well described by the Lévy distribution, motivating further phenomenological studies. In this work, we perform a three-dimensional analysis of the source shapes for different particle-pair types in $\sqrt{s_\text{NN}}=200$ GeV Au+Au collisions generated with the EPOS event generator. In contrast to our previous analyses, where we fitted the one-dimensional projections of the pair distribution function, $D(\rho_j)$ ($j \in \{\text{out}, \text{side}, \text{long}\}$), now we reconstruct the three-dimensional $C(\boldsymbol{q})$ correlation function and fit it directly, as in experimental analyses. Furthermore, we now apply full three-dimensional fits instead of fitting the one-dimensional projections. We find that this approach results in smaller Lévy index values, while the direction averaged Lévy scale and the correlation strength remain largely unchanged, except for kaons. In the case of pions, the $\alpha$ values are closer to the PHENIX data, showing a good agreement above 20\% centrality.

</details>


### [98] [Exothermic Higgs portal: from LUX-ZEPLIN recoil to Gamma Rays](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.01158)
*Subhojit Roy,Jure Zupan*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: Proposes a minimal Higgs-portal two-state scalar dark matter model (φ₁, φ₂) with ~100 GeV mass and ~few-hundred-keV splitting to explain LZ nuclear-recoil excess via exothermic scattering; predicts testable gamma-ray signals (sub-MeV from φ₂→φ₁γγ, GeV continuum from φ₁φ₂ annihilation) and evades CMB/Sun-capture constraints.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide a viable thermal relic explanation for the LUX-ZEPLIN (LZ) nuclear-recoil candidate without relying on the high-velocity tail of the DM velocity distribution—as required in endothermic models—by invoking inelastic exothermic scattering from nearly degenerate scalars.

Method: Extends the Standard Model with a single complex scalar φ; introduces Higgs-portal couplings and mass terms that split φ into two real scalars φ₁ and φ₂ with small mass splitting (~hundreds of keV); computes thermal relic abundance, exothermic nuclear scattering rates (for LZ), and indirect detection signatures (γγ decay, annihilation continuum), while checking CMB and solar capture constraints.

Result: The model yields O(1) expected events in LZ for m_{φ₁,φ₂} ≈ 100 GeV and δm ≈ few × 100 keV; predicts distinctive indirect signals: a broad sub-MeV photon line from φ₂ → φ₁γγ (testable by INTEGRAL/SPI, COSI) and a GeV–100 GeV continuum from φ₁φ₂ annihilation (constrained by Fermi-LAT dwarf observations); satisfies Planck CMB limits and suppresses solar capture annihilation.

Conclusion: A minimal thermal Higgs-portal scalar doublet with small mass splitting provides a consistent, testable, and phenomenologically rich explanation of the LZ anomaly, linking direct, indirect, and cosmological observables in a unified framework.

Abstract: We show that a minimal extension of the Standard Model (SM) by a single complex scalar $\phi$ provides a viable inelastic exothermic, thermal relic, interpretation of the recently reported nuclear-recoil candidate from LUX-ZEPLIN (LZ). The mass terms and Higgs-portal interactions split $\phi$ into two nearly degenerate real scalars, $\phi_{1,2}$. For mass-splitting of a few hundred keV, $\phi_2$ is cosmologically long-lived and the two states have comparable relic abundances. Taking $\phi_{1,2}$ masses to be $\sim100\,$GeV, with off-diagonal Higgs coupling fixed by relic abundance, the exothermic down-scattering yields an order-one event expectation for the LZ exposure, without relying on the high-velocity tail of the dark matter velocity distribution required in endothermic scenarios. This scenario can be tested using different nuclear targets and with indirect dark matter searches: $\phi_2\to\phi_1\gamma\gamma$ decays give a broad sub-MeV photon continuum that can be probed by INTEGRAL/SPI and COSI; $\phi_1\phi_2$ annihilation produces a GeV-scale continuum, extending up to $\sim$100 GeV, that is probed by Fermi-LAT observations of dwarf galaxies; current CMB constraints are satisfied and annihilation among dark matter particles captured in the Sun is strongly suppressed. Next-generation experiments could probe correlated scattering, decay, and annihilation signatures of this thermal Higgs-portal scenario.

</details>


### [99] [Lightest-mass suppression and weaker Majorana cancellation in Froggatt--Nielsen textures](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.01305)
*Boliang Yu,Ruixin Zhou*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper analyzes how the effective Majorana mass—central to neutrinoless double-beta decay—is shaped by interference among three complex electron-flavour contributions, comparing seesaw-based and direct mass-generation models; it quantifies how amplitude–phase coherence, especially solar-pair interference and spectral texture, systematically shifts median effective masses and demonstrates that independent uniform phases inflate predictions by 29%, revealing irreducible joint amplitude–phase structure in the neutrino mass matrix.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how the effective Majorana mass (|m_ββ|) emerges not just from neutrino masses and mixing but from coherent interference of complex amplitudes—and to assess whether standard parameterizations (e.g., using only masses and |U_ei|² weights) discard essential phase–amplitude correlations encoded in realistic mass-generation mechanisms like the right-handed neutrino seesaw.

Method: Statistical analysis of matrix ensembles: compares a truncated-normal benchmark with normal neutrino mass ordering under direct vs. seesaw-generated mass spectra; applies reweighting to isolate interference effects; uses fixed-amplitude phase-exchange controls and conditional phase law testing; evaluates texture-dependent trade-offs between amplitude suppression and cancellation strength in mean-square |m_ββ|.

Result: In the normal-ordering benchmark, seesaw generation reduces lightest-neutrino mass median by 3.2× but increases |m_ββ| median by 13%; reweighting reveals >90% of the mean-square increase stems from weakened destructive interference between solar-pair terms (i.e., m₁|U_e1|² and m₂|U_e2|²); fixed-amplitude phase exchange confirms response follows conditional phase law; alternate texture shows smaller amplitudes can dominate over improved cancellation; replacing induced phases with uniform phases (keeping posterior masses and |U_ei|² fixed) raises direct |m_ββ| median by 29%.

Conclusion: The effective Majorana mass is not determined solely by neutrino masses and electron-flavour PMNS weights—it depends critically on coherent, model-dependent amplitude–phase correlations. Standard phenomenological treatments that marginalize over phases or assume uniformity discard structurally significant information; accurate predictions require preserving the joint distribution of amplitudes and phases generated by underlying mass mechanisms.

Abstract: The effective Majorana mass is the length of a sum of three complex electron-flavour contributions. A mass-generation model determines both their sizes and how they cancel. We compare direct effective coefficients with a three-right-handed-neutrino seesaw measure at fixed Froggatt--Nielsen charges and common lepton conditions. In the normal-ordering benchmark truncated-normal ensemble, seesaw generation lowers the lightest-mass median by a factor of 3.2 while raising the effective-mass median by 13\%. A comparison reweighted to a common lightest-mass distribution locates nearly all of the coherent mean-square increase in weaker destructive solar-pair interference. In a fixed-amplitude phase-exchange control, the response follows the conditional phase law. A second texture exposes the competing spectral effect: smaller amplitudes can outweigh weaker cancellation in the original mean square. The interference information also affects how a prediction can be used. Replacing induced phases by independent uniform phases at unchanged posterior masses and electron weights raises the benchmark direct median by 29\%. The matrix ensemble therefore predicts a joint arrangement of amplitudes and phases whose coherent information is lost when only masses and electron-flavour weights are retained.

</details>


### [100] [Resolving the Two-Fold Ambiguity in Tau Momentum Reconstruction via a Self-Consistent Weighting Method](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.01533)
*Rong-Gang Ping*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper addresses kinematic ambiguity in reconstructing τ lepton momenta in e⁺e⁻ → τ⁺τ⁻ events due to undetected neutrinos, showing that equal-weight treatment of the two-fold ambiguity biases angular coefficient extraction; it proposes a self-consistent weighting scheme based on a fixed-point theorem using the production angular distribution f(cosθ), validated via Monte Carlo at √s = 3.686 GeV and 4.8 GeV.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To correct systematic bias in angular coefficient extraction caused by naïve equal-weight assignment of the two-fold kinematic ambiguity in τ⁺τ⁻ reconstruction—arising from missing neutrino momenta—and to enable unbiased angular analyses without requiring event-by-event ambiguity resolution.

Method: Derives a self-consistent weighting scheme using a fixed-point theorem: assigns normalized weights w_i = f(cosθ_i)/[f(cosθ₁)+f(cosθ₂)] for the two candidate solutions per event; proves that the resulting weighted distribution h(x) = f(x)[1+ε(x)] satisfies |ε(x)| ≪ 1 under detailed balance; validates convergence and bias suppression via full Monte Carlo simulation at two center-of-mass energies.

Result: The fixed-point iteration converges to the true angular coefficient α within statistical uncertainty in Monte Carlo studies at √s = 3.686 GeV (ψ(2S) threshold) and 4.8 GeV; the bias from equal-weight treatment is quantified and shown to be resolvable even in the nearly isotropic QED limit; the method achieves robust, model-consistent angular analysis.

Conclusion: Equal-weight handling of the two-fold τ momentum ambiguity induces measurable and avoidable bias in angular coefficient extraction; the proposed f(cosθ)-based fixed-point weighting provides a theoretically grounded, numerically stable, and experimentally viable solution for precision angular analyses in e⁺e⁻ → τ⁺τ⁻.

Abstract: The reconstruction of $\tau$ lepton momenta in $e^{+}e^{-} \to \tau^{+}\tau^{-}$ processes suffers from a two-fold kinematic ambiguity due to undetected neutrinos. We show that the common practice of retaining both solutions with equal weight systematically biases the extracted angular coefficients: the bias grows with the size of the $\cos^{2}\theta$ modulation of the production distribution $f(\cos\theta)$, but remains resolvable even in the nearly uniform QED limit at the $\psi(2S)$ threshold. We propose a self-consistent weighting method based on a fixed-point theorem: assigning normalized weights $w_{i} = f(\cos\theta_{i})/[f(\cos\theta_{1})+f(\cos\theta_{2})]$ yields a weighted distribution $h(x) = f(x)[1+\varepsilon(x)]$ with $\varepsilon(x) \ll 1$ under a detailed balance condition. Monte Carlo simulations at $\sqrt{s}=3.686$~GeV and $4.8$~GeV show that the fixed-point iteration converges to the true angular coefficient $\alpha$ within statistical uncertainty, providing a robust framework for angular analysis without event-by-event ambiguity resolution.

</details>


### [101] [Electroweak Thermal Histories in the Minimal Type-II Seesaw Model with a Renormalization Group-Improved Effective Potential](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.01634)
*Cheng-Wei Chiang*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper investigates electroweak phase transitions in the minimal type-II seesaw model using a renormalization group-improved finite-temperature effective potential. It finds that standard criteria (e.g., $v_c/T_c > 1$) fail to identify genuinely strong first-order transitions; actual nucleating, percolating, and completing transitions are weak and fast—insufficient for baryogenesis or detectable gravitational-wave signals with upcoming space-based detectors.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To assess whether the minimal type-II seesaw model can realize a strong first-order electroweak phase transition capable of enabling electroweak baryogenesis and producing observable gravitational waves.

Method: Computation of the finite-temperature effective potential with full renormalization group improvement; systematic analysis of neutral-field phase degeneracies and transition dynamics (nucleation, percolation, completion); evaluation of $v_c/T_c$, sphaleron washout constraints, and gravitational-wave spectra.

Result: No genuinely strong first-order electroweak phase transition is found; identified transitions are weak and fast; sphaleron rates remain too high to preserve baryon asymmetry; predicted sound-wave gravitational-wave signals lie below LISA-type detector sensitivities; results are highly sensitive to RG improvement and renormalization scale choice.

Conclusion: The minimal type-II seesaw model, under current thermal field-theoretic treatment, does not support viable electroweak baryogenesis or observable stochastic gravitational-wave backgrounds from the electroweak phase transition.

Abstract: We study electroweak thermal histories in the minimal type-II seesaw model with a renormalization group-improved finite-temperature effective potential. The commonly used criterion $v_c/T_c>1$, with $v_c$ the broken-phase field at the critical temperature $T_c$, applied to every first-order degeneracy of the neutral-field phase structure in our survey, does not identify a strong electroweak phase transition. Treating those degeneracies as completed transitions would assign them a gravitational-wave signal they have not been shown to produce. Transitions that do nucleate, percolate and complete are found for the model. They are weak and fast, too weak on the standard sphaleron estimate to protect a baryon asymmetry from washout. Their sound wave spectra lie far below the sensitivity of planned space detectors. Their properties depend strongly on the renormalization group improvement and on the renormalization scale.

</details>


### [102] [Heavy-quark limit of $B$-meson distribution amplitudes in light-front holography with longitudinal confinement](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.01641)
*Reza Khosravi*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper compares two frameworks—Invariant Mass Ansatz (IMA) and Li–Maris–Zhao–Vary (LMZV)—for modeling the B-meson light-cone distribution amplitude φ_B⁺(ω) in the heavy-quark limit, analyzing their behavior for the inverse moment λ_B and decay constant f_B. IMA yields a δ-function–like collapse of φ_B⁺ and incorrect f_B scaling (∝ m_B⁻⁵/⁴), while LMZV gives stable O(Λ_QCD) width and λ_B, yet still fails to reproduce canonical HQET scaling (f_B ∝ m_B⁻¹/²) without current matching.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To assess how different light-front holographic prescriptions for finite quark masses affect the heavy-quark limit behavior of the B-meson light-cone distribution amplitude φ_B⁺(ω), especially its inverse moment λ_B and decay constant f_B — critical inputs for exclusive B-decay calculations and consistency with HQET.

Method: Analytical analysis of the heavy-quark limit (m_B → ∞) of light-front holographic wave functions for heavy–light mesons; comparison of two mass-incorporation schemes: (i) Invariant Mass Ansatz (IMA), and (ii) longitudinal confinement via the Li–Maris–Zhao–Vary (LMZV) potential; derivation of asymptotic scaling of φ_B⁺(ω), λ_B, and f_B using the local axial-vector current at zero transverse separation.

Result: IMA yields φ_B⁺(ω) → δ(ω − m_q), λ_B,IMA → m_q, and f_B ∝ m_B⁻⁵/⁴; LMZV yields stable O(Λ_QCD)-width φ_B⁺(ω), λ_B,LMZV^LP = m_q + σ/2, but f_B ∝ m_B⁻¹ — both fail canonical HQET scaling f_B ∝ m_B⁻¹/² without systematic current matching.

Conclusion: A non-singular heavy-quark limit of the longitudinal wave function (as in LMZV) is necessary but insufficient to ensure correct HQET scaling of local current matrix elements like f_B; proper matching of light-front currents to HQET operators is essential.

Abstract: The heavy-quark limit of light-front holographic wave functions for heavy--light mesons is investigated, with particular emphasis on the leading-twist $B$-meson light-cone distribution amplitude, $\phi_B^+(\omega)$. Two prescriptions for incorporating finite quark masses are compared: the invariant mass ansatz (IMA) and the longitudinal confining framework based on the Li--Maris--Zhao--Vary (LMZV) potential. In the IMA, although the peak of the distribution amplitude remains at a soft spectator momentum, its characteristic width decreases as $\Delta\omega \propto m_B^{-1/2}$ in the heavy-quark limit, causing the longitudinal distribution to collapse asymptotically to $\delta(\omega-m_q)$. Accordingly, the inverse moment approaches the finite limit $\lambda_{B,\mathrm{IMA}} \to m_q = \mathcal{O}(\Lambda_{\mathrm{QCD}})$. In contrast, the LMZV framework approaches a finite leading-power (LP) profile with a stable width of order $\mathcal{O}(\Lambda_{\rm QCD})$ and a stable inverse moment, $\lambda_{B,\mathrm{LMZV}}^{\mathrm{LP}} = m_q + \sigma/2 = \mathcal{O}(\Lambda_{\mathrm{QCD}})$, which is independent of the heavy-quark mass. The heavy-quark scaling of $f_B$ is also examined using the local axial-vector current at zero transverse separation. While the LMZV longitudinal wave function has a non-singular heavy-quark limit, the bare light-front current does not reproduce the canonical heavy-quark effective theory scaling $f_B \propto m_B^{-1/2}$: the decay constant scales as $f_B \propto m_B^{-1}$ in the LMZV framework and as $f_B \propto m_B^{-5/4}$ in the IMA. Thus, a non-singular heavy-quark limit of the longitudinal wave function is necessary but insufficient to ensure the correct heavy-quark scaling of local current matrix elements without systematic current matching.

</details>


### [103] [Holographic study of the scalar meson $K_0^*(1430)$ with dynamical longitudinal confinement](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.01662)
*Osra Hemmati,Reza Khosravi*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper studies the light scalar meson $K_0^*(1430)$ using light-front holographic QCD with combined transverse (holographic) and longitudinal (LMZV potential) confinement, computing decay constants, distribution amplitudes, and semileptonic $B \to K_0^* \ell^+\ell^-$ form factors to predict differential branching ratios and longitudinal lepton polarization asymmetries.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To improve theoretical predictions for the non-perturbative structure and semileptonic decays of the controversial light scalar meson $K_0^*(1430)$, which lacks a clear quark-model interpretation and suffers from model-dependent uncertainties in existing approaches.

Method: Light-front holographic QCD augmented with longitudinal confinement via the analytically solvable LMZV potential; invariant mass ansatz; computation of decay constants, parton distribution function, $\xi$-moments, twist-2 and twist-3 distribution amplitudes; derivation of transition form factors for $B \to K_0^* \ell^+\ell^-$.

Result: Improved predictions for decay constants, distribution amplitudes, and $B \to K_0^* \ell^+\ell^-$ transition form factors; resulting differential branching ratios and longitudinal lepton polarization asymmetries as functions of $q^2$; benchmark results comparable to other theoretical models.

Conclusion: Combined transverse and longitudinal confinement in light-front holography yields more reliable non-perturbative descriptions of $K_0^*(1430)$, enabling quantitative predictions for $B \to K_0^* \ell^+\ell^-$ decays that serve as testable benchmarks for LHCb and Belle II.

Abstract: Within the framework of light-front holographic QCD, where transverse confinement is inherently realized through holography, the structure and mass spectrum of the light scalar meson $K_0^{*}(1430)$ are investigated by employing the invariant mass ansatz and enhancing the model with a longitudinal confinement potential (specifically, the 't Hooft equation and the analytically solvable LMZV potential). The combined transverse and longitudinal confinement dynamics lead to more reliable calculations of key observables, including decay constants, parton distribution function, $\xi$-moments, and twist-$2$ and twist-$3$ distribution amplitudes. Transition form factors for the semileptonic decay $B \rightarrow K_0^{*} \ell^+ \ell^-$ are computed from these amplitudes, yielding predictions for differential branching ratios and longitudinal lepton polarization asymmetries as functions of $q^2$. The results are compared with other theoretical models, offering valuable insights into the non-perturbative dynamics of QCD. The predicted observables provide theoretically motivated benchmarks for future experimental investigations of $B\rightarrow K_0^*(1430)\ell^+\ell^-$ decays at LHCb and Belle II.

</details>


### [104] [Accounting for correlations in the $ωπ$ spectral function and its implication on the leading model dependent contribution to the $τ^- \to π^-π^0 ν_τγ$ decay](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.01683)
*Leonardo Esparza Arellano,Fabiola Elena Fortuna Montecillo,Antonio Rojas Ramos,Genaro Toledo Sánchez*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper analyzes the ωπ spectral function from τ⁻ → π⁻ων_τ decay using vector meson dominance (VMD), employs correlated parameter sampling via Metropolis Monte Carlo, validates CVC via e⁺e⁻ → ωπ cross-section and PDG branching ratio agreement, and computes the ω-channel contribution to G_EM(t) in τ⁻ → π⁻π⁰ν_τγ—including interference—with significantly reduced uncertainties due to proper correlation handling; implications for (g−2)_μ isospin-breaking corrections are refined in precision.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To improve uncertainty quantification in the ωπ spectral function and related electromagnetic corrections by properly accounting for parameter correlations in the VMD form factor, and to test CVC consistency while refining the long-distance EM correction G_EM(t) for tau-based (g−2)_μ extraction.

Method: Vector meson dominance modeling of the ωπ form factor; Metropolis Monte Carlo sampling of parameter space with full covariance; consistency checks against SND e⁺e⁻ → ωπ cross section and PDG τ⁻ → π⁻ων_τ branching ratio; computation of G_EM(t) from the ω channel and its interference with the structure-independent amplitude in τ⁻ → π⁻π⁰ν_τγ.
