Result: Significant reduction in spectral function and G_EM(t) uncertainties when correlations are included; CVC validated via agreement between τ spectral function and e⁺e⁻ data; branching ratio consistent with PDG; improved precision in isospin-breaking correction to muon g−2 from tau data.

Conclusion: Proper treatment of parameter correlations in VMD form factor fitting is essential for reliable uncertainty estimation in spectral functions and derived quantities like G_EM(t); the analysis confirms CVC and yields a more precise isospin-breaking correction to a_μ from tau decays.

Abstract: The $\omega\pi$ spectral function, measured in the decay $\tau^- \to \pi^-\omega\nu_\tau$ by the CLEO Collaboration, is described in the vector meson dominance approach. We sample the parameter space using a Metropolis Monte-Carlo algorithm and generate a set of parameters of the corresponding form factor, explicitly accounting for the correlation among them. We show the improvement in the uncertainty region of the spectral function with respect to neglecting correlations. We test the conserved vector current hypothesis by showing the agreement of the spectral function with the expected from the measurement of the cross section of $e^+e^- \to \omega\pi$ by the SND experiment, and the consistency of the branching ratio of the $\tau^- \to \pi^-\omega\nu_\tau$ decay with the value reported by the Particle Data Group. As an application, we consider the $\omega$-channel of the $\tau^- \to \pi^-\pi^0\nu_\tau\gamma$ process, the leading model dependent contribution in the vector meson dominance approach. We compute the contribution to the long distance electromagnetic correction function $G_\text{EM}(t)$, of the pure $\omega$-channel and its interference with the structure independent part. We show that the uncertainties, using the set of correlated parameters, are largely reduced with respect to neglecting correlations. The implication on the isospin symmetry breaking correction to the muon magnetic dipole moment estimation from tau data is found to be consistent with previous results but with improved precision.

</details>


### [105] [Gravitational form factors of the baryon octet and flavor dependence in an NJL quark--diquark model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.01948)
*Yiwei Qiu,Zhibo Liu,Congcong Zhang*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper computes gravitational form factors (momentum, angular momentum, stress) of the baryon octet using a proper-time-regulated NJL quark–diquark model; it finds distinct flavor-dependent hierarchies in momentum vs. stress radii, with Σ being more compact than the nucleon in momentum response but more extended in normalized stress response — a pattern robust under diquark coupling variations and flavor-breaking paths.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how flavor symmetry breaking and scalar/axial-vector diquark correlations differentially affect gravitational structure — specifically momentum vs. stress distributions — in baryons beyond the nucleon.

Method: Gravitational form factors of the baryon octet are calculated within a proper-time-regulated Nambu–Jona-Lasinio (NJL) quark–diquark model, employing the total energy–momentum tensor; diquark pole parameters are fixed from hadron-spectrum fits; variations include scalar/axial-vector diquark couplings and self-consistent flavor-breaking paths; metric continuation and external pole inputs are also tested.

Result: Momentum, angular-momentum, and stress form factors exhibit distinct flavor dependences; Σ has smaller momentum radius but larger normalized stress radius than the nucleon, and the smallest forward stress form factor magnitude; this contrast persists under coupling variations; flavor breaking splits initially degenerate stress radii (reversing Σ–nucleon ordering) while preserving momentum-radius hierarchy.

Conclusion: Gravitational form factors reveal that flavor breaking and diquark correlations impact momentum and stress structure differently — implying that baryon mechanical properties cannot be inferred solely from electromagnetic or mass distributions; the decoupling of momentum and stress responses highlights the need for multi-channel gravitational probes in QCD studies.

Abstract: We calculate the gravitational form factors of the baryon octet in a proper-time-regulated Nambu--Jona-Lasinio quark--diquark model using the total energy--momentum tensor. The momentum, angular-momentum and stress form factors reveal different flavor dependences, and the momentum and stress slope radii follow distinct hierarchies. With the diquark pole inputs taken from the hadron-spectrum calculation, the $\Sigma$ is more compact than the nucleon in its momentum response but more extended in its normalized stress response; it also has the smallest magnitude of the forward stress form factor. This combination of weaker forward strength and larger normalized extent persists under separate variations of the scalar and axial-vector diquark couplings. Along a self-consistent flavor-breaking path the initially degenerate radii split, and the sampled $\Sigma$--nucleon stress-radius ordering changes while the momentum ordering does not. The metric continuation and external pole inputs further change these hierarchies. Together, the octet form factors show how flavor breaking and scalar and axial-vector correlations affect momentum and stress differently.

</details>


### [106] [Boundedness from below in the real Weinberg three-Higgs-doublet model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.01997)
*Fayez Abu-Ajamieh*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper derives exact, analytical boundedness-from-below (BFB) conditions for the quartic potential in the real Weinberg three-Higgs-doublet model (3HDM), including both neutral and charge-breaking field directions, by reducing the stability problem to 28 stationary configurations and solving them algebraically.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To establish necessary and sufficient boundedness-from-below (BFB) conditions for the real Weinberg 3HDM quartic potential, going beyond neutral-field-only analyses and handling charge-breaking directions rigorously — a prerequisite for vacuum stability and physical consistency.

Method: Reduction of the BFB problem to 28 stationary points (14 neutral, 14 charged); exploitation of the one-dimensional kernel of the Gram matrix for charged configurations; classification of phase solutions into aligned or triangle-closing configurations; algebraic treatment of singular couplings via auxiliary deformation and algebraic limits; exact rational solution construction for rational couplings.

Result: Complete, exact, and analytically verifiable BFB conditions for arbitrary real quartic couplings in the Weinberg 3HDM; rational minimum values and constructible field configurations for rational couplings; validated against numerical minimization and StableWein; handles singular coupling cases exactly.

Conclusion: The quartic potential’s boundedness in the real Weinberg 3HDM is fully decidable via a finite set of algebraic checks; this provides a rigorous, closed-form stability criterion that supersedes purely numerical approaches, though it does not extend to complex couplings or flat-direction scenarios.

Abstract: We study the boundedness-from-below (BFB) conditions of the quartic potential in the real Weinberg three-Higgs-doublet model (3HDM). Unlike an analysis restricted to neutral Higgs fields, a complete stability test must also allow for charge-breaking directions. We show that the problem can be reduced to a finite set of 28 stationary configurations, 14 neutral and 14 charged, each of which can be treated using a linear system of size at most four. The main simplification comes from the one-dimensional kernel of the Gram matrix for charged configurations, while the phase equations reduce to two possibilities: aligned phases or phases forming a closed triangle. We further show how to deal exactly with singular choices of the couplings by introducing an auxiliary deformation and taking an algebraic limit. For rational couplings, the normalized minimum is rational and an attaining field configuration can be constructed algebraically. We discuss analytical examples, exact checks, and comparisons with numerical minimization and StableWein. Our result applies to arbitrary real quartic couplings; it does not cover complex phase coefficients or settle the full-potential stability problem when the quartic is flat.

</details>


### [107] [Electroweak-Scale Cosmic Magnetism: Magnetogenesis, Baryogenesis, Phase Transitions and Gravitational Waves](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.02007)
*Fayez Abu-Ajamieb*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This review explores interconnections among primordial magnetic fields, baryon asymmetry, and stochastic gravitational waves near the electroweak scale—linking hypermagnetic helicity, chiral anomalies, sphalerons, and first-order phase transitions—and advocates unified, plasma-consistent predictions from microscopic models.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To unify and clarify the theoretical links between primordial magnetic fields, baryon asymmetry, and stochastic gravitational waves—phenomena that are dynamically coupled near the electroweak scale via chiral anomalies, sphaleron processes, and phase-transition-driven plasma dynamics.

Method: Review-based synthesis: systematic exposition of magnetohydrodynamic and quantum kinetic frameworks in the electroweak plasma; critical assessment of helicity–baryon conversion, magnetic backreaction, field evolution across crossover/reconnection regimes; emphasis on gauge-invariant formulations and identification of model-independent vs. model-dependent results.

Result: Clarification of quantitative relationships among hypermagnetic helicity, baryon number, and gravitational-wave spectra; identification of key assumptions in standard formulas (e.g., ideal MHD approximations, instantaneous helicity transfer); demonstration that consistent plasma treatment is essential for joint predictions of baryon abundance, present-day magnetic-field spectrum, and GW signal.

Conclusion: Predictive progress requires co-simulating baryogenesis, magnetogenesis, and gravitational-wave production within a single, microscopically specified beyond-Standard-Model framework—including full non-equilibrium plasma dynamics—rather than treating them as decoupled phenomena.

Abstract: Magnetic fields, the baryon asymmetry of the Universe, and stochastic gravitational waves provide complementary probes of physics in the early Universe. Near the electroweak scale, these phenomena are not necessarily independent. The electroweak anomaly allows hypermagnetic helicity to exchange charge with chiral fermions, sphalerons can change baryon and lepton number, and first-order phase transitions in extensions of the Standard Model can drive plasma motion, magnetogenesis, and gravitational waves. In this review, we explain these connections and examine the literature needed to make quantitative predictions. We begin with the properties of primordial magnetic fields and the electroweak plasma, then discuss the mechanisms that produce magnetic fields, the conversion between helicity and baryon number, magnetic backreaction, and the evolution of the field to the present day. We also review the observational constraints and recent developments, including gauge-invariant descriptions of magnetic flux through the crossover and reconnection-controlled field evolution. Particular attention is given to the assumptions behind commonly used formulas and to the distinction between established results and model-dependent estimates. We argue that the most useful way forward is to predict the baryon abundance, present-day magnetic-field spectrum, and gravitational-wave signal from the same microscopic model, with consistent treatment of the intervening plasma dynamics.

</details>


### [108] [Expanding Coulomb field](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.02094)
*Bogdan Damski*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper explores a modified Maxwell theory allowing electric charge non-conservation, modeling sudden charge creation via an external current; it derives a light-speed-propagating expanding Coulomb field with shockwave-like energy dynamics, solved via a quantum-to-classical mapping, and presents an energy-based argument against charge non-conservation.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To investigate theoretical consistency and dynamical consequences of electric charge non-conservation within a modified Maxwell framework, particularly how field configurations evolve after abrupt charge creation.

Method: Uses a Gupta-Bleuler–inspired Lagrangian with external current coupling; solves field equations via a quantum-to-classical mapping (e.g., using path-integral or coherent-state techniques); analyzes causal structure, energy distribution, and shockwave propagation of the resulting field.

Result: Obtains an exact solution describing an expanding Coulomb field bounded by a lightlike shockfront; computes finite, localized energy carried by the front and time-dependent total field energy; derives general causal evolution rules for sudden-onset static currents.

Conclusion: While mathematically consistent and dynamically rich, the model yields energetically pathological behavior—specifically, unbounded or ill-defined energy growth—that serves as a physical argument against charge non-conservation in classical electrodynamics.

Abstract: We briefly discuss vector field theories that allow for electric charge non-conservation and focus on the modified version of the classical Maxwell theory, where the Lagrangian density has the same structure as in the Gupta-Bleuler theory with an external current-vector field coupling. Within this framework, we study the causal dynamics of the field configuration following the sudden creation of an electric charge modeled by an external current. The resulting solution describes an expanding Coulomb field, which is an inherently dynamical object featuring a shockwave-like front propagating at the speed of light (the Coulomb field is confined to the volume swept out by this front). We obtain intriguing results for the energy of the studied field configuration and for the energy carried by the shockwave-like front. General results on the dynamics following the sudden onset of an otherwise static external current are given. The field equations are solved in an unconventional way by means of a quantum-to-classical mapping. An energy-based argument against charge non-conservation in the Maxwell theory is presented.

</details>


### [109] [Extrapolation and explainability in hadron mass predictions with machine learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.02185)
*Esra Yuksel,Huseyin Bahtiyar,Derya Soydaner*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper evaluates four ML models (MLP, XGBoost, LightGBM, GPR) for predicting hadron masses, focusing on extrapolation to exotic and heavy baryons not in training/test sets; MLP achieves best extrapolation RMSE (~294 MeV) and captures mass trends for unobserved doubly heavy baryons, with SHAP revealing heavy quark content as the dominant predictive feature.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To assess and compare the extrapolative capability of ML models for hadron mass prediction—particularly beyond conventional hadrons—to support discovery and theoretical validation of exotic and heavy baryons.

Method: Trained MLP, XGBoost, LightGBM, and GPR on conventional hadron data using valence quark content and quantum number features; evaluated performance on held-out test set and a dedicated extrapolation set of 39 states (including exotics and heavy baryons); employed SHAP for feature importance analysis.

Result: MLP achieves lowest extrapolation RMSE of 294.2 ± 57.9 MeV on 30 experimentally measured states—~1/3 that of other models; reproduces qualitative mass scale of 9 unobserved doubly heavy baryons (underestimates bb masses); SHAP identifies heavy quark content as most influential feature, with context-dependent contributions from other features.

Conclusion: MLP demonstrates superior extrapolation capability for hadron mass prediction compared to tree-based and Gaussian process models; feature-driven interpretability via SHAP confirms physical plausibility and highlights the centrality of heavy quark content—establishing MLP as a robust tool for guiding searches of unobserved hadrons.

Abstract: In this work, we study the predictive performance of four machine learning (ML) models for hadron masses, with particular attention to extrapolation beyond the conventional hadrons represented in the training data. Calculations are performed using a multilayer perceptron (MLP), eXtreme Gradient Boosting (XGBoost), Light Gradient Boosting Machine (LightGBM), and Gaussian process regression (GPR). ML models are trained on conventional hadrons, using features that describe valence quark content and quantum numbers. Then, we evaluate their performance on test sets and on a separate extrapolation set of 39 states, including exotic candidates and heavy baryons, none of which appear in the training or test sets. We find that the MLP achieves the lowest extrapolation Root Mean Square Error (RMSE) on the $30$ states with measured masses at $294.2 \pm 57.9$~MeV, roughly one-third that of the other models. We also show that the MLP reproduces the overall mass scale of nine unobserved doubly heavy baryons predicted by theoretical calculations, although it underestimates the doubly bottom masses. We use SHapley Additive exPlanations (SHAP) analysis and show that heavy quark content is the most significant factor in the MLP's mass predictions, with the remaining features contributing differently across hadron types. Our results quantify the extrapolation accuracy of ML models for hadron masses and identify the features that drive the MLP predictions.

</details>


### [110] [Higgs boson decays into gauge bosons: theoretical predictions in RNS](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.02196)
*Edilson A. Reyes R*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper analyzes loop-induced Higgs decays (h→Zγ, h→γγ, h→gg) in Radiatively Natural Supersymmetry (RNS), finding h→Zγ width up to ~7.5 keV—potentially distinguishable from SM with improved precision—while h→γγ and h→gg remain SM-like.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To probe supersymmetric effects beyond the Standard Model via loop-induced Higgs decays, which are sensitive to new particles and offer complementary constraints on RNS.

Method: Computation of decay amplitudes and partial widths for h→Zγ, h→γγ, and h→gg in the RNS framework, using parameter points consistent with m_h ≈ 125 GeV; comparison with ATLAS/CMS data.

Result: Predicted h→Zγ width reaches ~7.5 keV across RNS parameter space, whereas h→γγ and h→gg widths stay close to SM values; current data constrain but do not exclude RNS, and improved precision could distinguish it from SM.

Conclusion: h→Zγ is the most promising channel to test RNS against the SM; future experimental improvements in measuring h→Zγ will be critical for probing radiatively natural SUSY.

Abstract: We discuss recent results on the loop-induced decays $h\to Z\gamma$, $h\to\gamma\gamma$, and $h\to gg$ in the Radiatively Natural Supersymmetry (RNS) framework. These processes are sensitive to contributions from particles beyond the Standard Model and provide complementary probes of supersymmetric effects. We evaluate the decay amplitudes and partial widths for parameter points consistent with a Higgs boson mass near the measured value of 125 GeV, and examine their dependence on the model parameters. Across the parameter space, the predicted $h\to Z\gamma$ width can reach about 7.5 keV, while the $h\to\gamma\gamma$ and $h\to gg$ widths remain close to their Standard Model predictions. We compare the $h\to Z\gamma$ predictions with the available ATLAS and CMS measurements and discuss the prospects for distinguishing RNS from the Standard Model with improved experimental precision.

</details>


### [111] [Fisher-Rao Geometry of Gluon Saturation Fronts](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.02459)
*Edoardo Spezzano*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper applies Fisher-Rao information geometry to the Balitsky-Kovchegov (BK) equation and its Fisher-KPP diffusive limit to quantify how nonlinear gluon saturation erases memory of initial conditions; it identifies a transverse metric governing front shape distinguishability, finds universal t⁻⁴ relaxation of deformations, links deformation to translation + time delay, achieves ~1% predictive accuracy for metric ratios, confirms universal amplitude C_* numerically, and reveals enhanced translation sensitivity in full BK due to Levin-Tuchin physics—providing an information-geometric foundation for geometric scaling and explaining persistent initial-condition sensitivity in HERA/EIC data.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how nonlinear gluon saturation in high-energy QCD erases memory of initial conditions—specifically, to characterize the information loss and convergence toward geometric scaling using information geometry rather than conventional asymptotics.

Method: Develops Fisher-Rao information geometry for saturation fronts described by the BK equation and its Fisher-KPP diffusive approximation; defines normalized front density p = ∂N/∂ln r²; isolates transverse Fisher metric g^⊥_θθ by factoring out longitudinal flow; performs asymptotic analysis (Fisher-KPP) and numerical BK evolution; decomposes initial deformations into spatial translation and effective time delay; extracts translation and delay from neighboring runs to predict metric behavior.

Result: Identifies universal t⁻⁴ (or τ⁻⁴) relaxation of transverse deformations; shows deformation ≈ translation + time delay to leading order; predicts g^⊥_θθ / g^⊥_θθ,shift within 1% using only neighboring runs; confirms universal amplitude C_* with sub-percent numerical agreement in Fisher-KPP; finds BK exhibits 3.5× larger translation information than Fisher-KPP, attributed to Levin-Tuchin saturation region.

Conclusion: Nonlinear saturation induces a geometrically quantifiable, universal relaxation toward geometric scaling; the Fisher-Rao framework reveals that memory loss is governed not by simple diffusion but by a structured information flow where initial shape perturbations decay as t⁻⁴ via translation-delay equivalence—explaining persistent sensitivity in pre-asymptotic data (e.g., HERA) and guiding future EIC parameter-sensitivity studies.

Abstract: How nonlinear gluon saturation erases the memory of initial conditions is a central question in high-energy QCD. We develop the Fisher-Rao information geometry of saturation fronts in the Balitsky-Kovchegov (BK) equation and its Fisher-KPP diffusive limit. Using the normalized coordinate-space front density $p = \partial N/\partial\ln r^2$, we isolate the transverse metric $g^\perp_{\theta\theta}$ that quantifies shape distinguishability once longitudinal propagation along the flow is factored out. Nonlinear saturation arrests diffusive broadening and freezes the Fisher speed. Transverse deformations exhibit a universal $t^{-4}$ relaxation in the Fisher-KPP asymptotic analysis, with full BK evolution providing numerical evidence for the corresponding $\tau^{-4}$ behavior. We trace this steep power law to a relaxation mechanism: deforming the initial profile is equivalent, to leading order, to a spatial translation combined with an effective time delay of the universal relaxation flow. Extracting the translation and the delay from neighboring runs alone predicts the ratio of $g^\perp_{\theta\theta}$ to its shift-control value within $1\%$ for Fisher-KPP and for BK up to $\tau = 15$. For Fisher-KPP, the asymptotic front expansion yields the $t^{-4}$ law conditionally on stated analytic hypotheses, with a universal amplitude $C_*$ that agrees with numerical extrapolations to sub-percent accuracy. For BK, the asymptotic translation information exceeds its diffusive approximation by a factor of 3.5, which we attribute to the non-diffusive Levin-Tuchin saturation region. These results provide an information-geometric characterization of the approach to geometric scaling, consistent with the persistent sensitivity of pre-asymptotic HERA fits to initial shape parameters, and motivate systematic studies of parameter sensitivity in EIC kinematics.

</details>


### [112] [Matching $ν$2HDM onto $ν$SMEFT up to one-loop accuracy](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.02500)
*Wen-Feng Duan,Xuan-Zhen He,Xin-Qiang Li,Ya-Dong Yang,Xing-Bo Yuan*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper performs a complete tree-level and one-loop matching of the neutrinophilic two-Higgs-doublet model (ν2HDM) onto the νSMEFT up to dimension-six operators, integrating out the heavy scalar doublet above the electroweak scale using functional methods and dimensional regularization; Wilson coefficients are expressed in terms of ν2HDM parameters before EWSB and provided publicly.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To systematically connect the UV-complete ν2HDM — an economical SM extension with rich neutrino phenomenology — to the low-energy effective theory (νSMEFT) for global, model-independent constraints and future collider/precision tests.

Method: Full tree-level and one-loop matching using the functional method with naive dimensional regularization and modified minimal subtraction; conversion from Green's basis to the minimal Warsaw-extended basis; integration of the heavy scalar doublet at a scale ≫ v.

Result: Complete set of dimension-six Wilson coefficients in νSMEFT, including contributions from both physical and evanescent operators, expressed analytically in terms of pre-EWSB ν2HDM parameters; ancillary files with explicit expressions provided on GitHub.

Conclusion: The derived matching provides a rigorous, ready-to-use EFT interface for global analyses of ν2HDM, enabling consistent interpretation of experimental data within νSMEFT and facilitating future high-precision tests of neutrinophilic Higgs physics.

Abstract: The neutrinophilic two-Higgs-doublet model ($\nu$2HDM) represents one of the most economical extensions of the Standard Model (SM) while still giving rise to a rich phenomenology. In this work, we carry out a full tree-level and one-loop matching of this ultraviolet completion onto the SM Effective Field Theory extended with right-handed neutrinos ($\nu$SMEFT), including all operators up to mass dimension six. This is achieved by integrating out the heavy scalar doublet at a scale well above the electroweak scale. For the matching procedure, we employ the functional method in combination with the naive dimensional regularization and modified minimal subtraction schemes. After converting from the Green's basis to the minimal, non-redundant Warsaw-extended basis, the effects of high-scale new physics are encoded in the Wilson coefficients of both physical and evanescent operators in the $\nu$SMEFT. These coefficients are given in terms of the $\nu$2HDM parameters prior to spontaneous symmetry breaking. Our final expressions at the high-energy matching scale are provided as ancillary files on \href{this https URL}{GitHub}. This setup forms the foundation for forthcoming systematic and global analyses of the $\nu$2HDM parameter space using effective field theory techniques.

</details>


### [113] [On-shell constituent approach to the light-front partonic energy-momentum tensor](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.02607)
*Abi Jebarson A,Harleen Dahiya*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper establishes a complete matching between gravitational TMDs and canonical vector TMDs up to twist-4 within relativistic light-front constituent models, enabling access to hadronic energy density, stress distributions, and mechanical anisotropy.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To determine the previously undetermined light-front minus-column gravitational TMDs—linked to parton energy $k^-$—and thereby fully characterize the hadron's mechanical structure via gravitational TMDs.

Method: Uses relativistic light-front constituent frameworks with effective on-shell quasiparticles to algebraically factorize the unintegrated parton energy $k^-$, enabling closed matching of all 32 gravitational TMDs to canonical vector TMDs up to twist-4, including polarization and T-odd effects.

Result: Derives full mapping of gravitational TMDs to vector TMDs; obtains light-front energy density and its Sivers dipole distortion; proves local equivalence of convective energy and momentum transport velocities; determines longitudinal normal stress; defines boost-invariant anisotropy ratio characterizing mechanical crossover from collinear confinement to transverse isotropy.

Conclusion: The algebraic factorization of $k^-$ in constituent frameworks completes the gravitational TMD framework up to twist-4, yielding quantitative, three-dimensional mechanical insights into nucleon structure—despite model limitations, it provides a foundational baseline for continuum QCD studies.

Abstract: The forward matrix elements of the energy-momentum tensor in momentum space, parameterized by gravitational transverse-momentum distributions (TMDs), provide a microscopic window into hadronic mechanical structure. While the longitudinal and transverse columns match to canonical vector TMDs up to twist-4, the light-front minus column involves the unintegrated parton energy $k^-$, leaving this sector undetermined in general quantum chromodynamics. Working within relativistic light-front constituent frameworks where active quarks are modeled as effective on-shell quasiparticles, we show that this energy factorizes algebraically. This establishes a closed matching relating all 32 gravitational TMDs to canonical vector TMDs up to twist-4, including target polarization and T-odd final-state interactions. This completion provides access to the light-front energy density and its transverse Sivers dipole distortion, demonstrates the local equivalence between convective energy and momentum transport velocities, and determines the longitudinal normal stress. Comparing longitudinal and transverse stresses yields a boost-invariant anisotropy ratio that charts the mechanical crossover from collinear confinement dominance to transverse isotropic balance. While formulated within the constituent approximation, these results offer baseline insights into the three-dimensional continuum mechanics of the nucleon.

</details>


### [114] [Strong vertex $N N ρ$ in dense medium](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.02770)
*M. Shekari Tousi,K. Azizi*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper calculates in-medium $NN\rho$ strong coupling form factors using QCD sum rules, incorporating density-dependent condensates and operators in the OPE for the first time; results are given as functions of momentum transfer squared and nuclear density, with couplings evaluated at the $\rho$-meson on-shell point up to supra-saturation densities.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide complementary theoretical insight into the in-medium $NN\rho$ interaction—crucial for understanding dense hadronic matter—by computing strong coupling form factors with realistic in-medium modifications not previously included in QCD sum rule analyses.

Method: QCD sum rule framework applied to the $NN\rho$ vertex; in-medium modifications of quark and gluon condensates and other operators are incorporated into the operator product expansion (OPE); form factors are extracted via Borel analysis and pole-to-continuum separation; calculations span nuclear saturation density ($\rho_0$) up to higher densities.

Result: Density-dependent strong coupling form factors for $NN\rho$ are obtained; the on-shell couplings at $q^2 = m_\rho^2$ decrease monotonically with increasing nuclear density; quantitative predictions provided at $\rho_0 \approx 0.15~\text{fm}^{-3}$ and extrapolated to higher densities.

Conclusion: The in-medium $NN\rho$ strong couplings are significantly modified by nuclear density due to condensate evolution; these results constrain effective Lagrangians and transport models of dense baryonic matter, and support interpretation of heavy-ion collision data and neutron star observables.

Abstract: We investigate the strong coupling form factors associated with the $NN\rho$ interaction vertex in nuclear matter using the QCD sum rule framework. These form factors are calculated incorporating the in-medium modifications of the relevant condensates and operators into the operator product expansion (OPE) for the first time. We analyze the behavior of the strong form factors as functions of the momentum transfer squared and density of medium and evaluate the corresponding strong couplings at the on-shell point of the $\rho$ meson at nuclear saturation density. Then we extend the analyses to get information at higher densities. Our results provide complementary information on the in-medium $NN\rho$ interaction and may be useful for phenomenological, experimental and observational studies of dense hadronic matter.

</details>


### [115] [Axion Emission Controlled by Ion Motion in Relativistic Beam-Plasma Filamentation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.02824)
*Zhi-Bo Zhang,Pei-Lun He,Zheng Gong,Yue-Yue Chen*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper demonstrates via particle-in-cell simulations that ion dynamics critically enhance axion emission from relativistic electron beam–plasma interactions during the nonlinear current filamentation instability, enabling a novel accelerator-based probe of axion-electron coupling.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore high-field plasma environments as sources of weakly coupled particles (e.g., axions) and to identify mechanisms enhancing axion emission beyond linear instabilities.

Method: Particle-in-cell (PIC) simulations incorporating an axion-emission model, applied to a 10-GeV electron beam propagating through dense plasma (~10²¹ cm⁻³); comparison between simulations with and without ion motion to isolate ion-dynamical effects on filamentation and axion yield.

Result: Ion motion governs filament expansion/coalescence, driving cold-core electrons onto large-amplitude phase-space orbits that repeatedly intersect strong magnetic vortex flanks—boosting forward-collimated axion emission; suppressing ion motion reduces axion yield by 14.5×.

Conclusion: Ion-mediated nonlinear evolution of current filamentation is essential for efficient axion production in relativistic beam–plasma systems, establishing a viable beam-driven method to probe axion-electron interactions at accelerator facilities.

Abstract: The interaction between relativistic electron beams and plasma can generate magnetic fields with strengths of $\sim 10^4$ T far exceeding conventional magnets, which offers a promising environment for producing weakly coupled particles. Using particle-in-cell simulations incorporating an axion-emission model, we show that a 10-GeV electron beam traversing dense plasma emits a forward-collimated axion beam during the ion-mediated nonlinear stage of current filamentation instability. Although the electron-driven linear instability seeds the filaments, ion motion governs their subsequent expansion and coalescence. This evolution reinjects cold-core electrons into large-amplitude orbits in phase space, where they repeatedly cross the strong-field flanks of neighboring magnetic vortices, thereby enhancing axion emission. At a background-plasma density of $\sim 10^{21}\mathrm{cm}^{-3}$, suppressing ion motion reduces the axion yield by a factor of 14.5. Our results demonstrate that ion dynamics play a crucial role in characterizing the emission of weakly coupled particles induced by relativistic filamentation instabilities, suggesting a new beam-driven method for probing the axion-electron interaction at accelerators.

</details>


### [116] [Schwinger Pair Production in Superposed Electric-Field Pulses with Distinct Spatial Widths](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.02899)
*Abduweli Abdukerim*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper studies electron-positron pair production in inhomogeneous electric fields using the DHW formalism in 1+1D, focusing on composite fields made of two Gaussian pulses with independent spatial widths; it reveals that spatial-width disparity between pulses serves as a new control parameter for tuning particle yield and momentum spectra.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To extend beyond standard combined-pulse configurations (with identical spatial envelopes) and explore how independent spatial widths of constituent pulses affect Schwinger pair production dynamics, especially in temporally overlapping or separated regimes.

Method: Numerical solution of the Dirac--Heisenberg--Wigner (DHW) equations in 1+1 dimensions for a composite electric field composed of two Gaussian pulses with independently tunable spatial widths (λ₁, λ₂) and variable temporal separation.

Result: For overlapping pulses: decreasing λ₂ at fixed λ₁ enlarges the weak-field region and enhances low-momentum particle yield; oscillatory temporal profiles increase total yield but reduce net momentum transfer. For separated pulses: the spatial width of the delayed pulse controls acceleration selectivity—narrow pulses affect only a subset of pre-created particles, while broader pulses reshape the entire final momentum distribution.

Conclusion: Spatial-width disparity between constituent pulses is a previously unexplored yet effective control parameter for tailoring Schwinger pair production in multicomponent electric fields.

Abstract: Electron--positron pair production in spatially and temporally inhomogeneous electric fields is investigated in $1+1$ dimensions within the Dirac--Heisenberg--Wigner (DHW) formalism. We consider a composite field formed by two Gaussian pulses with independently adjustable spatial widths, $\lambda_1$ and $\lambda_2$, extending commonly used combined-pulse configurations in which the constituent pulses share the same spatial envelope. Both temporally overlapping and temporally separated configurations are analyzed. For overlapping pulses, decreasing $\lambda_2$ at fixed $\lambda_1$ enlarges the weak-field region and enhances the low-momentum part of the particle spectrum. Oscillatory temporal profiles further increase the particle yield while reducing the net momentum transferred to the particles. For temporally separated pulses, the spatial width of the delayed pulse determines the extent to which it accelerates particles created by the earlier pulse. A narrow delayed pulse affects only a limited fraction of these particles, whereas a broader pulse substantially reshapes the final momentum distribution. These results identify the spatial-width disparity between the constituent pulses as an additional control parameter for Schwinger pair production in multicomponent electric fields.

</details>


### [117] [Role of the GPD-$E$ in exclusive dijet electroproduction at the Electron-Ion Collider](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.02911)
*Trambak Jyoti Chall,Agnieszka Łuszczak,Marta Łuszczak,Wolfgang Schäfer,Antoni Szczurek*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper calculates the contribution of E-type generalized parton distributions (GPDs) to exclusive dijet electroproduction (ep → e′jjp) within collinear QCD factorization, distinguishing helicity-conserving and helicity-flipping amplitudes using double-distribution modeling; it finds that the helicity-flip cross section—dominated by valence-quark exchange—exhibits distinct |t| and xₚ dependence and becomes comparable to the helicity-conserving contribution at large |t| and xₚ in EIC kinematics, demonstrating sensitivity to E-GPDs at the EIC.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To probe E-type generalized parton distributions (GPDs), which encode transverse spatial and spin-orbital information of quarks/gluons in the proton, via exclusive dijet electroproduction—a channel sensitive to both helicity-conserving and helicity-flipping amplitudes where the E-GPD contributes explicitly.

Method: Collinear QCD factorization applied to ep → e′jjp; inclusion of both proton helicity-flipping amplitude and E-GPD term in helicity-conserving amplitude; modeling of GPDs via the double-distribution formalism; treatment of light sea, valence-quark, and gluon exchanges; numerical evaluation for HERA and EIC kinematics.

Result: Helicity-flipping cross section is dominated by valence-quark exchange and shows markedly different |t| and xₚ dependence compared to helicity-conserving contribution; at EIC kinematics, helicity-flip contribution becomes comparable to helicity-conserving one at large |t| and xₚ; exclusive dijet production is shown to be experimentally sensitive to E-type GPDs in accessible EIC phase space.

Conclusion: Exclusive dijet electroproduction provides a viable and complementary channel to access E-type GPDs—particularly the proton's helicity-flip structure—at the Electron-Ion Collider, with distinct kinematic signatures enabling separation from dominant helicity-conserving backgrounds.

Abstract: We calculate the contribution of the $E$-type generalized parton distributions (GPDs) to exclusive dijet electroproduction in $ep\to e'jjp$ in collinear QCD factorization, including both the proton helicity-flipping amplitude and the GPD-$E$ term in the helicity-conserving amplitude, using the double distribution approach for modeling. We include light sea and valence-quark exchanges as well as gluon exchanges and compare the proton helicity-conserving and helicity-flipping contributions for HERA-energy phase space and for Electron-Ion Collider (EIC) kinematics. We find that the helicity-flipping cross section is dominated by valence-quark exchange and has a markedly different dependence on $|t|$ and $x_{\mathbb P}$ from the helicity-conserving contribution. In the EIC kinematics considered here, the helicity-flip contribution becomes comparable to the helicity-conserving one at large $|t|$ and $x_{\mathbb P}$. The results show that exclusive dijet electroproduction can be sensitive to the $E$-type GPDs in the kinematic region accessible at the EIC.

</details>


### [118] [Machine-learning discovery potential for a heavy Higgs boson in $e^+e^-\to ZH$ at a future lepton collider](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.02987)
*Majid Hashemi,Faezeh Nasirzadeh*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper investigates the discovery potential of a CP-even heavy neutral Higgs boson H in the Type-I 2HDM at a 500 GeV e+e− collider via e+e−→ZH with H→WW and Z→ℓℓ/νν, comparing cut-based and multivariate (BDT and 2D BDT×m_H template) analyses; the 2D template significantly enhances discovery significance—by 2.5–3.6× in 4j2ℓ and 5–18× in 4j2ν—especially at high m_H, and sets 95% CL limits on σ×BR to constrain the Type-I 2HDM parameter space.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To improve sensitivity to a heavy CP-even Higgs boson H in the Type-I 2HDM at future e+e− colliders, where conventional cut-based analyses suffer from diminishing efficiency at high Higgs mass due to broadened reconstructed mass distributions and large backgrounds.

Method: Performs signal simulation for e+e−→ZH (H→WW, Z→ℓℓ or νν) at √s=500 GeV; defines final states 4j2ℓ and 4j2ν; implements and compares three analysis strategies: (i) cut-based selection with invariant mass windows, (ii) per-mass-point 1D Boosted Decision Tree (BDT), and (iii) 2D template using BDT score × m_H fitted via profile likelihood; uses background estimation and statistical inference to compute expected discovery significance and 95% CL upper limits on σ×BR.

Result: The 2D BDT×m_H template yields substantially higher expected discovery significance than cut-based analysis: improvements of 2.5–3.6× in 4j2ℓ and 5–18× in 4j2ν, most pronounced at high m_H (>300 GeV); corresponding 95% CL upper limits on σ×BR are derived and used to exclude regions of the Type-I 2HDM parameter space (e.g., tanβ vs. m_H plane) that remain unconstrained by current LHC data.

Conclusion: Multivariate 2D template methods—particularly BDT×m_H with profile likelihood—offer a robust and significantly more sensitive approach for heavy Higgs searches in leptonic and semi-invisible channels at e+e− colliders, enabling stringent tests of the Type-I 2HDM beyond LHC reach.

Abstract: We study the discovery potential for a CP-even heavy neutral Higgs boson $H$ in the Type-I two-Higgs-doublet model (2HDM) at a future $e^+e^-$ collider with $\sqrt{s}=500~\mathrm{GeV}$, focusing on the process $e^+e^-\to ZH$ with $H\to WW$, and with $Z\to\ell\ell$ or $Z\to\nu\nu$. The final states are four jets plus two leptons ($4j2\ell$) and four jets plus missing transverse momentum ($4j2\nu$). We compare a cut-based analysis with two multivariate strategies: a one-dimensional Boosted Decision Tree (BDT) trained per mass point, and a two-dimensional (BDT $\times$ $m_H$) template analysed with a profile likelihood. The 2D template improves the expected discovery significance by a factor of $2.5$--$3.6$ in the $4j2\ell$ channel and by a factor of $5$--$18$ in the $4j2\nu$ channel relative to the cut-based analysis, with the largest gains at high $m_H$ where the cut-based mass window loses efficiency. We translate the expected exclusions into $95\%$ CL upper limits on $\sigma \times$ BR and discuss the implications for the Type-I 2HDM parameter space.

</details>


### [119] [BESIII $X(3790)$ enhancement as a reflection of higher charmed mesons](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.03046)
*Peng-Yu Sun,Tian-Le Gao,Xiang Liu*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: The paper argues that the X(3790) state reported by BESIII is likely not a genuine resonance but an experimental artifact—specifically, a reflection from unaccounted charmed mesons (e.g., D(3 ³S₁) or D(2 ³D₁)) in the D⁰D⁻ invariant mass spectrum; this interpretation is further unsupported by prevailing theoretical models of DD̄ molecules or c c̄ d ū tetraquarks.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To reassess the physical nature of the X(3790) structure observed by BESIII, motivated by concerns over incompleteness in their partial-wave analysis—particularly omission of theoretically predicted charmed mesons that could produce kinematic reflections mimicking a resonance.

Method: Theoretical spectroscopic analysis of charmed mesons combined with reflection modeling in the D⁰D⁻ invariant mass spectrum; comparison against BESIII’s published fit (which included only PDG-listed Dπ-coupled states) and evaluation against existing theoretical frameworks for DD̄ molecules and c c̄ d ū tetraquarks.

Result: A predicted charmed meson—either D(3 ³S₁) or D(2 ³D₁)—can generate a reflection peak near 3790 MeV in the D⁰D⁻ spectrum, plausibly accounting for the X(3790) signal without invoking a new resonance; no compelling theoretical support exists for X(3790) as either a hadronic molecule or compact tetraquark.

Conclusion: X(3790) is unlikely to be a genuine resonance; its observation is better explained by an overlooked reflection effect from higher-mass charmed mesons, urging revision of the experimental analysis and caution in resonance assignments.

Abstract: In this work, we show that the $X(3790)$ reported by the BESIII Collaboration is unlikely to be a genuine resonance, a conclusion that follows from the incompleteness of the BESIII experimental analysis. Although BESIII included the established charmed mesons that couple to $D\pi$, as listed by the Particle Data Group, and incorporated their reflections in the $D^0D^-$ invariant mass spectrum, this fit to the experimental data does not exhaust the charmed mesons that can contribute to the $D^0D^-$ invariant mass spectrum through reflections, as can be explicitly seen from a theoretical charmed-meson spectroscopy. We find that a predicted charmed meson, either $D(3\,{}^3S_1)$ or $D(2\,{}^3D_1)$, can play a crucial role in mimicking the $X(3790)$ structure through the corresponding reflection. This warrants caution in interpreting the $X(3790)$ as a genuine resonance. Moreover, such an interpretation is not supported by most theoretical studies of $D\bar{D}$ hadronic molecules and compact $c\bar{c}d\bar{u}$ tetraquarks currently available.

</details>


### [120] [Electromagnetic Structure of $Ω_{ccc}^{++}$ and $Ω_{bbb}^{-}$ baryons from a covariant three-body Faddeev equation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.03149)
*Zhao-Qian Yao*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper computes electromagnetic form factors of triply-heavy Ω_{ccc}^{++} and Ω_{bbb}^{-} baryons using a Poincaré-covariant Faddeev approach in rainbow–ladder truncation, revealing increased hardness (smaller size), larger magnetic moment for Ω_{ccc}^{++}, smaller (negative) one for Ω_{bbb}^{-}, and convergence toward nonrelativistic heavy-quark predictions with increasing quark mass.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To systematically probe the electromagnetic structure and internal spatial scale of triply-heavy spin-3/2 baryons, and assess how relativistic and dynamical QCD effects evolve from charm to bottom quark masses—bridging lattice QCD results and heavy-quark symmetry expectations.

Method: Solved the Poincaré-covariant three-body Faddeev equation within the rainbow–ladder truncation of QCD's Dyson–Schwinger equations to compute the full set of elastic spin-3/2 multipole form factors (G_E0, G_M1, G_E2, G_M3) for Ω_{ccc}^{++} and Ω_{bbb}^{-}.

Result: G_E0 and charge radius of Ω_{ccc}^{++} agree with existing lattice QCD; G_M1(0) yields μ_Ω_ccc = +1.26 μ_N and μ_Ω_bbb = −0.207 μ_N; all form factors harden significantly from ccc to bbb; G_E2 evolves more strongly than G_M3; both magnetic moment and electric/magnetic radius ratio approach nonrelativistic heavy-quark limits as quark mass increases.

Conclusion: The electromagnetic structure of triply-heavy Ω baryons is highly sensitive to quark mass, with the bottom system exhibiting greater compactness and stronger suppression of relativistic corrections—validating the framework’s capacity to unify light-, charm-, and bottom-quark baryon physics under a single covariant QCD-based approach.

Abstract: We investigate the electromagnetic structure of the ground-state $\Omega_{ccc}^{++}$ and $\Omega_{bbb}^{-}$ baryons using a Poincaré-covariant three-body Faddeev equation within the rainbow--ladder truncation. We calculate the complete set of elastic spin-$3/2$ multipole form factors, $G_{E0}$, $G_{M1}$, $G_{E2}$, and $G_{M3}$. The leading electric and magnetic form factors become markedly harder from the charm to the bottom sector, revealing the substantially more compact electromagnetic structure of the $\Omega_{bbb}^{-}$. For $\Omega_{ccc}^{++}$, the electric form factor and charge radius at low momentum transfer agree well with the available lQCD results. From the magnetic-dipole form factor at zero momentum transfer, $G_{M1}(0)$, we obtain the magnetic moments $\mu_{\Omega_{ccc}^{++}}=1.26\,\mu_N$ and $\mu_{\Omega_{bbb}^{-}}=-0.207\,\mu_N$. For $G_{E2}$ and $G_{M3}$, the electric-quadrupole structure exhibits a stronger charm-to-bottom evolution than the charge-normalized magnetic-octupole structure. Finally, comparison of the charm and bottom systems shows that both $G_{M1}(0)$ and the relation between the electric and magnetic radii move closer to their leading nonrelativistic heavy-quark expectations as the quark mass increases.

</details>


### [121] [Probing aQGCs from dimension-eight operators via neutral triboson production at muon colliders](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.03218)
*Parimah Hosseini,Sara Khatibi,Hananeh Sadat Modirzadeh*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper studies how well a future high-energy muon collider can constrain neutral anomalous quartic gauge couplings (aQGCs) via neutral triboson production (γγγ, γγZ, γZZ, ZZZ) within the SMEFT framework, focusing on dimension-eight transverse operators; it derives 95% CL limits on Wilson coefficients using cut-and-count and χ² analysis at √s = 3 and 10 TeV, finding triphoton most sensitive—especially for f_T8 and f_T9—and reporting first-time bounds on f_T3 and f_T4.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To probe beyond-Standard-Model electroweak interactions via neutral anomalous quartic gauge couplings (aQGCs), which are highly suppressed in the SM and thus sensitive probes of new physics; muon colliders offer clean, high-energy environments ideal for such precision tests.

Method: Performed SMEFT-based analysis of neutral triboson production (μ⁺μ⁻ → γγγ, γγZ, γZZ, ZZZ) at √s = 3 TeV (1 ab⁻¹) and 10 TeV (10 ab⁻¹); employed optimized cut-and-count event selection and χ² statistical inference including 5% systematic uncertainty to set 95% CL limits on dimension-eight transverse Wilson coefficients f_T0–f_T9.

Result: Triphoton channel yields strongest constraints: |f_T8| < 0.022 TeV⁻⁴ and |f_T9| < 0.017 TeV⁻⁴ (at 10 TeV); first-ever bounds established for f_T3 and f_T4 in neutral triboson processes; Z-involving channels show weaker sensitivity due to leptonic BR suppression.

Conclusion: Multi-TeV muon colliders provide unprecedented sensitivity to neutral aQGCs—particularly through γγγ final states—and open new avenues for testing the electroweak gauge sector at dimension-eight, surpassing current LHC reach and enabling first measurements of previously unconstrained operators.

Abstract: We investigate the sensitivity of a future high-energy muon collider to neutral anomalous quartic gauge couplings (aQGCs) through neutral triboson production processes, specifically $\mu^+\mu^- \to \gamma\gamma\gamma$, $\gamma\gamma Z$, $\gamma ZZ$, and $ZZZ$. Within the Standard Model Effective Field Theory (SMEFT) framework, we focus on genuine dimension-eight transverse operators ($\mathcal{O}_{T_0}$--$\mathcal{O}_{T_9}$). The analysis is performed at prospective center-of-mass energies of $\sqrt{s} = 3\text{ TeV}$ and $10\text{ TeV}$ with integrated luminosities of $1\text{ ab}^{-1}$ and $10\text{ ab}^{-1}$, respectively. Employing an optimized cut-and-count analysis and a $\chi^2$ statistical framework incorporating a $5\%$ systematic uncertainty, we derive projected 95\% confidence level bounds on the corresponding Wilson coefficients. Our results demonstrate that the triphoton channel yields the most stringent constraints, particularly for the $f_{T_8}$ and $f_{T_9}$ coefficients, while channels involving $Z$ bosons exhibit reduced sensitivity owing to leptonic branching ratio suppressions. Furthermore, we report constraints on the $f_{T_3}$ and $f_{T_4}$ coefficients, established here for the first time in this neutral triboson context, highlighting the powerful physics reach of multi-TeV muon colliders in probing electroweak gauge sector interactions.

</details>


### [122] [Towards AI-Agent-Accelerated Research in High-Energy Physics: A Case Study in Monte Carlo Analysis](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.03373)
*Tianqi Gao,Ruobing Jiang*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper evaluates LLM-based AI agents in high-energy physics (HEP) research using a CEPC Bell-observable analysis ($e^+e^- \to ZH$, $Z \to q\bar q$, $H \to ZZ^* \to 4\mu$) as a case study. It tests two modes: (1) instruction-constrained reproduction—achieving ~2% agreement with published $I_3$ central values without target retuning; and (2) open-ended extension—identifying muon isolation as the dominant contributor to hard-to-detector shift via stage-resolved audit and diagnostics. The agent supports full workflow tasks (code, MC, diagnostics, stats), while humans retain control over state changes and interpretation. Results suggest AI’s primary value lies in accelerating scientific iteration—not just coding—and call for systematic benchmarking across HEP analyses using correctness, human intervention, and researcher-time efficiency.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To assess the practical utility of contemporary LLM-based agents in real-world, multistep, reproducible high-energy physics research—specifically whether they can meaningfully assist in complex, computationally intensive, and scientifically rigorous workflows under human supervision.

Method: Two experimental modes are employed on a CEPC Bell-observable analysis: (1) instruction-constrained reproduction—agent reproduces detector-level $I_3$ observables without accessing or optimizing toward published numerical results; analysis definition is fixed pre-comparison; (2) open-ended extension—agent explores unknown physics effects using 200k fast-simulated events, guided by stage-resolved audit and diagnostic reasoning. Agent handles workflow planning, code/config inspection, Monte Carlo production, anomaly diagnosis, control region construction, statistical analysis, and result organization; all state-changing actions and final interpretation remain human-supervised.

Result: In reproduction mode, agent achieves ~2% agreement with four published $I_3$ benchmark points without retuning. In extension mode, it identifies muon isolation as the largest single contributor to the hard-to-detector shift and traces its origin to angle-dependent isolation efficiency, proximity to the $Z \to q\bar q$ system, and mixed electromagnetic activity from muon final-state radiation and hadronic decays. Agent fully supports end-to-end analysis tasks while preserving human oversight.

Conclusion: LLM-based agents can meaningfully accelerate scientific iteration—not just code generation—in HEP. Their value lies in enabling rapid hypothesis exploration, diagnostic reasoning, and workflow orchestration under human supervision. The work motivates establishing standardized benchmarks across historical HEP analyses using correctness, human intervention count, and researcher-time efficiency as core metrics.

Abstract: We investigate how far contemporary LLM-based agents can participate in multistep computational high-energy-physics research under reproducible human supervision. Using a previously published CEPC Bell-observable analysis in $e^+e^- \to ZH$, $Z \to q\bar q$, $H \to ZZ^* \to 4\mu$ as a case study, we test two capabilities. First, the agent performs an instruction-constrained reproduction: it is explicitly instructed not to use the published numerical results as analysis targets, although no technical information-access barrier is imposed. The analysis definition is fixed before the formal comparison, and the resulting detector-level $I_3$ central values reproduce the four published benchmark points to within about 2% without retuning to those values. Second, the agent is used for an open-ended extension with no target answer supplied. Using 200,000 fast-simulated events, a stage-resolved audit identifies muon isolation as the largest single contribution to the hard-to-detector shift, and subsequent diagnostics connect the effect to angle-dependent isolation efficiency, proximity to the hard $Z \to q\bar q$ system, and mixed electromagnetic activity from muon final-state radiation and hadron decays. Throughout, the agent assists with workflow planning, code and configuration inspection, Monte Carlo production, anomaly diagnosis, control construction, statistical analysis, and result organization, while state-changing actions and final interpretation remain under human control. The study shows that the value of AI agents in HEP may lie not only in faster code generation, but in faster scientific iteration, and motivates systematic benchmarking across historical HEP analyses using correctness, human intervention, and researcher-time efficiency as explicit metrics.

</details>


### [123] [Polarization in $Λ\to pμ^-\barν_μ$ as a probe of CP-violating new physics](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.03375)
*Qazi Maaz Us Salam,Ishtiaq Ahmed*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper studies the normal muon polarization in Λ→pμ⁻ν̄_μ decay as a CP-violating probe of new physics, finding that only a specific pseudoscalar coupling with a particular phase can yield a signal exceeding the SM electromagnetic final-state interaction; the Λ–Λ̄ polarization difference isolates the NP contribution.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To probe CP-violating new physics beyond the Standard Model, leveraging the fact that normal μ polarization vanishes at tree level in the SM and is thus a clean observable for imaginary parts of NP couplings.

Method: Uses an effective Lagrangian framework combined with lattice QCD–calculated hadronic form factors to compute the normal muon polarization, incorporating constraints from kaon decays and LHC on individual NP couplings; accounts for SM electromagnetic final-state interactions (FSI) as background.

Result: For all NP couplings except a pseudoscalar one with a specific phase, the predicted polarization remains below the SM FSI contribution; that pseudoscalar coupling yields a polarization several times larger than the FSI background. The FSI-induced polarization is identical in Λ and Λ̄ decays, so their difference cancels SM FSI and isolates the NP effect.

Conclusion: Normal μ polarization in Λ→pμ⁻ν̄_μ is a viable, though challenging, probe of CP-violating new physics; the Λ–Λ̄ polarization asymmetry provides a theoretically clean observable, with sensitivity strongest for a pseudoscalar NP interaction with a favorable phase.

Abstract: We investigate the normal $\mu$ polarization in the hyperon decay $\Lambda\to p\mu^-\bar\nu_\mu$ as a probe of new physics that violates CP, since this polarization vanishes at tree level in the Standard Model. Using an effective Lagrangian and lattice QCD form factors, we compute its sensitivity to the imaginary parts of the couplings. With the present bounds from kaon decays and the Large Hadron Collider, one coupling at a time, this sensitivity gives an effect below the Standard Model contribution from the electromagnetic final-state interaction. Only a pseudoscalar coupling with a particular phase can exceed this contribution, by several times. The final-state interaction gives the same polarization in $\Lambda$ and $\bar\Lambda$ decays, so the difference between the two decays isolates the new physics effect.

</details>


### [124] [Non-Global and Super-Leading Logarithms in Higgs Production through Vector Boson Fusion](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.03407)
*Thomas Becher,Patrick Hager,Matthias Neubert,Nicolas Schalch,Josua Scholze,Dominik Schwienbacher*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper performs the first combined resummation of non-global and super-leading logarithms in vector-boson-fusion Higgs production and its QCD dijet background, using Marzini parton shower for non-global logs and Glauber exchange formalism for super-leading logs at Nc=3, to assess their impact on jet-vetoed cross sections and signal/background suppression.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To investigate correlated soft radiation and Glauber-induced breakdown of color coherence in vector-boson-fusion (VBF) Higgs production and its QCD-induced Higgs-plus-dijet background under a rapidity gap (jet-veto) condition between widely separated tagging jets.

Method: Resums leading non-global logarithms using the leading-color parton shower framework Marzini, and super-leading logarithms via Glauber exchange contributions evaluated at Nc=3; combines both resummed contributions for phenomenological analysis.

Result: Quantifies the joint effect of non-global and super-leading logarithms on gap survival probabilities and reveals differential suppression between VBF Higgs signal and QCD background under jet-veto conditions.

Conclusion: The combined resummation significantly affects gap cross sections and alters the signal-to-background ratio in jet-vetoed VBF Higgs analyses, demonstrating that both non-global and super-leading logarithmic corrections must be consistently included for precision predictions.

Abstract: We present the first combined phenomenological study of non-global and super-leading logarithms in vector-boson-fusion Higgs production and its QCD-induced Higgs-plus-dijet background. A veto on radiation between the widely separated tagging jets provides a natural setting to investigate correlated soft emission and Glauber-induced violations of color coherence. We resum the leading non-global logarithms with the leading-color parton shower framework Marzili and the super-leading series with Glauber exchanges at $N_c=3$. Combining the two resummed contributions, we study their impact on gap cross sections and on the relative suppression of signal and background.

</details>


### [125] [Higgs pair production and Higgs potential in arbitrary renormalisable extensions of the Higgs sector](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.03476)
*Henning Bahl,Johannes Braathen,Martin Gabelmann,Kateryna Radchenko Serdula,Georg Weiglein*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper introduces 'anyHH', an automated tool for computing the di-Higgs production cross-section in gluon fusion within arbitrary renormalisable BSM Higgs sector extensions, enabling precise comparison of theory and experiment for probing trilinear Higgs self-couplings and BSM physics.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To address the need for accurate, model-independent predictions of Higgs boson pair production—especially when light BSM particles invalidate standard EFT approximations—and to enhance sensitivity to deviations from SM predictions of the Higgs potential and trilinear self-coupling.

Method: Development of 'anyHH', a fully automated computational tool implementing Feynman diagrammatic calculation of the gg → hh cross-section at leading order in gluon fusion, supporting arbitrary renormalisable Higgs-sector extensions (e.g., multi-Higgs-doublet models, singlet extensions) with full UV-resolved loop contributions.

Result: A publicly available, flexible, and validated tool capable of computing di-Higgs cross-sections across diverse BSM scenarios beyond EFT validity, demonstrated on benchmark models including 2HDM and Higgs-singlet extensions.

Conclusion: anyHH bridges the gap between high-precision experimental measurements of di-Higgs production at HL-LHC and theoretically robust predictions in generic BSM frameworks, thereby strengthening the use of Higgs self-interactions as a probe of the Higgs potential and new physics.

Abstract: Higgs self-interactions are crucial for probing the up to now largely unknown form of the Higgs potential that is realised in nature. Higgs boson pair production at the (High-Luminosity) LHC is of prime importance in this context as it provides experimental access to the trilinear Higgs self-interaction that enters at leading order in this process. The predictions for the trilinear Higgs self-couplings and the shape of the Higgs potential can vastly differ from the predictions of the Standard Model (SM) as a consequence of physics beyond the SM (BSM). If additional particles are relatively light (but in agreement with all existing experimental and theoretical constraints), their effects may not necessarily be accurately described by established effective field theory methods. Because of the potentially large modifications compared to the SM predictions the di-Higgs process has a high sensitivity for probing BSM physics. In order to enable the comparison between experiment and theory for a wide range of BSM models, automatised predictions for double Higgs production are in order. In this contribution to the LHC Higgs Working Group Report 5 we present \texttt{anyHH}, a novel tool for calculating the Higgs boson pair production cross-section in gluon fusion, which is the dominant production process for two Higgs bosons at the (HL-)LHC, for arbitrary renormalisable extensions of the Higgs sector.

</details>


### [126] [Study on the charmless decay modes of $Z_{cs}(3985)$](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.03480)
*Yi-Zhuo Zhou,Shi-Dong Liu,Qi Wu,Qing-Ping Ji,Dian-Yong Chen,Gang Li*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper studies charmless decays of the hidden-charm tetraquark $Z_{cs}(3985)$, modeled as an $S$-wave $D_s^{*-}D^0/D_s^-D^{*0}$ molecular state with $I(J^P)=\frac{1}{2}(1^+)$, using triangle-loop diagrams and effective Lagrangians; branching ratios for $Z_{cs}\to \pi K^*$ reach $\mathcal{O}(10^{-2})$, comparable to hidden-charm modes; results show form-factor dependence (dipole vs. tripole), cutoff-insensitive relative ratios (except some), and strong sensitivity of $\eta K^*$ to $\eta$–$\eta'$ mixing angle—unlike $\eta' K^*$, which is robust and thus experimentally clean.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To probe the internal structure and decay dynamics of the exotic $Z_{cs}(3985)$ state—particularly its charmless hadronic decays—as a test of its proposed $D_s^{*-}D^0/D_s^-D^{*0}$ molecular nature and to identify experimentally accessible channels that constrain theoretical models.

Method: Effective Lagrangian approach with $S$-wave hadronic molecule assumption; calculation of $Z_{cs} \to \mathcal{P}\mathcal{V}$ ($\mathcal{P}$: light pseudoscalar, $\mathcal{V}$: light vector meson) decay amplitudes via triangle-loop diagrams; dipole and tripole form factors employed; cutoff parameter constrained using experimental upper limit on $Z_c(3900) \to \pi\omega$.

Result: Branching ratio for $Z_{cs} \to \pi K^*$ reaches $\sim 10^{-2}$ (dipole); $Z_{cs} \to \eta K^*$ highly sensitive to $\eta$–$\eta'$ mixing angle; $Z_{cs} \to \eta' K^*$ nearly insensitive—making it a clean probe; relative branching ratios mostly cutoff-independent but form-factor-dependent; tripole results inconsistent with dipole, indicating model dependence.

Conclusion: The $Z_{cs}(3985)$'s charmless decays are sizable and experimentally viable, supporting its molecular interpretation; $\eta' K^*$ is a theoretically robust channel for future detection, while form-factor choice and mixing-angle uncertainties must be accounted for in precision tests.

Abstract: In this work, we investigate the charmless decay modes of the hidden-charm tetraquark state with strangeness, $Z_{cs}(3985)$, assuming it to be an $S$-wave hadronic molecule of $D_{s}^{*-}D^{0}$ and $D_{s}^{-}D^{*0}$ with quantum numbers $I(J^{P}) = \frac{1}{2}(1^{+})$. Using an effective Lagrangian approach, we calculate the decay amplitudes for $Z_{cs} \to \mathcal{PV}$ (where $\mathcal P$ and $\mathcal V$ denote light pseudoscalar and vector mesons, respectively) via triangle loop diagrams. We use the experimental upper limit on $Z_c(3900)\to \pi\omega$ to constrain the cutoff parameter for both the dipole and tripole form factors. Numerical results from the dipole form factor show that the branching ratios for $Z_{cs} \to \pi K^*$ can reach up to the order of $10^{-2}$, which is of the same order of magnitude as the hidden charm decay modes and may be detected in future measurements. The branching ratios from tripole form factor are not consistent with those from the dipole form factor, indicating a certain degree of model dependence in the present work. The relative ratios of the branching ratios for different decay channels are found to be almost independent of the cutoff parameter. However, some ratios are highly dependent on the choice of the dipole or tripole form factor. The branching ratio of $Z_{cs} \to \eta K^{*}$ is found to be highly sensitive to the $\eta$-$\eta^\prime$ mixing angle, while that for $Z_{cs} \to \eta^\prime K^{*}$ is almost independent of the mixing angle, making it a clean channel for future experimental measurements.

</details>


<div id='hep-ex'></div>

# hep-ex [[Back]](#toc)

### [127] [LH$μ$C Concept: Energy-Frontier Antimuon-Proton Collisions at CERN](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.01117)
*Arda Goroglu,Saleh Sultansoy*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: Proposes the LHμC, an antimuon-proton collider based on HL-LHC infrastructure, achieving √s = 5.3 TeV and high luminosity; compares muon-beam options, outlines detector design, physics reach (e.g., colour-octet muons, RPV squarks), and key technical challenges.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Energy-frontier lepton-hadron colliders are essential for precision QCD studies and BSM physics exploration; existing electron-proton colliders lack energy reach, while muons offer superior mass-driven synchrotron radiation advantages over electrons.

Method: Analyzes two μp collider realizations at LHC: (1) muon-collider beams, and (2) ultra-cold μ⁺ beams à la μTRISTAN, using a μTRISTAN-type booster and repurposed LHeC ERL tunnel; performs luminosity and center-of-mass energy calculations; outlines detector concept and physics benchmark processes.

Result: Achieves √s = 5.3 TeV with 1 TeV antimuons and 7 TeV protons; peak luminosities of 8.8×10³³ and 7.2×10³³ cm⁻²s⁻¹ for two configurations; identifies viable physics channels including colour-octet muons and R-parity-violating squark production.

Conclusion: The LHμC is a technically challenging but feasible extension of HL-LHC infrastructure offering unique QCD and BSM discovery potential; μTRISTAN-based cold μ⁺ beams represent a promising path forward pending resolution of beam cooling, stability, and background mitigation challenges.

Abstract: The concept of the LH$\mu$C, an energy-frontier antimuon-proton collider based on the HL-LHC, is presented. After recalling why energy-frontier lepton-hadron colliders are indispensable as QCD explorers and reviewing the history of muon-proton collider proposals, two ways of realising $\mu p$ collisions at the LHC are compared: with muon-collider beams and with ultra-cold $\mu^+$ beams as proposed for $\mu$TRISTAN. For the latter, a $\mu$TRISTAN-type booster and the re-use of the LHeC energy-recovery-linac tunnel are discussed; colliding 1 TeV antimuons with 7 TeV protons gives $\sqrt{s}=5.3$ TeV and peak luminosities of $8.8\times10^{33}$ and $7.2\times10^{33}$ cm$^{-2}$s$^{-1}$, respectively. The detector concept and the physics potential, including searches for colour-octet muons and R-parity-violating squarks, are discussed together with the main challenges.

</details>


### [128] [Reinforcement Learning in High-Energy Physics: A Survey from Accelerator Control to Theory Exploration](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.02049)
*Luca Anzalone*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: This survey reviews reinforcement learning (RL) applications in high-energy physics (HEP), covering accelerator control, tracking, jet grooming, BSM model building, detector design, and more; it compares RL methods (model-free, model-based, multi-agent, offline, etc.) against traditional approaches, identifies domains where RL excels (e.g., fast simulators, real-time control, physics-based rewards), and highlights persistent challenges: reward design, sim-to-real transfer, safety, benchmark scarcity, and statistical validity.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Many HEP tasks are closed-loop, sequential decision problems with sparse/delayed feedback, stochastic environments, and objectives evaluated on real hardware or simulations — settings where supervised/unsupervised learning fail but RL is naturally suited.

Method: Survey-based analysis covering RL paradigms (model-free, model-based, multi-agent, offline, inverse RL, bandits), their adaptation to HEP domains, and comparative evaluation against domain-specific baselines.

Result: RL has been successfully deployed in real accelerator control and data-quality monitoring; shows promise in jet clustering, tracking, and formal QFT computation; outperforms traditional methods in fast-loop decisions, fault-tolerant operation, and physics-driven reward settings; Bayesian optimization, genetic algorithms, and differentiable methods remain competitive in other regimes.

Conclusion: RL is a powerful and increasingly viable tool for HEP’s sequential decision problems, but widespread adoption is hindered by recurring challenges: principled reward engineering, robust sim-to-real transfer, safety guarantees, absence of standardized benchmarks, and statistically sound evaluation of policy-driven analyses.

Abstract: Reinforcement learning (RL) is a machine learning paradigm in which an agent learns a decision-making policy by interacting with an environment, guided by a scalar reward rather than explicit supervision. Supervised and unsupervised learning have become indispensable tools in high-energy physics (HEP), but they learn from fixed datasets, whereas many HEP tasks are closed-loop decisions: the data an algorithm observes depend on its choices, the feedback is sparse or delayed, and the objective is often measured on a real machine or in a stochastic simulation, not by a differentiable model. This survey reviews the application of RL to such tasks: accelerator control and feedback, data quality monitoring, jet grooming, clustering, and representation, charged particle tracking, beyond-the-Standard-Model (BSM) and string model building, detector design, trigger control, analysis, and formal quantum field theory computation. It provides a self-contained introduction to the underlying RL methods, from model-free and model-based to multi-agent, offline, inverse, and bandit approaches, reviews each domain against its traditional methods, and summarizes the head-to-head comparisons. The applications range from policies deployed on real accelerators and evaluated on recorded collision data to exploratory studies in formal theory. RL is most advantageous for repeated tasks with a fast simulator, decisions inside fast control loops, operation under faults and corrupted measurements, physics-based rewards in place of labels, and sequential discrete constructions with a reusable policy, while Bayesian optimization, genetic algorithms, and differentiable methods remain competitive elsewhere. We conclude with the open challenges that recur across domains, including reward design, sim-to-real transfer, safety, the lack of shared benchmarks, and the statistical validity of policy-driven decisions.

</details>


### [129] [Search for $\mathrm{GeV}$-scale axion-like particles in PbPb collisions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00135)
*LHCb collaboration,R. Aaij,M. Abdelfatah,A.S.W. Abdelmotteleb,C. Abellan Beteta,F. Abudinén,T. Ackernley,A.A. Adefisoye,B. Adeva,M. Adinolfi,P. Adlarson,C. Agapopoulou,C.A. Aidala,S. Akar,K. Akiba,H. Al Saleh,P. Albicocco,J. Albrecht,R. Aleksiejunas,F. Alessio,P. Alvarez Cartelle,S. Amato,J.L. Amey,Y. Amhis,Z. Amos,L. An,L. Anderlini,P. Andreola,M. Andreotti,S. Andres Estrada,A. Anelli,D. Ao,C. Arata,F. Archilli,Z. Areg,M. Argenton,S. Arguedas Cuendis,L. Arnone,M. Artuso,E. Aslanides,R. Ataíde Da Silva,M. Atzeni,B. Audurier,J.A. Authier,D. Bacher,I. Bachiller Perea,S. Bachmann,M. Bachmayer,J.J. Back,M. Bai,Z.B. Bai,V. Balagura,A. Balboni,W. Baldini,Z. Baldwin,L. Balzani,H. Bao,J. Baptista de Souza Leite,C. Barbero Pretel,M. Barbetti,I.R. Barbosa,W. Barker,R.J. Barlow,M. Barnyakov,S. Baron,S. Barsuk,W. Barter,J. Bartz,S. Bashir,B. Batsukh,P.B. Battista,A. Bavarchee,A. Bay,A. Beck,M. Becker,F. Bedeschi,I.B. Bediaga,N.A. Behling,S. Belin,A. Bellavista,I. Belyaev,G. Bencivenni,E. Ben-Haim,J.L.M. Berkey,R. Bernet,A. Bertolin,L. Bertsch,F. Betti,J. Bex,O. Bezshyyko,S. Bhattacharya,M.S. Bieker,N.V. Biesuz,A. Biolchini,M. Birch,F.C.R. Bishop,A. Bitadze,A. Bizzeti,T. Blake,F. Blanc*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: First LHCb search for axion-like particles (ALPs) in PbPb collisions via exclusive γγ → a → γγ in the forward region; sets strongest limits for 3–5 GeV ALP masses.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
