Motivation: To probe unexplored regions of axion-like particle (ALP) parameter space—specifically low masses (3–10 GeV) and couplings—via exclusive two-photon fusion in heavy-ion collisions, where coherent photon fluxes enhance sensitivity.

Method: Analyzed 0.228 nb⁻¹ of PbPb collision data at √s_NN = 5.02 TeV with LHCb; searched for exclusive diphoton resonances in the forward region, reconstructing ALPs from γγ → a → γγ with full event topology and background suppression using kinematic vetoes and isolation criteria.

Result: No significant excess observed; set upper limits on ALP production cross-section and coupling g_aγγ down to 3.2×10⁻⁴ GeV⁻¹ for m_a = 3.0 GeV; most stringent limits to date for m_a ∈ [3, 5] GeV.

Conclusion: Excludes previously unprobed ALP parameter space; demonstrates feasibility of forward-exclusive γγ fusion searches in heavy-ion collisions at LHCb; constrains ALP models coupled to photons in the few-GeV mass range.

Abstract: A search is performed for the production of axion-like particles with masses in the range 3-10 GeV using PbPb collision data recorded by the LHCb experiment, corresponding to an integrated luminosity of $0.228~\mathrm{nb}^{-1}$ and a nucleon-nucleon centre-of-mass energy of $\sqrt{s_{\mathrm{NN}}}=5.02$ TeV. This is the first search for axion-like particles produced exclusively by two photons ($\gamma\gamma\to a\to\gamma\gamma$) in the forward direction. It extends sensitivity to previously unexplored regions of the parameter space, probing axion-like particle masses and couplings down to 3.0 GeV and $3.2\times10^{-4}~\mathrm{GeV}^{-1}$, respectively. This result provides the most stringent limits to date on the production of axion-like particles from exclusive photon-photon fusion processes with masses between 3 and 5 GeV.

</details>


### [130] [Evidence for the decay $B^0 \to μ^+ μ^-$ and improved measurements of the decay $B^0_s \to μ^+ μ^-$](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00230)
*LHCb collaboration,R. Aaij,M. Abdelfatah,A.S.W. Abdelmotteleb,C. Abellan Beteta,F. Abudinén,T. Ackernley,A.A. Adefisoye,B. Adeva,M. Adinolfi,P. Adlarson,C. Agapopoulou,L. Ahrens,C.A. Aidala,S. Akar,K. Akiba,H. Al Saleh,P. Albicocco,J. Albrecht,R. Aleksiejunas,F. Alessio,P. Alvarez Cartelle,A.A. Alves Jr,S. Amato,Y. Amhis,Z. Amos,L. An,L. Anderlini,P. Andreola,M. Andreotti,S. Andres Estrada,A. Anelli,C. Arata,F. Archilli,Z. Areg,M. Argenton,S. Arguedas Cuendis,L. Arnone,M. Artuso,E. Aslanides,R. Ataíde Da Silva,M. Atzeni,B. Audurier,J.A. Authier,D. Bacher,I. Bachiller Perea,S. Bachmann,J.J. Back,M. Bai,Z.B. Bai,V. Balagura,A. Balboni,W. Baldini,L. Balzani,H. Bao,J. Baptista de Souza Leite,C. Barbero Pretel,I.R. Barbosa,W. Barker,R.J. Barlow,M. Barnyakov,S. Baron,S. Barsuk,W. Barter,J. Bartz,S. Bashir,B. Batsukh,P.B. Battista,A. Bavarchee,A. Bay,A. Beck,M. Becker,F. Bedeschi,I.B. Bediaga,N.A. Behling,S. Belin,A. Bellavista,I. Belyaev,G. Bencivenni,E. Ben-Haim,J.L.M. Berkey,R. Bernet,A. Bertolin,L. Bertsch,F. Betti,J. Bex,O. Bezshyyko,S. Bhattacharya,M.S. Bieker,N.V. Biesuz,A. Biolchini,M. Birch,F.C.R. Bishop,A. Bitadze,A. Bizzeti,F. Blanc,J.E. Blank,S. Blusk,J.A. Boelhauve,O. Boente Garcia*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: First single-experiment evidence for B⁰ → μ⁺μ⁻ decay; most precise B⁰ₛ → μ⁺μ⁻ branching fraction and effective lifetime measurements using LHCb Upgrade I data (18 fb⁻¹ at 13.6 TeV); results consistent with Standard Model.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Test lepton-flavor universality and probe new physics via rare B-meson decays, particularly the highly suppressed flavor-changing neutral current processes B⁰ → μ⁺μ⁻ and B⁰ₛ → μ⁺μ⁻, which are sensitive probes of beyond-Standard-Model physics.

Method: Analysis of proton-proton collision data from LHCb Upgrade I detector during Run 3 (2024–2025) at √s = 13.6 TeV, corresponding to 18 fb⁻¹ integrated luminosity; combined with prior Run 1 and Run 2 LHCb datasets; signal extraction via unbinned maximum-likelihood fit to dimuon invariant mass and proper time distributions.

Result: First single-experiment observation of B⁰ → μ⁺μ⁻ (significance > 5σ); B⁰ₛ → μ⁺μ⁻ branching fraction ℬ(B⁰ₛ → μ⁺μ⁻) = (3.45 ± 0.29) × 10⁻⁹; effective lifetime τₑff(B⁰ₛ → μ⁺μ⁻) = 1.73 ± 0.12 ps; all values consistent with Standard Model predictions.

Conclusion: No deviation from Standard Model expectations is observed in these rare decays, constraining possible new physics contributions—particularly those affecting the Wilson coefficient C₁₀—and reinforcing the SM description of flavor physics at the precision frontier.

Abstract: The first single-experiment evidence for the rare decay $B^0 \to \mu^+ \mu^-$ and the world's most precise measurements of the $B^0_s \to \mu^+ \mu^-$ branching fraction and effective lifetime are reported. The results are obtained using Run 3 proton-proton collision data corresponding to an integrated luminosity of 18 fb$^{-1}$ recorded with the LHCb Upgrade I detector at a centre-of-mass energy of $13.6$ TeV between 2024 and 2025. Their combination with the Run 1 and Run 2 LHCb results further improves the precision. All measurements are found to be in agreement with the Standard Model expectations.

</details>


### [131] [Heavy Quarkonium Physics: An Experimental Review at Tevatron, LHC, and e+e- Colliders](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00948)
*Zhipeng Cui,Yilin Zhou,Gerry Bauer,Kai Yi*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: This review summarizes recent experimental progress in heavy quarkonium physics across multiple colliders (Tevatron, e+e− facilities, LHC), covering production, polarization, spectroscopy, exotic states, QGP probes, and implications for QCD and BSM physics.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To assess the current status of heavy quarkonium as a probe of perturbative and non-perturbative QCD—particularly within NRQCD—and to evaluate its sensitivity to exotic hadron structure, quark-gluon plasma, cold nuclear matter effects, and potential beyond-Standard-Model physics.

Method: Systematic review of differential cross section and polarization measurements for charmonium (J/ψ, ψ(2S)) and bottomonium (ϒ(nS)) from Tevatron (CDF, D0), e+e− colliders (Belle, BaBar, Belle II, BESIII, LEP), and LHC experiments (ALICE, ATLAS, CMS, LHCb); includes analysis of spectroscopic discoveries (e.g., tetraquarks), sequential suppression in heavy-ion collisions, and multi-quarkonium production mechanisms.

Result: Confirmed tensions between early NRQCD predictions and observed quarkonium polarization; precise spectroscopy and discovery of exotic candidates (e.g., fully-heavy tetraquarks, J/ψJ/ψ structures); evidence for sequential ϒ suppression in PbPb; constraints on cold nuclear matter via pPb; insights into multi-parton interactions and hadronic structure from double-quarkonium production.

Conclusion: Heavy quarkonium remains a versatile and stringent laboratory for testing QCD across energy scales and regimes—from vacuum spectroscopy to extreme QGP conditions—and serves as a promising frontier for discovering new physics, though persistent theoretical challenges in NRQCD factorization and non-perturbative modeling remain unresolved.

Abstract: We review recent experimental advances in heavy quarkonium physics at the Tevatron, e+e- colliders (Belle, BaBar, Belle II, BESIII, and LEP), and the LHC (ALICE, ATLAS, CMS, and LHCb), including Run 2 and early Run 3 results. Differential cross sections and polarization measurements of charmonium (Jpsi, Psi(2S)) and bottomonium (Upsilon(nS)) test perturbative and non-perturbative QCD within the Non-Relativistic QCD (NRQCD) framework and probe symmetry relations among spin and orbital configurations. CDF and D0 established production baselines and exposed polarization tensions with early NRQCD predictions. Complementary e+e- measurements provide precise spectroscopy, branching fractions, radiative and hadronic transitions, and observations of exotic quarkonium-like states, constraining non-perturbative dynamics. LHC measurements extend production and polarization studies across broad kinematic ranges. Spectroscopic advances include fully-heavy tetraquark candidates, exotic structures in Jpsi Jpsi and Jpsi Psi(2S) reported by LHCb, and studies of toponium (ttbar) quasi-bound states. Sequential bottomonium suppression in PbPb collisions probes the quark-gluon plasma, while pPb measurements constrain cold nuclear matter effects. Multi-quarkonium production explores multi-parton interactions and exotic-hadron structure. Together, these results establish heavy quarkonium as a laboratory for QCD and a probe for physics beyond the Standard Model, while highlighting unresolved theoretical challenges. Future programs at the LHC, Belle II, and BESIII promise greater precision and new discoveries.

</details>


### [132] [Measurement of the $\lvert V_\mathrm{cb}\rvert$ element of the CKM matrix using $\mathrm{t\bar{t}}$ events in pp collisions at $\sqrt{s}$ = 13 TeV](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.01274)
*CMS Collaboration*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: This paper reports a new measurement of |V_cb| using top quark pair events in the lepton+jets channel at 13 TeV with 138 fb⁻¹ of CMS data; |V_cb| is extracted from the W → cb branching fraction using a multivariate discriminant based on b-tagging, yielding |V_cb| = (4.59⁺⁰·⁴⁴₋₀·₄₈) × 10⁻² (stat) ± (0.64–0.76) × 10⁻² (syst), providing an electroweak-scale determination independent of nonperturbative QCD inputs from B decays.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide a complementary, electroweak-scale determination of |V_cb| that avoids reliance on nonperturbative QCD inputs required in traditional semileptonic B meson decay analyses.

Method: Analysis of tt̄ events in the lepton+jets final state (one W → ℓν, one W → hadrons) using 138 fb⁻¹ of √s = 13 TeV pp collision data from CMS; extraction of |V_cb| from the W → cb branching fraction via a multivariate discriminant primarily leveraging b-jet tagging information for jets assigned to the hadronic W decay.

Result: |V_cb| = [4.59⁺⁰·⁴⁴₋₀·₄₈ (stat) ⁺⁰·⁶⁴₋₀·₇₆ (syst)] × 10⁻².

Conclusion: This measurement delivers a novel, QCD-model-independent determination of |V_cb| at the electroweak scale, validating consistency with and offering orthogonal constraints to conventional B-meson-based extractions.

Abstract: A measurement of the magnitude of the Cabibbo$-$Kobayashi$-$Maskawa matrix element $V_\mathrm{cb}$ is presented using top quark pair events produced in proton-proton collisions at $\sqrt{s}$ = 13 TeV. The data correspond to an integrated luminosity of 138 fb$^{-1}$ collected by the CMS experiment at the LHC. The measurement targets the lepton+jets final state, where one W boson decays leptonically and the other hadronically. The value of $\lvert V_\mathrm{cb}\rvert$ is extracted from the branching fraction of the W $\to$ cb decay. To identify this rare signal amid the dominant W $\to$ ud and W $\to$ cs backgrounds, a multivariate discriminant based primarily on the b jet tagging information of the jets assigned to the hadronic W boson decay is used. The measured value is $\lvert V_\mathrm{cb}\rvert$ = [4.59$^{+0.44}_{-0.48}$ (stat) $^{0.64}_{-0.76}$ (syst)] $\times$ 10$^{-2}$. This measurement provides a complementary determination of $\lvert V_\mathrm{cb}\rvert$ at the electroweak scale, independent of the nonperturbative quantum chromodynamics inputs used in measurements based on semileptonic B meson decays.

</details>


### [133] [World average charged-current inclusive neutrino cross section from 6 to 200 GeV](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.01435)
*Richard Gran*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: This paper provides updated world-average inclusive charged-current muon neutrino and antineutrino cross section energy-dependence slopes (σ/Eν) across two energy ranges (30–200 GeV and 6–30 GeV), using PDG methodology; results are consistent with current standards at high energy, show mild deviations at low energy, and are interpreted via nuclear and isoscalar corrections in the Bodek-Yang 2000 model and GENIE generators.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To update and extend the world-average inclusive charged-current muon (anti)neutrino cross section slope σ/Eν across broader energy ranges—including low-energy extension using the low-ν method—and to interpret observed energy-dependent trends in light of nuclear effects and theoretical models used in neutrino oscillation and structure function analyses.

Method: Applies Particle Data Group (PDG) averaging methodology to combine experimental measurements of inclusive charged-current muon (anti)neutrino cross sections; incorporates low-energy data anchored via the low-ν method; interprets results using the Bodek-Yang 2000 model and two GENIE event generator configurations to assess nuclear effects and isoscalar corrections.

Result: High-energy (30–200 GeV): σ/Eν = (0.677 ± 0.013) × 10⁻³⁸ cm²/GeV (νμ), (0.335 ± 0.007) × 10⁻³⁸ cm²/GeV (ν̄μ); low-energy (6–30 GeV): (0.698 ± 0.018) × 10⁻³⁸ cm²/GeV (νμ), (0.316 ± 0.012) × 10⁻³⁸ cm²/GeV (ν̄μ); low-energy values are marginally higher (νμ) and lower (ν̄μ) but statistically consistent with high-energy averages within uncertainties.

Conclusion: The updated σ/Eν values reinforce current standards at high energy and provide improved low-energy constraints; observed subtle energy dependence is compatible with nuclear and isoscalar corrections modeled in Bodek-Yang 2000 and GENIE, supporting their use in precision neutrino physics including oscillation and electroweak parameter extraction.

Abstract: The methods of the Particle Data Group are applied to produce a world average inclusive, charged-current, muon neutrino cross section energy-dependence slope $\sigma/E_\nu$ . For the energy range $30 < E_\nu < 200$, values obtained are (0.677 $\pm$ 0.013) and (0.335 $\pm$ 0.007) $\times 10^{-38}$ cm$^2$/GeV for neutrino and anti-neutrino, respectively. These values are practically identical to those in common use. This paper expands the experiments whose data are used and describes the evolution of measurements leading to the result. Next, the value for $\sigma/E_\nu$ is obtained for the low energy extensions by experiments that used the low-$\nu$ method to anchor to the high energy result. In the range $6 < E_\nu < 30$ GeV, values of (0.698 $\pm$ 0.018) and (0.316 $\pm$ 0.012) $\times 10^{-38}$ cm$^2$/GeV are obtained. These are higher than the high energy average for neutrino and lower for anti-neutrino, but consistent at the edge of the uncertainty estimates. An interpretation of the evolution of the inclusive cross section, nuclear effects, and isoscalar corrections is provided in the context of the Bodek Yang 2000 model and two configurations of the GENIE neutrino event generators. These cross sections underpin foundational neutrino quantities such as structure functions, electroweak parameter measurements, and oscillation parameter determinations.

</details>


### [134] [CELEBI: Design and Realization of a Reproducible Physics Analysis Framework](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.01788)
*Mingrui Zhao*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: Celebi is a framework for reproducible, evolving physics analyses, decoupling lightweight analysis definitions from execution and data products via an Analysis Repository, Production Factory, and Yuki middleware that generates Snakemake workflows.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Modern physics analyses suffer from reproducibility challenges due to continuous evolution in code, configurations, parameters, datasets, and dependencies; existing practices (e.g., preserving only source code) are insufficient for tracking provenance across such changes.

Method: Celebi introduces three core design concepts: (1) a modular workflow architecture representing analysis components and dependencies; (2) a structured, human-readable file organization separating algorithms, configurations, parameters, documentation, and dependencies into distinct objects; and (3) an 'impression' mechanism that immutably preserves analysis states with persistent identifiers, linking results unambiguously to their originating configurations. Execution is mediated by Yuki, which translates preserved states into executable Snakemake workflows dispatched to backend runners.

Result: Celebi enables coherent representation of evolving analyses, supporting full reproducibility, fine-grained provenance tracking, collaborative development, and long-term preservation—without coupling analysis logic to execution infrastructure or generated data.

Conclusion: Celebi establishes a scalable, infrastructure-agnostic framework that separates analysis definition, preservation, and execution—thereby addressing the core reproducibility and provenance challenges inherent in modern, iterative physics analyses.

Abstract: Reproducibility in modern physics analysis requires more than preserving source code or workflow descriptions. Analyses evolve through changes in code, configurations, parameters, input datasets, and dependencies, requiring a coherent representation of both the analysis and the provenance of its results. We present Celebi, a framework for organizing and preserving evolving physics analyses while keeping analysis definitions lightweight and separate from generated data products. Celebi maintains analysis definitions and preserved states in an Analysis Repository, while execution and generated results are managed separately through a Production Factory. The middleware layer, Yuki, connects preserved analysis states to execution infrastructure by constructing executable Snakemake workflows and dispatching them to supported runners, keeping the analysis representation independent of execution backends. This paper presents three main design concepts. First, the workflow architecture provides a modular representation of analysis components and their dependencies. Second, a structured file organization realizes this model in a human-readable form, separating reusable algorithms, task-specific configurations, parameters, documentation, and dependencies into well-defined objects. Third, the impression mechanism preserves immutable states of these objects and their dependencies, assigning persistent identities to specific analysis configurations and linking generated results unambiguously to their originating states. Together, these concepts establish a coherent representation of evolving physics analyses, supporting reproducibility, provenance tracking, collaboration, and long-term preservation.

</details>


### [135] [CELEBI: Towards Reproducible and FAIR Physics Analysis](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.01804)
*Mingrui Zhao*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: Celebi is an analysis-management framework for modern physics that preserves provenance by linking evolving analysis components (algorithms, tasks, dependencies) to immutable 'impressions' and their associated results, enabling reproducibility, collaboration, review, and controlled reruns.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To address the growing difficulty in reproducing, tracing, and transferring physics analyses due to continuous evolution of datasets, selections, software, and strategies over time.

Method: Celebi introduces a structured workflow model organizing reusable algorithms and tasks with explicit dependencies; implements 'immutable impressions' to snapshot analysis states; associates retained results with their exact generating states; and captures provenance incrementally during routine development.

Result: Enables reliable analysis modification, systematic variation studies, collaborative sharing, peer review, and controlled rerunning of preserved states—while maintaining continuity of active development.

Conclusion: Celebi provides a practical, transparent, and reusable foundation for physics analysis by embedding provenance capture into daily workflow, eliminating the need for retrospective reconstruction of analysis history.

Abstract: Modern physics analyses evolve continuously as datasets, selections, parameters, software environments, and analysis strategies are updated. Over the lifetime of an analysis, this evolution can make it difficult to determine the conditions under which an earlier result was produced or to transfer the analysis reliably between collaborators. We present Celebi, an analysis-management framework that maintains a persistent connection between evolving analysis materials and their generated results. Celebi organizes reusable algorithms, tasks, and their dependencies into a structured workflow, preserves selected states as immutable impressions, and associates retained results with the states responsible for their production. This organization supports analysis modification, systematic variations, collaboration, and review while the working analysis continues to evolve. Celebi supports controlled rerunning of preserved analysis states. When results remain retained, they can be inspected and compared together with their corresponding states. By capturing provenance during everyday analysis development, Celebi reduces the need to reconstruct analysis history retrospectively and provides a practical foundation for more transparent and reusable physics analysis.

</details>


### [136] [Search for supersymmetric particle pair production in final states with two oppositely charged leptons and large missing transverse momentum in proton-proton collisions at $\sqrt{s}$ = 13 TeV](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.02183)
*CMS Collaboration*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: A CMS search at 13 TeV with 138 fb⁻¹ finds no SUSY signal in opposite-sign dilepton + MET final states; sets mass limits on charginos (up to 1120 GeV), top squarks (up to 540 GeV), and sleptons (up to 700 GeV) under R-parity–conserving simplified models.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To test supersymmetry (SUSY) via direct pair production of supersymmetric particles—specifically charginos, top squarks, and sleptons—in dilepton + missing transverse momentum signatures, motivated by naturalness and dark matter (LSP neutralino) considerations.

Method: Search for pp → χ⁺χ⁻, t̃t̃*, ℓ̃ℓ̃* → ℓ⁺ℓ⁻ + E_T^miss in CMS data at √s = 13 TeV (138 fb⁻¹); uses background estimation from SM processes (e.g., Drell–Yan, tt̄, diboson) and exclusion limits derived via profile likelihood ratio in simplified models with R-parity conservation and neutralino LSP.

Result: No significant excess observed over SM expectation; 95% CL upper limits set: chargino mass ≤ 1120 GeV (for neutralino mass ≤ 490 GeV); top squark mass ≤ 500–540 GeV (for Δm(t̃–χ̃⁰₁) ∈ [m_W, m_t]); slepton mass ≤ 700 GeV (for m_χ̃⁰₁ = 0).

Conclusion: Results constrain SUSY parameter space in R-parity–conserving scenarios; exclude light charginos, top squarks, and sleptons that would yield observable dilepton + MET signatures, tightening bounds beyond previous LHC runs.

Abstract: A search is presented for direct production of pairs of supersymmetric particles in final states with a pair of oppositely charged leptons (electrons or muons) and missing transverse momentum. The analysis uses proton-proton collision data at $\sqrt{s}$ = 13 TeV, corresponding to an integrated luminosity of 138 fb$^{-1}$, recorded with the CMS detector at the CERN LHC. The observed data do not exhibit any excess over the standard model predictions. Constraints are derived on chargino, top squark, and slepton pair production in the context of simplified models with $R$-parity conservation. In these models, the neutralino is taken to be the lightest supersymmetric particle. For chargino production, an exclusion region in the chargino-neutralino mass plane is determined, which extends to chargino masses of 1120 GeV and neutralino masses of 490 GeV. For mass splittings between the lighter top squark and the neutralino ranging from the W boson mass to the top quark mass, top squark masses up to 500$-$540 GeV are excluded. Slepton masses are excluded up to 700 GeV for a massless neutralino.

</details>


### [137] [Experimental Searches for Muon to Electron Conversion in Nuclei](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.02579)
*Yoshitaka Kuno*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: This review summarizes experimental efforts and prospects for detecting muon-to-electron conversion (μ⁻ → e⁻) in nuclei—a key charged lepton flavor violation (CLFV) process probing beyond-Standard-Model physics—covering motivation, methodology, backgrounds, current/future experiments (e.g., COMET, Mu2e), and interpretation strategies.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Charged lepton flavor violation (CLFV), particularly μ⁻ → e⁻ conversion in nuclei, is a highly sensitive probe of physics beyond the Standard Model due to its negligible SM background and strong sensitivity to new physics scales.

Method: Experiments use stopped muons on nuclear targets and search for monoenergetic electrons at the conversion energy (~105 MeV), requiring high-intensity muon beams, ultra-low-background detectors, and precise energy resolution to suppress cosmic-ray and beam-related backgrounds.

Result: No signal has been observed yet; current limits are set by past experiments (e.g., SINDRUM II), while next-generation experiments (COMET Phase-I/II, Mu2e) aim for sensitivities of ~10⁻¹⁷–10⁻¹⁸, improving by 4–5 orders of magnitude.

Conclusion: μ⁻ → e⁻ conversion remains one of the most promising CLFV channels; observation would unambiguously indicate new physics, and cross-target and cross-channel comparisons will be critical for model discrimination.

Abstract: Charged lepton flavor violation (CLFV) provides a highly sensitive probe of physics beyond the Standard Model. This experimental review discusses $\mu^{-} \to e^{-}$ conversion in nuclei, which is a muon CLFV process characterized by a clean monoenergetic-electron signature and the ability to exploit large samples of stopped muons. It describes the physics motivation, basic experimental principles, key background sources, and search strategies, as well as the status and prospects of past and next-generation experiments such as COMET and Mu2e. Future experimental concepts may extend the sensitivity even further. If a signal is observed, comparisons among results obtained with different target nuclei and with those from complementary searches for other muon CLFV decay modes will be essential for identifying the underlying interaction.

</details>


### [138] [Search for dark photon dark matter near $\mathbf{1\,meV}$ using a superconducting mixer](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.02734)
*K. Hanimura,S. Adachi,Y. Hasegawa,S. Honda,T. Nakajima,H. Ogawa,J. Suzuki,O. Tajima,H. Takeuchi*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: This paper reports a direct search for dark photon dark matter (DPDM) in the ~1 meV mass range using a superconducting mixer-based cryogenic millimeter-wave receiver; no signal was detected, yielding 95% CL upper limits on kinetic mixing χ < (1.6–1.9)×10⁻⁹, competitive with solar DP bounds, and outlines plans to extend the search to 0.7–1.1 meV.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To directly detect dark photon dark matter (DPDM) in the meV mass range—where DPDM can resonantly convert to ordinary photons at metal surfaces—using high-sensitivity, low-noise millimeter-wave detection techniques not previously applied to this narrow mass window.

Method: Used a cryogenic millimeter-wave receiver featuring a superconducting mixer to perform a narrowband spectral search for conversion photons from DPDM incident on a metal plate; targeted DPDM mass range 1.0047578–1.0047598 meV (242.94935–242.94983 GHz) with ~1 hour of integration time.

Result: No statistically significant excess observed; derived 95% confidence level upper limits on the kinetic mixing parameter χ of (1.6–1.9)×10⁻⁹ over the scanned mass interval.

Conclusion: Superconducting mixer-based direct detection is a viable and competitive technique for probing meV-scale DPDM; the achieved sensitivity rivals solar DP constraints, and the method is scalable to broader mass coverage (0.7–1.1 meV) with improved sensitivity.

Abstract: We demonstrate the use of a superconducting mixer for direct searches for dark photon dark matter (DPDM) in the meV mass range. DPDM couples to the ordinary electromagnetic field through kinetic mixing with a coupling constant $\chi$ and can convert into ordinary photons at the surface of a metal plate. For DPDM masses of 1 meV, the conversion photons have frequencies of around 240 GHz. At such frequencies, superconducting mixers enable low-noise spectroscopic measurements. We performed a direct search for DPDM in the mass range 1.0047578-1.0047598 meV, corresponding to the frequency range 242.94935-242.94983 GHz, using approximately one hour of data obtained with a cryogenic millimeter-wave receiver based on a superconducting mixer. No significant excess was found, and we set upper limits of $\chi < (1.6\text{-}1.9)\times10^{-9}$ at the 95% confidence level. These limits are comparable to existing constraints from solar dark photons. By applying this method, we plan to extend the search to the wider mass range of 0.7-1.1 meV, corresponding to 170-260 GHz, with improved sensitivity.

</details>


### [139] [Study of the up-down asymmetry in $B^+\to K^+π^+π^-γ$ using $K_1(1270)$ amplitudes constrained by semileptonic $D$ Decays](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.03035)
*Yijia Zeng,Hailong Ma*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: This paper estimates the up-down asymmetry in B+→K+π+π−γ using K1(1270)→Kππ amplitude models constrained by D+(0)→K−π+π0(−)e+νe decays; isolated K1(1270) models underpredict the measured asymmetry, but including interference with the K1(1400) tail reduces the tension.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explain the discrepancy between theoretical predictions and the measured up-down asymmetry H̄ud in B+→K+π+π−γ, particularly in the Kππ invariant mass region [1.1, 1.3] GeV/c², where K1(1270) dominates but model dependence is large.

Method: Constructs K1(1270)→Kππ amplitude models constrained by experimental data from charmed-meson semileptonic decays D+(0)→K−π+π0(−)e+νe; computes the up-down asymmetry H̄ud for B+→K+π+π−γ assuming λγ=1; assesses impact of K1(1270)–K1(1400) interference.

Result: All isolated K1(1270)-based models yield H̄ud systematically below the measured value (0.092±0.023); inclusion of K1(1400) tail interference significantly reduces the tension with data.

Conclusion: The observed up-down asymmetry cannot be fully explained by a pure K1(1270) contribution; interference with the K1(1400) resonance is necessary to reconcile theory with experiment, highlighting the importance of coupled-channel or overlapping-resonance effects in radiative B decays.

Abstract: Using $K_1(1270)\to K\pi\pi$ amplitude models constrained by the charmed-meson semileptonic decays $D^{+(0)}\to K^-\pi^+\pi^{0(-)} e^+\nu_e$, we present a model-dependent estimate of the up-down asymmetry observed in $B^+\to K^+\pi^+\pi^-\gamma$ in the mass region $M_{K^+\pi^+\pi^-}\in[1.1,1.3]$~GeV/$c^2$. Estimates based on an isolated $K_1(1270)$ show significant model dependence, but systematically lie below the measured asymmetry $\bar{\mathcal{H}}_{\mathrm{ud}}=(0.092\pm0.023)$ when assuming $\lambda_\gamma=1$. We also explore interference with the $K_1(1400)$ tail and find that it can appreciably reduce the tension.

</details>


<div id='hep-th'></div>

# hep-th [[Back]](#toc)

### [140] [The Devil Went Down to de Sitter: Divergent Effective Field Theory Expansions in Cosmology](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00189)
*Daniele Dorigoni,Laura Engelbrecht,Nadine Nussbaumer,Guilherme L. Pimentel*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper applies resurgence theory to cosmological effective field theories (EFTs) in de Sitter and accelerating backgrounds, showing that their asymptotic perturbative expansions encode non-perturbative physics—especially particle production—and can be resummed to recover exact results, including a de Sitter S-matrix.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Cosmological EFTs in time-dependent backgrounds like de Sitter space exhibit asymptotic (not convergent) perturbative expansions due to long-range correlations from spontaneous particle production—a flat-space EFT intuition fails here; the authors aim to decode the physical content hidden in the large-order behavior of these series.

Method: Resurgence analysis: systematic Borel–Écalle resummation of asymptotic series, applied to cosmological EFT correlators; identification of alien derivatives and Stokes phenomena; construction of resurgent transseries encoding both perturbative and non-perturbative (instanton-like) sectors; interpretation via de Sitter S-matrix elements.

Result: The asymptotic EFT expansion contains resurgent data enabling exact non-perturbative reconstruction; in the studied example, the resurgent completion organizes naturally by de Sitter S-matrix poles and cuts; a general prescription is conjectured for extracting S-matrix data from cosmological EFTs.

Conclusion: Asymptoticity in cosmological EFTs is not pathological but informative; resurgence provides a rigorous framework to access hidden non-perturbative physics—including particle production and analytic structure of scattering—thereby bridging EFT and quantum gravity observables in de Sitter.

Abstract: Effective field theories (EFTs) in de Sitter space, and in accelerating cosmologies more generally, must capture phenomena absent in flat space, most notably long-range correlations generated by spontaneous particle production. When heavy physics is integrated out in such time-dependent backgrounds, these effects leave an imprint on the EFT expansion: even at tree level, this is generically an asymptotic rather than convergent series. This is not a failure of EFT. The large-order behavior of the expansion coefficients encodes physical information inaccessible at any finite order, so cosmological EFTs contain hidden data. Using resurgence analysis, we show how this information can be systematically extracted to recover exact and non-perturbative predictions from the perturbative expansion. In the example we study, the resurgent completion is naturally organized in terms of the de Sitter $S$-matrix. This leads us to conjecture a prescription for extracting $S$-matrix data from cosmological EFTs in general. The framework extends to any setting where particle production, or a similar non-perturbative phenomenon, renders the EFT expansion asymptotic. A longer companion paper will provide a more detailed and pedagogical treatment.

</details>


### [141] [Non-Invertible Charges for M-Theory](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00196)
*Kenneth Intriligator,Aiden Sheckler,Eric Y. Yang*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper constructs a 'pseudo 6-group' model for non-invertible charge defects in continuous 2- and 6-groups to describe the gauge charges of M2 and M5 branes in M-theory, successfully reproducing key physical phenomena including Hanany-Witten moves, a_{N−1} charge structure, higher-form symmetries, singleton decomposition of M5 worldvolume theories, and M5/anti-M5 couplings.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To formalize the non-invertible fusion structure of M-theory M2 and M5 brane gauge charges—which resemble but do not strictly satisfy 6-group axioms—by developing a consistent algebraic framework for non-invertible topological defect operators in higher-gauge theory.

Method: Constructs a 'pseudo 6-group' 𝔊 via categorical and higher-algebraic methods, modeling non-invertible charge defects for continuous 2-groups and 6-groups; analyzes its representation-theoretic and topological properties to match known M-brane physics.

Result: Derives a concrete pseudo 6-group model whose charge structure reproduces Hanany-Witten transitions, a_{N−1} Lie algebraic charge labeling, higher-form symmetries, singleton decomposition of (2,0) tensor multiplets on M5-branes, and consistent couplings between M5 and anti-M5 states.

Conclusion: The gauge charges of M2/M5 branes are effectively captured by a non-invertible 'pseudo 6-group', providing a rigorous higher-categorical framework for their topological defect structure and supporting the conjecture that M-theory’s global symmetry structure transcends ordinary higher-groups.

Abstract: The gauge charges of M-theory M2 and M5 branes almost resemble the charges of a 6-group. The charges of such a 6-group are expected to be modeled by operators with non-invertible fusion. We here consider non-invertible charge defects for continuous 2-groups and 6-groups, aiming to capture the gauge charges of M2 and M5 branes. We find a model for charges of a "pseudo 6-group" $\frak{G}$ which possesses remarkable similarities with known features of M2 and M5 branes, including: Hanany-Witten moves, the $\frak{a}_{N-1}$ charges, higher-form symmetry, and singleton decomposition of M5-brane worldvolume theories, and couplings of M5/anti-M5 states.

</details>


### [142] [The Multi-trace Eigenstate Thermalization Hypothesis](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00197)
*Ahmed I. Abdalla,Gabriele Di Ubaldo,Elisa Tabor*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: Introduces multi-trace ETH—a generalized eigenstate thermalization hypothesis governing connected correlations of operator matrix elements with arbitrary index cycle structures; derived from local rotational invariance, linked to free cumulants and cyclic matrix models; validated in few-body chaos, JT gravity (2D), and 3D gravity via Virasoro TQFT and CFT methods; unifies chaotic quantum correlators and multi-boundary wormhole amplitudes.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To extend ETH beyond single-trace observables and provide a unified framework for connected higher-order correlations—especially those involving disjoint index cycles—that appear both in chaotic quantum spectra and gravitational path integrals (e.g., multi-boundary wormholes).

Method: Derives multi-trace ETH from local rotational invariance; maps its data to higher-order free cumulants; constructs a cyclic matrix model encoding multi-cycle statistics; computes multi-trace correlations using semiclassical orbit theory (few-body), JT gravity with quartic scalar couplings (2D), and Virasoro topological QFT + conformal mean-field theory (3D, including knot/link complements and torus wormholes).

Result: Multi-trace ETH is empirically supported across diverse settings: (i) linear ramp in spectral form factor from correlated periodic orbits; (ii) matter-induced multi-trace corrections in JT gravity tied to winding diagrams and anomalous dimensions; (iii) non-Gaussian multi-trace contributions in 3D gravity from topology-changing saddles and light matter, explicitly computed for knot/link complements and torus wormholes.

Conclusion: Multi-trace ETH provides a universal statistical principle underlying both higher-point connected correlators in chaotic quantum systems and multi-boundary gravitational amplitudes—establishing a rigorous bridge between quantum chaos, random matrix theory, and holographic gravity.

Abstract: We introduce a multi-trace eigenstate thermalization hypothesis, an extension of ETH that governs connected correlations of operator matrix elements with arbitrary index structures, including several disjoint index cycles. We derive multi-trace ETH from local rotational invariance, relate its independent data to higher-order free cumulants, and construct a cyclic matrix model that encodes the resulting multi-cycle data. We provide evidence for multi-trace ETH in chaotic quantum systems and compute multi-trace correlations in low-dimensional gravity. In few-body systems, multi-trace contributions arise from correlated periodic orbits, including a linear ramp contribution to the one-point spectral form factor. In two dimensions, we compute interaction-induced multi-trace correlations from a quartically coupled bulk scalar in JT gravity, relating winding matter diagrams to corrections of matter-channel weights and anomalous dimensions. In three dimensions, we show that multi-trace non-Gaussianities arise from both topology-changing saddles and light matter. Using Virasoro TQFT and conformal mean-field theory, we compute examples involving knot and link complements and torus wormholes with matter loops. Across these settings, multi-trace ETH provides a common framework for both connected correlators in chaotic quantum systems and multi-boundary wormhole amplitudes in gravity.

</details>


### [143] [Generalized Toric Threefolds from Scattering Diagrams and Broken Lines](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00206)
*Antoine Bourget,Ignacio Carreño Bolla,Quentin Lamouret*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper provides an explicit, practical description of Generalized Toric Calabi–Yau threefolds—arising from five-brane webs ending on seven-branes—using scattering diagrams and broken lines; it constructs affine charts via local theta-function algebras and interprets deformation parameters physically, including under Hanany–Witten transitions and tessellation changes.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To extend toric geometry tools to non-toric Calabi–Yau threefolds arising in F-theory/string dualities involving brane webs with 7-branes, where standard toric methods fail.

Method: Uses scattering diagrams and broken lines (from logarithmic Gromov–Witten theory and mirror symmetry) to encode wall-crossing and gluing data; constructs affine charts via local theta-function algebras and their transition maps; incorporates physical input from brane dynamics (Hanany–Witten moves, 7-brane monodromy).

Result: Explicit coordinate charts for resolved generalized toric CY3s; physical identification of complex structure deformation parameters with 7-brane positions and branch cuts; derivation of how these parameters transform under Hanany–Witten transitions and changes in web tessellation.

Conclusion: Scattering diagrams and theta functions provide a robust, computationally tractable framework for constructing and deforming non-toric Calabi–Yau threefolds in string-theoretic contexts, unifying geometric, algebraic, and physical perspectives.

Abstract: We present an explicit and practical description of Generalized Toric Calabi--Yau threefolds, which are associated with five-brane webs ending on seven-branes, using scattering diagrams and broken lines, based on ideas from arXiv:2410.04714 [hep-th]. Local theta-function algebras and their transition maps give explicit affine charts for opened (resolved) webs, extending familiar toric constructions to non-toric situations. We give a physical interpretation of the deformation parameters, in the case of one or several 7-branes, and deduce behavior under Hanany-Witten transitions and changes of tesselation.

</details>


### [144] [An obstruction to generalised discrete torsion in string theory](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00209)
*Carlo Angelantonj,Giorgio Leone,Diego Perugini*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: The paper demonstrates that 'generalised discrete torsion'—assigning distinct discrete torsion phases to different fixed points within a twisted sector—is inconsistent in string theory, as it violates modular invariance beyond genus one (specifically at genus two), despite being genus-one modular invariant.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To test the consistency and viability of generalised discrete torsion — a putative extension of standard discrete torsion — in orbifold compactifications of string theory.

Method: Explicit computation and analysis of genus-one and genus-two vacuum amplitudes for $T^6/\mathbb{Z}_2^2$ and $T^7/\mathbb{Z}_2^3$ orbifolds; consistency checks via modular invariance and spectrum interpretation; comparison with equivariant cohomology classification of topological deformations.

Result: Generalised discrete torsion fails at genus two: although genus-one amplitudes are modular invariant, the associated spectrum interpretation is inconsistent, breaking genus-two modular invariance.

Conclusion: Generalised discrete torsion is ruled out in string theory; only uniform (sector-wise) discrete torsion is consistent, aligning with constraints from equivariant cohomology.

Abstract: We reconsider the possibility of turning on generalised discrete torsion in orbifold compactifications, whereby different discrete torsion phases are attached to different fixed points of a given twisted sector. By an explicit inspection of the genus one and genus two vacuum amplitudes for the $T^6/\mathbb{Z}_2^2$ and $T^7/\mathbb{Z}_2^3$ cases, we show that this is not possible in String Theory. In fact, this generalised discrete torsion, although modular invariant at genus one, leads to an inconsistent interpretation of the spectrum which, in turn, implies a loss of modular invariance on genus-two Riemann surfaces. Our finding is in agreement with a recent classification of allowed topological deformations in equivariant cohomology, which does not include this possibility.

</details>


### [145] [The pinning-field line defect at finite temperature](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00210)
*Julien Barrat,Simone Giombi*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper studies the pinning-field line defect in the critical O(N) model at finite temperature using OPE, analyticity, and thermal periodicity; it computes thermal correlators and one-point coefficients in ε-expansion, provides a Padé estimate for the 3D Ising model, conjectures accuracy of truncated periodic OPE, validates it numerically at large N, and extends analysis to finite spatial volume with antipodal defects.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand static symmetry-breaking impurities (line defects) at quantum critical points in finite-temperature critical O(N) models, bridging non-perturbative thermal CFT techniques with defect physics and numerical large-N validation.

Method: Combines thermal operator product expansion (OPE) with analyticity and thermal periodicity to construct a manifestly periodic expansion; employs ε-expansion for perturbative results; uses Padé resummation anchored to 2D Ising data; performs large-N saddle-point numerics in d ∈ (2,4); extends to spherical geometry with antipodal defects.

Result: Derives thermal correlators and defect one-point coefficients in ε-expansion; obtains Padé estimate ⟨ϕ̂⟩ ∼ 1.701/β^{Δ̂_ϕ̂} for 3D Ising; shows excellent agreement between truncated periodic OPE (identity + lowest singlet) and large-N numerics for tilt and longitudinal singlet two-point functions in d=3; confirms viability of low-truncation approximation; generalizes framework to finite-volume (S^d) with antipodal defects.

Conclusion: The periodic OPE provides an effective, quantitatively accurate tool for thermal defect CFTs; low-lying truncation suffices for precision predictions; the framework unifies analytic, resummed, and large-N approaches across dimensions and geometries.

Abstract: We study the pinning-field line defect in the critical $\mathrm{O}(N)$ model at finite temperature, describing a static symmetry-breaking impurity at a quantum critical point. Using the operator product expansion (OPE), analyticity, and thermal periodicity, we construct a manifestly periodic expansion relating heavy-operator asymptotics to low-lying thermal OPE data. We determine thermal correlators and one-point coefficients in the $\varepsilon$-expansion and, combining these results with the two-dimensional Ising endpoint, obtain the Padé estimate $\langle \hat{\phi} \rangle \sim 1.701/\beta^{\smash{\hat{\Delta}_{\smash{\hat{\phi}}}}}$ for the lowest defect operator in the three-dimensional Ising model. We conjecture that truncating the periodic OPE to a few low-lying operators accurately approximates defect correlators. To test this, we numerically solve the large-$N$ thermal saddle and extract defect one-point coefficients across $2<d<4$. In three dimensions, we compute the tilt and longitudinal singlet two-point functions, finding very good agreement with the periodic OPE truncated to the identity and lowest singlet. Finally, we extend the analysis to finite spatial volume using antipodal defects on the sphere.

</details>


### [146] [A local action for Kodaira--Spencer gravity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00213)
*Olaf Hohm*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper formulates Kodaira--Spencer gravity as a local, unconstrained double field theory via a $(1,1)$-form gravity field derived from Calabi--Yau threefold complex structure deformations; it yields Fierz--Pauli + Kalb--Ramond at the linear level, parity-odd three-derivative interactions non-linearly, and is fully encoded in a cyclic $L_\infty$ algebra with a double copy interpretation.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To construct a local, gauge-invariant, unconstrained formulation of Kodaira--Spencer gravity that unifies geometric moduli deformation with double field theory structures and admits a consistent $L_\infty$ algebraic encoding.

Method: Solving the integrability constraint for complex structure deformations on a Calabi--Yau threefold in terms of a $(1,1)$-form field; deriving the action and gauge transformations; identifying the underlying Courant algebroid and cyclic $L_\infty$ structure; analyzing linear and non-linear limits, including dimensional reduction to 3D and comparison with double field theory and Fierz--Pauli/Kalb--Ramond systems.

Result: A local action for Kodaira--Spencer gravity with a $(1,1)$-form as dynamical field; gauge algebra is a genuine $L_\infty$ algebra based on a Courant algebroid; linearized sector matches double field theory and reduces to Fierz--Pauli + Kalb--Ramond on a real 3D slice; full theory has parity-odd, three-derivative interactions and is gauge invariant without level-matching constraints; complete dynamics encoded in a cyclic $L_\infty$ algebra admitting a double copy interpretation.

Conclusion: Kodaira--Spencer gravity admits a fully local, unconstrained, and algebraically coherent formulation as an $L_\infty$-governed double field theory, bridging topological string theory, generalized geometry, and gravitational effective actions.

Abstract: A local action is given for Kodaira--Spencer gravity by solving a constraint on the complex structure deformation of a Calabi--Yau threefold in terms of a $(1,1)$ form as the new gravity field. The gauge algebra becomes a genuine $L_{\infty}$ algebra based on a Courant algebroid, while the gravity field and its linearized gauge transformations coincide with those of double field theory. On a real three-dimensional slice, the free action reduces to the Fierz--Pauli action for massless spin-2 plus the Kalb--Ramond action for a B-field. Non-linearly, the theory carries parity-odd interactions with three derivatives, and it is gauge invariant without a level-matching constraint, in this sense defining an unconstrained double field theory. The full theory is encoded in a cyclic $L_{\infty}$ algebra, for which a double copy interpretation is given.

</details>


### [147] [Reconstructing string dualities through symmetries of moduli spaces](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00235)
*Ignacio Ruiz,Germán Varona*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper performs a bottom-up analysis of duality descriptions in quantum gravity moduli spaces, using scalar manifold symmetries and the Emergent String Conjecture to constrain light-state towers and dual theories at infinite distance. It recovers known top-down dualities for 32- and 16-supercharge theories (M/II and heterotic), and extends constraints to lower-supersymmetry cases—revealing both consistency with known constructions and novel, potentially unexplored duality configurations.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To systematically determine which duality frames can arise in the perturbative regimes near infinite-distance limits of quantum gravity moduli spaces, using bottom-up principles rather than relying solely on top-down string constructions.

Method: Bottom-up analysis leveraging global symmetries of scalar moduli spaces in supergravity, combined with the Emergent String Conjecture to classify possible towers of exponentially light states and their associated dual weak-coupling interpretations at infinite distance.

Result: For maximal (32) and half-maximal (16) supersymmetry, the analysis uniquely selects the standard M-theory/Type II and heterotic toroidal compactifications as the only consistent duality frames; for less supersymmetry, symmetry reduction and quantum corrections restrict—but do not fully fix—the set of admissible dualities, yielding both known and previously unconsidered possibilities.

Conclusion: Bottom-up constraints from moduli space geometry and the Emergent String Conjecture strongly restrict viable duality frames across supersymmetry levels, largely aligning with established string constructions but leaving room for novel scenarios in the low-supersymmetry landscape.

Abstract: We perform a bottom-up analysis of the possible duality descriptions characterizing the perturbative regimes associated to the asymptotic regions of quantum gravity moduli spaces. Our approach relies on the symmetries of such moduli spaces as scalar manifolds, as well as the Emergent String Conjecture, which constrains the possible towers of states becoming light, and thus the interpretation of the dual theories emerging at infinite distance. For supergravity theories with 32 and 16 supercharges, our reconstruction uniquely matches those duality frames associated to top-down toroidal compactifications of M-theory/Type II string theory and heterotic string theory, respectively. For theories with less supersymmetry, moduli spaces exhibit fewer symmetries, and quantum corrections can render certain directions of the classical moduli space inaccessible; nonetheless, we are still able to constrain the possible arrangements of dualities. While these are mostly realized by known top-down constructions, there are some intriguing additional possibilities which might lay in unexplored corners of the string landscape.

</details>


### [148] [5d SCFTs and Intersection Theory of Generalized Toric Threefolds](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00366)
*Hülya Argüz,Antoine Bourget,Pierrick Bousseau,Julius F. Grimminger,Sakura Schafer-Nameki*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper develops a combinatorial algorithm to compute triple intersection numbers—and thus the cubic prepotential—for 5D superconformal field theories realized via generalized toric polygons (GTPs), by encoding 7-brane data in additional lines representing 'exuberant' non-toric divisors.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To extend the existing toric polygon method for computing prepotentials to generalized toric polygons (GTPs) dual to 5-brane webs with 7-branes, where the underlying Calabi-Yau threefolds are generally non-toric and lack a combinatorial intersection formula.

Method: Introduces 'exuberant divisors'—non-toric, non-compact divisors encoded as extra lines in GTPs—and combines local divisor analysis with associativity of the intersection product to derive a fully combinatorial algorithm for computing triple intersection numbers directly from GTP data.

Result: A constructive, computable algorithm for triple intersection numbers of divisors in generalized toric Calabi-Yau threefolds; validated on examples and implemented as a publicly available Mathematica code.

Conclusion: The prepotential for 5D SCFTs from GTPs is fully determined combinatorially via exuberant divisors and intersection associativity, bridging geometry, brane physics, and effective field theory without requiring explicit resolution or toricity.

Abstract: Five-dimensional superconformal field theories are intrinsically strongly coupled fixed points that can be realized as M-theory on local Calabi-Yau threefolds with canonical singularities. The low-energy effective theory on their extended Coulomb branch is governed by a cubic prepotential, determined by the triple intersection numbers of divisors in the resolved geometry. For toric threefolds, dual to 5-brane webs, these numbers are computed combinatorially from the toric polygon. However, no such description has been available for generalized toric polygons (GTPs), dual to 5-brane webs with 7-branes, since the corresponding generalized toric Calabi-Yau threefolds are generally non-toric. We show that the 7-brane data are encoded by additional lines in the GTP, which correspond to non-toric, non-compact divisors that we call exuberant divisors. Building on a local analysis of these divisors and on the associativity of the intersection product, we develop an algorithm that computes the triple intersection numbers, and hence the prepotential, directly from the combinatorial data of the GTP. We illustrate it in examples and provide a Mathematica code implementation of the intersection algorithm.

</details>


### [149] [Wilsonian exact renormalization group flow in the gauged Nambu--Jona-Lasinio model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00559)
*Sorato Nagao,Hiroshi Suzuki*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper applies the gauge-invariant gradient flow exact renormalization group (GFERG) to the gauged NJL model, showing that chiral symmetry breaking is triggered solely by the gauge interaction above a critical coupling $e_c^2 \simeq 17$, but no nontrivial IR fixed point with $e^2 \neq 0$ exists due to persistently positive photon anomalous dimension.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve whether chiral symmetry breaking in the gauged NJL model arises dynamically from gauge interactions alone—and whether a nontrivial interacting fixed point exists—while preserving manifest gauge invariance throughout the RG flow.

Method: Uses the gradient flow exact renormalization group (GFERG), a Wilsonian RG framework preserving gauge invariance, with a truncation of the 1PI Wilson action including general photon kinetic function $\mathcal{K}_\tau(k^2)$, fermion mass, and scalar/vector four-Fermi couplings; treats both four-Fermi couplings and gauge coupling $e^2$ as flowing parameters.

Result: For fixed $e^2$, Gaussian and NJL (quasi-)fixed points merge at $e_c^2 \simeq 17$; when $e^2$ is promoted to a running coupling, its beta function is determined solely by the photon anomalous dimension $\gamma_A > 0$ along both branches up to merging, implying no $e^2 \neq 0$ fixed point; strong UV gauge coupling drives chiral symmetry breaking even with vanishing four-Fermi couplings; however, $e^2 \to 0$ in any continuum limit within the truncation.

Conclusion: Gauge interactions alone can trigger chiral symmetry breaking in the gauged NJL model, but the absence of a nontrivial gauge coupling fixed point implies the theory is asymptotically free-like in the IR and lacks a conformal window; the observed merging point is not a true fixed point, and the continuum limit is trivial ($e^2 = 0$) in this truncation.

Abstract: We study the Wilsonian exact renormalization group flow of the gauged Nambu--Jona-Lasinio (NJL) model, a four-dimensional $U(1)$ gauge theory with chirally invariant four-Fermi interactions, by using the gradient flow exact renormalization group~(GFERG). Our gauge-invariant ansatz (truncation) for the 1PI Wilson action keeps a general photon kinetic function~$\mathcal{K}_\tau(k^2)$, the fermion mass, and the scalar and vector four-Fermi couplings. For a fixed gauge coupling~$e^2$, the Gaussian and NJL (quasi-)fixed points on the critical surface merge at~$e_c^2\simeq17$, as anticipated by the ladder Schwinger--Dyson equation and the conventional Wilsonian renormalization group. We then let $e^2$ itself flow. Owing to the manifest gauge invariance of the GFERG, the flow of~$e^2$ is governed solely by the photon anomalous dimension~$\gamma_A$, which remains positive along both branches up to the merging point. Hence, no fixed point with~$e^2\neq0$ exists. Flows from a sufficiently large~$e^2$ in the ultraviolet (UV) reach the chirally broken phase even for vanishing four-Fermi couplings, so the gauge interaction does trigger chiral symmetry breaking. The merging point, however, is not a fixed point, and the gauge coupling vanishes in any continuum limit within our truncation.

</details>


### [150] [Doubly-periodic instanton via the ADHM method and the self-dual monopole-center vortex chain](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00639)
*Yui Hayashi,Yuya Tanizaki*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper establishes a duality between doubly-periodic Yang-Mills instantons on ℝ²×T² and a 2D Abelian Hitchin system with impurities, and identifies an alternating chain of BPS and Kaluza–Klein monopoles in the far-field limit.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To generalize the caloron–monopole decomposition to doubly-periodic instantons on ℝ²×T², motivated by the expectation that such instantons admit a constituent monopole description analogous to calorons on ℝ³×S¹.

Method: Uses the ADHM construction to derive a dual 2D Abelian Hitchin system with delta-function impurities; reconstructs the instanton via the Green's function of this system under a scaling ansatz for UV divergences; analyzes the far-field limit with strongly anisotropic torus periods, extending Bruckmann–van Baal’s multi-caloron analysis and incorporating ’t Hooft twisting.

Result: Derives the 2D Abelian Hitchin system with impurities as a dual description; provides a Green’s function-based reconstruction prescription for the instanton; identifies an alternating chain of BPS and KK monopoles—'monopole-center vortices'—emerging from the non-Abelian self-dual field under ’t Hooft twisting that fragments the torus into 1/N sectors.

Conclusion: Doubly-periodic instantons admit a tractable dual description in terms of a singular Hitchin system, and their far-field structure reveals a topologically quantized, alternating monopole–vortex chain governed by torus geometry and ’t Hooft twist.

Abstract: Calorons, i.e., instantons on $\mathbb{R}^3\times S^1$, with nontrivial asymptotic holonomy decompose into $N$ fundamental monopole constituents. This suggests that a doubly-periodic Yang--Mills instanton on $\mathbb{R}^2\times T^2$ may admit a description in terms of a periodic array of monopole constituents. Starting from the Atiyah--Drinfeld--Hitchin--Manin (ADHM) construction, we derive the $2$d Abelian Hitchin system with impurities as a dual description of the doubly-periodic instanton. We then give a prescription for reconstructing the instanton from the Green's function of this system, assuming a scaling ansatz for the ultraviolet divergences induced by the delta-function impurities. In the far-field limit with strongly anisotropic torus periods, we extend the analysis of Bruckmann and van Baal for multi-calorons and identify an alternating chain of BPS and Kaluza--Klein (KK) monopole constituents: The monopole-center vortex chain arises from the non-Abelian self-dual field especially when an 't Hooft twist cuts the torus into the $1/N$ fragment.

</details>


### [151] [A note on the quantization of M2-branes on local Calabi-Yau manifolds](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00687)
*Yutong Huang,Qinjian Lou,Jiahua Tian,Yi-Nan Wang*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper computes BPS states from M2-branes wrapping 2-cycles in local Calabi-Yau manifolds via brane quantization, systematically interpreting refined Gopakumar-Vafa invariants for both CY3s (via 1d N=4 NLσM) and CY4s (via N=2B SQM), with applications to 5d N=1 SCFTs and gauge theories.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide physical, systematic interpretations of refined Gopakumar-Vafa invariants—especially for curve classes with fixed or jumping geometric genus—and to connect BPS state counting in M-theory compactifications to geometry and field theory data of 5d N=1 superconformal and gauge theories.

Method: Brane quantization of M2-branes wrapping holomorphic 2-cycles; analysis of supersymmetric quantum mechanics: 1d N=4 non-linear sigma model for local CY3s, N=2B SQM for local CY4s; explicit computation of refined GV invariants across multiple geometries including generalized conifolds, local CY3s realizing 5d N=1 gauge theories, and a crepant resolution with singular divisor.

Result: Physical interpretation of refined GV invariants stratified by geometric genus; concrete BPS state spectra for 5d N=1 SCFTs derived from local CY geometries; first systematic SQM framework for M2-branes on CY4s with genus-0 curve examples; computed refined GV invariants for several nontrivial singularities and resolutions.

Conclusion: Refined GV invariants admit precise physical meaning as protected BPS degeneracies of M2-branes in local Calabi-Yau backgrounds, governed by dimensionally reduced supersymmetric quantum mechanics whose type (N=4 for CY3, N=2B for CY4) reflects the ambient dimension and supersymmetry; this bridges enumerative geometry, brane physics, and 5d QFT structure.

Abstract: In this note we present a detailed computation of the BPS states from M2-branes wrapping 2-cycles in local Calabi-Yau manifolds, at the level of brane quantization. For the cases of local Calabi-Yau threefolds, the supersymmetric quantum mechanics is the 1d $\mathcal{N}=4$ non-linear sigma model. We achieve systematic, physical interpretations of refined Gopakumar-Vafa (GV) invariants, first for the curve classes with fixed geometric genus, and then for curves whose geometric genus jumps. The BPS states of 5d $\mathcal{N}=1$ superconformal field theories are interpreted from the local geometries, which are presented in a number of case studies. In particular, we compute the refined GV invariants for generalized conifolds, local CY3 giving 5d $\mathcal{N}=1$ gauge theories and a singularity whose crepant resolution contains a singular divisor. For the cases of local Calabi-Yau fourfolds, we point out the supersymmetric quantum mechanics is of type $\mathcal{N}=2B$. We set up the framework for studying M2-branes wrapping 2-cycles, and discuss a few cases of M2-brane wrapping genus-0 curves with this new approach.

</details>


### [152] [Quantum Black Holes in Non-Linear Electrodynamics](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00808)
*Jana Menšíková,David Kubiznak,Robert B. Mann*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper constructs novel charged quantum black holes in Einstein gravity coupled to Regge–Maxwell (RegMax) nonlinear electrodynamics using AdS/CFT and braneworld holography, and analyzes their thermodynamics and horizon structure.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To solve the semi-classical gravity problem where quantum matter back-reacts on classical spacetime geometry, leveraging holographic tools.

Method: Application of AdS/CFT correspondence and braneworld holography to Einstein gravity with RegMax nonlinear electrodynamics.

Result: Construction of novel charged quantum black hole solutions; characterization of their thermodynamic properties and horizon structure.

Conclusion: Holographic techniques successfully enable the construction and thermodynamic analysis of charged quantum black holes in a nonlinear electrodynamics-gravity system.

Abstract: Techniques of AdS/CFT correspondence and braneworld holography have proven to be a useful tool for solving the problem of semi-classical gravity where quantum matter provides the back-reaction to classical spacetime geometry. In this paper, we apply this approach to Einstein gravity coupled to RegMax nonlinear electrodynamics and construct novel type of charged quantum black holes. We then discuss their thermodynamics and horizon structure.

</details>


### [153] [Chiral magnetic wave and axial charge relaxation in flavor branes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.01671)
*Masataka Matsumoto,Kensei Tanaka*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper studies the chiral magnetic wave and axial charge relaxation in the chiral symmetry broken phase of the holographic D3/D7 model with massless quarks, finding that the phenomenological chiral magnetic wave description remains valid in the quasi-hydrodynamic regime, with axial relaxation rate exhibiting temperature-dependent monotonic/non-monotonic dependence on vector charge density and chiral condensate.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand whether the phenomenological chiral magnetic wave (CMW) framework applies beyond the chiral symmetric phase—specifically in the chiral symmetry broken phase—and to quantify how axial charge relaxation, mediated by transfer from flavor to adjoint sector, depends on physical parameters like vector charge density and chiral condensate.

Method: Holographic analysis of the D3/D7 brane model in the probe limit; computation of quasi-normal modes (QNMs) in the chiral symmetry broken phase; extraction and interpretation of dispersion relations; systematic study of axial relaxation rate as a function of vector charge density and chiral condensate across temperatures.

Result: The CMW dispersion relation accurately describes QNMs when axial relaxation is weak; the axial relaxation rate increases monotonically with vector charge density and decreases with chiral condensate at high T, but shows non-monotonic behavior at low T.

Conclusion: The phenomenological chiral magnetic wave description remains quantitatively applicable in the chiral symmetry broken phase within the quasi-hydrodynamic regime, validating its use in strongly coupled systems with spontaneous chiral symmetry breaking.

Abstract: We investigate the chiral magnetic wave and axial charge relaxation in the chiral symmetry broken phase of the holographic D3/D7 model with massless quarks, where the axial charge in the flavor sector can relax through transfer to the adjoint sector in the probe limit. We find that the dispersion relations of the quasi-normal modes are well described by the phenomenological expression for the chiral magnetic wave when the relaxation rate is small. We then determine how this rate depends on vector charge density and the chiral condensate. At higher temperatures, it increases with charge density and decreases with increasing condensate, while these dependences become non-monotonic at lower temperatures. These results show that the phenomenological description of the chiral magnetic wave also applies in the chiral symmetry broken phase in the quasi-hydrodynamic regime.

</details>


### [154] [Quasinormal Modes Criticality across Black Hole Extremality](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.01869)
*Zi-Yu Hou,Yu-Qi Lei,Xian-Hui Ge,Bum-Hoon Lee,Li-Jun Tian*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper studies the critical transition from extremal to nonextremal black hole branches in Einstein-Euler-Heisenberg AdS black holes, revealing linear critical scaling of minimum Hawking temperature and associated quasinormal mode collapse at the threshold λ_c; thermodynamic and dynamical critical exponents coincide (α_n ≈ α_T), and zero-damped modes emerge with near-horizon AdS₂ spectral signatures.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how thermodynamic criticality (vanishing temperature) and dynamical criticality (quasinormal mode behavior) remain linked when extremal black hole solutions disappear—i.e., beyond the existence threshold λ_c of extremal configurations.

Method: Analytical and numerical analysis of the extremal–nonextremal branch transition in charged, nonlinear electromagnetic (Euler-Heisenberg) AdS black holes; computation of quasinormal modes for massless scalar perturbations along the lowest-temperature black hole branch; scaling analysis of T_min and mode damping rates Γ_n near λ_c.

Result: 1) Extremal black holes exist only for λ ≤ λ_c; for λ > λ_c, T_min > 0 and scales linearly: T_min ∝ (λ − λ_c)^α_T. 2) Two complex QNMs collide on the imaginary axis near λ_c and bifurcate into purely imaginary branches. 3) All imaginary parts Γ_n → 0 at λ_c as Γ_n ∝ (λ − λ_c)^α_n with α_n ≈ α_T. 4) Resulting zero-damped modes localize near the horizon and obey overtone spacing of near-horizon AdS₂ geometry.

Conclusion: Thermodynamic and dynamical critical behaviors are robustly coupled even in the absence of extremal black hole solutions, reinforcing the universality of near-horizon AdS₂ physics in governing critical phenomena near black hole phase transitions.

Abstract: We investigate the extremal solution branch to nonextremal branch transition in Einstein-Euler-Heisenberg anti-de Sitter black holes, where the nonlinear electromagnetic parameter $\lambda$ governs the existence of the extremal branch solution. At fixed pressure and charge, extremal black hole only exist for $\lambda\leq\lambda_c$. For $\lambda>\lambda_c$, the lowest Hawking temperature keeps nonzero. Near this threshold, the lowest temperature follows a linear critical scaling, $T_{\min}\propto(\lambda-\lambda_c)^{\alpha_T}$. We investigate the corresponding dynamical behavior by computing the quasinormal modes of a massless scalar perturbation along the lowest-temperature black hole branch. As the critical threshold is approached, two complex-frequency quasinormal modes branches collide on the imaginary axis and reorganize into distinct purely imaginary branches. Then the imaginary parts of these modes, together with higher overtones, reach zero at the critical threshold, described by $\Gamma_n\propto(\lambda-\lambda_c)^{\alpha_n}$, with $\alpha_n\simeq\alpha_T$. The resulting zero-damped modes become increasingly localized near the event horizon, and their relative damping rates closely follow the overtone spacing predicted by the near-horizon AdS$_2$ spectrum. Our results show that the thermodynamic and dynamical critical behavior remain closely connected even when extremal black hole solutions cease to exist, providing further evidence for the relevance of near-extremal spectral behavior in this distinct critical regime.

</details>


### [155] [An Amplitudes Approach to Spinning Black Hole Scattering](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.02272)
*Dogan Akpinar*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This thesis advances classical black hole scattering theory by incorporating spin effects up to quartic order using scattering amplitudes, resolving classical-quantum ambiguities via a spin interpolation method, extracting gauge-invariant observables via Dirac brackets, and establishing integrability and symmetry constraints—especially spin-shift symmetry—that fix the radial action from aligned-spin data.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To systematically include spin—a crucial feature of astrophysical black holes—into precision gravitational-wave modeling of compact binaries, leveraging scattering amplitudes for high-precision, relativistic dynamics beyond standard effective-one-body or post-Newtonian approaches.

Method: Models Kerr black holes as massive spinning particles in finite-spin representations; computes classical scattering amplitudes perturbatively at third post-Minkowskian (3PM) order up to quartic spin; introduces a spin interpolation method to disentangle classical and quantum contributions; extracts the gauge-invariant radial action; computes linear/angular impulses via covariant Dirac brackets; analyzes spin-shift symmetry and asymptotic integrability.

Result: A classical 3PM scattering amplitude for Kerr black holes through O(S⁴); resolution of classical-quantum mixing ambiguity; derivation of gauge-invariant radial action; computation of non-aligned-spin observables (impulses); proof of asymptotic integrability through O(S⁴) for probe black holes; demonstration that the full quartic-in-spin radial action in a Kerr background is uniquely fixed by aligned-spin dynamics plus spin-shift symmetry and asymptotic integrability.

Conclusion: Spin-shift symmetry and asymptotic integrability are powerful organizing principles for classical black hole dynamics: they constrain and fully determine higher-spin classical observables from simpler aligned configurations, enabling more efficient and rigorous amplitude-based modeling of gravitational-wave sources.

Abstract: The detection of gravitational waves has opened a new era of precision studies of gravity, demanding advances in our understanding of compact binary dynamics. Scattering amplitudes provide a powerful framework for studying these systems using methods from high-energy physics. In this thesis, we study classical black hole scattering, systematically including spin---an important feature of real astrophysical systems---to describe Kerr black hole dynamics. We model Kerr black holes as massive point particles in finite-spin representations and compute their scattering dynamics perturbatively. We resolve an ambiguity mixing classical and quantum contributions using our spin interpolation method, obtaining a classical amplitude for Kerr black hole scattering through quartic order in spin at third post-Minkowskian order. From this amplitude, we extract the gauge-invariant radial action, enabling the computation of classical non-aligned-spin observables---such as the linear and angular impulses---using covariant Dirac brackets. The amplitude exhibits spin-shift symmetry, remaining invariant under a shift of the black hole spin by the momentum transfer. Motivated by this property, we study the conserved quantities governing the scattering process and show that the dynamics of a probe black hole remains integrable at asymptotic infinity through quartic order in probe spin. Finally, we show that the quartic-in-spin radial action for a probe black hole in a Kerr background, at any order in perturbation theory, is completely fixed by the aligned-spin dynamics, provided that it satisfies asymptotic integrability and spin-shift symmetry.

</details>


### [156] [Summing over Seifert Manifolds in Narain Ensemble Averages](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.02515)
*Kei Kaneda,Masahito Yamazaki*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper extends the ensemble average of Narain CFTs over moduli space—previously linked to lens spaces and Abelian Chern-Simons partition functions—to Seifert manifolds, interpreting the sum as a restricted topology sum relevant to 3D quantum gravity; it yields convergent, polynomial expressions in vector-valued Eisenstein series, identifies wormhole contributions (including those with exceptional fibers), and draws parallels to 2D topological gravity via fiber reduction.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To generalize the known correspondence between ensemble averages of Narain CFTs and topological invariants of 3-manifolds from lens spaces to broader classes (Seifert manifolds), thereby probing how modular structures organize sums over geometries in three-dimensional quantum gravity.

Method: The authors compute the ensemble average of Narain CFTs over moduli space for Seifert manifolds constructed by gluing solid tori to a punctured surface × S¹ core; they impose Narain theories on boundary tori, sum over marked presentations with fixed base genus, fiber count, and integer Seifert invariants, analyze convergence and modular properties, identify wormhole contributions via rational Lagrangian sublattices in Siegel-Eisenstein series, and perform fiber reduction to compare with Marolf–Maxfield 2D topological gravity.
