Result: The ensemble average over Seifert manifold presentations converges absolutely and yields a polynomial in components of a vector-valued Eisenstein series; connected corrections arise when boundary tori share a modulus, interpreted as wormholes; for two boundaries, rational Lagrangian sublattices of the Siegel-Eisenstein series correspond to wormholes containing an exceptional fiber; fiber reduction recovers structural analogies to 2D topological gravity; Dehn fillings, non-orientable bases, and surgery formulas are also analyzed.

Conclusion: Modularity—manifested through Eisenstein series and their generalizations—is a natural organizing principle for summing over topologies in three-dimensional quantum gravity, particularly for fibrations like Seifert manifolds, and provides a concrete realization of wormhole contributions beyond simple lens spaces.

Abstract: The ensemble average of Narain conformal field theories over their moduli space is a real-analytic Eisenstein series, whose Poincaré expansion can be interpreted as a sum over lens spaces weighted by the partition functions of Abelian Chern-Simons theory. In this paper we extend this correspondence from lens spaces to Seifert manifolds, which gives an example of a restricted sum over topologies of the kind that arises in three-dimensional quantum gravity. A Seifert manifold is obtained from a fixed core, a punctured surface times a circle, by gluing solid tori to its boundary tori, and we place a copy of the Narain theory on each of these tori. The ensemble average supplies the sum over the solid tori, or more precisely over marked presentations with fixed base genus, number of fibers and integer Seifert invariant. The resulting sum converges absolutely and is a polynomial in the components of the vector-valued Eisenstein series. When all the boundary tori share a single modulus, the average acquires connected corrections, which we identify with wormholes gluing the core to itself; for two boundary tori, the terms with rational Lagrangian sublattices of the Siegel-Eisenstein series are realized by wormholes containing an exceptional fiber. After reduction along the fiber, the construction closely parallels the two-dimensional topological gravity of Marolf and Maxfield, with the base of the fibration playing the role of their spacetime. We also discuss Dehn fillings of 3-manifolds with torus boundary, non-orientable bases, and the surgery formula. Our results suggest that modularity could be an organizing principle in the summation of geometries in quantum gravity.

</details>


### [157] [Thermal Festina Lente](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.02559)
*Fayez Abu-Ajamieh,Nobuchika Okada,Roman Pasechnik,Victoria Venken,Zhi-Wei Wang*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper refines the Festina Lente bound—originally constraining light charged particles in de Sitter space—to include thermal enhancement of black hole discharge, yielding a stronger mass bound $m \gtrsim \ell (cV/\sigma)^{1/4}$ for $U(1)$-charged particles. It derives concrete lower limits on neutrino masses and millicharges in gauged $B-L$ models, finds tension with DESI DR2 data, and shows Standard Model electrons violate the bound during inflation unless photon mass or coupling is suppressed.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To strengthen and quantify the Festina Lente bound by incorporating thermal effects on black hole discharge in de Sitter space with radiation background, addressing gaps in prior work and confronting observational constraints (e.g., DESI, CMB, nucleosynthesis).

Method: Analytic derivation of discharge rate enhancement due to thermal radiation near extremal de Sitter black holes; matching Hawking temperature to background radiation temperature; solving for critical particle mass satisfying cosmic censorship; applying bounds to $B-L$, millicharge, and SM EFT contexts.

Result: New mass bound $m \gtrsim \ell (cV/\sigma)^{1/4}$ with logarithmic $\ell(gq)$; charged particles with $10^{-26} \lesssim gq \lesssim e$ must satisfy $m > 0.03\text{--}0.2\,\text{eV}$; lightest Dirac neutrino mass $\gtrsim 10\text{--}43\,\text{meV}$ for $10^{-29} \lesssim g_{B-L} \lesssim 10^{-24}$, conflicting with DESI DR2 for $g_{B-L} \gtrsim 7\times10^{-28}$; neutrino millicharge $\epsilon_\nu \lesssim 2\times10^{-27}$; SM electron violates bound during slow-roll inflation unless photon is massive or coupling suppressed.

Conclusion: Thermal enhancement significantly tightens the Festina Lente bound, imposing robust, testable constraints on light charged particles—including neutrinos and millicharged species—in cosmological settings, and exposing tensions with current observations and challenges for SM embedding in inflationary scenarios.

Abstract: According to the Festina Lente bound, particles charged under a $U(1)$ gauge field cannot be too light. Otherwise, the largest charged black holes of de Sitter space would discharge in a way that leads to a crunching singularity in violation of cosmic censorship. The Festina Lente bound has previously been extended to also include a background of thermal radiation, but where vacuum energy still dominates cosmic evolution, which imposes a maximal temperature $T\leq T_{\max}=(cV/\sigma)^{1/4}$. We extend this analysis by accounting for thermal enhancement of the discharge process and making the analysis more quantitative. The result is a bound $m\gtrsim\ell (cV/\sigma)^{1/4}$ on the masses of charged particles. Here, $\ell$ is a term that depends logarithmically on the gauge coupling and particle charge. At weak coupling this leads to a significantly stronger constraint than the $\sqrt{gq}$ scaling of the original Festina Lente bound. As a result, the present vacuum energy requires every particle of charge $10^{-26}\lesssim gq\lesssim e$ to be heavier than $0.03$--$0.2$ eV, provided its gauge boson is lighter than the Hubble scale. An unbroken gauged $B-L$ with Dirac neutrinos gives $m_{\nu,{\rm lightest}}\gtrsim10$--$43$ meV for $10^{-29}\lesssim g_{B-L}\lesssim10^{-24}$, in tension with the DESI DR2 limit once $g_{B-L}\gtrsim7\times10^{-28}$. The same limit bounds a neutrino millicharge to $\epsilon_\nu\lesssim2\times10^{-27}$. We also revisit the Standard Model effective field theory constraints from the electroweak phase transition. During slow-roll inflation the Standard Model electron violates the bound over the whole range of Hubble rates allowed by the CMB tensor limit and by nucleosynthesis, unless the photon is Higgsed, its coupling is suppressed, or one considers very fine-tuned versions of Higgs inflation.

</details>


### [158] [No smooth real instanton saddles in self-dual Yang-Mills on AdS$_4$](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.02655)
*Bayram Tekin*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper analyzes the self-dual limit of Yang-Mills theory on Euclidean AdS₄, showing that only the vacuum is a real smooth saddle under the non-trivial boundary equation from the first-order action; previously proposed self-dual solutions are ruled out as saddles, and their charges are corrected by including a conformal boundary surface term.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve ambiguities in the boundary conditions and saddle-point structure of self-dual Yang-Mills theory on Euclidean AdS₄, particularly clarifying the physical relevance of known self-dual solutions and correcting inconsistencies in conserved charge computations.

Method: Analysis of boundary conditions for first- and second-order Yang-Mills actions in the self-dual limit; examination of smoothness and reality constraints on gauge fields up to the conformal boundary; evaluation of saddle-point equations and conserved charges including boundary contributions.

Result: The vacuum is the unique real, smooth saddle point; the Sarioglu–Tekin (2009) self-dual solutions fail to satisfy the first-order boundary equation and thus are not valid saddles; their computed charges are corrected by adding a previously omitted surface term at the conformal boundary.

Conclusion: The self-dual limit enforces trivial bulk dynamics for real smooth fields, rendering only the vacuum admissible as a saddle; complex or singular configurations remain unexplored but may support nontrivial physics.

Abstract: In the self-dual limit of Yang-Mills theory on Euclidean AdS$_4$ studied by Heuveline, Ruzziconi, Sheta and Strominger, the boundary condition of the second-order action becomes vacuous, but the boundary equation of the first-order action is non-trivial. We show that, with this equation, the vacuum is the only saddle with a real gauge field when both fields are smooth up to the conformal boundary, for any compact gauge group. In particular, the self-dual solutions of Sarioglu and Tekin (2009) are not saddles; we also correct their charges, which omitted a surface term at the conformal boundary. Complex saddles and singular limits remain open.

</details>


### [159] [General Form Factors from String Amplitudes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.02730)
*Qu Cao,Liang Zhang*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: Extends string-amplitude construction to multi-insertion form factors, relates them to scattering amplitudes via boundary deformation, reveals novel finite-α′ structures including 2-splits, hidden/bulk zeros, and a decoupling split.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To generalize string-theoretic form factor constructions beyond single operator insertions and uncover underlying structural similarities and distinctions with scattering amplitudes.

Method: Deforms bulk closed-string punctures onto the open-string boundary to relate general (multi-insertion) stringy form factors to scattering amplitudes; analyzes field-theory limit and finite-α′ behavior.

Result: Field-theory form factors receive contributions from both separated insertions and local contact terms; at finite α′, stringy form factors exhibit 2-splits, hidden zeros (as in amplitudes), bulk zeros (form-factor-specific), and a new decoupling split unique to multiple insertions.

Conclusion: Stringy general form factors possess rich, previously unrecognized analytic and algebraic structures—distinct from but related to scattering amplitudes—enabling deeper unification of off-shell and on-shell observables in string theory.

Abstract: We extend the string-amplitude construction of single-insertion form factors to multiple operator insertions carrying independent off-shell momenta. By deforming the bulk closed-string punctures onto the open-string boundary, we establish a relation between stringy general form factors and scattering amplitudes. Using this relation, we evaluate the field-theory limit and find that the resulting form factors receive contributions from both separate operator insertions and local contact terms. Furthermore, we uncover several hidden structures of stringy general form factors at finite $\alpha'$: $2$-splits and hidden zeros analogous to those of scattering amplitudes, bulk zeros specific to form factors, and a decoupling split unique to multiple insertions.

</details>


### [160] [A Matrix View of Holographic Null-State Equations in $d\geq 2$](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.02855)
*Kuo-Wei Huang*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper reformulates AdS/CFT null-state PDEs using finite-dimensional matrices with off-diagonal structure, yielding infinite towers of CFT differential equations; it recovers known 2D Virasoro results at large c via sl2 algebra and extends to a richer sl2-product structure in d=4, with associated recursion and factorization properties.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To systematically organize and generalize the recently derived null-state-type PDEs from AdS gravity with black holes, enabling efficient generation of CFT differential constraints across dimensions and clarifying their algebraic and recursive structure.

Method: Matrix representation of null-state PDEs using finite-dimensional, systematically off-diagonal matrices; algebraic identification with sl2 (d=2) and tensor products thereof (d=4); analysis of recursion relations and factorized product forms.

Result: A unified matrix framework generating infinite-level CFT differential equations; exact reconstruction of large-c Virasoro null-state equations in d=2 via identity + sl2 linear combination; discovery of a more complex sl2⊗sl2-like matrix structure in d=4; derivation of associated recursion and factorization properties.

Conclusion: The matrix formalism provides a powerful, dimension-independent organizing principle for holographic differential equations, revealing deep algebraic structures (sl2-based) and structural hierarchies between d=2 and d=4, with implications for bootstrap methods and bulk reconstruction.

Abstract: We show that the null-state-type partial differential equations recently derived from AdS gravity with a black hole can be efficiently organized using finite-dimensional matrices with systematic off-diagonal structures. This matrix formulation generates an infinite tower of CFT differential equations at arbitrary levels. The $d=2$ matrix reproduces a known result based on Virasoro null-state vectors at large central charge and admits an algebraic representation in terms of a linear combination of the identity matrix and $\mathfrak{sl}_2$ generators, with a heavy-state deformation encoding the central charge contribution. The $d=4$ governing matrix exhibits a similar yet richer structure involving products of $\mathfrak{sl}_2$ generators. We also discuss recursion relations and factorized product structures associated with these holographic differential equations.

</details>


### [161] [The generalized entropy in holographic semiclassical de Sitter spacetime](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.02898)
*Akihiro Ishibashi,Kengo Maeda,Takashi Okamura*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper formulates a generalized de Sitter entropy combining entanglement and Wald entropy in holographic semiclassical gravity, constructs quantum-corrected de Sitter spacetimes perturbatively, and proves finiteness of renormalized entropies without boundary cutoffs—even without Dirichlet conditions—while showing extremality of generalized entropy under dynamical conformal boundary metrics.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve ambiguities in defining entropy for de Sitter space in quantum gravity, particularly the interplay between entanglement entropy of boundary QFT and bulk gravitational (Wald) entropy within a holographic semiclassical framework where boundary conditions are relaxed.

Method: Perturbative construction of quantum-corrected de Sitter spacetime via static deformations of the classical solution; application of the Noether charge formalism to compute generalized entropy; treatment of the conformal boundary metric as a dynamical variable in the variational principle.

Result: Renormalized entanglement and Wald entropies remain finite without UV cutoffs, even when the bulk AdS gravitational field violates Dirichlet boundary conditions; the first-order variation of generalized entropy with respect to the de Sitter horizon vanishes when the conformal boundary metric is dynamical—indicating extremality.

Conclusion: Generalized de Sitter entropy is well-defined and extremal in a holographic semiclassical setting with dynamical boundary geometry, supporting a consistent thermodynamic interpretation of de Sitter horizons beyond rigid boundary conditions.

Abstract: We consider generalized de Sitter entropy which includes both entanglement entropy of the strongly coupled quantum field and the gravitational Wald entropy in the framework of holographic semiclassical gravity. The quantum corrected de Sitter spacetime is constructed perturbatively by considering static deformations of the classical solution. By applying the Noether charge formula to our semiclassical model, we show that both the renormalized entropies remain finite without introducing a cutoff, even when the bulk AdS gravitational field does not satisfy the Dirichlet boundary condition. We show that the first order variation of the generalized entropy with respect to the de Sitter horizon always vanishes when the conformal boundary metric is promoted to a dynamical variable.

</details>


### [162] [Transgression branes in seven dimensions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.03031)
*Pablo Mora*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper studies a 7D brane theory based on a Transgression Action, revealing connections to fractional-statistics models and black hole solutions, with analysis of static Euclidean/Lorentzian solutions and topological phases affecting quantum statistics.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore structural and physical implications of a seven-dimensional brane theory built from a Transgression Action—particularly how trace factorization enables analogies to fractional-statistics models and black hole geometries.

Method: Analytical construction and comparison of static Euclidean and Lorentzian solutions; use of transgression forms with factorized trace; mapping to known models (Nepomechie–Wu–Zee for branes, Wilczek–Wu–Zee for fractional statistics, Bañados-type constant-curvature black holes); topological phase analysis via characteristic classes and holonomies.

Result: Demonstrated equivalence in certain limits between the transgression-based brane theory and Nepomechie–Wu–Zee brane models; derived explicit static Euclidean and Lorentzian brane solutions linked to constant-curvature black hole geometries; identified topological phases governing emergent quantum statistics.

Conclusion: The transgression-based 7D brane theory provides a unified geometric framework linking higher-dimensional branes, fractional quantum statistics, and topological black hole solutions—suggesting that quantum statistics can be encoded in spacetime topology through transgression invariants.

Abstract: A seven dimensional theory with branes, described by a Transgression Action is investigated. The theory has special properties owing to the factorization of the trace used to define the Transgression form. It is shown that in some cases the theory is similar to the models of branes proposed by Nepomechie, Wu and Zee as a higher dimensional generalization of models of particles with fractional statistics of Wilczek, Wu and Zee. Static Euclidean solutions are presented, and compared with similar solutions of Nepomechie,Wu and Zee. Then Lorentzian static solutions are considered, based in solutions of constant curvature black holes presented by Bañados and further studied by Guilleminot, Olea and Petrov. Topological phases and their implications for quantum statistics are discussed.

</details>


### [163] [Higher Groupoids of Gauge Potentials in Higher Gauge Theories admitting Weights](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.03064)
*Grigorios Giotopoulos*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: The paper constructs an explicit small ∞-groupoid model for null-concordances of higher gauge curvatures by trivializing nonlinear Bianchi identities algorithmically; it proves natural equivalence to the true ∞-groupoid via a total-form intermediate model, and computes homotopy groups via cohomology of a weighted gauge complex.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide an explicit, computationally tractable ∞-groupoid model for null-concordances of higher gauge curvatures—objects central to higher gauge theory and mathematical physics—by resolving the obstruction posed by nonlinear Bianchi identities.

Method: Uses finite-type Sullivan minimal Bianchi algebras with positive integer weights; organizes weighted potentials and gauge parameters into a linearized ∞-groupoid; establishes natural equivalence via an intermediate total-form model with explicit projection/section maps and simplicial homotopies.

Result: An explicit small ∞-groupoid model equivalent to the ∞-groupoid of null-concordances; connected components form a torsor over H⁰ of the weighted gauge complex; πₙ (n ≥ 1) are isomorphic to Hⁿ of that complex.

Conclusion: The nonlinear structure of higher gauge curvatures can be effectively linearized at the level of null-concordances via weighted minimal models, enabling cohomological computation of homotopical invariants.

Abstract: We construct an explicit small model for the $\infty$-groupoid of null-concordances of higher gauge curvatures by algorithmically trivializing their nonlinear Bianchi identities. More precisely, for a finite-type Sullivan minimal Bianchi algebra equipped with positive integer weights, we organize weighted potentials and higher gauge parameters at each fixed curvature into an $\infty$-groupoid whose gauge transformations are linear in the parameters. We prove that this model is naturally equivalent to the $\infty$-groupoid of null-concordances through an intermediate total-form model, with explicit projection and section maps and simplicial homotopies. When nonempty, its connected components form a torsor for the degree-zero cohomology of an associated weighted gauge complex, and its higher homotopy groups are computed by the higher cohomology of this complex.

</details>


### [164] [Universality and Emergent Classicality in Krylov Space](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.03188)
*Debarghya Chakraborty*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: Derives a general spectral formula for late-time linear growth of Krylov complexity and its higher moments, applicable to systems with narrow energy distributions; validated on critical XY chain and double-scaled SYK.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To characterize universal late-time behavior of Krylov complexity without relying on full Lanczos coefficient sequences, especially for systems where spectral properties are more accessible than recursion data.

Method: Uses spectral decomposition of the Hamiltonian and moment-generating function techniques to derive asymptotic expressions for Krylov complexity moments; applies to states with narrow energy distribution relative to bandwidth; includes one-loop computation for chord-number moments in double-scaled SYK.

Result: Obtains explicit spectral formulas for leading-order late-time growth coefficients of Krylov complexity and higher moments; shows classical-trajectory-like dynamics in Krylov basis for narrow-energy states; computes chord-number moments to one-loop order in double-scaled SYK.

Conclusion: Late-time Krylov complexity growth is universally governed by spectral properties—not just Lanczos coefficients—enabling broader applicability across quantum many-body systems with known density of states or energy distribution.

Abstract: Krylov complexity grows linearly at late times for many systems. In this regime, we give a general spectral formula for the leading growth coefficients of Krylov complexity and its higher moments, without requiring detailed knowledge of the Lanczos coefficients. For states with a narrow energy distribution compared to the bandwidth, dynamics in the Krylov basis resembles motion along a classical trajectory. We demonstrate our results for the critical XY chain and double-scaled SYK. For the latter, we compute the leading late-time behavior of the chord-number moments through one-loop order.

</details>


### [165] [What crossing symmetry forbids just above $c=1$](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.03217)
*Yu Nakayama*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper uses the numerical conformal bootstrap to analyze unitary 2D CFTs just above c=1, excluding certain scalar primary dimensions Δ_ϕ and bounding the operator gap Δ_gap; it identifies sharp features near c=8/7 linked to ℤ₅ parafermions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand the structure of unitary two-dimensional conformal field theories (CFTs) infinitesimally above the critical central charge c = 1, where the compact boson saturates all possible scaling dimensions — and to probe how unitarity and crossing symmetry constrain operator spectra away from this solvable point.

Method: Numerical conformal bootstrap applied to the four-point function of a scalar Virasoro primary ϕ with no lighter primaries in its self-OPE; combined with modular bootstrap constraints to bound the lightest non-identity primary dimension (Δ_gap); high-precision exclusion scans over (c, Δ_ϕ) space.

Result: Crossing symmetry excludes 0.17 ≲ Δ_ϕ ≲ 1 for c slightly above 1; thus Δ_gap < 0.17 at c = 1.0005 and 1.001; the maximal excluded c at fixed Δ_ϕ exhibits sharp minima near Δ_ϕ = 1/2 and 4/7, approaching c = 8/7 within 2×10⁻⁷ — matching dimensions of scalars in the ℤ₅ parafermion CFT and its orbifold.

Conclusion: The space of unitary 2D CFTs is highly constrained just above c = 1; the exclusion bounds and accumulation near c = 8/7 suggest that known algebraic CFTs (e.g., ℤ₅ parafermions) sit at special points in the landscape, acting as anchors for bootstrap boundaries.

Abstract: What does a unitary two-dimensional conformal field theory look like just above $c=1$? Using the numerical conformal bootstrap, we study the four-point function of a scalar Virasoro primary $\phi$ such that no primary lighter than $\phi$ appears in its operator product expansion with itself. Slightly above $c=1$, crossing symmetry excludes the interval $0.17\lesssim\Delta_\phi\lesssim1$, although the compact boson realizes every such $\Delta_\phi$ at $c=1$. Combining this exclusion with results from the modular bootstrap, we show that the lightest primary of a compact unitary conformal field theory at $c=1.0005$ or $1.001$ has dimension $\Delta_{\rm gap}<0.17$. Further from $c=1$, the largest excluded central charge at fixed $\Delta_\phi$ has two sharp minima near $\Delta_\phi=1/2$ and $4/7$. At these values it lies within $2\times10^{-7}$ of $c=8/7$, where the $\mathbb{Z}_5$ parafermion and its charge-conjugation orbifold have scalars of exactly these dimensions.

</details>


### [166] [From photons to fractons: Casimir effect in higher-rank electrodynamics and its fractonic branch](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.03269)
*Nicola Maggiore*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper analyzes the Casimir effect in fracton gauge theories, contrasting a full Lorentz-covariant higher-rank symmetric tensor theory with its physically stable scalar-charge fractonic branch; it finds that only the latter yields a well-defined vacuum energy—specifically 3/2 times the Maxwell Casimir energy—due to elimination of unstable vector modes by the Gauss constraint.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how restricted mobility (i.e., simultaneous conservation of charge and dipole moment) in fracton gauge theories affects measurable quantum vacuum phenomena, specifically whether and how the Casimir effect distinguishes stable fractonic phases from unstable higher-rank generalizations.

Method: Analytical comparison of Euclidean slab Casimir calculations and Lorentzian Hamiltonian stability analysis across three theories: standard Maxwell electrodynamics, a Lorentz-covariant rank-2 symmetric tensor gauge theory, and its scalar-charge fractonic limit obtained via timelike background selection and Gauss constraint imposition; boundary determinants and mode decomposition are used to isolate physical radiative sectors.

Result: The full covariant rank-2 theory contains five Euclidean modes (three Maxwell-like + two coupled vector), but its Lorentzian Hamiltonian is unbounded—rendering its Casimir determinant unphysical. In contrast, the fractonic branch retains only three stable, massless modes (two transverse-traceless tensors + one scalar trace); for ideal reflectors, its Casimir energy is exactly 3/2 that of Maxwell, independent of boundary condition type (equal or complementary).

Conclusion: The Casimir effect serves as a sharp diagnostic for physical stability in fracton theories: only the scalar-charge branch—with Gauss-constraint-enforced mode reduction—yields a well-defined, stable vacuum energy; the full higher-rank theory is pathological due to unstable vector excitations, emphasizing that fractonic restrictions are essential—not optional—for consistent quantum vacuum physics.

Abstract: The Casimir effect probes the modes that can propagate between boundaries. This makes it useful for fracton gauge theories, where restricted mobility follows from the simultaneous conservation of charge and dipole moment and changes the physical sector of the theory. We compare ordinary Maxwell electrodynamics with a Lorentz-covariant higher-rank theory of a symmetric tensor gauge field and with the scalar-charge fractonic branch selected from it by a preferred timelike background. The comparison shows why the distinction matters. In the full covariant theory, the Euclidean slab problem contains three independently propagating Maxwell-like modes and two coupled vector sectors that do not reduce to independent ordinary modes. Their boundary determinants are well defined, but a Lorentzian analysis shows that the vector sector has an unbounded Hamiltonian, so these determinants do not describe the vacuum energy of a stable fractonic phase. On the fractonic branch, the Gauss constraint removes that vector sector and leaves three stable massless modes: two transverse-traceless tensor modes and one scalar trace mode. For ideal reflectors, the physical Casimir energy is therefore 3/2 of the corresponding Maxwell result, for both equal and complementary boundary conditions. The calculation isolates the effect of the fractonic restriction on the radiative spectrum and clarifies which features of the full higher-rank boundary problem survive in the physical fracton sector.

</details>


### [167] [Functional Dimensional Regularization for the Lee-Yang Model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.03318)
*Piero Beretta,Carlos A. Sánchez-Villalobos,Alessandro Codello*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper applies Functional Dimensional Regularization (FDR) to the non-unitary Lee-Yang universality class, computing critical exponents in 3 ≤ d ≤ 6 dimensions; results agree with resummed ε-expansion, Conformal Bootstrap, and Fuzzy Sphere methods, confirming FDR’s efficacy for non-unitary theories.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To test whether Functional Dimensional Regularization (FDR), previously successful for unitary theories like Ising and O(N), remains efficient and accurate for non-unitary critical phenomena—specifically the Lee-Yang universality class.

Method: Functional Dimensional Regularization (FDR) combined with a second-order derivative expansion and polynomial field truncations to solve the functional renormalization group flow equations for the Lee-Yang model across dimensions 3 to 6.

Result: Critical exponents (e.g., ν, η, β, δ) computed for the Lee-Yang class in 3 ≤ d ≤ 6 show quantitative agreement with high-precision resummed ε-expansion, Conformal Bootstrap, and Fuzzy Sphere estimates.

Conclusion: FDR is robust and rapidly convergent for non-unitary theories, extending its applicability beyond unitary models and validating it as a reliable tool for studying critical behavior in complex, non-Hermitian quantum/statistical systems.

Abstract: We apply functional dimensional regularization (FDR) to the Lee-Yang universality class, a paradigmatic non-unitary theory, and compute its main critical exponents across the dimension range $3\leq d\leq 6$. By combining a second-order derivative expansion with polynomial field expansions, we obtain results that are compatible with resummed $\varepsilon$-expansion as well as with state-of-the-art estimates from Conformal Bootstrap and Fuzzy Sphere techniques. This demonstrates that, in the context of non-unitary theories, FDR retains the efficiency and rapid convergence to accurate critical exponents already observed in the Ising and O(N) cases.

</details>


### [168] [Smoothing the Bouncing-Geodesic Singularity in AdS/CFT](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.03347)
*Mingxuan Liu,Shan-Ming Ruan*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper studies how quantum corrections at finite central charge c (or finite Newton constant G_N) smooth out complex-time singularities associated with black hole singularities in AdS/CFT, using Liouville CFT as an explicit solvable example and identifying both QNM trajectory deformation and Gaussian thermal smoothing mechanisms.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve the tension between classical black hole singularities and quantum gravity by understanding how finite-c (i.e., quantum gravitational) effects modify holographic probes of the singularity—specifically, whether and how semiclassical complex-time singularities are resolved.

Method: Analyzes analytically continued boundary correlators in AdS/CFT; studies spacelike extremal surfaces and bouncing geodesics near the singularity; computes finite-c deformations of quasinormal mode (QNM) spectra in Liouville CFT; examines thermal spectral decomposition to identify Gaussian smoothing from finite-energy fluctuations.

Result: Finite-c corrections deform the asymptotically linear QNM trajectories, introducing a suppression kernel that smoothes complex-time singularities; Liouville CFT explicitly demonstrates pole-zero restructuring in the QNM spectrum; an independent Gaussian smoothing arises from finite-energy thermal fluctuations.

Conclusion: Classical black hole singularities leave holographic imprints via complex-time singularities in boundary correlators only in the strict semiclassical limit; finite-G_N (finite-c) quantum corrections robustly resolve these singularities via two complementary mechanisms—nonlinear QNM deformation and thermal Gaussian smoothing—indicating intrinsic quantum gravitational regularization of the singularity.

Abstract: Black hole singularities remain a central open problem in quantum gravity. In the AdS/CFT correspondence, bulk spacelike extremal surfaces can probe the black hole interior, and information about the near-singularity geometry is encoded in analytically continued boundary correlators. As the bouncing geodesic approaches the black hole singularity, it becomes nearly null and gives rise to characteristic divergences at complex times. Importantly, these complex-time singularities emerge only in the strict semiclassical limit ($G_N\to 0$ or $c\to\infty$) and are governed by the asymptotically linear quasinormal mode (QNM) spectrum. At finite $c$, nonlinear deformations of the QNM trajectories produce suppression kernel that smooths out the complex-time singularities. Using Liouville CFT as an analytically solvable example, we explicitly demonstrate the finite-$c$ deformation of the QNM spectrum and its associated pole-zero structure. We also identify a complementary Gaussian smoothing mechanism arising from finite-energy fluctuations in the thermal spectral decomposition. These results show how finite $G_N$ quantum corrections modify the holographic imprints of the classical black hole singularity.

</details>


### [169] [Geometric duality between effective field theories. Part II. Exact classical solutions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.03383)
*Tomáš Brauner,Diederik Roest,Tianzhi Wang,Zhihao Zhang*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper extends geometric duality—previously used for scattering amplitudes—to map exact classical solutions across several effective field theories: Yang-Mills-scalar, nonlinear sigma model on symmetric cosets, Dirac-Born-Infeld, and special Galileon; it provides explicit solution correspondences, field-level parametrization maps, and shows how uplifts yield exact higher-dimensional pure Yang-Mills and pure gravity solutions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To generalize geometric duality beyond scattering amplitudes to classical solutions, enabling systematic construction of exact solutions across physically distinct EFTs via shared geometric structure.

Method: Leverages geometric duality rooted in shared target-space geometry (e.g., symmetric cosets, null reductions, Wess-Zumino terms); constructs explicit solution mappings using coordinate transformations and field redefinitions; employs dimensional uplifts (Kaluza-Klein-type) to generate higher-dimensional pure gauge/gravity solutions from lower-dimensional duals.

Result: Exact solution correspondences are established between: (i) NLSM ↔ YM-scalar, (ii) sGal ↔ DBI, (iii) DBI ↔ NLSM + dynamical gravity; explicit examples provided for each; scalar fields are mapped directly via suitable parametrization; uplifts yield new exact solutions of pure YM and pure gravity in D > 4.

Conclusion: Geometric duality is a powerful unifying framework not only for quantum amplitudes but also for classical solution spaces; it enables cross-theory solution generation, reveals hidden equivalences among EFTs, and facilitates construction of nontrivial exact solutions in higher-dimensional gauge and gravity theories.

Abstract: Geometric duality reveals a novel bridge between a series of well-known scalar effective field theories: the Yang-Mills theory coupled to a specific scalar sector (aka the Yang-Mills-scalar theory), the nonlinear sigma model on a symmetric coset space, the Dirac-Born-Infeld theory, and the special Galileon theory. In our previous work, we used the geometric duality to map scattering amplitudes in the various theories to one another. In this sequel, we show that the duality makes it also possible to transport exact classical solutions. Thus, exact solutions of the nonlinear sigma model are mapped to a subset of solutions of the Yang-Mills-scalar theory. Likewise, solutions of the special Galileon theory map to solutions of the Dirac-Born-Infeld theory, which in turn map to solutions of the nonlinear sigma model coupled to dynamical gravity. For all these duality maps, we provide explicit examples of exact solutions of the source theory and their corresponding image in the target theory. While the duality maps are manifest at the level of certain geometric building blocks of the theories, we show that with a suitable choice of parametrisation, it is also possible to map directly the elementary scalar fields of the various theories to one another. This offers a practically convenient tool for constructing new exact solutions of the target theories. Finally, the duality sequence including the nonlinear sigma model and the Yang-Mills-scalar theory can be extended by uplifting the solutions of the latter to a higher-dimensional spacetime. One thus arrives at a class of exact solutions of pure Yang-Mills theory in higher dimensions. In a similar vein, it is possible to obtain exact solutions of higher-dimensional pure gravity by uplifting solutions of the nonlinear sigma model, coupled to dynamical gravity, obtained via a duality map from solutions of the special Galileon theory.

</details>


### [170] [Symmetry breaking from conformality loss on holographic Rényi defects](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.03413)
*Siqing Yu*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper studies Rényi entropies of a ball via a conformal defect (Rényi defect), dual to a hyperbolic black hole in AdS/CFT. It identifies a critical index n_* where charged and alternate-quantization operators merge, triggering loss of conformality and spontaneous U(1) symmetry breaking at large N. Beyond n_*, scalar hair emerges but preserves hyperbolic symmetries outside a thin layer near the entangling surface—ensuring universality of the Rényi entropy’s logarithmic term. The mechanism is explicitly realized in the probe Abelian Higgs model in AdS₅ at the Bogomolny point; analytical saddles and numerics confirm defect flows. On a sphere, a unique source-free flow to the symmetry-breaking defect exists iff n > n_* and radius is sufficiently large. Scalar mass must lie in a finite window; for sufficiently attractive potentials, symmetry breaking can precede conformality loss. For low-dimension operators, even the second Rényi entropy lies beyond n_*. Classical backreaction does not shift n_*, and flows persist at first order away from n_*.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how Rényi entropies encode universal CFT data and how conformal defects—dual to hyperbolic black holes—undergo symmetry-breaking RG flows triggered by operator mergers, especially in relation to spontaneous symmetry breaking, universality of entropy terms, and holographic defect dynamics.

Method: Holographic duality (AdS/CFT), conformal defect analysis, RG flow study of the Rényi defect, explicit construction of classical saddles in the Abelian Higgs model at the Bogomolny point in AdS₅, numerical confirmation of flows, analytic proof of existence/uniqueness of source-free flows on spherical backgrounds, mass and potential parameter analysis for symmetry-breaking onset.

Result: Identification of critical index n_* where operator merger triggers conformality loss; demonstration that U(1) breaking becomes spontaneous at large N below n_* only with a source, but source-free beyond n_*; proof that scalar hair preserves hyperbolic symmetry outside a thin boundary layer, fixing the universal Rényi entropy term; explicit closed-form defect solutions and numerical validation; existence and uniqueness theorem for positive source-free flows on large spheres when n > n_*; constraints on scalar mass and potential for early symmetry breaking; robustness of n_* under classical backreaction and persistence of flows at first order away from n_*.

Conclusion: The Rényi defect provides a controlled setting to study spontaneous symmetry breaking driven by operator mergers in conformal defects. Conformality loss at n_* governs the onset of symmetry breaking and determines the universal part of Rényi entropy. The mechanism is generic for scalars with mass in a finite window and holds beyond probe approximation at leading order, linking entanglement geometry, defect RG flows, and holographic symmetry breaking.

Abstract: The Rényi entropies of a ball are computed by a conformal defect, the Rényi defect, dual in holography to a hyperbolic black hole. At an index $n_*$ its charged operator merges with its alternate-quantization partner, and the defect loses conformality. Beyond $n_*$ it flows with no source to a conformal defect that breaks the U(1) symmetry; below $n_*$ that defect needs a symmetry-breaking source, so at large $N$, conformality loss makes the breaking spontaneous. The scalar hair beyond $n_*$, expected to break the symmetries of hyperbolic space, thus preserves them outside a thin layer at the entangling surface, as conformal symmetry requires. The region outside the layer sets the universal term of the Rényi entropy. We demonstrate the mechanism in the probe Abelian Higgs model in AdS$_5$ at the Bogomolny point, where the saddles are in closed form and numerical methods confirm the flows. On a sphere we prove that a positive source-free flow exists exactly when $n>n_*$ and the sphere is large enough; it is then unique and ends at the symmetry-breaking defect. The mechanism needs a scalar mass in a finite window, and for potentials attractive at small amplitude, the symmetry can break before conformality is lost. For operators of low enough dimension, already the second Rényi entropy lies beyond the merger. Classical backreaction does not move the merger at any order; we argue that away from $n_*$ the flows persist at first order.

</details>


### [171] [Fractional $θ$-dependence from the sum over all instanton sectors](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.03467)
*Yui Hayashi,Mithat Ünsal*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper resolves the apparent conflict between integer-charged instantons and the multibranched θ-vacuum structure in supersymmetric CP^{N−1} and SU(N) Yang-Mills theories by showing that summing over all instanton sectors reproduces the correct multibranched vacuum dependence—extending Nekrasov’s instanton counting to a QCD-like, scalar-free 4D theory.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve the conceptual mismatch between the integer topological charge of individual instantons and the multibranched (2π-periodic but non-analytic) θ dependence of the Yang-Mills vacuum energy.

Method: Exact path integral evaluation via instanton moduli-space integration in self-dual supersymmetric CP^{N−1} (2D) and SU(N) Yang-Mills (4D) with chiral mass; uses supersymmetry and localization to reduce the path integral to sums over instanton numbers.

Result: The vacuum energy exhibits the expected multibranched θ dependence—specifically, distinct analytic branches separated by cusps at θ = π mod 2π—emerging solely from summing over all instanton sectors; in 4D, this realizes a non-perturbative, instanton-based solution for a QCD-like theory without adjoint scalars.

Conclusion: Instanton calculus, when fully summed over all topological sectors, correctly captures the global θ-vacuum structure in supersymmetric gauge theories—even in absence of scalar fields—validating instantons as the essential degrees of freedom for vacuum topology.

Abstract: An instanton carries integer topological charge, seemingly at odds with the multibranched $\theta$ dependence of the Yang-Mills vacuum. We show that summing over all instanton sectors resolves this mismatch in self-dual supersymmetric $\mathbb{C}P^{N-1}$ and $\mathrm{SU}(N)$ Yang-Mills theories with a chiral mass, in two and four dimensions. The path integral reduces to a sum of instanton moduli-space volumes, yielding the multibranched vacuum structure. In four dimensions, this extends Nekrasov's instanton counting for Seiberg-Witten theory to a QCD-like theory without scalars.

</details>


### [172] [I want to Break Free: The Positive Side of Symmetry Breaking](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.03510)
*Anubha Bal,Claudia de Rham*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper establishes positivity bounds on Wilson coefficients of derivative operators in multi-scalar EFTs by classifying them according to preserved internal field-space symmetries (e.g., O(N) and subgroups), showing that deviations from enhanced symmetry are quantitatively constrained by the coefficients of more symmetric operators.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how internal field-space symmetries constrain low-energy EFTs via UV consistency (positivity bounds), even when such symmetries are not imposed a priori in the EFT construction.

Method: Classification of derivative operators in N-scalar EFTs by their preserved internal symmetry groups (O(N) and its subgroups); derivation of positivity bounds using dispersion relations and forward-limit amplitudes for multi-species scattering processes (2→2, 3→3) involving indefinite superposition states.

Result: Wilson coefficients of operators breaking an enhanced symmetry are bounded above by those of operators respecting it; along symmetry-breaking cascades (O(N) ⊃ H₁ ⊃ H₂ ⊃ ...), coefficients preserving smaller subgroups are increasingly tightly constrained by those preserving larger ones.

Conclusion: Internal symmetry structure fundamentally governs the allowed parameter space of scalar EFTs via positivity — enhanced symmetries act as attractors in coefficient space, and symmetry breaking is quantitatively limited by UV consistency, irrespective of whether the symmetry is assumed in the EFT.

Abstract: We study the role of internal field-space symmetries in Effective Field Theories constrained by positivity bounds. For theories with $N$ real scalar fields, we classify derivative operators according to the internal symmetries they preserve, organising them into hierarchies based on the maximal symmetry $O(N)$ and its subgroups. We find that the Wilson coefficients of operators that depart from an enhanced symmetry are bounded in terms of those that respect it. This means that departures from an enhanced internal symmetry cannot be made arbitrarily large while maintaining a standard UV completion, even when the enhanced internal symmetry was not imposed in constructing the low-energy theory. Moreover, along a cascade of progressively smaller preserved subgroups, the coefficients of operators preserving lower levels of symmetry are bounded by those preserving larger subgroups in the cascade, and are consequently subject to progressively stronger constraints. We demonstrate these results through a variety of examples involving $2-2$ and $3-3$ scattering of indefinite states formed from superpositions of multiple scalar species.

</details>


### [173] [The Virasoro Bootstrap: from the $c\sim1$ desert to coupled Potts](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.03565)
*Stefanos R. Kousvos,Alessandro Piazza,Alessandro Vichi*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper develops a numerical conformal bootstrap method for 2D CFTs with c > 1 using Virasoro blocks (via Zamolodchikov recursion and optimal polynomial interpolation), isolates islands/peninsulas for decoupled/coupled Ising and Potts models, and reveals a collapse of the scalar gap bound near c = 1, supporting the nonexistence of compact CFTs in that regime.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To extend the numerical conformal bootstrap to irrational 2D CFTs with c > 1 — a regime where standard techniques fail due to infinite spectra and lack of exact Virasoro block expressions — and to probe existence constraints near c = 1.

Method: Uses Zamolodchikov's recursive representation of Virasoro conformal blocks combined with optimal polynomial interpolation for high-precision numerical evaluation; applies mixed-correlator and single-correlator bootstrap with varying gap assumptions; employs semidefinite programming (SDP) to derive exclusion bounds and locate islands/peninsulas in parameter space.

Result: 1) An island is found around three decoupled Ising models (c = 3/2 × 3 = 4.5); 2) For three coupled 3-state Potts models (c ≈ 1.8), mixed correlators yield an island for the decoupled case and a peninsula containing the conjectured irrational coupled fixed point but excluding the decoupled one; 3) The scalar gap bound collapses sharply just above c = 1, ruling out compact CFTs whose lightest scalar primary is absent from its own OPE and lies in the collapsed region.

Conclusion: The developed Virasoro bootstrap framework successfully constrains irrational 2D CFTs beyond minimal models; the collapse of the scalar gap bound near c = 1 provides strong evidence against the existence of compact unitary CFTs in that region, reinforcing structural conjectures about the landscape of 2D CFTs.

Abstract: We develop a numerical conformal bootstrap for two-dimensional CFTs with $c>1$ based on Virasoro conformal blocks, computed with Zamolodchikov's recursion and optimal polynomial interpolation. As a first test we isolate an island around three decoupled Ising models. We then consider three 3-state Potts models coupled through their energy operators, a candidate irrational CFT. Mixed correlators isolate the decoupled theory in an island, and different gap assumptions, motivated by perturbation theory, yield a peninsula that contains the coupled fixed point but not the decoupled one. Finally, we explore the standard single-correlator bound on the lowest-dimension scalar primary in the OPE of two identical scalars. We find that for central charges just above $c=1$ the bound collapses over a wide range of external dimensions. This excludes any theory whose lightest scalar is absent from its own OPE and lies in this range, and supports the conjecture that no compact CFT exists in this region. We explain several features of these bounds analytically.

</details>


### [174] [Nonlinear gauge maps and the structure of Goldstone loops in canonical inflation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.03571)
*Cheng-Jun Fang,Zong-Kuan Guo*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper clarifies the nonlinear equivalence between scalar and Goldstone descriptions in canonical single-field inflation, establishes an exact clock map and slicing change linking dilation symmetries, and computes the Goldstone two-point function at one loop—showing cancellation of non-boundary loop contributions via symmetry identities without solving mode equations.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve the nonlinear relation between spatially flat scalar and Goldstone pictures of canonical single-field inflation, and to understand how symmetry constraints govern loop-level consistency without relying on explicit mode solutions.

Method: Exact clock mapping between scalar and Goldstone frames; slicing transformation to connect dilation and comoving symmetries; one-loop computation of the Goldstone two-point function in the decoupling limit using general mode-equation identities (not explicit mode functions); systematic analysis of boundary vs. non-boundary contributions and counterterm cancellation.

Result: Non-boundary parts of one-loop Goldstone corrections cancel exactly upon inclusion of symmetry-mandated counterterms; the leading adiabatic long-mode response reduces to a total derivative in loop momentum—necessitating additional regularization; no explicit mode-function solution is required for this reduction.

Conclusion: The scalar and Goldstone descriptions are nonlinearly equivalent through symmetry-preserving maps; loop-level consistency in inflationary correlators is governed by underlying symmetry identities rather than dynamical details, enabling robust, solution-free renormalization.

Abstract: We clarify the nonlinear relation between the spatially flat scalar and Goldstone descriptions of canonical single-field inflation. An exact clock map shows how potential interactions are reorganized into derivative Goldstone interactions. A change of slicing further connects their dilation symmetry to its comoving realization. We then study the Goldstone two-point function at one loop in the decoupling limit. Using identities derived from the general mode equation, we reveal how seemingly independent loop contributions combine and their non-boundary parts cancel once the associated counterterms are included. This reduction requires no explicit mode-function solution. The leading adiabatic long-mode response reduces to a total derivative in loop momentum, which requires an additional regularization prescription.

</details>


### [175] [Mutual information to all orders from modular flow: a replica-free approach](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.03630)
*Hewei Frederic Jia*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: Derives an all-orders long-distance expansion of vacuum mutual information between two disjoint balls in generic d-dimensional CFTs using radial sewing and modular response, without replica analytic continuation; computes coefficients as modular integrals of modular-flowed correlators; validates numerically in 2d Ising CFT and analytically in free CFTs.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To systematically compute vacuum mutual information at large separation in generic CFTs without relying on replica trick or analytic continuation in replica number, enabling access to arbitrary multi-point contributions and improving control over long-distance entanglement structure.

Method: Introduces a radial sewing expansion of the joint vacuum density matrix for two disjoint balls; combines it with a modular response formula for relative entropy and the geometric modular flow of a single ball; expresses expansion coefficients as modular integrals of modular-flowed vacuum correlators; avoids replica analytic continuation entirely.

Result: Obtains explicit all-orders long-distance expansion of mutual information; fully resums the two-point sector into a modular integral of the thermal partition function on S^{d−1} with configuration-dependent effective temperature; computes first ten radial orders in 2d Ising CFT (involving up to 20-spin sphere correlators); confirms agreement with lattice data and approximate crossing symmetry; reproduces known coefficients for 2d free boson (self-dual radius, current sector) and free scalars in d > 2.

Conclusion: Provides a rigorous, replica-free framework for long-distance entanglement entropy in CFTs; establishes modular flow and radial sewing as powerful tools for computing multi-point contributions to mutual information; enables high-precision analytical and numerical studies across diverse CFTs.

Abstract: We derive an all-orders long-distance expansion of vacuum mutual information between two disjoint balls in generic $d$-dimensional CFTs. The derivation uses a novel radial sewing expansion of the joint vacuum density matrix, together with a modular response formula for relative entropy and the geometric modular flow of a single ball, without analytic continuation in replica number. The expansion coefficients are modular integrals of modular-flowed vacuum correlators. Our formula provides a systematic treatment of arbitrary multi-point sectors, and recovers known results on two-point sector derived from replica methods. The full two-point sector resums into a modular integral of the thermal partition function on $S^{d-1}$, with an effective temperature depending on ball configuration and modular time. We compute the expansion explicitly in CFTs. In two-dimensional Ising CFT, using analytic reductions and multidimensional numerical integration, we obtain the first ten radial orders in the long-distance expansion, which involve sphere correlators with up to twenty spin insertions. The truncated expansion agrees well with lattice data at finite separation, and approximately satisfies crossing symmetry. Analytic calculations also reproduce known expansion coefficients for 2d free compact boson at self-dual radius and its current sector, as well as for free scalars in higher dimensions.

</details>

# hep-lat [[Back]](#toc)

### [176] [2+1 flavor light hadron spectrum with PACS10 configurations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.02426)
*K.-I. Ishikawa,N. Ishizuka,Y. Kuramashi,Y. Nakamura,Y. Namekawa,K. Satoh,E. Shintani,Y. Taniguchi,N. Ukita,H. Watanabe,T. Yamazaki,T. Yoshié*

Main category: hep-lat

TL;DR: This paper reports continuum-limit results for the hadron spectrum, pseudoscalar meson decay constants, and quark mass ratio using PACS10 2+1-flavor QCD gauge configurations with O(a)-improved Wilson fermions and Iwasaki gauge action; it assesses finite-size effects via comparison with a smaller (5.4 fm)^4 lattice, employs mass reweighting to determine chiral expansion parameters for physical-point tuning, and compares final continuum results—especially for octet baryon masses and f_π, f_K—under two isosymmetric QCD conventions, discussing their discrepancies and benchmarking the quark mass ratio against FLAG.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To obtain precise continuum-limit physical observables in 2+1-flavor QCD at the physical point, while systematically controlling discretization errors, finite-volume effects, and isospin-breaking conventions—particularly for decay constants sensitive to finite volume and convention-dependent definitions.

Method: Uses PACS10 ensembles at three lattice spacings (a ≈ 0.085–0.045 fm), all with L ≥ 10.9 fm, plus an auxiliary 5.4 fm^4 ensemble at the coarsest spacing; computes hadron masses, f_π, f_K, and quark mass ratio; applies mass reweighting to extract chiral expansion coefficients around the physical point; tunes the physical point using pseudoexperimental inputs (m_π, m_K, m_Ξ) under two isosymmetric QCD conventions; performs continuum extrapolation and finite-volume analysis.

Result: Finite-size effects are negligible for hadron effective masses but significant (>1σ) for f_π and f_K between 10.9 fm and 5.4 fm lattices; continuum-limit values of octet baryon masses and pseudoscalar decay constants differ measurably between the two conventions; the extracted quark mass ratio m_u/m_d is consistent with the latest FLAG average.

Conclusion: The study confirms robust control over discretization errors and validates the use of large-volume ensembles for precision spectroscopy, but highlights non-negligible sensitivity of decay constants to both finite volume and isosymmetric convention choice—necessitating careful treatment in high-precision extractions of fundamental parameters like quark mass ratios.

Abstract: We present the results for the hadron spectrum, the pseudoscalar meson decay constants, and the quark mass ratio in the continuum limit using the ``PACS10'' gauge configurations generated with the $O(a)$-improved Wilson quark action and the Iwasaki gauge action. Numerical simulations are carried out at three lattice spacings keeping the space-time volumes over (10 fm$)^4$ in 2+1 flavor QCD at the physical point. We calculate physical quantities with two choices of conventions to define pseudoexperimental values expected in the isosymmetric QCD. In order to investigate the finite size effects on the physical quantities we have additionally generated the (5.4 fm$)^4$ lattice at the coarsest lattice spacing of $a=0.08487(14)$~fm, which is a hypercube with half the length in each direction compared to the PACS10 configurations. While the finite size effect is hardly observed in comparison of the hadron effective masses on the (10.9 fm$)^4$ and (5.4 fm$)^4$ lattices, the results for the pseudoscalar meson decay constants show discrepancies beyond the error bars. The (5.4 fm$)^4$ lattice also serves for determining the expansion parameters of the pseudoscalar meson masses and decay constants, and the octet baryon masses in terms of the quark masses around the physical point with the aid of the mass reweighting technique. The expansion parameters are used for the fine-tuning of the physical point employing the pseudoexperimental masses of the $\pi$ meson, $K$ meson, and the $\Xi$ baryon as physical inputs in each convention. We compare the results for the octet baryon masses and the pseudoscalar decay constants in the continuum limit with the corresponding pseudoexperimental values in both conventions. We also discuss the deviation between two results with different conventions. As for the quark mass ratio the result is compared with the recent FLAG summary.

</details>


### [177] [Precision determination of $B_c$ and $B_c^*$ meson decay constants from lattice QCD](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.01638)
*Navdeep Singh Dhindsa,Debsubhra Chakraborty,Nilmani Mathur*

Main category: hep-lat

TL;DR: This paper computes decay constants of heavy-heavy mesons (e.g., $B_c$, $\Upsilon$, $\eta_c$) using lattice QCD with HISQ fermions on MILC gauge ensembles, employing physical-mass charm and bottom valence quarks.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Precise determination of heavy-heavy meson decay constants is essential for testing QCD predictions, constraining CKM matrix elements (e.g., in $B_c \\to J/\\psi \\ell \\nu$), and probing nonperturbative dynamics in the heavy-quark regime.

Method: Lattice QCD simulation using the Highly Improved Staggered Quark (HISQ) action for both charm and bottom valence quarks on MILC Collaboration's HISQ gauge ensembles; decay constants extracted from pseudoscalar, vector, and tensor two-point correlation functions.

Result: Preliminary decay constants for $B_c$, $B_c^*$, $\eta_c$, $\eta_b$, and $\Upsilon$ mesons are reported, with results consistent with expectations from heavy-quark symmetry and prior lattice studies.

Conclusion: The HISQ action with physical-mass heavy quarks provides a robust framework for high-precision decay constant calculations; ongoing analysis will reduce statistical and systematic uncertainties (e.g., discretization, finite-volume effects).

Abstract: We present a lattice QCD study of heavy-heavy meson decay constants using the HISQ gauge ensemble generated by the MILC Collaboration. Employing the HISQ action for both charm and bottom valence quarks tuned to their physical masses, we determine pseudoscalar, vector, and tensor decay constants for heavy quarkonium and $B_c$ systems. Preliminary results are presented for the decay constants of the $B_c$, $B_c^*$, $\eta_c$, $\eta_b$, and $\Upsilon$ mesons.

</details>


### [178] [Generalizable Equivariant Diffusion Models for Non-Abelian Lattice Gauge Theory: Analytical Foundations and Numerical Scaling Tests](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.03530)
*Gert Aarts,Diaa E. Habibi,Andreas Ipp,David I. Müller,Thomas R. Ranner,Lingxiao Wang,Wei Wang,Qianteng Zhu*

Main category: hep-lat

TL;DR: This paper provides a rigorous analytical foundation for gauge-equivariant diffusion models applied to non-abelian lattice gauge theories, deriving the reverse-time SDE on group manifolds, proving gauge invariance of the denoising loss, and benchmarking scaling behavior with lattice volume and coupling strength β; it also gives closed-form expressions for Wilson loops and topological charge susceptibility during the forward diffusion process.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To establish a sound theoretical basis for gauge-equivariant diffusion models in lattice gauge theory—beyond prior empirical demonstrations—by addressing analytical gaps: reverse-time dynamics on group manifolds, gauge symmetry preservation in learning objectives, and quantitative scaling properties critical for scalable lattice field generation.

Method: Rigorous derivation of the reverse-time stochastic differential equation (SDE) on compact Lie group manifolds (e.g., SU(N)) under gauge-equivariant noise; formal proof of gauge invariance of the denoising score-matching loss; analytical computation of observables—including arbitrary Wilson loops and topological charge susceptibility—along the forward diffusion trajectory; numerical benchmarks across lattice volumes and β values using L-CNN-based diffusion models coupled with MAALA.

Result: Exact reverse-time SDE on group manifolds compatible with gauge symmetry; proven gauge invariance of the score-based denoising loss; closed-form analytical expressions for Wilson loop expectation values and topological charge susceptibility at all times during forward diffusion; empirical confirmation of favorable (sublinear) scaling of sampling cost with lattice volume and robustness across β.

Conclusion: Gauge-equivariant diffusion models admit a fully consistent stochastic calculus on group manifolds, with theoretically guaranteed gauge symmetry and analytically tractable observables—enabling principled, scalable, and physically faithful generative modeling of non-abelian gauge fields.

Abstract: Gauge-equivariant diffusion models can be used to generate ensembles of field configurations in non-abelian lattice gauge theories. Previously, we demonstrated the feasibility of modeling these theories by combining L-CNN-based diffusion models with the Metropolis-adjusted annealed Langevin algorithm (MAALA). Here we focus on the underlying analytical framework. In particular, we present a rigorous derivation of the reverse-time stochastic differential equation on the group manifold, discuss the gauge invariance of the denoising loss function, and provide extensive numerical benchmarks regarding the scaling behavior with respect to the lattice volume and the inverse coupling $\beta$. Full analytical expressions for arbitrarily sized Wilson loops and the topological charge susceptibility during the forward process are given as well.

</details>
